# Design/build deviation ledger — P04

Decision: 2026-09-13. User selected **Option A: intended design authority plus a small deviation ledger**.

## Authority and change control

The [GDD](../design-godot-v7.md) and its explicitly adopted decision contracts define **intended behavior**. More specific, explicitly adopted resolutions supersede the historical rule they identify. A silent discrepancy between two current design sources is unresolved documentation, not permission to pick either version; reconcile it with the accepted decision and update the mirrors.

Verified code, resource data and scene/configuration bindings at a **pinned game revision/build** describe current implementation. They are evidence about that build, not authority to reverse an approved target design. Reading a resource proves its stored value, not that the game loads or applies it correctly; implementation and runtime claims need corresponding evidence.

When target and verified build differ, record the discrepancy below, implement/validate the accepted behavior or obtain an explicit design change. An intentional deviation stays open or explicitly deferred with its reason and acceptance condition; it does not become the new target merely because it shipped. Unknown/unavailable implementation stays **unverified**, not automatically compliant or defective.

Where the target deliberately leaves a number provisional or unspecified, record that fact. Do not invent a requirement or silently promote an unverified resource into the intended design. Later approval updates the GDD, contracts, mirrors and relevant [consistency checks](../scripts/README.md) together.

## Confirmed current-build deviations

**Superseded 2026-09-13 by the Package 11 closeout.** When this section was written no pinned game source or build was available to this docs workspace. That is no longer true: Package 11 was implemented directly in the game repository, nine reconnaissance dossiers read the build in full at commit `31fed14`, and twenty workstreams verified their own areas against it. The confirmed entries are in the Package 11 table below; the historical-lead table that follows is dispositioned rather than deleted.

For each future confirmed entry, record:
- Stable deviation ID, affected system/hero/level/mode and current state: confirmed-open, explicitly deferred, or resolved.
- Intended behavior with accepted decision/date and document reference.
- Actual observed behavior/value with pinned game commit/build, file/scene/resource identifiers and dated reproduction/test evidence.
- Owner when assigned; otherwise **Unassigned**. Do not invent a person, date or delivery commitment.
- Resolution path and concrete acceptance criteria, including content coverage/migration/balance where required; link explicit approval for any target change.
- Closing build/evidence and remaining exclusions. Retain the record after closure rather than deleting its history.

A partial fix remains partial. A configuration edit alone does not close missing scene placement, save migration or runtime validation. Status promotion follows [P02's evidence rules](PRODUCTION_SCOPE.md#p02--current-status-and-evidence).

## Confirmed open deviations — Package 11

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **VERIFY-PAR-SECONDS** — F01 level par and F11 remaining-route budgets | V01a: shared-level `parSeconds` is the **slowest eligible hero's median Normal required-route time** with the normally unlocked kit and no purchased upgrades, rounded upward; each distinct Level 4A route gets its own benchmark. F11 additionally requires a **separately measured** `remainingRouteSeconds` per level/variant/checkpoint/difficulty, plus its depleted-resource baseline and a `budgetVersion`. | Package 11 A3 (branch `p11/A3`) authors **provisional** values, because no measurement pass has happened. Par is seeded as `authored room count x 90 s`, rounded up to the nearest 30 s: L02 360, L03 540, L04 360, L05 270, L06 360, L07 630, L08 360, L09 360, L10 360, L11 360, L12 540, L13 450, L14 540, L15 540. Remaining-route budgets are seeded as fractions of par in `StoryLevelControllerBase.RemainingRouteFractionFor`: **Entry 0.90, Middle 0.45, PreBoss 0.00** (PreBoss has no timed route, so it falls to the 25-point floor). A `BudgetVersion` field now exists on the attempt record (`StoryAttemptState`, value 1), so only the measurement half remains. **Amended 2026-09-29 (Package 13 W2, S27):** Level 4A is retired, so the "each distinct Level 4A route gets its own benchmark" clause lapses; the measurement set is the fourteen timed Levels 2–15. Level 5 now also carries the Eraser debut encounter on its boat deck, which its measured par must include; its seeded 270 s did not. **Amended 2026-10-12 (gameplay update):** every seeded par is now further from any measurement: encounter locks force every fight on Levels 2–15 (`USER-ENCOUNTER-LOCKS`; Level 5's 270 s now covers four locks), the −20 % run speed makes traversal × 1.25 (`USER-MOVEMENT-SPEED-CUT`) and turn-taking lengthens each fight (`USER-ENEMY-TURN-TAKING`). Until a re-measurement, one named constant, `TimelineIntegrityRules.ProvisionalParScale` = **1.5** (*provisional*, plan §2.3), scales the par at its single consumption point through `ProvisionallyScaledSeconds`: `StoryManager.BeginIntegrityClock` (so `ParSecondsForCurrentLevel` = authored × 1.5; Level 2's 360 s runs on a 540 s clock) and `SetRecoveryRouteSeconds` (the F11 route budgets × 1.5, so the recovery minima keep their point values). The controllers keep their authored `ParSeconds`; Florence and the Tutorial stay untimed. A par run now ends at 66.7 / 55.6 / 44.4 % on Easy / Normal / Hard (it was 50 / 33.3 / 16.7). The bots' `-MaxSeconds 0` budget carries the scale (`PlaytestOptions.AutoMaxSeconds` = ceil(max(par × 1.5, 300) × 2.0 / 1.5 / 1.2) + 60; Level 2 on Normal 600 → 870 s). Pinned by `EncounterLockRulesTests` and, rewritten in place, `TimelineIntegrityTests.TheDenominatorIsFixedAtLoadAndNeverRecalculatedFromSurvivors` and `StoryLevelControllerBaseTests.EveryTimedCampaignLevelAuthorsAParSecondsAndTheClockArmsFromIt`. **The Phase C bot batch (`gud*`: Levels 1–15, Joan and Lincoln, `play`, Easy / Normal / Hard, seeds 0–1, `-MaxSeconds 0`; 2026-10-13), reviewing the scale against measured run times:** every completed run reached the PreBoss anchor, where the gauge freezes, with Integrity to spare — Easy (57 completions) 66–97 % on the timed levels, Level 12 the lowest at 66–85 %; Normal (31) 72–93 %, Level 5 the lowest at 72–77 % and Level 11 at 74–76 %; Florence, untimed, reads 100. With no scale the used fraction grows × 1.5, so the worst would end near 49 % (Level 12 Easy) and 61 % (Level 11 Normal): no completion would have become a Timer collapse. The batch's one Timer collapse was a bot stuck in Level 10's trapdoor recess after the boss fell (a nav gap). The bots play faster than a person exploring, so the batch neither requires the scale nor shows it too generous for a human: **keep 1.5 provisional until a human pass** (`D:/Projects/absolum_research/update_2026-10-12/phaseC_batch2.md`). | Unassigned | A measurement pass replaces every par with a median Normal required-route time and every route fraction with a per-anchor, per-difficulty measured value, adds `budgetVersion`, and re-validates that each enabled anchor reaches the pre-boss lock with positive Integrity. The table is pinned in `StoryLevelControllerBaseTests.EveryTimedCampaignLevelAuthorsAParSecondsAndTheClockArmsFromIt`, so the replacement is a single-file edit. **These numbers must never be quoted as measured.** |

Encounter balance for L3/L4 (+140 and +260 HP) has not been re-validated; the boss-arena width checks are unaffected because HP does not move `RangedRangeThreshold`. |

| **DEFER-CPU-SNAPSHOT** — F19 CPU admission rolls and rollback resimulation | `CPU_COMBAT_POLICY.md` (2026-09-12): "Roll admission once per opportunity using the seeded match PRNG… Snapshot the admission result, opportunity/attack IDs and PRNG state for deterministic resimulation." | Package 11 A9b (branch `p11/A9b`) implements the once-per-opportunity rule with `_grabOpportunityID` / `_attackExecutionID` inside `FighterCpuController`, whose xorshift `_randomState` is derived from the per-match world seed (`FighterSimulationDriver` line ~145, pinned by `FighterMatchSeedTests`) but is **deliberately outside the rollback snapshot** — the controller is an input source, exactly like a human pad, and Package 6 §2.5 states that determinism comes from the seed plus the observation sequence. `FighterCpuBehaviorTests.TheExpandedTableStaysBitIdenticalForTheSameSeedAndObservationStream` is the standing pin on that property. Produced input frames are recorded and replayed on rollback, so an admission decision is never re-sampled; the document's literal request (snapshotting AI state into the simulation) would make CPU frames simulation-derived rather than recorded, which is the architecture Package 6 rejected. `GrabOpportunityID` and `AttackExecutionID` are exposed as read-only controller properties. **Package 12 (2026-09-27): held under plan D15 pending a user ruling and left untouched.** | Unassigned | Either (a) an explicit ruling that the recorded-input model satisfies the contract's intent, closing this entry with no code change, or (b) a Package 7 netcode pass that moves CPU input generation into the snapshot — which requires a Klotho component ID (none is assigned; §2.7 forbids self-assignment), a protocol change for CPU-vs-human input provenance, and re-running the rollback readiness gate. **Do not "fix" this by re-sampling the RNG during resimulation**: that reintroduces the desync this architecture prevents. |

| **DEFER-ROSTER-ENUM** — `FighterCharacterID` is an enum, not roster data | V7.5's roster-agnostic mandate: never hardcode roster size, never enumerate the cast in load-bearing ways. Package 11 A6b routed every production consumer through the manifest-backed `FTT.Core.CharacterRoster`. | `FTT.FighterSim.FighterCharacterID` remains an enum, and is the one documented exception. Its ordinals serialize into deterministic snapshots and into the network protocol, and `zoneTypeID = (int)id * 10 + slot` derives from them, so it cannot become data without a rollback and protocol pass. The **append-only contract** is written into `CharacterRoster`'s doc comment and `AGENTS.md`: a new member takes the next ordinal, nothing is reordered, renumbered or reused. | Unassigned | A tenth character is the forcing event. Either (a) accept the enum permanently under the append-only contract, closing this entry, or (b) a Package 7 pass that moves character identity out of the snapshot, which requires a protocol version bump and a full re-run of the rollback readiness gate. Until then, adding a roster row means adding an enum member — never editing an existing one. **Amended 2026-09-29 (Package 13 W5, plan D3):** the roster swap exercised the contract for the first time — `Tubman = 9` appended, `Pocahontas = 8` kept as an `[Obsolete]` reserved ordinal, `RollbackInputPacket.ProtocolVersion` 3 → **4** (a v3 packet is refused), persistent-object type 4, zone type 83 and kit phases 19–20 retired, never reused. Rollback readiness passed across the nine mirrors with Tubman at 9. Option (a) is now the de-facto practice; the row stays open until it is ratified. |
| ~~**DEFER-SEALING-ANCHOR**~~ — N01's per-level sealing interaction | N01 generalises Level 15's `TemporalCoreAnchor` to every boss level: boss defeat → defeat dialogue → regain control beside the boss's physical pickup and a marked sealing anchor → a single Interact "Seal Timeline — Complete Level" → one atomic transaction → restoration vignette → results, with `AwaitingSeal` **persisted**. | Package 11 A10 implements the **once-only commit half** of the transaction; the anchor interaction itself is not built. **Closed 2026-09-27 (Package 12 W8, branch `p12/W8`), D12(a): AwaitingSeal is persisted.** Boss defeat commits `attemptState.sealReadiness` (boss-defeated, anchor ID, pending boss pickup) as an F10 critical event through `FTT.Environment.SealReadiness`; the defeat beats play; the generic sealing anchor (`TemporalCoreAnchor.CreateSealingAnchor`, prompt `seal_timeline_prompt`) arms beside the boss pickup; a single Interact commits the completion transaction **before** the restoration vignette (the exit beat) and the results; remaining Extractors take `ShutDownBySeal` (inert, no credit). A pre-seal load rebuilds the boss defeated (never respawned), re-arms the anchor and respawns an uncollected boss pickup once. Covers Levels 1–14 and all nine 4A variants (Florence hand-wired through the shared rules; the Level 13 Mirror through `CommitNonStandardBossDefeat`); Level 15 keeps its scene Prime Anchor but persists readiness the same way. Refused during Time Freeze, the Post-Landing Hold and death. Deviations: the prompt is static text, not a live C01c binding glyph; the vignette is the existing exit dialogue plus a `seal_timeline_sealed` notice (placeholder); Level 14's local-objective copy and the Act III destination wording are unchanged. See `docs/handoffs/P12_W8.md`. | Package 12 W8 | Closed on headless evidence: `SealingAnchorTests` (6), `SecretCachePlacementTests` (anchor sweep). | **DEFER-STEAM-RPT** — Steam Remote Play Together | M01 asks for a Remote Play Together entry, hidden when Steam integration is unavailable. | Steamworks is not installed and `NetworkManager.cs`'s `SteamTransportAvailable` is a hard `false`, so "hide when unavailable" would hide it permanently. Remote Play Together needs **no netcode** — it is host-side streamed local multiplayer, so the existing local shared-screen 1v1 path *is* the feature. Package 11 A6 therefore ships **only** the localized notice (`fighter_remote_play_notice`, a focusable label in the Character Select footer), not an entry point. | Unassigned | Steam SDK integration (Package 7 scope), after which the notice can become a real entry gated on `SteamTransportAvailable`. No netcode work is required for the feature itself. |
| ~~**VERIFY-SECRET-CACHE**~~ — the optional dust pool has no physical source | F05 budgets an optional pool per level, split between Extractors and a discoverable secret, and states that a thorough run pays **1,000 base dust**. | **No campaign level authors a `SecretCache`.** The allocator, the manifest rows and the `{level}.secret` source IDs all exist and allocate correctly, but nothing in any scene issues them, so the discovery half of every optional pool is unreachable and a thorough run cannot reach 1,000 today. Level 4A is the sharpest case: `LegacyLevelControllerBase` builds neither a secret cache nor an Extractor, so its whole 10-dust optional pool has no physical source; each variant's manifest reserves `level_04a_<hero>.secret` for one to be attached to. **Closed 2026-09-27 (Package 12 W8, branch `p12/W8`).** `SecretCache` now issues its reserved `{level}.secret` award once as a physical pickup (`DustAwardSource.Secret`), paid and attributed at collection, and a resume that finds a secret found but uncollected respawns the pickup once. `StoryLevelControllerBase` places the cache after `BuildLevel` (so the sealed 4A `BuildLevel` gets it too) at a per-level `SecretCachePosition`, abstract on `LegacyLevelControllerBase`. Caches are authored in Levels 2–15 and all nine 4A variants (Level 1 reserves none by design), off the critical path and reachable with the worst character's double jump. | Package 12 W8 | Closed on headless evidence: `SecretCacheAwardTests` (3), `SecretCachePlacementTests` (3), and `ThoroughRunCollectionWalkTests`, which loads every level a run visits, completes every physically placed Extractor/hold node and secret through its real path, and collects exactly 1,000 base dust for every hero. Nobody has walked to a secret in a playthrough. |
| **DEFER-SAVE-VERSION-SPLIT** — one schema counter for two payloads | Separate story and global payloads would ideally version independently, so a global-only change does not invalidate story saves. | `SaveSchemaMigrator.CurrentVersion` is **shared by both payloads**, so Package 11's global-side v6 work (the binding rename, the match-settings normalization, the seen-dialogue union) also bumped every story payload. Not urgent — the coupling costs a redundant story migration pass, nothing more. |
| ~~**VERIFY-05**~~ — Death Rewind recovery (R01–R03, 2026-09-26) | 300-frame (5 s) buffer with 2.5 s full playback; Story recovery returns control at landing without entering `Respawning`; a 60-frame Post-Landing Hold keeps the world frozen and invulnerable while Integrity and hero timers run; 2 s protection starts at the thaw ([temporal contract](TEMPORAL_STATE_CONTRACT.md)). | **Closed 2026-09-27 (Package 12 W1, branch `p12/W1`).** R01 shipped at `21b0747`. R02: `PlayerController.CompleteStoryRewind(landing, hp, grounded)` revives through the explicit Story edge `Dead -> Idle/Airborne`; no `Respawning` enter or exit fires (pinned through `PlayerController.StateTransitioned`). R03: `ChronalRewindManager.PostLandingHoldFrames` (60) ends the death rewind, the Level 0 scripted rewind, the in-session Collapse resume and the Anchor Snap; the frozen set is the union of the Time Freeze and rewind sweeps (plan D3(a)); `ChronalRewindManager.IsWorldHeld` gates `Hitbox.OnAreaEntered` and `Hurtbox.TakeHit`, so frozen actors take no damage and pay no meter, Rally, checkpoint or extractor credit; Time Freeze activation and `InteractionArea` wait for the thaw; Integrity, the Time Freeze cooldown and hero timers run; protection is armed by `PlayerController.EndRecoveryHold` at the thaw; `EventBus.OnRecoveryLanded` / `OnRecoveryWorldThawed` bracket the hold; the reused thaw warning plays through `AudioManager.PlayCriticalCue` 30 frames before the thaw and survives Reduced Temporal Effects; the music duck ends on `RewindPresentationPhase.Thawed`; a pending boss T01b beat resumes after the thaw; the hold is armed in memory only and a save load never grants one. Residuals tracked elsewhere: the sealing anchor does not exist (`DEFER-SEALING-ANCHOR`); `PressurePlate` is not a freezable (as under Time Freeze); the thaw cue stream is null under the silent placeholder audio kit (Package 10); `PostLandingThawWarningFrames` = 30 is provisional. See `docs/handoffs/P12_W1.md`. | Package 12 W1 | Closed on headless evidence: `PostLandingHoldTests` (11), `BossHistoricalRecoverySuspensionTests`, `DeathTriggeredRewindTests`, `ScriptedRewindTests`, `StoryCombatRulesTests`, `StoryDropsAndRewindTests`. Package 12 (`docs/PACKAGE12_DESIGN_ALIGNMENT_PLAN.md`) opened most of its rows inline in the Package 11 table above as each workstream merged. The rows below were consolidated at the Phase C closeout (2026-09-27): the four held items that had no row, and the adopted recommendations that §9 recorded but the ledger did not. All were observed at `main` after the Phase C commits; none has been played.

Chief Engineer Wren is not added and the hub is still one corridor. **Amended 2026-09-29 (Package 13 W3):** Wren is now a hub NPC (Training Wing, one tip per act), Sarah's Act I/II sets play one line per visit in order with after-Level-N gates (`StorySaveData.LastHubLineVisit`), the Act I→II and Act II→III boundary scenes play on arrival, and the triage readout stands beside the portal. **The portal is still never gated** and the hub is still one corridor. | Unassigned | The portal refuses a mission while the period's required set is unseen, with a pinned test per boundary; a Wren post if the design keeps him. |

| **DEFER-H7-STORY-TERMINAL** — M01 fast-fall and terminal velocity (plan D1(a)); **narrowed 2026-10-08** to the launch terminal and the base gravity; **narrowed again 2026-10-09** to the launch terminal, the launch gravity and Story's short hop (base gravity ruled per mode) ; **narrowed 2026-10-16** to the launch terminal, the launch gravity and Story's launch-band short-hop clause (the short-hop half closed by decision 5.1) | M01: fast-fall is 20 u/s and "snaps to terminal". **Superseded 2026-10-08 by `USER-MOVEMENT-FEEL` for normal control** (a fast-fall that ramps to 15 u/s and a 12 u/s terminal, both modes); the launch terminal and H-7's gravity unification stay with this row. **User ruling 2026-10-09 ("Document both builds"):** each mode's real base gravity is its intended one — Story 18 u/s² × (0.8 + 0.4 × Weight), weight-coupled and campaign-tuned; the sim a flat 30 u/s² with no weight term, because Fighter Mode is competitively normalized. The GDD now states both (master and mirror: physics table :954, Jump Arc :982, Gravity & Fall Speed :1463–1464, the H-7 note :1467, the identity-axis note :1590; `docs/gameplay.html` and `characters.html` in the docs workspace), so the base-gravity difference is no longer a deviation. The ruling does not cover the launch gravity, the launch terminals or Story's short hop; they stay with this row. | Adopted D1(a): fast-fall is 20 in both modes and the sim clamps every fall at 20; Story's **ordinary** terminal stays 600 px/s (10 u/s) until the H-7 gravity unification, so only Story's fast-fall path reaches 20. **Amended 2026-10-08 (`USER-MOVEMENT-FEEL`):** the normal-control terminal is now shared at 12 u/s (Story 720 px/s, `UniversalMovementRules.TerminalFallSpeed`) and fast-fall ramps to a shared 15 u/s cap, so neither half of D1(a) is current. Still divergent: the launch terminals (Story's launch states keep 600 px/s = 10 u/s, `StoryAirPhysics.LaunchTerminalFallSpeedPixels`; the sim clamps hitstun and daze at 20, `LaunchTerminalFallSpeed`), the launch gravity (Story ×1.8 on its base, the sim flat), the base gravities (Story 18 u/s² × (0.8 + 0.4 × Weight), 21.6 at Weight 1.0; the sim a flat 30) and Story's short hop. Open with that narrowed scope; record retained. **Amended 2026-10-09 (user ruling above):** the base gravities leave this row as intended per mode; the build already runs both (`StoryAirPhysics.BaseGravity` 18 with `WeightCouplingBase` 0.8 / `WeightCouplingPerWeight` 0.4; `FighterAirPhysics.Gravity` −30), so no build change follows. Still divergent: the launch terminals (Story 10 u/s, the sim 20), the launch gravity (Story ×1.8 on its base while falling, the sim its flat base) and Story's short hop (the sim's jumps are fixed-height). Open with that scope; record retained. **Amended 2026-10-16 (decision 5.1, `USER-SIM-SHORT-HOP`; CM-MOVE, merged `23fcd22`):** the short-hop half is closed — the sim now has Story's ×2.5 short hop through one constant (`UniversalMovementRules.ShortHopGravityMultiplier`, which `StoryAirPhysics.ShortHopGravityMultiplier` forwards), gated in both modes on a rise from the fighter's own Jump press this airtime (Q5, `USER-SHORT-HOP-OWN-JUMP-ONLY`), with the apex hang only while Jump is held (`USER-APEX-HANG-HELD`); the sim's jumps are no longer fixed-height. Still divergent: the launch terminals (Story 10 u/s, the sim 20), the launch gravity (Story ×1.8 on its base while falling, the sim its flat base) and Story's launch-band short-hop clause (`StoryAirPhysics.SelectBand`'s launch branch cuts a released rise in a launch state; untouched, plan §8). Open with that scope; record retained. | Unassigned | H-7 unifies Story and sim launch gravity and the launch terminal, with the per-level reachability suites re-run (they now measure through `StoryAirPhysics`, so a retune moves them in one place); Story's short hop is settled in the same pass or by a ruling. **Amended 2026-10-09:** base gravity was removed from these criteria (they read "unifies Story and sim base gravity, launch gravity and the launch terminal") — the user ruled each mode's own base intended and the GDD states both. **Amended 2026-10-16:** Story's short hop is settled (both modes have it); the criteria keep the launch terminal, the launch gravity and Story's launch-band short-hop clause. |
| ~~**DEFER-TOGGLE-BLOCK-ECHO-CHORD**~~ — G13 Toggle Block exits (plan D8(a)) | G13: Block Hold/Toggle, released by charge depletion, jump or roll. | Adopted D8(a): the latch is input-layer only (`BlockToggleLatch`, no sim state) and releases on the player's own Jump/Roll/Block press. Because Roll is an exit, the Echo Step **chord** (Block+Roll) cannot fire out of a latch; Echo Step's direct binding still works. | Unassigned | A ruling on whether the chord must survive Toggle mode; if so, an input-layer exception for the chord. **Closed 2026-10-25 as moot (`USER-REMOVE-ECHO-STEP`):** the Echo Step verb and its chord were removed, so there is no chord left to survive Toggle mode; the retained record above is history. |

The anchor is single-press, so Phase C plays the `seal_charge` hum when the anchor **arms** and `seal_lock` when the seal is accepted (`EventBus.OnSealingAnchorChanged`); both streams are silent. | Unassigned | Package 10 presentation, plus a ruling on whether the seal should be a hold (which would move the charge cue onto it). |

## Confirmed open deviations — Package 13

Package 13 (`docs/PACKAGE13_REVIEW_ALIGNMENT_PLAN.md`, the 2026-09-29 story/ability/character review alignment) closed or invalidated the rows marked above as each workstream merged. The rows below were consolidated at the Phase C closeout (2026-09-29) from the plan's §2 decisions, the §9 deviations and the eight `docs/handoffs/P13_*.md`. All were observed at `main` `378eb68` (the last code commit of Phase C); **none has been played, looked at or balanced.**

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |

The *input-binding* state "Unbound" (a slot deliberately cleared, C01c) is a different word and must not change. | The master `design-godot-v7.md` (docs-3 `d695d7b`) reads "Direct Severed" in the Controls table's Ultimate / Grab / Echo Step rows and in the C01c remapping bullet, and `docs-3/docs/COMFORT_SETTINGS.md` lines 101–103, 128, 130 and 136 likewise. **The repo mirror `design-godot.md` and the repo copy of `COMFORT_SETTINGS.md` restore "Unbound" on exactly those lines** (Package 13 Phase 0), so the mirror deliberately differs from the master there. The build's binding sense (`InputBindings.SetUnbound`, `controls_binding_unbound`) is correct and untouched. | Unassigned (docs) | The master and its contract copy are corrected the same way; the mirror is then re-synced byte-identical. Until then, **never re-sync the mirror blindly** — diff those lines first. |

The rollback gate gained a production-loadout convergence case and passes with 0 breaches (the closeout's re-measure: worst median 0.084 ms, worst p95 0.168 ms, largest snapshot 4473 B), and every per-kit suite that assumed press-tick contact was re-timed through `tests/Determinism/SpecialCastTestKit.cs` with its assertions unchanged. Only a hand-built contract with `ActiveFrames <= 0` keeps press-tick timing (the 0 / 1 / 0 fallback); no production Special does. Foresight keeps its 322 stance, tracked on 323 as a Delegated cast. | Unassigned | A sim Special phase machine (startup/active/recovery, cancellable by hitstun) reading the loadout frame data, with the rollback-readiness and per-stage hash suites re-run and every per-kit test that assumes press-tick contact updated. **Met 2026-10-16.** Reopen if a sim Special resolves off its authored frames. |

| **VERIFY-TEMPEST-ULT-HOLD** — the Tempest windbox and a held victim (Package 13 W7a) | S01/S02: the Tempest never moves a victim held by an Ultimate cinematic (or a grab, or an immovable boss). | Story refuses `UsingUltimate` and knockback-immune bosses; the sim refuses grab states only. W7a shipped before component 321 existed, so `FighterTempestRules.Push` does not check `FighterUltimateActivationRules.IsCaptured`. | Unassigned | The sim push skips a 321-captured fighter, with a pin and the hash suites re-run. |
| **VERIFY-RAIL-CHARGE-STARTUP-ARMOR** — Story Rail Charge armor window (Package 13 W7b) | LN02: Rail Charge is armored only during travel. | The sim's armor is exactly the 30 travel frames. **Story's also covers the 12-frame startup**, because the shared `StoryCombatRules.HyperArmorPreventsInterruption` window is Startup and Active. | Unassigned | A per-ability armor window in `StoryCombatRules` (or a Rail Charge override), with a Story pin. |
| **VERIFY-TUNING-SPECIAL-TOTAL-FOLD** — multi-hit totals folded into the sim tuning component (Package 13 W7b) | One canonical value per tuning number. | `FighterLoadoutFactory.SpecialDamage` still folds multi-hit Melee/Projectile totals into `FighterTuningComponent` (Joan S2 24, Mozart S1 12); the W7b paths read the per-hit contract instead. Readers of the tuning total (the CPU, the Move List) see the folded value. **Amended 2026-10-16 (decision 1.2, CM-SIM):** the phase machine's generic melee branch (`FighterCombatSystem.BuildIntent`) applies the folded total once per execution (`FlagMeleeResolved`), and in the production roster only single-hit Splitting Strike reaches that branch, where the folded and per-hit values are equal (Divine Piercing resolves its thrusts and Foresight its answer elsewhere); so only a hand-built default contract could apply a multi-hit fold in a sim hit, while the CPU and the Move List still read the folded totals. | Unassigned | Either the fold is deleted and its readers use the per-hit contract, or it is documented as a derived display total with a pin that it equals `HitCount × Damage`. |

## Confirmed open deviations — playtest feel pass 2026-10-04, workstream ARENA

The 2026-10-04 playtest-driven feel / anti-spam / pathing pass (`docs/PLAYTEST_FEEL_2026-10-04_PLAN.md`, items P1, P2, P3 lead, P5 and F8), as amended by its fix round (fix-arena A1–A5 and G5; fix-feel M1, the player-side line of sight). First observed in the ARENA worktree on top of `main` `5d3ce25`; the rows below describe `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-07 after both rounds were merged (still uncommitted on `5d3ce25`). **Nobody has played or looked at any of it**; the evidence is the Story playtest bots and the GdUnit suites (`BossArenaLeashTests`, `ArenaGeometryContentTests`, `ArenaEnemyAttackGeometryTests`, `StoryCameraRigTests`).

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **ARENA-FLORENCE-DOORWAY** — Level 1's boss arena entrance and edge | Design §17 (`design-godot.md:3517`): the boss engages when the player enters the arena. The P5 brief: no layout changes beyond the gate fixes. | Florence's arena west wall ran the full height (y 0–1080) over the floor at 900 since the first commit, so no hero could enter (Level 1 has no movement ability) and the old radius reveal started the fight through the wall. The wall now stops at `Level01Controller.ArenaDoorwayTopY` (700), a 200 px ground-level doorway. The arena rect (reveal and leash) starts at the Phase 2 west barricade's inner face (`Room4OffsetX + 180`), so the boss-room checkpoint (`florence_checkpoint_2`, +100) stands in a 160 px antechamber and is struck before the reveal. A hero still west of the arena when the barricades close is moved inside. **Fix round:** the workshop door joins `StoryGateRules.DoorGroup` (A1), and the arena ward (A5, `ARENA-LEASH-EDGE-ANSWER`) can hold against a hero at the checkpoint only while the engaged Borgia Inquisitor is still beyond its 420 px reach; once it has closed to its edge (it does so on its own), no point from the doorway's inner face (`ArenaInnerLeftX`) to the edge is warded (`BossArenaLeashTests.FlorencesAntechamberIsNeverWardedAgainstABossHoldingItsEdge`). The `play` bot cannot reach the arena: it stops at the gear-puzzle workshop door (x 8600), a bot limitation. **Amended 2026-10-12 / 13:** the workshop door is now also the print-shop encounter lock's exit barrier (`level_01_florence_lock_print_shop`, which needs the gears as well as its guards), and since FIX-BOT the bot solves the gear train (`GearPlan`, three presses) and completes Level 1 (Normal Joan, `fxbotfinal`, 217 s). | Unassigned | A human walkthrough of the doorway and the antechamber, or an authored arena entrance that replaces them. `EveryBossArenaCanBeEnteredOverItsWestThreshold` and `ThePreBossBreathersAndTheirBeatsStandOutsideTheArenaReveal` stay green. |
| **ARENA-LEASH-EDGE-ANSWER** — what a leashed boss does about a target outside its arena | `design-godot.md:1624`: bosses are bounded to stage platforms. The design does not say how a leashed boss treats a target outside the arena. | *Provisional.* **The answer:** a boss pinned at its leash edge answers an outside target only inside its engagement range (`max(MeleeRangeThreshold, RangedRangeThreshold)` × 60 px — 420 px for the Borgia Inquisitor up to 720 px for the Siege Cannon), and then **never** with `SummonMinions` or `Teleport`, not even through the no-deadlock fallback (`BossController.SelectAbilityIndex(distance, targetOutsideArena)`); with nothing else ready it rests. Beyond that range it holds the edge, idle and facing the target. The first cut answered at any distance, which let the RangeClass.Any summon kits (Tribunal, Forge Sentinel, Tragedy King, First Severed) raise a wave every cooldown while the hero stood outside. **The arena ward (fix round A5, provisional)** closes the plink spot that rule left (a hero outside the arena and beyond the boss's range attacking it with long-range kit unanswered): `BossController.IsArenaWarded` holds while the boss is engaged and leashed and its target stands both more than `ArenaWardMarginPixels` (60, one world unit) outside the arena's x-range **and** beyond its engagement range (`IsWithinEngagementRange`, the same test chase uses to commit an attack). While warded, `OnHurtboxHit` refuses every hit first (no damage, knockback, status, combo mark, victim hitstop or phase/interrupt work, and with 0 dealt no meter or Rally), `ApplyStatusEffect` refuses a status applied without a hit (the Relativity Rift's linger; a rewind slot restore bypasses it), and a Venom tick deals nothing while the tick cadence and the status clock keep running. A refused hit reads as a `UIPalette.UnboundCold` flash for 0.12 s and, for a player-dealt hit, a `UIPalette.Slate` "0" damage number (honouring the damage-number setting). Never warded: a dormant boss (the waking hit from outside lands — `ARENA-WAKE-REVEAL`), an unleashed boss, a hero inside the engagement range (so melee spacing against a boss holding its edge, about 58–103 px outside, lands normally) and scripted `TakeDamage(int)`. It is derived state (engagement, leash, target), so pool and rewind need no new reset beyond the `WardedHitsRefused` counter. The fix round's first cut used the 60 px edge margin alone, which warded that melee spacing while the boss kept swinging; review made it relative to the boss's range and added the status and DoT guards. The Level 13 Mirror has no ward (`ARENA-MIRROR-NO-WARD`). No `play`-bot run (Easy `fxward2` on Levels 2/4/5/7/11, Normal `fxwardn` on 2/5/8/9) was ever outside an arena after a reveal, so the re-approach from outside is covered only by unit cases; the runner has no start-position option. | Unassigned | A user ruling: keep the ward (ratifying its 60 px margin and its read), answer an outside target at any distance with a non-summon ranged kit instead, or move the leash and reveal to the room boundary so the outside spot disappears. `BossArenaLeashTests` pins today's rule (`AnEngagedBossRefusesEveryHitWhileItsHeroStandsOutsideTheArenaBeyondItsReach`, `ABossPinnedAtItsEdgeTakesEveryHitFromAnOutsideHeroWithinItsReach`, `AVenomTakenBeforeTheHeroStepsOutDealsNothingWhileTheBossIsWarded`, `AnUnleashedOrDormantBossIsNeverWardedAndAPoolCycleClearsTheWard`). |
| **ARENA-WAKE-REVEAL** — a dormant boss hit from outside its arena | Design §17: the intro (name card, establishing beat, free telegraph) plays as the player enters the arena. The design does not cover the boss being struck first from outside. | *Provisional.* The struck boss wakes into a revealed fight: HUD bar, `BossRevealed` (objective, boss-intro dialogue, boss music), still leashed, with the name card, the 1.5 s freeze and the free telegraph skipped. The intro is not recorded as seen, so a later attempt still gets it. Entering the arena afterwards never runs the intro freeze over a fighting boss. **Amended 2026-10-12:** unchanged by the encounter locks — boss arenas are not locks in this package; every reveal path, the woken-by-a-hit one included, now also asks the hero's Story camera to frame the arena (`BossEncounterController.Reveal` → `RequestFraming`; `USER-STORY-CAMERA-ZOOM`). | Unassigned | A ruling on whether a woken boss should still show the name card (without the freeze). |
| **ARENA-WEST-EDGE-AT-OLD-REVEAL-LINE** — where each arena starts | Design §17: the reveal fires on entering the boss arena. | *Provisional.* For Levels 2, 3, 5–12, 14 and 15 each arena rect's west edge is the old reveal-radius line, not the room transition into the boss room, so the pre-boss anchors, absence beats and `PreBossTrigger`s stay outside it and the boss is leashed to that sub-range of the room. Since fix-round A4 the edge is one derived value per level: `BossArenaWestX = BossSpawnX − BossArenaApproachPixels`, where the approach is the old radius (900 px on Levels 2, 5–8, 10 and 11; 800 on 3 and 9; 700 on 12 and 14; 750 on 15), and the encounter is built at `BossSpawnX` through `StoryLevelControllerBase.BuildBossEncounter`'s arena overload, which takes no reveal distance — the rect is the reveal (the radius overload survives for the `SealingAnchorTests` fixture). Level 4's edge is its courtyard room boundary (`Room4StartX` 8640, 40 px east of the old 9400 − 800 line); Florence's is its barricade face (row above). `SecretCachePlacementTests` and `AbsenceBeatContentTests` now read each level's `ArenaBounds` instead of hand-copied radii (the latter's Level 4 literal was already 40 px off). | Unassigned | A ruling that the edge should be the physical room boundary (then the pre-boss beats move west of it), or acceptance of the old reveal line. `ArenaGeometryContentTests.EveryDerivedArenaEdgeStandsItsApproachInFrontOfTheSpawnTheEncounterUses` pins the derivation on all twelve levels. |
| **ARENA-PROVISIONAL-CONSTANTS** — numbers the design does not give | `design-godot.md:3119-3132` gives the Story camera table but no lead cap or cut threshold; the P3 brief asks for a clamped lead. | Single named constants, none measured: `StoryCameraRules.MaxLookAheadPixels` (3 units) and `SnapDistancePixels` (900); `EnemyAttackGeometryRules.MaxLeadSeconds` (0.75), `ResampleRadiusPixels` (480), `BodyAimHeightPixels` (32), `WallClearancePixels` (2), `WallProbeRays` (3); `StoryGateRules.ColumnTopY` (−2400) and `ColumnMarginPixels` (8). Fix round: `BossController.ArenaWardMarginPixels` (60) and the ward's flash (`ArenaWardFlashSeconds`, 0.12 s) from A5; each level's `BossArenaApproachPixels` from A4 (values under `ARENA-WEST-EDGE-AT-OLD-REVEAL-LINE`). **Amended 2026-10-12 (`USER-STORY-CAMERA-ZOOM`):** `MaxLookAheadPixels` and `SnapDistancePixels` stay in world pixels at the new 1.5 zoom; the camera numbers the update added (`ViewZoom`, `FramingMarginPixels`, `ZoomDampingSeconds`, `BossFramingMinZoom`) are recorded in `PROVISIONAL-CAMERA-ZOOM-RULES`. | Unassigned | Package 9 playtests ratify or replace each value in its one rulebook. |
| **ARENA-MIRROR-NO-WARD** — Level 13's Mirror and the arena ward | `ARENA-LEASH-EDGE-ANSWER`'s ward: a leashed boss cannot be plinked from outside its arena beyond its reach. | The clone is clamp-leashed to `Level13Controller.MirrorArenaBounds` (x 7200 to the east wall's inner face; its reveal is still the 700 px `MirrorRevealDistance` radius). Fix-round A2 moved the clamp after the clone's own physics step (`MirrorParadoxController.LeashPhysicsPriority` = 100), so no move, Echo Step or warp ends a frame outside (`BossArenaLeashTests.TheMirrorCloneEndsEveryPhysicsFrameInsideItsArena`). It has **no ward**: the clone is a `PlayerController` driven by the Fighter CPU decision table, so there is no boss engagement range to measure against, and its hit intake is the shared Story player path. A hero west of the arena can still hit the clone with ranged kit; whether the clone answers is the CPU's own range policy, which nobody has measured. | Unassigned | A ruling: a Mirror-only refusal keyed to the clone's longest kit reach, a west wall that closes when the Mirror is revealed, or acceptance that the clone answers with the hero's own ranged kit. |
| **F8-LEAD-AXIS** — the Story camera's look-ahead axis | `design-godot.md:3130` (Story camera table): "Lookahead Time `0.3s` — Slight camera lead in movement direction". | *Provisional.* The follow rig leads on the **horizontal** axis only: `StoryCameraRules.LookAheadTarget(velocityX)` is `LookAheadSeconds` (0.3) × horizontal velocity, clamped to ±`MaxLookAheadPixels` (3 units = 180 px), and eased into the confiner's lead. Vertical tracking is the dead/soft zone, Y damping 0.3 and the one-unit-up offset, with no lead, because a jump's vertical velocity reverses mid-arc and a vertical lead would bob the view on every hop. With the design's X damping (0.5) a steady runner still trails the view centre slightly, so the lead mostly cancels the follow lag rather than putting the view ahead. Raised by the round-1 ARENA review; nobody has looked at it. **Amended 2026-10-12 (`USER-STORY-CAMERA-ZOOM`):** the zoom is now a rig input: at `StoryCameraRules.ViewZoom` 1.5 the 180 px lead cap is 14 % of the 1280 px view (9.4 % of 1920 at 1.0); the dead and soft zones stay view fractions; while a boss or Mirror framing is live the X follow target is the framed arena centre (`StoryCameraRules.FramedCentre`, leashed inside the hero's soft zone). Y is unframed and still has no lead. | Unassigned | A user ruling: accept horizontal-only (amend the table to "horizontal movement direction"), or add a vertical lead with a rule for jump arcs (for example falls only) plus a `StoryCameraRigTests` case. |
| **P3-HORIZONTAL-LEAD** — how enemy shots lead a moving target | Plan P3: "Enemy projectiles lead a moving target". The design gives no lead model. | *Provisional.* At fire time a shot re-acquires the living player nearest the telegraphed point (within `ResampleRadiusPixels`, 480), aims at body height (`BodyAimHeightPixels`, 32 px above the feet) and leads through `EnemyAttackGeometryRules.LeadPoint`: the target's **horizontal** velocity only, a first-order intercept iterated twice, flight time capped at `MaxLeadSeconds` (0.75 s). Vertical velocity is ignored (a linear vertical lead on a jumping hero aims into the sky or the floor), so a well-timed hop still dodges a led shot. A target that crossed to the shooter's other side keeps the telegraphed aim. Raised by the round-1 ARENA review. | Unassigned | A ruling to keep horizontal-only or add a gravity-aware vertical lead, measured with the `play` bot's projectile share of `damage_by_source`. |
| **PROVISIONAL-PLAYER-MELEE-LINE-OF-SIGHT** — the player's swings need Environment line of sight (fix-round M1) | P2's rationale — a swing does not reach through a wall — applied to the player; the design is silent on the player side. | *Provisional.* Story only. `Hitbox.RequiresEnvironmentLineOfSight` is set by `CharacterFactory` on the player's `MeleeHitbox` alone (the basic string and both directional strikes). A new contact with an `EnemyController` or `BossController` hurtbox is dropped unless an Environment ray from the hurtbox's `CollisionShape2D` centre back to the swinger's `PlayerController.BodyCentreGlobalPosition` (it follows the crouch) is clear: `EnvironmentProbe.IsClear(..., ignoreBodiesAtOrigin: true)`, one-way platforms never block. Casting from the target and skipping every body that contains the target's centre means a wall-phasing Rift Phantom caught mid-wall — inside a multi-shape wall too — is not sheltered by the wall it is passing through, matching its own P2 clip, while one whose centre is past the wall's far face is blocked both ways (the review found the first cut, cast from the swinger, made a mid-wall phantom immune to basics while it could still hit back). One centre-to-centre ray per contact, tested once when the contact begins (a wall that clears later in the same active window is not re-tested); a low wall between the two centres blocks even where the hitbox rect clears it. Not gated: strike surfaces (fractures, extractors), the Mirror clone (a `PlayerController`), projectiles, zones, constructs and kit shape queries. The sim never reads it. The enemy-side clip still uses the default `ignoreBodiesAtOrigin: false`, so a phantom's own swing from inside a multi-shape wall is clipped by its other shapes (Level 13's geometry is all single-rect bodies). | Unassigned | A human review of swings at ledges and low walls, and a ruling on whether Specials' shape queries take the same rule. `ArenaEnemyAttackGeometryTests.APlayerSwingNeverLandsOnAnEnemyBehindAWall`, `AWallPhasingEnemyInsideAWallIsNotShelteredByIt` and `AWallPhasingEnemyInsideAMultiShapeWallIsNotShelteredByIt` pin it. |
| **VERIFY-AREAPULSE-WALLS** — enemy AreaPulse hitboxes ignore walls | P2: enemy hitboxes do not reach through walls. | P2 clips only forward (mirrored) `MeleeStrike` and `ChargeDash` hitboxes; since fix-round A3 a strike probes once at activation and a charge re-probes only on an active frame its owner moved, through one reused ray query per executor (`EnvironmentProbe.CreateReusableQuery`). `AreaPulse` hitboxes (radial, unmirrored: the `shell_burst`, `electric_nova` and `cold_gas_vent` mob pulses and seven boss pulses, e.g. the Chronal Inventor's `resonator_pulse` at 170 px) are not occluded, so a caster pressed against a thin wall can hit through it. No reported door case is affected: fix-round G8 found the bots' "hit through the Level 6 rockfall / Level 3 court door" reports were Collapse Tremor debris (`env`) and pre-P1 boss roaming, and `TheRockfallAndTheCourtDoorStopForwardSwingsFromEitherSide` pins the forward clip on both real doors. | Unassigned | Either clip a pulse's reach against Environment, or rule that radial pulses are area denial that ignores walls; a pin either way. |
| **P5-GATE-COLUMN-SWEEP** — every gate stands under a column no hero can clear | `PACKAGE5_CAMPAIGN_PLAN.md` L02: gates cannot be jumped; plan P5. | Round 1 added columns rising to `StoryGateRules.ColumnTopY` over Florence's workshop door, Level 2's Gates A and B, Level 3's Court of Honor door, Level 6's rockfall slab, Level 9's RadarGate and Level 12's curtain. The round-1 review found the audit incomplete (Level 4's CourtyardGate, top y 140 with nothing above it, clearable by Einstein's double jump plus an upward Warp; Level 11's breastwork earth stopping at y 0). Fix round A1 added columns over Level 4's CourtyardGate, Level 8's VaultDoor (to the tomb ceiling) and Level 11's breastwork and both lane-roof masses (Cleopatra could double-jump the roofs from the array top), and replaced the hand list with enumeration: every `BuildDoor` door (and Florence's own) joins `StoryGateRules.DoorGroup` (`story_gate_door`), and `ArenaGeometryContentTests.EveryPuzzleGateStandsUnderAColumnNoHeroCanClear` requires every door and `ForcefieldBarrier` (at least 11) to stand under a registered column covering its width, foot within 40 px of the gate top, out of reach — best double jump × 1.25 at the level's lowest gravity plus a 240 px movement-ability allowance — from every standable within 1000 px. Columns per level: L1 1, L2 2, L3 1, L4 1, L6 1, L8 1, L9 1, L11 3, L12 1. Level 8's shows as a 32 px strip at Room 3's east edge above the tomb ceiling and a 12 px sliver in the chamber's top-left pocket. **Amended 2026-10-12 (`USER-ENCOUNTER-LOCKS`):** encounter-lock barriers are not gates: they stay out of `StoryGateRules.DoorGroup` and are not `ForcefieldBarrier`s, so the column counts above are unchanged (Level 10 still has none). Because an exit barrier is closed from level build and rises to `ColumnTopY`, `ArenaGeometryContentTests`' reach sweep (`AddReachIssues`) now skips a standable whose top is at `ColumnTopY` as a launch point, as it already skipped gate columns (GU-E), and its moving-platform endpoint check exempts a mover a lock holds at its near stop whose far stop lies inside a closed lock barrier of the same level (GU-L12; Level 13's two shards). At a gate with a column, a lock's barrier spans only the column's foot to the floor. | Unassigned | A visual review of the column strips in the editor (Level 8's most visibly); the sweep stays green. |
| **LAYOUT-CRAWLSPACE-MOVES** — no standable surface wedged under a ceiling (fix-round G5) | Bot game bug 11 (plan §3): Level 4's top tower step left 62 px under the walkway, less than the 64 px hero. The P5 brief said "no layout changes beyond the gate fixes". | Level 4's Bastille tower top step (one-way 5200/380, top 370, under the walkway's 308 underside) moved to `Level04Controller.TowerTopStepX/Y` = 4880/380, west of the walkway's end at 5000; the climb is 100 px then 78 px, inside Lincoln's single jump (`TheParisTowerTopStepClearsTheWalkwayWithinTheWeakestSingleJump`). The new sweep `ArenaGeometryContentTests.NoStandableSurfaceIsWedgedUnderASolidCeiling` (headroom ≥ 64 + 4 px wherever a hero can get onto the surface: from 52 px for a one-way, whose 12 px landing margin lifts a rising hero onto it, from 64 px for a solid) found two more and moved them: Level 6's west roof one-way 1000/700 → 1000/720 (82 px under the 1120 stump) and Level 11's battery-ridge step 2680/730 → 2680/750 (78 px under the ridge Extractor; it is not a route onto the ridge, which is climbed from the sandbags). Left as authored: unenterable solid pockets (Level 1's gear platforms under the print-shop shelves, stacked Level 7/10 ledges) and Level 14's top scaffold rung, 68 px under `level_14_extractor_0` (it passes the margin; the extractor table is authored data). The bots' frame-simulated nav graph (Lincoln) still climbs Level 4 (ground → 750 → 610 → 470 → 370 → walkway) and Level 6 (ground → roof → stump); no bot route crosses the three platforms. | Unassigned | A human look at the three moved platforms and the Level 14 rung in the editor; the sweep stays green. |

## Confirmed open deviations — playtest feel pass 2026-10-04, workstream ENEMY

The same pass's Story enemy-combat items (`docs/PLAYTEST_FEEL_2026-10-04_PLAN.md` §2.2 S1–S5, F5 victim half, F9, P4, the AbilityOrigin mirror), as amended by its fix round (fix-enemy E1–E4; E5, the pool/rewind/kill-throw reset of the grab, throw and slam-bounce latches through `EnemyController.ResetGrabAndSlamState`, is a build fix with no row). Every number is a single constant in `FTT.Combat.StoryEnemyCombatRules` (`scripts/Combat/StoryEnemyCombatRules.cs`), Story-only; nothing in `scripts/FighterSim/` references it. Observed in `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-07 (uncommitted on `5d3ce25`). Evidence: `StoryEnemyCombatRulesTests`, `StoryEnemyCombatTests`, `StoryEnemyCombatReviewTests`, `EnemyControllerTests` and the Story playtest bots. **Nobody has played it.**

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **PROVISIONAL-STORY-CONFIRM-PRORATION** — string + Special burst on Story mobs | User complaint 2026-10-04: string + both Specials is "basically an insta-kill". The design has no PvE proration (`design-godot.md:1606` and `:1490` reject juggle decay for Fighter PvP only). | *Provisional.* **Amended 2026-10-11 by USER-FIRST-SPECIAL-UNTAXED (D4):** the chain's **first** Special lands untaxed — no ×0.6 and no chain decay on any of its hits — though its hits still advance the chain counter; everything below now applies to a **second or later** Special. A Special-class hit whose **first** hit lands on a mob already in hitstun (a confirm) deals ×0.6 on every hit of that Special in the chain; inside one uninterrupted stun chain each hit after the 3rd decays ×0.85, floored at ×0.5 (a late confirm can reach ×0.3). The chain spans the Armored Recovery its own poise trip opens and resets on natural stun expiry, the end of that window, death, spawn/despawn and rewind. Ultimates, Tick deliveries and hazards are exempt and do not advance it. Bosses are not prorated (no hitstun). **Amended 2026-10-16:** a prorated hit (the ×0.6 tax or the ×0.85 decay; `StoryEnemyCombatRules.IsProrated`) now floats a dimmed, smaller number (decision 8.2, `USER-DIM-REDUCED-NUMBERS`), so the rule is visible; the standard basic floor of 6 (decision 8.1) sits under the chain scale, so hit 4 of a chain decays a floored 6 to 5 (`PROVISIONAL-STORY-BASIC-FLOOR-ORDER`). | Unassigned | A user ruling on the rule and its numbers, informed by the bot matrix (`summary_*_after*.csv`): string + S1 + S2 should no longer kill a full-HP standard mob in one cycle while a Special thrown from neutral keeps full value. |
| **PROVISIONAL-STANDARD-POISE** — standard mobs gain the V7.4 stagger budget | V7.4 Enemy Stagger Discipline scopes the budget to elites and bosses (`design-godot.md:1445`). | *Provisional.* **Amended 2026-10-11 by USER-FIRST-SPECIAL-UNTAXED (D4):** the budget is **2.325 s** (139.5 frames; it was 1.9 s) and drains **4.0 s/s** (it was 3 s/s); the ×1.5 cost now applies only to a second or later Special in the chain (the chain's first Special charges ×1.0); derivation and residuals in `PROVISIONAL-FIRST-SPECIAL-TAX-RULES`. Standard-tier mobs get a 2.325 s budget (a full string under the S5 floors costs 1.70 s, plus the chain's first Special, so it completes); a taxed Special-sourced stun costs ×1.5; a trip opens a 0.6 s Armored Recovery with the committed counterattack; the standard budget drains 4 s/s while unstunned (elites keep 1 s/s). Throws and a bowled standard's knockdown charge nothing; a bowled **elite** keeps the V7.4 budget charge it took before the pass (the applied stun, no floor — elites are grab-immune but not bowling-immune; the first cut had silently dropped it, fix round E3). Fix round E2: Ultimate-sourced stuns — the Ultimate attack class or an authored Ultimate origin, `StoryEnemyCombatRules.ChargesStandardPoise` — charge no standard poise (the stun still applies), as S2 and V7.4's diminishing special stun already exempt Ultimates; otherwise a meter-paid multi-hit Ultimate (Lincoln's five 0.5 s smashes, 2.5 s) tripped the budget (then 1.9 s; still under 2.325 s) mid-cast and had its own last hits refused by the counterattack. The elite budget keeps charging Ultimate stuns. No poise accrues while the mob is frozen or `DrillInvulnerable`. | Unassigned | A ruling to keep or reverse; if kept, amend design §4 Enemy Stagger Discipline. |
| **PROVISIONAL-BASICS-PACING** — basic string vs standard mobs | 2026-08-10 feel batch accepted "Story encounters take ~2× longer on basics" with no compensating retune. | *Provisional.* The universal basic set (`combo_1/2/3`, up-attack, down-air) deals ×1.25 to Standard-tier mobs on Story intake only (half away from zero, never below 1). Constructs, throws, elites and every shared `.tres` damage are untouched. **Amended 2026-10-16 (decision 8.1, `USER-STORY-BASIC-FLOOR`):** for Standard-tier mobs the ×1.25 is now `StoryEnemyCombatRules.StandardBasicPacingScale(base)` = max(1.25, 6 / base) (`StandardBasicDamageFloor` 6), so a basic deals at least 6 before proration and Punish; elites and every other intake are unchanged. Hits to kill a Normal Chrono-Slasher are now 5–6 for every hero (Leonardo, Cleopatra, Tesla and Mozart needed 7 under the flat ×1.25, derived). | Unassigned | A ruling; the bot `basics` median frames-to-kill is the measure. |
| **S1-STUNNED-KNOCKBACK-FRICTION** — knockback on stunned Story mobs | Plan S1 (build bug): knockback replaces velocity, so the string and finisher should move mobs; the per-frame zeroing of a stunned mob's velocity predated that rule. The plan gives no numbers. | Build fix with agent-chosen numbers: a stunned mob's horizontal velocity decays at 1800 px/s² grounded and 600 px/s² airborne (`StoryEnemyCombatRules.StunnedGroundFrictionPixelsPerSecondSquared` / `StunnedAirDragPixelsPerSecondSquared`; flying mobs keep their stunned hover), and a ground mob's knockback heading over a drop is cancelled at the edge (probe depth `LedgeProbeDepthPixels`, 64 px, grounded; `KnockbackAirborneLedgeProbeDepthPixels`, 480 px, airborne). **Fix round E1:** a stun *opened* by a hit or call that writes no knockback — an impulse-free construct bite or coil arc, a zero-knockback Special, a standalone direct `ApplyStun` such as a Static Charge or a Kinetic Splitting bounce that finds the mob neither stunned nor armored — zeroes the mob's horizontal speed (and a flyer's vertical) instead, so the friction never slides chase, patrol or ChargeDash speed (the first cut let a charging mob stunned by a turret bolt slide about 100–225 px). A *refresh* of a running stun keeps the slide an earlier knockback wrote, and a throw's flight and a bowled victim's knockback count as knockback (`EnemyController.TryApplyKnockback` reports whether knockback was written). **Since 2026-10-11** a Standard or Elite mob's **finisher** flight decelerates at `max(S1, the D6a carry deceleration)` until touchdown (`USER-FINISHER-CARRY`, `PROVISIONAL-SPACING-WALLS-RULES` 1). **Amended 2026-10-12 (gameplay update, `USER-STORY-STATIC-KNOCKBACK`):** a finisher flight now decelerates at the D6a carry deceleration **alone**, in place of S1, grounded or airborne, until the flight ends, not at `max(S1, it)`; every other launch keeps S1. The knockback S1 decays is static in Story now (no missing-HP term). **Amended 2026-10-13 (FIX-ENEMY):** a **thrown flyer** (any flyer, lock member or not) reuses S1's air drag, `StoryEnemyCombatRules.StunnedAirDragPixelsPerSecondSquared` (600 px/s²), and its flight ends at rest; before, `ProcessThrownFlight` ended a throw only on floor contact, so a thrown flyer kept the throw's velocity forever (`PROVISIONAL-LOCK-MEMBER-REACH` (3)). | Unassigned | None beyond the user's review. `StoryEnemyCombatReviewTests` pins the open and refresh paths. |
| **S5-HIT-INDEXED-STUN-FLOOR** — basic-string stun floor on Story mobs | The shared `BasicComboRules.EnemyBasicStunFloorFrames` (24) claimed to cover the string's connect gaps but did not (31 / 41–43 ticks). | Build fix: string hits 1 and 2 floor the applied stun at 34 / 44 frames (`StoryEnemyCombatRules.BasicStringStunFloorFrames`); the finisher, up-attack and down-air keep 24. Non-resistant mobs therefore stun 4 frames longer than their authored 30/40. The V7.4 **elite** budget is charged at the pre-S5 24-frame floor, so elite poise is unchanged. | Unassigned | None beyond the user's review. |
| **F9-DEALT-HIT-FEEDBACK** — camera shake and damage numbers on hits the player deals to mobs/bosses | Design §8 shake table: shake comes from `AbilityData.ScreenShakeIntensity`. | Ability payloads with a positive authored intensity shake at ×12 for their authored duration (matching the player-hit path); basics and unauthored payloads use `min(6, 1 + 0.15 × dmg)` px (+2 for a launch), 0.08 s (+0.06). Floating damage numbers float for every player-dealt damaging hit and honour the damage-number setting. No haptics are added on the mob side. **Amended 2026-10-16 (decisions 7.1 and 8.2):** a non-Ultimate Special now shakes by max(authored × 12, the damage curve) for max(its authored duration, the curve's), while Ultimates keep their authored shake and basics the curve, and the player-side path covers hits on and by the Level 13 clone (`USER-STORY-ABILITY-SHAKE`; the Emancipator shakes 8 px). A prorated hit's number floats dimmed (`UIPalette.DamageNumberReduced`) at ×0.8, and a punished one keeps the Punish treatment (`USER-DIM-REDUCED-NUMBERS`). | Unassigned | A visual review in the editor. |
| **PROVISIONAL-ENEMY-CHASE-LEASH** — chase persistence and ledge/wall awareness | `design-godot.md:2986`: a mob returns to its waypoints when the player leaves `deAggroRadius`; mobs are slower than the player and encounters may be skipped. User complaint 2026-10-04: "too easy to bypass everything / pathing is not very good". | *Provisional.* An aggroed mob keeps chasing while its target is inside the mob's room x-bounds (the first room trigger containing its spawn x) or within 1.5× `DeAggroRadius`. In Chase, ground mobs stop at a drop deeper than 64 px and at Environment walls instead of walking off or pushing, and hold position under a target ≥ 48 px directly above. No jumping (design forbids it for ground mobs). Patrol and Returning are unchanged. Consequence noted by the fix round and left for this ruling: a mob in Chase idles at a ledge or wall for as long as its target stays in its room. **Amended 2026-10-12 (gameplay update):** the premise "encounters may be skipped" is superseded inside encounter locks (`USER-ENCOUNTER-LOCKS`): an engaged lock member keeps chasing its target anywhere inside its area (`KeepsChasingTarget`; this leash is bypassed) and is re-engaged each tick if it loses its target, while a dormant member neither aggroes nor chases. Engagement-director waiters still leash, and stop at ledges and walls in either direction on the ring (`USER-ENEMY-TURN-TAKING`). **Amended 2026-10-13 (FIX-ENEMY):** the 64 px ledge stop is lifted for an engaged melee lock member whose target stands more than 64 px below: it walks off toward the target when floor lies within 480 px inside its area (`PROVISIONAL-LOCK-MEMBER-REACH` (4)); ranged members and every mob outside a lock keep the stop. The acceptance's `bypass` bot was redefined on 2026-10-12: it now fights every member of the lock it stands in, so its hits-after-pass measure only the mobs no lock owns (`passed_alive_outside_locks`). | Unassigned | A ruling, measured with the `bypass` bot's hits-after-pass. |
| **KILL-FREEZE-VICTIM** — hitstop on a lethal hit on a Story mob | `BasicComboRules`: lethal hits skip hitstop (written for Fighter KOs; the design is silent for PvE). | A lethal direct hit holds the corpse in its flashed hitstun pose for the hit's own launch-aware victim hitstop (`StoryEnemyCombatRules.VictimHitstopFrames` = `BasicComboRules.HitstopFrames(dealt, launches)`, the attacker's rule, F6's +3 launch bonus included — fix round E4) before the death animation; the kill event, dust and collision removal stay immediate. Lethal ticks get no freeze. Bosses keep the skip. | Unassigned | None beyond the user's review. |

## Confirmed open deviations — 2026-10-04 playtest feel pass (workstream FEEL)

The user asked the 2026-10-04 pass (`docs/PLAYTEST_FEEL_2026-10-04_PLAN.md`) to iterate on what improves feel; these rows are the **provisional rulings** its player-side workstream made under that request, each reversible in one place, plus the design deviations the round-1 review found unrecorded and the fix round's (fix-feel G2, G7, R11, R12; M1 is under workstream ARENA). Observed in `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-07 (uncommitted on `5d3ce25`). Nobody has played them; the feel probe (Joan, Level 2) is the only measurement.

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **PROVISIONAL-HITSTOP-WEIGHT** — the shared hitstop curve (plan F6) | `design-godot.md` "Hitstop / Hitlag (V7)": every landed direct hit freezes both parties for **3–8 frames** scaled by damage (3 at ≤5, linear to 8 at ≥25), blocked hits 2. | `BasicComboRules.HitstopFrames(damage, launches)`: the floor is **4** (`HitstopMinFrames`), an authored launcher (the finisher, Up-Attack, Down-Air, the throws, Lincoln's hit 2, launching Specials/Ultimates) adds **+3** (`LaunchHitstopBonusFrames`), capped at **11** (`HitstopCeilingFrames`); blocked hits stay 2. Both modes call the one rule — Story's attacker (`OnMeleeHitConfirmed`, `ApplyAbilityCasterHitstop`) and player victim with the payload's launch flag, the sim's `ApplyFighterHit` with the hit's `launches`, and (fix round E4) the Story mob and boss victim and a mob's kill freeze through `StoryEnemyCombatRules.VictimHitstopFrames(dealt, launches)`, which is `BasicComboRules.HitstopFrames(dealt, launches)` with the same dealt damage the attacker uses — so both parties of a launching hit freeze for the same frames (round 1 left the enemy side on the one-argument rule, so a launching hit that froze the attacker 7 frames froze the mob 4). A killing blow on a Story mob still freezes the attacker. Every Fighter hash moves. Probe: Joan's basics freeze 4 frames per hit (was 3). **Amended 2026-10-16 (decisions 8.3 and 9.4):** 8.3: "**Keep 4–11; judge by hand in the pad session.**" Unchanged; the perfect block keeps the 2-frame blocked hitstop (`USER-PERFECT-BLOCK`). Plan §5 row 4 judges the weight. | Unassigned (user ruling) | The user keeps or reverses it. Reverting is `HitstopMinFrames = 3` and `LaunchHitstopBonusFrames = 0` plus the pins in `FeelPassRulesTests` and `StoryEnemyCombatRulesTests.TheVictimHitstopIsTheAttackersLaunchAwareRule`; `StoryEnemyCombatReviewTests.ALaunchingHitFreezesAMobABossAndACorpseForTheAttackersLaunchAwareFrames` pins the victim side. **Amended 2026-10-16:** plan §5 row 4 is the hands-on judgement. |
| **PROVISIONAL-STANDARD-MOB-NO-LAUNCH** — enemy hits on the Story player (plan F7) | `VERIFY-ABILITY-LAUNCHES` left the enemy/boss/hazard half open: M05 says only authored launchers launch, but non-player sources author no launch flag and kept "any knockback launches", so every mob poke tumbled the hero into a 30-frame knockdown and 10-frame get-up. | `HitPayload.SourceIsStandardMob` (stamped by `EnemyController` on its attack hitbox for `EnemyTier.Standard` and by `EnemyAbilityExecutor` on the pooled shots a standard mob fires; reset by `EnemyProjectile.Setup`). `PlayerController.ResolveHitLaunches` never launches the Story player for such a hit — grounded hitstun with a horizontal slide, no tumble, no DI, no knockdown — **unless the payload authors `Launches`**. Elites, bosses and hazards keep the legacy rule. Story-only; the sim never reads the field. | Unassigned (user ruling) | A ruling on which enemy/boss/hazard hits launch (amending `VERIFY-ABILITY-LAUNCHES`); if per-ability, author an `EnemyAbilityData` launch flag and retire the tier stamp. |
| **PROVISIONAL-QUEUED-CHAIN-PRESS** — the basic-string input buffer (plan F3) | `design-godot.md` Basic Attack & 3-Hit Combo String: subsequent attacks chain through the 24-frame chain-hold/buffer window; holding attack through the window continues the chain. The plan adds: active-frame and hitstop presses buffer, and a press made after the next swing started counts for the following swing. | Both modes: a press in startup, **active frames** or the **hitstop freeze** buffers the next hit (the old active-frame exclusion is gone), holding BasicAttack at the recovery end or through the hold continues the chain (`BasicComboRules.ChainContinues`), and — beyond the plan's wording — a **second** press made while the next hit is already buffered is queued for the hit after it (Story `_queuedAttackBuffered`; sim `FighterBasicAttackRules.FlagQueuedBuffered`, a spare `AttackFlags` bit). Without the queue, the probe's "three presses on a 12-frame rhythm" still gave two hits (the 2nd and 3rd presses both fall inside hit 1). One deep, so the authored string pace is unchanged — N presses give at most N swings, never a faster one. Time Freeze discards, never buffers. **Amended 2026-10-12 (`USER-AERIAL-SINGLE-STRIKE`):** the queue serves the grounded string only; the air strike drops a press made during it (no buffer, no queue, no hold-to-continue), and a buffered or held continuation that would begin airborne ends the string instead of chaining. The `ThreePresses…` pin in `FeelPassStoryTests` and its other chain cases moved to a grounded harness. | Unassigned (user ruling) | The user keeps the one-deep queue or reverts it to a single buffer (drop the queue flag in both modes and the two `ThreePresses…` pins in `FeelPassStoryTests` / `FeelPassSimTests`). |
| **F2-SINGLE-FRAME-CLOCK** — what times a player hitbox (plan F2) | `design-godot.md:1018`: hitboxes and hurtboxes are "driven by **AnimatedSprite2D frame callbacks** for frame-perfect accuracy"; `:1035`: "An `AnimatedSprite2D` frame callback activates the hitbox `Area2D` at the authored active frames" (also `:1248`, and `:2517` for Joan). | No sprite or `AnimationPlayer` callback drives any player hitbox. Every Story swing (template-timed or authored-profile) and every `BaseSpecial` phase runs on `PlayerController`'s physics frame clock from the `BasicComboRules` / `AbilityData` frame tables — the one clock hitstop suspends. `CharacterFactory` no longer builds a `CombatAnimationPlayer`, and `resources/Animations/placeholder_combat_animation_library.tres` (a second copy of the frame tables, on an idle clock hitstop never paused) is deleted. Sprites are posed from the windows (`AttackPoseRules`); the sim's phase machine was already frame-clocked. Raised by the round-1 FEEL review. | Unassigned | Amend `design-godot.md` §Combat Mechanics (`:1018`, `:1035`, `:1248`) to name the physics frame clock as the hitbox authority with the sprite posed from it; a production animation library must be driven from that clock, never drive it. `FeelPassStoryTests.HitstopExtendsTemplateAndAuthoredProfileSwingsByExactlyTheFreeze` and `StoryCombatRulesTests.NoSecondAttackClockDuplicatesTheFrameTables` stay green. |
| ~~**SIM-SPECIAL-POSE-THIRDS**~~ — Fighter presentation of on-press Specials (plan F1) — **closed 2026-10-16** with `DEFER-SIM-SPECIAL-PHASES` | `design-godot.md:3878` (the retro 3-frame contract): each animation's three key poses are stretched across the authored frame windows; plan F1 poses Specials from their authored startup/active/recovery. | The sim resolves Specials on press (`DEFER-SIM-SPECIAL-PHASES`), so `FighterSimulationDriver` poses a Special's (and the generic `ultimate` sheet's) cosmetic hold in equal thirds of its 18 `AbilityPresentationFrames` (`AttackPoseRules.PoseForHold`), not the authored windows; the same Special shows its strike pose at different times in the two modes. Posed from real sim phases: basic swings (`AttackPhase`), Tubman's Foresight and — since fix round R11 — the activation strike for its whole duration (`FighterUltimateActivationRules.IsCasterBusy`: wind-up, strike, the cinematic held on the strike pose as Story poses the Mirror clone's Ultimate, the 45-frame whiff recovery on the follow-through, with no cosmetic hold trailing it; round 1 left the whiff-recovery branch unreachable). The movement ability is never posed in either mode: it plays as a reel (`FighterSimulationDriver.PosesAbilityHold`, R11; round 1 posed it in thirds in Fighter only). Presentation only, read-only over sim state. Raised by the round-1 FEEL review. **Closed 2026-10-16 with `DEFER-SIM-SPECIAL-PHASES` (CM-SIM, merged `5434151`):** `FighterSimulationDriver.ResolveAbilityPresentation` poses `special_1` / `special_2` from component 323 for the whole clocked cast (Startup the wind-up, Active the strike, Recovery the follow-through; `SpecialCastPose` → `AttackPoseRules.PoseFor`), with no cosmetic hold trailing it, so a Special shows its strike pose on its authored active frames in both modes. The thirds path is kept only for a 0 / 1 / 0 fallback cast (`FlagFallbackTiming`, hand-built contracts) and the press-tick Ultimate edge; production Specials and the activation strike are posed from their phases. `FeelPassSimTests.TheWholeSpecialCastIsPosedFromItsPhases` pins it, and `FighterPresentationSyncTests.AcceptedFighterSpecialHoldsTheAuthoredAbilityPose` was rewritten to the phases. | Unassigned | Closes with `DEFER-SIM-SPECIAL-PHASES`: when sim Specials gain phases the driver poses them from those phases and the thirds path is deleted. `FeelPassSimTests.ThePresentationPosesFollowTheSimPhases`, `TheWholeActivationStrikeIsPosedFromItsPhase` and `TheFighterMovementAbilityPlaysItsReelRatherThanAPose` pin today's mapping. **Met 2026-10-16**, except that the thirds path is not deleted: it now serves only the hand-built fallback and the press-tick Ultimate edge. |
| **ZONE-RESOLVED-HIT-FREEZE** — a Story/Fighter difference in which ability hits freeze | `design-godot.md:1020-1023` ("Hitstop / Hitlag"): every landed direct player-authored hit — specials and ultimates included — freezes both parties; zone and DoT ticks are exempt. The design does not say which multi-pulse abilities are impacts and which are ticks. | Story authors Leonardo's Golden Ratio (`leonardo/special_1.tres`), Mozart's Requiem Chord (`mozart/special_1.tres`, whose burst pulses inherit it) and all nine Ultimates `Delivery = 0` (`HitDelivery.DirectHit`), so since F5 their first landed hit per execution freezes the caster (`PlayerController.AbilityHitFreezesCaster`) and each landed hit freezes a mob or boss victim. The sim delivers the same hits as zone pulses with `appliesHitstop: false` — Golden Ratio as zone type 21, the Requiem burst as `FighterReachKitRules.RequiemBurstZone`, and every Ultimate's cinematic as one ultimate-slot zone (type `CharacterID × 10 + 3`, `FighterUltimateRules.SpawnCinematicZone`) — so in Fighter Mode neither fighter freezes on them. `PlayerController.ApplyAbilityCasterHitstop`'s doc comment still says "The Fighter simulation already freezes both parties of every direct hit", which is not true for these. Raised by the round-1 FEEL review. | Unassigned | A ruling on whether these hits are impacts (the sim's zone pulses for them pass `appliesHitstop: true` with a once-per-execution caster freeze) or ticks (Story authors them `Tick`); then one parity test pins both modes and the comment is corrected. |
| **F5-CASTER-FREEZE-ATTRIBUTION** — which cast a late hit's caster freeze belongs to (fix round R12) | `design-godot.md:1020` freezes both parties of each landed direct hit; plan F5 limits the caster's freeze to once per execution. The design does not cover a hit that lands after the next cast began. | A pooled shot carries the `BaseSpecial.ExecutionSerial` of the cast that fired it (`PlaceholderProjectile.SourceExecutionSerial` → `Hitbox.SourceExecutionSerial` → `HitPayload.SourceExecutionSerial`, stamped by `BaseSpecial.SpawnPlaceholderProjectile`), and so do the deferred second stages (the E=mc² burst, Yorick's sonic wave, the Requiem Chord's burst pulses); 0 means the current execution. `BaseSpecial.TryClaimCasterHitstop(serial)` claims **monotonically** (a serial at or below the last claimed one is refused), so a late shot from cast N never spends cast N+1's freeze. Residuals: a late shot from a cast that never froze does not freeze once a newer cast already has (a rare under-freeze, never a double one), and a late shot that does claim still freezes the caster in whatever it is doing now. Before R12 the freeze went to whichever execution was current when the hit landed (round-1 FEEL review). `MozartRequiemChord` tracks one chord and one burst at a time, so two chords in flight would cross-attribute; that is unreachable at the authored 7 s cooldown against a 3 s chord lifetime. | Unassigned | A ruling that the monotonic rule is the intent, or a per-execution claim set. `FeelPassStoryTests.ALateShotFromAnEarlierCastNeverSpendsTheCurrentCastsFreeze` and the three deferred-stage cases (`ADeferredEmc2Burst…`, `ADeferredYorickWave…`, `ARequiemBurstIsChargedToTheCastThatFiredTheChord`) pin today's rule. |

## Confirmed open deviations — playtest feel pass 2026-10-04, fix round (level bugs)

The fix round's level items (fix-level G1, G3, G4, G6; G8 was not a bug — see `VERIFY-AREAPULSE-WALLS`), each from a game bug the Story playtest bots surfaced (`docs/PLAYTEST_FEEL_2026-10-04_PLAN.md` §3). Observed in `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-07 (uncommitted on `5d3ce25`). Closed rows record build fixes on headless evidence and are kept for their history. Nobody has played any of it; `play`-bot runs on the fix tree (Joan, Easy) completed Levels 3, 9 and 12.

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~**FIX-L06-WINCH-DETECTION**~~ — Pompeii's rockfall winch (G1) | `PACKAGE5_CAMPAIGN_PLAN.md` L06: the rockfall winch puzzle is solvable; Package 12 W8 (M02) moved the movable weights onto Environment. | **Closed (fix round G1).** `PressurePlateTemplate.tscn`'s `collision_mask` was 513 (Player + PersistentObject, the props' old layer), so no `WeightedObject` entered a plate by physics — only the west pan's authored load was registered, by hand — and Godot does not wake a sleeping `RigidBody2D` when the static shape under it is disabled, so the pumice stayed asleep at (4260, 320) after its wedge broke and the rockfall at x ≈ 5400 never opened. Now the mask is 65 (Player + Environment; `PressurePlate.RegisterBody` still weighs only `WeightedObject`s and the hero, so floors and walls weigh nothing), and `DamageableEnvironmentObject.DestroyObject` wakes every `movable_weight` body within `RestingWeightWakeMarginPixels` (64) of the broken shape, deferred behind the deferred shape write inside a physics flush. Level 6 is the only campaign user of plates and weights. **Still open, bot side:** the `play` bot does not know to stand on the east pan as the missing counterweight once the pumice lands there (RightPan 2 of 3), idles at the rockfall (≈5352, 900) and collapses on the timer. | Fix round G1 | Closed on headless evidence: `Level06RockfallPhysicsTests` (2: the template sees the player and the weights; on the real Level 6 scene, breaking the wedge drops the pumice onto the east pan, the hero balances the winch and the rockfall opens). A `PlaytestRouteHints` step for the east pan would let the bots measure Level 6. |
| **LIFT-STOP-RULE** — moving-platform endpoints (G3) | Bot game bug 7 (plan §3): the Level 12 spire lift descended onto a hero standing in its footprint and drove the hero through the floor and out of the world. The design gives no endpoint rule. | Build fix with a content contract: every `PathMovingPlatform` stop and `TrapdoorPlatform` leaf in Levels 1–15 stands clear of static geometry or sinks into it as a **flush walk-on stop** — top on the solid's top (±0.5 px) and no deeper than the solid (`ArenaGeometryContentTests.NoMovingPlatformEndpointOverlapsStaticGeometry`, at least 29 endpoints, through the new `PathMovingPlatform.AuthoredOrigin`). The refused shape is the half-sunk stop: the spire lift's 240 × 32 deck stopped centred at 1900, its top 16 px proud of the 1900 regolith and its bottom 16 px inside it. It now stops flush — scene position 7050/1916, waypoint −1516 (the top stop is unchanged at 400) — and `Level12Controller.Lifts` reads `SpireLiftBottomY` (`SurfaceY + 16`), pinned to the scene. Level 14's cargo lift keeps its existing flush stop on the 1700 deck (an interim fix-round change that made it a 32 px step on the critical path, which standard mobs cannot climb, was not kept). Level 7's SkiffA west stop moved −140 → −80, because its hull slid 60 px into the gun pier (`Level07Controller.Skiffs` changed with it). | Unassigned | None beyond the user's review. `TheEndpointContractRefusesAHalfSunkStopAndAllowsAFlushOrClearOne`, `Level12ContentTests.TheLiftTableIsTheAuthoredSceneAndTheSpireLiftBoardsWithoutAStep` and `Level14ContentTests.TheCargoLiftBoardsWithoutAStepAtBothDecks` stay green. |
