| **PROVISIONAL-LIFT-HOLD-OVER-HERO** — a descending deck and a hero beneath it (G3) | The same bug; the design is silent on elevator safety. | *Provisional.* A descending `PathMovingPlatform` whose next step would overlap a body on `CrushGuardMask` (the Player layer only) whose origin lies more than `RiderFeetTolerancePixels` (2) below the deck top **holds** (`IsHeldByBodyBelow`, zero `PlatformVelocity`) and resumes once the hero steps out. Riders are carried as before; sideways and upward moves are untouched; one shape query per platform is reused. Enemies are deliberately not guarded, so a mob patrolling a bottom stop, or holding under a hero riding down (P4), cannot park a lift in mid-air. The alternative — the deck carries or pushes the hero out — was not built. | Unassigned | A user ruling (hold above the hero, or push/carry). `LiftDescentPhysicsTests` (3: a lift holds above a hero and lands once the hero steps out; a rider is carried all the way down; a flush walk-on stop holds, then lands level and is walked across) pins today's rule. |
| **PREBOSS-BEAT-AFTER-DEFEAT** — the post-boss chain and a late pre-boss trigger (G4) | N01: boss defeat → defeat dialogue → the sealing anchor arms → a single Interact seals → vignette → results; the level's pre-boss beat (`level_NN.preboss`) leads into the fight. | Bot game bug 8 (plan §3): on Level 5 a bot fought the Tidal Overseer short of its arena, then struck the PreBoss fracture beside the pre-boss trigger; once the sealing anchor never armed, once `level_05.preboss` played after `level_05.postboss`. Root cause: `DialogueManager.StartSequence(id)` drops a sequence requested over an active one but still returns true, so the post-boss chain waited for a completion that never came. Fix round G4, confined to `StoryLevelControllerBase` (`DialogueManager` is unchanged; an interim change to its return contract broke `Level15ContentTests`, `EpilogueFlowTests` and `NearCaptureBeatTests` and was reverted): the base's post-boss and exit beats go through `RunBeatWhenDialogueFree`, queued while `IsDialogueSequenceActive` and released from the completion event after the usual beat delay, and the pre-boss triggers of Levels 5, 12 and 15 go through `StartPreBossDialogue`, which plays nothing once `IsBossDefeated`, `SealAccepted` or `LevelComplete`. *Provisional:* a pre-boss beat whose trigger is first crossed after the boss fell is skipped for good, not deferred; a PreBoss fracture struck after the defeat leaves AwaitingSeal and the seal chain alone. Every other dialogue call is unchanged. Not changed: a checkpoint struck after level completion still runs `SaveManager.SaveCheckpoint` (reachable only with a fracture beside a sealing anchor). | Unassigned | A ruling only if the skipped lead-in should play somewhere else. `PostBossChainTests` (6, including the real Titanic scene where the Overseer falls first) pins today's chain. |
| ~~**FIX-L09-EXTRACTOR-SLIVERS**~~ — Extractors that leave a sliver of deck (G6) | Bot game bug 10 (plan §3): Level 9's watchtower Extractor left about 14 px of its walkway to stand on. | **Closed (fix round G6).** `Level09Controller.ExtractorPlacements` (IDs unchanged): `level_09.extractor_watchtower` (5620, 480) → (6194, 440), flush on the east end of the walkway deck (5550–6250 at 512); `level_09.extractor_radar_mast` (7130, 580) → (7404, 540), the same fault on the 7040–7460 gantry at 612. The scene's `FeedRelayTower` block moved x 6150 → 6020 to make room (ID and condition unchanged). | Fix round G6 | Closed on headless evidence: `ArenaGeometryContentTests.NoExtractorLeavesASliverOfTheDeckItStandsOn` (no Extractor leaves less than a 40 px hero width of a deck it stands on, pedestals exempt, over every level). A `play` run on Level 9 Easy completed. |

## Confirmed open deviations — the ability lockout (special global cooldown), 2026-10-08

A user-directed combat rule, built in both modes on `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-08 (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`; committed as `2e9ca79` on 2026-10-12). The rule and its 30 frames live once in `FTT.Combat.BasicComboRules` (`AbilityLockoutFrames`, `IsAbilityLocked`, `TickAbilityLockout`); Story keeps the counter on `PlayerController`, the sim in `FighterRuntimeComponent.AbilityLockoutFrames` (component 305's first int, the unread `BasicCooldownFrames` reused, so 305 stays exactly 128 B and no component ID was allocated). Suites: `AbilityLockoutRulesTests`, `StoryAbilityLockoutTests`, `FighterAbilityLockoutTests`. Nobody has played it.

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **USER-ABILITY-LOCKOUT** — a special global cooldown | **User direction 2026-10-08:** "half a second for using specials after using other abilities — the normal string attack should continue to behave as expected", preventing specials "at the same time as normal attacks or other specials/ultimates". Rulings asked and given: a basic swing arms it; the Ultimate is locked too; the movement ability is not involved; both modes. This supersedes `design-godot.md` :782 (Attacking → UsingSpecial "if special off cooldown"), :1034 and :1487 ("a special or the ultimate cancels a swing at any point" / "at any time"), the Cooldown System bullets :1094–1103 (per-slot cooldowns are the only input block), :3900 ("legal basic-to-Special/Ultimate cancels") and the CPU rows :3994 / :4009 (specials chained straight off a basic). **Amended by the user's 2026-10-11 rulings (`DECISIONS.md` D1, D1a, D10 and its addendum):** a basic swing that **lands** a hit may cancel straight into Special 1 or Special 2 (hit-confirm Specials only); whiffs still lock; a Special accepted within the last 30 frames keeps blocking Specials; **the Ultimate keeps the 30-frame lockout after every swing, landed or not, in both modes** (no hit-confirm for the Ultimate). **Landed** (D1a) = damage was applied, or a shield or barrier layer absorbed it; not a block-stance block, an invulnerability / arena-ward / Time Freeze / world-hold refusal, a whiff, or a hit on a construct, prop or strike surface. A Special 1/2 press during the hitstop freeze of a landed hit fires on the first tick after the freeze if it is legal then (**the freeze-only Special buffer**, both modes; not a general lockout buffer; the Ultimate is not buffered). A Special 1, Special 2 or Ultimate press the lockout refuses **flashes that slot's HUD icon** (D10, both modes, visual only); refused presses are still dropped. | Starting any basic swing (each chain hit, buffered and held continuations included, the up-attack, the down-air), casting Special 1 or 2, or having the Ultimate accepted sets a 30-frame lockout; Special 1, Special 2 and the Ultimate are refused while it runs (silently, spending no cooldown or meter, arming nothing). Basics are never refused; the movement ability, roll, air dodge, grab and Echo Step neither arm nor read it. Armed on tick T, the next special or Ultimate is legal on T + 30 in both modes. A special or the Ultimate still cancels a swing once the lockout has run out (late in grounded hit 3, in the chain hold). **Amended 2026-10-11 by `USER-HIT-CONFIRM-CANCEL` (D1):** a swing that lands releases Special 1/2 from its own lockout. Whiffs still lock. Special → Special and every swing → Ultimate keep the full 30 frames. **Completed at the 2026-10-11 finalize step:** "lands" is the D1a predicate (Story: the receiver's report, `Hurtbox.ReportLanded` → `HitPayload.ContactLanded`; sim: `FighterDamageRules.ResolveFighterHit` = `Landed` or `Absorbed`; a zero-damage payload never lands, the orchestrator's ruling). Specials read `BasicComboRules.SpecialGateFrames(A, S, C)` and the Ultimate reads A alone (`USER-HIT-CONFIRM-CANCEL`). A refusal is no longer silent: a press the lockout alone refused flashes its slot (`USER-NOT-YET-FLASH`). A Special press inside the attacker freeze of the fighter's own landed swing is held one deep and re-presented on the thaw tick (`USER-FREEZE-SPECIAL-BUFFER`, `PROVISIONAL-FREEZE-SPECIAL-BUFFER`); every other refused press is dropped. Per-hit Momentum (`USER-PER-HIT-MOMENTUM`) refunds both Special cooldowns on every landed swing. | User (design amendment) | The GDD master (`design-godot-v7.md`) amended at the lines cited and re-synced to the mirror; a hands-on session confirms 0.5 s reads right in both modes. The 2026-10-11 amendments are accepted through `USER-HIT-CONFIRM-CANCEL`, `USER-FREEZE-SPECIAL-BUFFER` and `USER-NOT-YET-FLASH`. |
| **PROVISIONAL-ABILITY-LOCKOUT-RULES** — details the direction left open | The direction gives the duration, the arming and the gated actions only. | *Provisional, agent-chosen:* (1) armed at **acceptance**, not at the cast's end. The sim fires specials on press (`DEFER-SIM-SPECIAL-PHASES`), and every Story Special runs 30+ frames, so in Story the lockout mostly bites through a block-cancelled Special recovery or a triggered Foresight. (2) It pauses in the fighter's own hitstop in both modes, and under Time Freeze in Story, like the ability cooldowns; it is never scaled by Story cooldown perks and never persisted. (3) Cleared by a Story death rewind, a checkpoint restore and the F22 Sudden Death reset; left to expire on a stock loss. (4) **Same-tick order, both modes:** a basic swing (including a chain continuation starting that tick: Story's `ChainSwingStartsThisTick` mirrors the sim, which starts swings in `FighterMovementSystem` before any ability is polled) → an Ultimate the meter pays for (`FighterCombatSystem.UltimatePressAccepted`; in the sim it now also takes the tick from the **movement ability**, as Story always polled it first) → Special 1 → Special 2. In the sim an accepted entity-system special also takes the tick from the movement ability, as Story polls specials first. The generic Special 1 block resolves before Tubman's Foresight and Joan's Divine Piercing. Lincoln's Splitting Strike no longer yields to an unpaid Ultimate press. A fighter that an activation strike captures this tick takes no further action that tick; without that, the ability system's early Ultimate claim let a held-back special reach `BuildIntent` as a phantom melee hit. (5) Refusal is silent: no cue, no input buffer, no HUD display of the lockout. (6) The CPU reads `CpuDecisionObservation.AbilityLockoutFrames` as it will stand at delivery (≤ the band's minimum reaction delay). Its Echo Step meter valuation ignores the lockout. While a viable Ultimate waits on the CPU's own lockout, it stops pressing BasicAttack, so a buffered chain hit cannot re-arm the lockout past the string. **Amended 2026-10-11 (D1, `PROVISIONAL-HIT-CONFIRM-RULES`):** item (3) — the clears extend to the D1 Special-sourced lockout S, the swing-landed latch C and the freeze-only Special buffer (Story `ClearAbilityLockout`, sim `FighterAbilityLockoutRules.Clear`); a stock loss still lets A and S expire on the respawn platform and leaves C, but drops a held freeze-buffer press. Item (6) — the CPU's Special rungs (close S1, close S2, ranged S1) and its mobility-Special check read the D1 Special gate at delivery (`SpecialLockoutClearAtDelivery`); the Ultimate commit and the wait rule keep the lockout. **Amended 2026-10-11 (D10, agent A2):** item (5) "Refusal is silent … no HUD display" is superseded by `USER-NOT-YET-FLASH`: a refusal the lockout alone caused now flashes that slot's HUD icon. There is still no lockout timer on screen, and no buffer beyond the freeze-only Special buffer (`PROVISIONAL-FREEZE-SPECIAL-BUFFER`). **Amended 2026-10-11 (the user's rulings, recorded at the finalize step):** item (1) — beside A, every Special 1/2 and Ultimate acceptance arms S, the Special-sourced lockout, which ticks with A; a landed swing then gates Specials on S alone, while the Ultimate always reads A (`USER-HIT-CONFIRM-CANCEL`). Item (4) — the same-tick order gains one rule: a meter-paid Ultimate held back only by the lockout claims the tick from Special 1/2, never from the movement ability (`PROVISIONAL-HIT-CONFIRM-RULES` (4)). Item (5) — "no input buffer" is amended by the user's freeze-only Special buffer (`USER-FREEZE-SPECIAL-BUFFER`): a Special 1/2 press inside the attacker freeze of the fighter's own landed swing is held and re-presented on the thaw tick, where the ordinary gates judge it; nothing else is buffered and the Ultimate never is. **Amended 2026-10-16 (decisions 1.2 and 9.3):** item (1) — the sim now runs each Special's authored phases (`USER-SIM-SPECIAL-TIMING`), so the Story reading holds in both modes: the lockout armed at acceptance mostly bites through a recovery the Block cancel or D3 cuts short (`USER-FIGHTER-SPECIAL-RECOVERY-CANCEL`), and a Special or Ultimate press during the fighter's own cast is discarded by the cast lock, never refused by the lockout, so it never flashes. Item (6) — the CPU's lockout predicates (`AbilityLockoutClearAtDelivery`, `SpecialLockoutClearAtDelivery`) also require `CastIdleAtDelivery`, and a Special or Ultimate edge is dropped at delivery while the CPU's own cast holds the tick (`USER-CPU-SPECIAL-TIMING-ONLY`). | Unassigned | A ruling on each, or a playtest that leaves them alone. A HUD display or an input buffer for a refused press, if wanted, is new work with a localization and HUD-contract pass. |
| **VERIFY-SAME-TICK-VERB-PARITY** — pre-existing Story/sim same-tick differences the lockout reviews surfaced (items (1) and (5) closed 2026-10-16) | One input, one outcome in both modes. The design authors no same-tick verb priority beyond `BasicComboRules.SelectRecoveryVerb`. | None of these were introduced by the lockout, and none were changed. (1) The sim accepts the Ultimate from the block stance, shieldstun and the 12-frame landing-tech lock; Story never polls it there. The sim's tech branch also leaves input live for specials. (2) In a swing's recovery or the chain hold, Story's special or Ultimate beats a same-tick Roll, Block or Echo Step chord, while the sim lets those win. With Jump, the sim does both the jump and the special. (3) On the tick an aerial string lands, Story returns to Idle and drops a same-tick special press that the sim casts. *(Amended 2026-10-12, `USER-AERIAL-SINGLE-STRIKE`: the aerial string is retired, so this item now concerns the tick the air strike lands, whose landing cancels it with no lag in both modes; not re-measured.)* (4) From neutral, a same-tick Jump or Roll + BasicAttack jumps or rolls in Story but swings in the sim. (5) A melee-intent special (Lincoln's Splitting Strike) still loses to a same-tick movement ability in the sim, because it resolves in `BuildIntent` after the movement ability starts. (6) On a tick the opponent grabs or captures the fighter, a same-tick Ultimate + special press yields nothing. **(7) Added 2026-10-11** — two pre-existing offsets that D1 makes player-facing: (a) Story's basic-swing box opens one tick after the sim's for the same press (Story's press tick runs no swing clock, so elapsed 6 = T+7 against the sim's T+6), and its contact reaches the attacker one physics flush later; (b) the sim reads S1/S2/Ultimate presses on a fighter's last hitstop tick (the movement system decrements 1 → 0 and continues; the entity and combat systems read 0), while Story drops every frozen tick. Net: a confirm through a freeze is legal 2 ticks earlier in the sim (T+10 against T+12 after the template opener), 1 tick earlier with no freeze. The freeze-only Special buffer (`PROVISIONAL-FREEZE-SPECIAL-BUFFER`) makes (b) moot for presses made inside the freeze: each mode fires them on its own thaw tick (the sim's last freeze tick, Story's first unfrozen tick). **Amended 2026-10-16 (combat and movement update):** items **(1) and (5) are closed**. (1): the sim refuses the Ultimate in the block stance, shieldstun, the tech lock, a Special cast, a knockdown and a busy A02 caster through the one `UltimatePressPayable` (`USER-NO-ULT-FROM-BLOCK`, decision 2.3), and the tech locks every action from its landing tick, its invulnerability now ending with the lock (13 ticks counting the landing tick, as Story's one 12-frame window: `FIX-SIM-TECH-LOCK-ACTIONS`, decision 1.1(3), and CM-FIXA-SIM ruling (b)). (5): an accepted melee Special spends at press and takes the tick from a same-tick movement ability (`FIX-SPLITTING-STRIKE-FREE-WHIFF`, decision 1.2). CM-FIXA-SIM ruling (d) also matched the recovery Block-cancel tick to Story (the sim raises only the stance; every other verb needs a fresh press). Item (3) was not re-measured. Item (7)(b) stands, but a busy Special caster's, a knocked-down fighter's and a busy A02 caster's buttons are now discarded on their hitstop ticks. **New, recorded, not changed:** (8) **Story fires a Special's effect one tick before the sim:** `StorySpecialTimingProbeTests` measures Einstein's E=mc² (14 / 6 / 20) Active with its projectile on T+13 = T+S−1 (the controller reads the press on T and the child ability node clocks after it on the same tick), against the sim's T+S (`PROVISIONAL-SIM-SPECIAL-PHASE-RULES` (1)). (9) **The Block-release tick:** on the tick Block is released the sim reads `Held` without Block and can accept a paid Ultimate, while Story's `ProcessBlocking` returns to Idle and drops the press (CM-SIM §7; not tested). | Unassigned | A ruling on each, then one shared same-tick table (in the manner of `SelectRecoveryVerb`) read by both modes, with parity pins. **Amended 2026-10-16:** (1) and (5) are met; (8) and (9) join the list. |
| ~~**VERIFY-GAMEPAD-ULTIMATE-CHORD**~~ — pre-existing input defects surfaced by the lockout — **closed 2026-10-16** ((a) and (c) by decision 9.2, `USER-PAD-ULTIMATE-CHORD-LENIENCY`; (b) met 2026-10-11) | `COMFORT_SETTINGS.md` C01c: the gamepad Ultimate is the Movement Ability + Special 2 preset chord ("LB+RB at defaults"). | (a) Nothing suppresses the chord's component presses (`InputManager.SampleGodotDevice`). Pressed on the same tick, the Ultimate now wins in both modes (above). Pressed a frame apart, the first button casts its own verb: a Special 2 then arms the lockout and the chord's Ultimate is refused. Story's `UsingSpecial` state already refused it before the lockout; the sim used to fire both. (b) **Fixed 2026-10-11** (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`; committed as `2e9ca79` on 2026-10-12). `project.godot` bound Special 2 to joypad button 5, the movement ability to 4 and the chord to 4 + 5. In Godot 4's `JoyButton` enum those are **Back** and **Guide**, not the shoulders (9 / 10); "LB+RB" was the Godot 3 numbering. Special 2 therefore sat on the Guide button, which the OS or Steam usually claims, and the movement ability shared Back with Time Freeze, so a gamepad movement press in Story also froze time (`TimeFreezeController` polls `Input.IsActionJustPressed`). The defaults are now the GDD Controls table's: Movement Ability `LeftShoulder` (9), Special 2 `RightShoulder` (10), the chord 9 + 10. Time Freeze moved to L3 (7) by user direction on 2026-10-11 (`USER-GAMEPAD-TIME-FREEZE-L3`). `GamepadDefaultBindingTests` (4 cases, read from `ProjectSettings`, all four failing on the old numbers) pins both shoulders, the chord equal to its recipe's component defaults, no authored action on Guide, and no default button shared by two actions outside the chord. Saved overrides get no schema migration; a load-time repair rewrites the stale rows the Controls tab could not have made on purpose (`PROVISIONAL-GAMEPAD-OVERRIDE-NO-MIGRATION`). (c) Found while fixing (b), not changed: at runtime the gamepad chord is not the C01c recipe. It is the Ultimate action's own two joypad events, which `InputManager.ReadUltimatePressed` reads as a conjunction. So the gamepad direct slot is not "initially Unbound" (the chord fills it, and capturing a direct gamepad button replaces the chord instead of adding to it); the Controls tab's Ultimate-chord On/Off toggle changes nothing at runtime (only Grab and Echo Step consult `IsShortcutEnabled`); and remapping the Movement Ability or Special 2 changes the chord's label, which renders the components, but not the chord. **2026-10-11 (D1, §1.7 of spec A):** a meter-paid Ultimate held back only by the lockout claims the tick from Special 1 and Special 2 in both modes (`PROVISIONAL-HIT-CONFIRM-RULES` (4)), so the chord pressed inside a hit-confirm cannot cast its Special 2; its movement-ability component is unchanged and fires as today. The freeze-only Special buffer holds nothing from a tick that presses the Ultimate, so a same-tick chord's Special 2 is never held either; a split chord whose Special 2 half lands first inside the freeze is held and cast alone at the thaw, as (a) already does outside a freeze (`PROVISIONAL-FREEZE-SPECIAL-BUFFER`). **Closed 2026-10-16 (decision 9.2; CM-INPUT, merged `da08d70`; CM-FIXA-INPUT, merged `98e5e4a`).** (a): while the Ultimate is affordable a lone LB or RB waits 3 frames (`UltimateChordWindow.LeniencyFrames`) for its partner; the partner arriving gives the Ultimate alone with both components masked until they are released. A full-meter chord inside the lockout now casts nothing — neither its Special 2 (the 2026-10-11 D1 rule above) nor its movement ability (`UltimateChordStoryTests.AFullMeterChordInsideTheLockoutCastsNoMovementAbility`). (c): the gamepad Ultimate is the C01c recipe read from the live Movement Ability and Special 2 pad bindings behind `ShortcutEnabled(InputShortcuts.UltimateAction, Joypad)`, so the Controls tab's chord toggle changes the runtime and remapping a component moves the chord; `project.godot`'s `gameplay_ultimate` lost its two joypad events (keyboard U stays), so the pad's direct slot is initially empty, and a captured direct pad button is a route of its own beside the chord. A saved {9, 10} or {4, 5} pad half is dropped at load (`RepairStaleUltimatePadChord`), and a pre-update "chord off" that leaves no pad route is turned back on (`RestoreUnreachableUltimateChord`). `GamepadDefaultBindingTests` now pins no shared default pad button, with no chord exception. | Unassigned | (a) A ruling on chord recognition (a short chord window, or masking the component edges when the chord forms). (b) Met 2026-10-11. (c) The gamepad Ultimate recognized from the recipe's live component bindings behind its shortcut flag, with the direct gamepad slot authored Unbound (C01c), or a ruling that the build's shape stands. **Met 2026-10-16:** (a) by the chord window, (c) by the recipe at runtime; (b) was met 2026-10-11. |
| **PROVISIONAL-GAMEPAD-OVERRIDE-NO-MIGRATION** — saved binding rows that still carry the Godot 3 shoulder numbers | C01c: preserve independent device overrides; no binding is silently stolen. The task that moved the defaults (2026-10-11) ruled out a save-schema bump. | *Provisional, agent-chosen:* no schema migration, and a load-time repair instead. `InputBindingService.BuildOverrides` saves only actions that differ from the project defaults, so a profile that never touched the Movement Ability, Special 2 or the Ultimate inherits the new shoulders at once. A profile that rebound any of them, even on the keyboard only, saved that action's whole event list, so its row still holds the old gamepad half (4, 5, or 4 + 5). Most such rows are not ambiguous: a Controls-tab capture replaces a device half with exactly one event (there is no chord editor), and `InputBindingConflicts.FindConflictingAction` refuses an event any action already holds, so under the old defaults Back and Guide could not be captured at all. `InputBindingSet.RepairGodot3ShoulderRows(projectDefaults)` (pure data, idempotent, no stamp) therefore runs from `SaveManager.ApplySavedInputBindings` just before `InputBindingService.Apply` on every load, rewriting a stale gamepad half onto the action's current project default and keeping the rest of the row: (1) an Ultimate half of exactly Back + Guide, always — only the old default has that shape; (2) a Movement Ability half of exactly Back, or a Special 2 half of exactly Guide, only while another action's effective gamepad half (its row, nothing if its gamepad slot is Unbound, else its default; judged on the payload as loaded) also holds that button — until 2026-10-11 the typical case was the Movement Ability sharing Back with Time Freeze's default (the double trigger of `VERIFY-GAMEPAD-ULTIMATE-CHORD` (b)). Since D11 moved Time Freeze to L3, a stale Movement-on-Back row is repaired only while another loaded row holds Back: a saved Time Freeze row that kept Back, a stale Ultimate chord of Back + Guide (judged as loaded, before its own repair), or any other action saved on Back. The commonest stale profile — a pre-fix keyboard-only Movement rebind with no Time Freeze row — is therefore no longer repaired: it keeps the Movement Ability on Back (it works; nothing double-fires), LB does nothing alone for that profile, and the Controls chord label, which renders the recipe's components, reads "Pad Button 4 + Pad Button 10" while the runtime chord stays LB + RB (`VERIFY-GAMEPAD-ULTIMATE-CHORD` (c)). A profile that captured L3 for another action while L3 was free now shares it with Time Freeze's default, the same undetected class as the pre-fix LB/RB captures. The repaired rows persist with the next global save. Left alone, because after the fix they may be deliberate: a lone Special 2 on Guide (Guide is now free to capture), and a Movement Ability on Back that no other loaded row shares (the commonest case since Time Freeze moved off Back, above). Not detected either: a profile that captured LB (9) or RB (10) for another action before the fix, when the shoulders were bound to nothing, now shares that button with the moved Movement Ability or Special 2 default, so one press fires two verbs; `FindConflictingAction` runs only at capture and nothing scans for a duplicate at load, and no automatic choice between the player's bind and the moved default is safe. The build is unreleased, so only development profiles are affected; the Controls tab's per-action reset or Reset All clears every case. `GamepadOverrideRepairTests` (5, pure C#) pins the rule and `InputBindingSchemaTests.SaveManagerRepairsAStaleGodot3UltimateChordBeforeApplyingIt` the load path. **Amended 2026-10-16 (decision 9.2):** the repair's Ultimate rule changed with the defaults — `project.godot`'s Ultimate has no pad events any more, so `InputBindingSet.RepairStaleUltimatePadChord` (called from `RepairGodot3ShoulderRows`) rewrites a saved Ultimate pad half of exactly {9, 10} or {4, 5} to that empty default, dropping it and keeping the keyboard half (the old {4, 5} rule is folded in; it does nothing when the project default is that pair). CM-FIXA-INPUT added `RestoreUnreachableUltimateChord`, run on every load after the repair: a saved "chord off" that leaves the pad Ultimate no route has its flag removed (a missing flag reads On), because the Controls tab now refuses to commit an unreachable profile. Both are pure, idempotent and logged; no schema change. The "outside the Ultimate chord" exception in this row's scan criterion no longer exists (`GamepadDefaultBindingTests.NoTwoActionsShareADefaultGamepadButton`). | Unassigned | Either accept it, or (i) a load-time scan for a joypad button shared by two effective actions outside the Ultimate chord, with a Controls notice naming both, for the pre-fix shoulder captures; (ii) a ruling on whether the lone Special 2 on Guide **and the lone Movement Ability on Back (unrepaired since D11)** should be repaired too (a one-shot flag on the global payload, which belongs to the next package's single schema bump). |

## Confirmed open deviations — movement feel (snappier run, jump arc, ramped fast-fall), 2026-10-08

A user-directed movement change, built in both modes on `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-08 (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`; committed as `2e9ca79` on 2026-10-12, beside the ability lockout above). The shared numbers live once in `FTT.Core.UniversalMovementRules` (`RunAccelerationFrames`, `RunDecelerationFrames`, `ApexHangSpeedBand`, `ApexHangGravityMultiplier`, `FallGravityMultiplier`, `TerminalFallSpeed`, `LaunchTerminalFallSpeed`, `FastFallSpeed`, `FastFallGravityMultiplier`). Story's own air constants and its per-tick step live in the new `FTT.Core.StoryAirPhysics` (`StepVertical`, called by `PlayerController.ApplyGravity`; plain C#, which also integrates jump arcs for the level code, the bots and the content tests), the sim's in the new `FTT.FighterSim.FighterAirPhysics` (`StepMovement`, `StepLaunch`, `RampStep`, `HasJumpedThisAirtime`). No component, snapshot field or protocol change; every Fighter hash moved. Suites: `StoryAirPhysicsTests`, `StoryMovementFeelTests`, `FighterMovementFeelTests`, plus one pin each in `Level09ContentTests`, `Level10ContentTests`, `TutorialCalibrationContentTests` and `FighterCpuBehaviorTests`. The rope-bar fix that `VERIFY-MOVEMENT-FEEL-FALLOUT` (a) asked for followed on 2026-10-09, also uncommitted (`PendulumAnchorTemplate.tscn`'s rope body on layer 0, `PlaytestNav`'s rope model, `PendulumRopeBarTests`). The Level 10 recess walls followed the same day, also uncommitted (`VERIFY-L10-ARENA-RECESS-EXIT`, the hero half), and then the relocation rule and the arena fall net that closed its boss half (`FTT.Combat.RelocationRules` / `SafeRelocation`; suites `RelocationRulesTests`, `SafeRelocationTests`, `BossArenaFallNetTests`, `Level10BossRelocationTests`), with their agent-chosen details in `PROVISIONAL-RELOCATION-CLEARANCE` and `PROVISIONAL-ARENA-FALL-NET`. A review of that fix found the net misfiring in Level 4's walkable Phase 2 pit and the rule reading a body on a ledge as already over nothing; both were fixed the same day, also uncommitted (`VERIFY-L04-PIT-FALL-NET`, closed: a 240 px margin under every arena's edge, and the origin's support read across the body's width; one new case each in `RelocationRulesTests`, `BossArenaFallNetTests`, `ArenaGeometryContentTests` and `Level04ContentTests`). A second review closed two of the rule's three remaining limits the same day, also uncommitted (`PROVISIONAL-RELOCATION-CLEARANCE`: the body's own box is swept along the travel beside the three wall rays, and a summon emerges from the part of its summoner that stands on something; two new cases each in `RelocationRulesTests` and `SafeRelocationTests`). Nobody has played it.

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **USER-MOVEMENT-FEEL** — snappier run, a hanging jump, a heavier fall, a slower ramped fast-fall | **User direction 2026-10-08:** "I want to make movement more snappy, currently the acceleration/decelleration is too slow and the player feels sluggish when moving. I also want to make updates to jumping. I want to increase the hang time, so reduce the gravity at the apex of the jump, and then increase the gravity as the player is falling to increase falling speed. I want to change the down ability or the down movement while jumping. Currently it's way too high, and the player falls almost instantly to the ground that should be lower. We should put a lower maximum fall speed." Rulings asked and given: everything in **both modes**; fast-fall works any time in the air but **ramps in** instead of snapping; the run ramp is **6 frames up / 4 frames to stop**. This supersedes `design-godot.md` :825 (the 14 / 12-frame run ramp), :827 and :1454 (fast-fall "immediately sets" / "snaps to" the 20 u/s terminal), the physics table's gravity rows :954–957 (fall ×2.5, short hop ×3.5 — no apex hang), Standard Jumping :971–975 (a jump shaped only by the short hop) and the Gravity & Fall Speed block :1450–1455 (the 20 u/s terminal outright; its base-gravity and H-7 lines now carry the shared multipliers on top). | Both modes: the grounded run reaches full speed on frame **6** and stops on frame **4**, counted from the stick, at every roster move speed. Under normal control (every airborne state except hitstun / tumble, knockdown, thrown and daze) one gravity multiplier per tick, chosen from the velocity before the step, first match wins: fast-fall (Down held) ×3, cancelling the float; the movement-ability float window, unchanged; Story only, the short hop (rising with Jump released) ×2.5; the **apex hang** ×0.5 while \|vy\| < 2 u/s, once the fighter has jumped this airtime; the fall ×2.2; else 1. The fall caps at **12 u/s** (Story 720 px/s), **15 u/s** (900 px/s) while fast-falling. The launch states keep their old gravity. Measured (integrated per tick, old → new): Story single jump Lincoln 129.2 → 132.6 px, airborne 44 → 53 ticks; Einstein 181.4 → 186.4 px, 58 → 69; Cleopatra 209.0 → 213.7 px, 66 → 77; ticks inside the band 7–10 → 18–24. Sim single jump Lincoln 2.108 → 2.146 u (46 → 48 ticks), Einstein 2.500 → 2.537 (50 → 52), Cleopatra 2.604 → 2.654 (51 → 54); hang ticks 7–8 → 14–15. Sim fast-fall from the apex lands in 15–17 ticks (the old snap 9–10, a plain fall 23–26). Feel probe (Level 2): 95 % of top speed on frame 6 after the push (was 15), stopped 4 frames after the release (was 13), stop slide 7–12 px (was 31–50). **Amended 2026-10-12 (`USER-MOVEMENT-SPEED-CUT`):** the 6 / 4-frame ramps, the jump-arc multipliers and caps and the hang are unchanged and still land on their frame at the cut run speeds (the sim's `RampStep` rounds up per move speed); the run speeds are × 0.8, so every run-speed-derived figure above (jump distances, the stop slide, the feel probe's distances) is pre-cut, while rises and airtimes are unchanged to 0.01 px (`StoryMovementSpeedCutTests`, `FighterMovementSpeedCutTests`). **Amended 2026-10-16** by decision 6.1 ("Hang only while Jump is held, both modes. | **PROVISIONAL-MOVEMENT-FEEL-RULES** — details the direction left open | The direction gives the shape (snappier ramps, more hang, a heavier fall, a slower fast-fall under a lower cap); the rulings give both modes, the ramp-in and 6 / 4 frames. Nothing else is specified. | *Provisional, agent-chosen:* (1) The numbers: a 2 u/s hang band (120 px/s) at ×0.5; the fall at ×2.2 (Story's was 1.8, the sim had none); a shared 12 u/s terminal (Story 10 → 12, sim 20 → 12; the sim's float glide now caps at 12 too); the fast-fall cap 20 → 15 at ×3. (2) **The ease rule:** a descent already above its cap (a released fast-fall, a launch that has just ended) eases down by one base-gravity step per tick instead of snapping; one that reaches the cap on this tick stops on it. (3) **The hang's discriminator is "jumped this airtime".** The sim reads `RemainingJumps < MaxJumpCount` (its ledge grab refills jumps and its climb jump sets `MaxJumpCount − 1`). Story reads a latch, `PlayerController.JumpedThisAirtime`, set by every jump (ground, coyote, buffered, air, and a ledge or swinging-rope jump-off) and cleared by floor contact in any state, a ledge or rope grab and the Tempest Apex jump refresh, but not by a launch (the sim leaves `RemainingJumps` alone in hitstun). Story's jump budget is unchanged by ruling, which is why the count alone could not say it. So a walk-off, a drop-through or a ledge drop gets no hang, and a ledge or rope jump-off (Story) or a climb jump (sim) does. (4) **Launch states unchanged:** Story ×1.8 with a hard 600 px/s clamp; the sim flat −30 clamped at 20 (`LaunchTerminalFallSpeed`, M01's old number). (5) **Fast-fall scope:** the sim on every normal-control airborne tick (gravity-suspending kit phases excepted); Story through `Airborne`, aerial swings (the down-air keeps stacking into a falling strike) and the air dodge, **not aerial Specials**, whose startup and active frames run half-time gravity with no sim counterpart (`DEFER-SIM-SPECIAL-PHASES`). (6) **Story push and release ticks:** the Idle → Running push tick takes the first ramp step and the Running → Idle release tick the first stop step. Both were dead state-change ticks, so Story read 15 / 13 against the sim's 14 / 12. The Idle push tick restores the tick's starting speed before its one step, so a re-press after a one-tick release takes one step, as in the sim. (7) **Standstill turnaround:** a push against the facing turns at once unless it reverses real travel (`PushReversesTravel`: the body moves the other way faster than one 4-frame stop step). From a standstill the hero faces the push and reaches full speed on frame 6 with no skid; a real reversal at speed keeps the skid, whose first tick now takes a stop step, so a full-speed reversal takes 10 frames (4 + 6), as in the sim. A reversal begun while still sliding from Idle still pays the 3-tick skid timer and can trail the sim by up to 2 ticks. A push with Down held still crouches. (8) **FP64 round-up:** `FighterAirPhysics.RampStep` rounds the per-frame step up by one raw unit when the division is inexact; truncation (7 / 6 at the default speed) left the ramp a frame late. (9) **CPU recovery-jump revalidation:** `FighterCpuController` flags a recovery jump (`ScheduledDecision.RecoveryJump`) and strips its Jump bit at delivery when the CPU's own `RemainingJumps` is 0, keeping the steer, per `CPU_RECOVERY.md`'s "revalidate legality immediately before each planned action". No new RNG draw, no snapshot state. It stops jumps queued before the first press was observed from overwriting Medium's one pending movement-ability edge. Point-mass harness, Medium, start vy −4, 400 seeds: old physics 344 survive, new physics 319, new physics with the fix 377; Easy from a standing start below the floor 36 → 0 (not asserted, matching the contract). The CPU's pit-risk probe now predicts the hang (`Observe(..., maxJumpCount)`). Still open: jumps queued inside one reaction window can spend the second jump mid-rise (Hard too), two edges inside one 3-tick pulse still overwrite each other through `_pendingEdges`, and every recovery input still carries the full reaction delay. (10) **Level geometry:** Level 10 room 3's yard wall (x 8280) rises to the bridging span's underside (`Level10Controller.YardWallTopY` = 628, was 700), closing a hang-enabled jump over it; Level 9 room 4's east boundary wall now runs from y 0 to `GroundY` (it stopped at 440, 352 px over the east castle deck, which five heroes' double jumps already cleared and the hang added Tesla and Leonardo); Level 0's gated double-jump step moved to (1900, 540), 240 px above the first step (`Level00Controller.DoubleJumpGatedStepX/Y`), because Lincoln's single jump (~133 px) had cleared the old 128 / 130 px steps. **Amended 2026-10-16:** item (3) — the hang's discriminator gains a second term in both modes: the apex hang applies only while Jump is held (decision 6.1, `USER-APEX-HANG-HELD`; the sim's component-323 latch, Story's `jumpHeld`), beside "jumped this airtime". Item (5) — the sim's aerial Specials now have a counterpart (decision 1.2): a cast that started airborne steps gravity × `UniversalMovementRules.AerialSpecialGravityMultiplier` (0.5, which Story's `ProcessUsingSpecial` forwards) in Startup and Active and discards Down, so neither mode fast-falls during an aerial Special. Item (9) — recovery jumps are now held full hops (decision 1.1(5), `FIX-CPU-JUMP-HOLD`); a queued second jump first releases Jump for one tick (`EdgeReleaseGapFrames`), so it arrives one tick later than before; the delivery revalidation is unchanged; and since CM-FIXB-CPU the hitstun-defense Block hold lets the planner's Jump and movement edges through once hitstun ends (`FIX-CPU-HITSTUN-HOLD-EATS-RECOVERY`). | Unassigned | A ruling on each, or a playtest that leaves them alone. `StoryMovementFeelTests`, `FighterMovementFeelTests`, `Level09ContentTests.TheEastBoundaryWallRunsTheFullHeightAndOutreachesEveryJumpFromTheCastleDeck`, `Level10ContentTests.TheYardWallLeavesNoGapUnderTheBridgeAndTheBridgeIsBeyondEveryJump`, `TutorialCalibrationContentTests.TheDoubleJumpGateNeedsTheSecondJumpFromEveryHero` and `FighterCpuBehaviorTests.ARecoveryJumpArrivingAfterTheJumpsAreSpentIsDroppedAndTheMovementAbilityStillFires` pin today's rules. |
| **VERIFY-MOVEMENT-FEEL-FALLOUT** — consequences the change surfaced and did not fix | The levels stay traversable as designed, and the change moves no other rule. | (a) **Rope chains:** with the hang a held swinging-rope release floated higher for longer and met the next rope's solid bar above its grab box (bot game bug 12, `PendulumAnchorTemplate`'s 16 × 320 Environment-layer rope shape); once the rope jump-off became a hang (3 above), the Level 7 Joan `play` run stopped completing, and a rope-only hang exclusion was ruled out by the ledge parity decision. **The bar was made non-solid on 2026-10-09** (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`; committed as `2e9ca79` on 2026-10-12): the template's rope body went from `collision_layer` 64 (Environment) to 0, its mask already 0, and the 16 × 320 shape stays but touches nothing. None of the seven placed ropes (Level 7 three, Level 10 four) overrides it, no script writes a pendulum's layer or mask, and `PendulumAnchor.cs` changed in comments only. The grab point (`LedgeGrabPoint`, Trigger layer / Player mask), the carry, the release launch, the swing, Time Freeze, rewind, the `puzzle_object` group and Level 10's audience throw never read the bar. Side effects, all intended: the bar no longer stops enemy projectiles, enemy wall and edge probes, the player's melee line-of-sight check, kit ground-snaps or the Echo Step / Warp destination checks, and no hero can stand on it. `PendulumRopeBarTests` (2 cases, real physics frames) pins layer 0 / mask 0 on the template and every placed rope, with the grab box still Trigger / Player and the carry on, and sends an all-bits ray and a dropped hero through a hanging bar, the hero into its grab box; both cases failed against the old template. The bot nav model (`PlaytestNav`) lost its solid bar (bar collisions, standing on a bar, waiting for a swinging bar's gap before a jump, and the "release only while the rope swings toward the target" rule, which existed for the bar). It now follows the controller's catch rule — first entry only, not rising faster than 150 px/s, inside the 3-per-airtime grab budget it reads from a private `PlayerController` field by reflection, a grab box met on the way counting as a catch of that rope — and its release timing: the jump one frame later from the rope's next hang position, the rope's push the frame after (the old model was about 10 % out, which is why Joan kept missing rope B). It refuses a release that would only re-catch the rope just let go of, sets aside after one full swing any release that never reached its own target and re-plans from the rope, and keeps the tapped short-hop release (Joan's Level 7 B → C hop takes it). Bots, Easy `play`, Joan and Lincoln, Levels 7 and 10, seeds 0–3 (`ropefix`): all 16 runs got through the rope channels, against 2 of 16 before. Completions before → after: L7 Joan 0 → 4 of 4; L7 Lincoln 0 → 0, all four crossing and then dying around the skiffs (x 6700–7900) — seed 0's first death was in the channel's sea mid-crossing (x 5587), the one rope-area death in the 16; L10 Joan 0 → 0, all four reaching the boss (a timeout, a timer collapse, two falls out of the world); L10 Lincoln 2 → 2 (seeds 0 and 2 both times; after, seed 1 beat the boss and collapsed on the timer and seed 3 fell out of the world). Channel crossings: Level 7 Joan 20–27 s, Lincoln 68–87 s; Level 10 Joan 12–29 s, Lincoln 32–152 s. **Read the comparison with care:** it mixes the scene change with the planner change (no A/B run separates them); the four seeds are not independent (L7 Joan seeds 0 and 2 both took 99.62 s, L7 Lincoln seeds 1 and 2 died on identical frames); the "before" seed 0 is the `fixfinal` run; and an earlier batch on the same rope code before the last planner fix (`ropefixC`) scored L7 Lincoln 1/4, L10 Joan 2/4 and L10 Lincoln 3/4, so nothing shows that last fix helped. **Still open:** the human pass over both rope chains; the bot tests the 56 × 56 grab box as axis-aligned although it rotates with the rope (up to 52° on Level 7, about ±39 px), so it under-predicts stray catches on ropes it passes. **Closed since:** the Level 10 boss-arena recess that three of the six failed Level 10 runs fell out of (`VERIFY-L10-ARENA-RECESS-EXIT`, below) — walls for the hero on 2026-10-09 (`recessfix`: no run fell out, Level 10 completions 2 → 7 of 8), then the relocation rule the same day for the Tragedy King, whose teleport past the east wall, not a boss fight lost on time, was the Joan seed 0 timeout in both `ropefix` and `recessfix` (`tpfix`: Level 10 8 of 8). The remaining failures come after the ropes: Level 7 Lincoln to the mortars, Extractor discharge and the sea around the skiffs. (b) Story's aerial Specials do not fast-fall (5 above). (c) Story's jump budget is unchanged by ruling: a Story ledge grab refills no jump and the jump-off spends none, so a double jump to a ledge leaves 0 air jumps where the sim leaves 1. (d) Level 0: for the lightest heroes a single jump plus an upward air dodge (about 1 unit of shift) can also reach the 240 px step (Cleopatra ~214 + ~55 px); no step height rules that out and stays inside Lincoln's ~265 px double jump. (e) Leonardo's double jump (354.8 px) now reaches Level 3's optional 348 px coil platforms; Level 10's balconies (268 px) and Level 9's castle decks (408 px) are still out of Lincoln's reach (265.1 px). A movement ability on top of a double jump could already clear the Level 10 span, and still can. Level 12's low gravity lets a double jump from the 400 px highline leave the top of the level, which has no ceiling (pre-existing; Joan 918 → 946 px). (f) Bots, Easy `play`, Levels 2–12, Joan and Lincoln, seed 0 (recounted from the summary files 2026-10-09; earlier prose said 11 / 14): 12 completions of 22 before the change (`mvbase`: Joan 8, Lincoln 4), 15 after it (`mvfinal`: 8 / 7), 15 after the review fixes (`fixfinal`: 7 / 8, L7 Joan lost, L10 Lincoln gained), and 17 at the end of the 2026-10-09 follow-ups — rope bars, Level 10 recess, relocation rule and fall net (`endstate`: Joan 9, failing L6 and L12; Lincoln 8, failing L6, L7 and L12); L6, L10 and L12 Joan made less progress in runs that failed both before and after, read as route quirks. **Amended 2026-10-12 (`USER-MOVEMENT-SPEED-CUT`):** the × 0.8 run-speed cut's audit re-checked the movement-sensitive geometry for the slowest hero (Lincoln: 228 px/s, single jump 201 px, double 353 px): the rope chains (the worst rope hop 97.8 px against 0.75 × the weakest single jump, 151 px), the Level 10 trapdoors and Level 0's double-jump gate still hold; the one lost jump edge was Level 11's railcut perch, widened 220 → 340 px (`PROVISIONAL-SPEED-CUT-RULES` (6)). Bot batches from before and after 2026-10-12 are not comparable. **Amended 2026-10-16:** (b) is now parity rather than a divergence — the sim's aerial Specials have a cast phase too, and it discards Down, so neither mode fast-falls during an aerial Special (`USER-SIM-SPECIAL-TIMING`). (c) stands: Story's `LedgeGrabPoint` still refills no jump and has no get-ups, while the sim gained ledge get-ups (`USER-FIGHTER-LEDGE-GETUPS`).
