New constants `BoardUndersideY`, `ArenaRecessWestX` / `ArenaRecessEastX` / `ArenaRecessFloorY` and `ArenaRecessWallHeight` feed the three room-4 floor calls, whose geometry and node names are unchanged. Siblings, unchanged: room 2's stage traps (3160, 3900, 4640) and stair-head drop (5660) open onto a closed cellar (floor x 2560–5760 at y 1500–1540, walls x 2540–2560 and 5740–5760 from y 1180 to 1540; the one crossing toward room 3 is the 20 px slit between room 3's floor underside, 1160, and the east wall top, 1180, under the stair-head one-way), and rooms 1 and 3 have unbroken floors. `Level10ContentTests.NothingBelowTheBoardsOpensSidewaysOntoTheVoid` floods the crouched hero box (40 × 44.8, so a crouch-slide gap counts as open) on a 2 px grid from under all seven openings (five traps, the stair head, the island) through every spot below 1160 it fits, solid statics blocking and one-ways and trapdoors not, fails if it reaches the level's bottom or sides, and pins both arena traps onto x 8970–9670 with the floor at 1260. `AHeroDrivenHardBothWaysInTheArenaRecessStaysInIt` puts Einstein at (9320, 1259) on the real scene and drives him east 100 frames and west 180, rolling every 30, in real physics frames (boss encounter processing and the audience watch off, no save slot): his centre stays in x 8990–9650, his feet never below 1260, and he reaches both walls. With the walls commented out the flood escaped from under both arena traps and the island ("a body reaches (8950, 1578.4), out of the level") and the drive ranged over x 8610.5–9846.0, while the room 2 starts stayed closed. Bots, Easy `play`, Joan and Lincoln, seeds 0–3 (`recessfix`, `summary_easy_recessfix_seed0..3.csv`): no run fell out of the world (3 of 8 in `ropefix`); Level 10 completions 2 → 7 of 8 (Joan 0 → 3, seeds 1–3; Lincoln 2 → 4). Joan entered the recess in all four seeds and her samples stop at the walls (x 8990 in seeds 2 and 3, 9650 in seed 1; seed 0 reaches 9638). Lincoln's traces never enter it, so his seed 3 win reads as run-to-run variation, and his seeds 0 and 2 both finished in 157.08 s, so the seeds are not independent. *The boss half, as found:* a wall stops only a body that physics moves. The Tragedy King's `exit_stage_left` Teleport (`resources/Bosses/Abilities/tragedy_king/exit_stage_left.tres`, 180–360 px) is resolved by `EnemyAbilityExecutor.Teleport`, which writes `GlobalPosition` to the far side of the target at the boss's own Y with no clearance or floor check, and the arena leash clamps x only (9000–10220, `BossController.ApplyArenaLeash`). A King that has dropped into the recess through a Phase 2 arena trap can therefore land in x 9690–10220 at recess height, under the east board beyond the new wall, and fall forever; the level cannot then be won. `L10_joan_play_easy_recessfix.json` (seed 0): the King in the recess at (9025, 1264) on frame 5461; at (9776, 1295), Attacking, on frame 6481 with Joan in the recess at (9507, 1260); at (9704, 2218) on frame 6541; at y ≈ 2.19e8 with 249 HP at the end, while the bot idles on the board above — the 780 s timeout. The `ropefix` seed 0 timeout is the same (the King at (9776, 1349) on frame 6481), so neither was a boss fight lost on time. Same class, not observed in the reruns: `EnemyAbilityExecutor.SummonMinions` places actors at the boss's x ± 96 / ± 144 at its own Y, so a King in the recess near either wall (the leash minimum, 9000, puts them at 8904 / 8856) spawns them beyond it; and Einstein's Story perk Quantum Entanglement blinks the hero 150 px backward on a guard break with no collision check (`BlockSystem`, `QuantumEntanglementDistance`), which crosses a 20 px wall. Relativity Warp shape-casts its destination and is not affected. The flood test models continuous movement only and cannot see a position write. **Boss half closed 2026-10-09** (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`; committed as `2e9ca79` on 2026-10-12, beside the walls). Every Story direct position write that is not a `MoveAndSlide` — the Teleport archetype, a SummonMinions spawn point and Einstein's Quantum Entanglement blink — now meets the world. `FTT.Combat.RelocationRules` holds the rule behind an `IRelocationWorld` and never queries the engine, so it is tested on plain rects; `FTT.Combat.SafeRelocation` answers its wall and floor questions with `EnvironmentProbe` and measures each body from its first enabled collision shape. The body travels from where it stands toward the destination on three rays across its own shape (centre and ±70 % of the half-height) and stops its half-width plus 2 px short of the first Environment wall; a body that falls then needs Environment or one-way floor within 480 px below the landing, or the landing steps back toward the start every 16 px to the first supported point, and with none short of the start the write is refused and the body stays put. Flyers skip the floor half and wall-phasers the wall half, so the Rift Phantom (a flying wall-phaser) blinks exactly as before. A world that cannot be asked (outside the tree, no physics space, inside a physics flush, a shapeless owner) leaves the requested point standing, as before. `EnvironmentProbe.FirstHitDistance` gained an optional `hitFromInside` (default false) for the floor probe, so existing callers are unchanged. **Call sites:** `EnemyAbilityExecutor.Teleport` for every Teleport ability (the Tragedy King's `exit_stage_left`, the Chrono-Warden's `phase_skip`, the Rift Phantom's `rift_step`, the Jackal Priest's `jackal_step`, `apex_eraser`'s `blink_erasure`, the siege cannon's `rail_warp`), ranges and telegraphs unchanged, a leashed boss's requested x clamped into its arena first (where the leash put it after the move anyway), and a refused teleport moving nothing and raising no `Teleported`, so no Jackal decoy is left on the spot; `EnemyAbilityExecutor.SummonMinions`, each minion spawned, measured by its own body and re-anchored through `ConfigureSpawn` before any physics step, at the summoner when refused; and `BlockSystem`'s Quantum Entanglement blink, which a guard break normally reaches inside a hit's physics flush, so it lands on a deferred call right after the flush (a direct call blinks at once; `QuantumEntanglementDistance` is now public). **Safety net** (`PROVISIONAL-ARENA-FALL-NET`): a leashed, living `BossController` whose feet end a physics frame below its arena's bottom edge (since the `VERIFY-L04-PIT-FALL-NET` fix, more than 240 px below it) is put back on its anchor after the leash clamp, and `MirrorParadoxController`'s own leash does the same for the clone. Seams: `LastTeleportLanding` (executor and `BossController`), `LastQuantumEntanglementLanding`, `ArenaFallRecoveries`. **Tests (+22, one `[TestSuite]` per file):** `RelocationRulesTests` (10, the rule on plain rects, including a replica of the recess at every `exit_stage_left` distance both ways; `[RequireGodotRuntime]` only because the GdUnit0501 analyzer refuses Godot's vector and rect types without it), `SafeRelocationTests` (6: a boss teleport into a wall and past a ledge, Phase Skip into a wall with the Rift Phantom still blinking through one, summons beside a wall, the blink in the open and against a wall, the blink inside a physics flush), `BossArenaFallNetTests` (4) and `Level10BossRelocationTests` (2, the real Globe scene: `exit_stage_left` from the recess toward the east and the west, four casts each, always lands inside x 9018–9622 on the 1260 floor, and the King then stands 45 physics frames without dropping; a King placed past the east wall under the boards is returned to (9900, 1070) and lands on the boards). With the old writes restored and the nets off, the guard-dependent cases failed for the intended reasons ("teleport landed at (120400,…), through the wall at x 120300", "Phase Skip landed at 132290, through the wall at 132150", "summon spawned at 140096, behind the wall at 140070", "The King fell to y 5735 and was never brought back", "exit_stage_left … put the King at (9797.862, 1260)", "Inside the flush the blink must wait"; the open-plus-wall blink case on a re-run, after a seam artefact). Only 11 of the 22 depend on the guards — the ten rule cases and the unleashed-boss case do not, and the Rift Phantom check sits inside the Chrono-Warden case — so the fix agent's "13 failed" overcounts. 33 related suites 389/389; the full suite once at 2803/2803. **Bots,** Easy `play`, Joan and Lincoln, seeds 0–3 (`tpfix`, `summary_easy_tpfix_seed0..3.csv`): Level 10 **8 of 8** `level_complete` (Joan 3 → 4 of 4 against `recessfix`, seed 0's timeout now a 169.9 s win with one death; Lincoln 4 → 4). The King entered the recess in all four Joan runs (1–8 one-second samples), never left it sideways and never went below y 1272, where `recessfix` seed 0 had 666 samples under the boards, falling to y 2.2e8. No `[arena-fall]` line appeared in any run, so the net never fired. Teleporter smoke runs (`play`): Levels 9, 13 (the Rift Phantom and the Mirror) and 14 (Archive Prime's drone summons through the new path) completed on Easy; Level 6 Easy collapsed at the winch (the known bot gap; the boss was never reached); the Chrono-Warden, which Easy's encounter count drops, was fought in two Normal runs — Level 9 collapsed on the timer, in line with its mixed Normal record, and Level 14, which has no earlier Normal baseline, ended in Game Over after 12 deaths (the last hit before each: `archive_prime` 5, `hologram_drone` 2, beams 2, `chrono_warden` 1, `unbound_eraser` 1, `chrono_slasher` 1; none after a teleport). No boss fell out of the world in any run. **Not covered:** a teleport or summon resolved inside a physics flush still lands unvalidated (none does today — every one has telegraph frames and fires from the physics step); T01b's historical recovery is not routed through the rule and still checks only x against the arena, so a historical position below the floor would send `apex_eraser` into a fall that only the net catches. | — | **Hero half met 2026-10-09** (the walls, the two `Level10ContentTests` cases and the `recessfix` reruns above). **Boss half met 2026-10-09**, against the criteria this row set: no direct position write leaves the recess — the Teleport archetype resolves a landing with floor under it and clearance for the body or falls back (step back, then refuse), summons spawn inside the walls, and Quantum Entanglement's blink checks its path — `Level10BossRelocationTests` teleports a King from inside the recess toward both walls on the real scene, and the `tpfix` reruns lost no boss below the stage. Closed on headless and bot evidence; nobody has played it. Carried elsewhere: the rule's numbers and limits (`PROVISIONAL-RELOCATION-CLEARANCE`), the net (`PROVISIONAL-ARENA-FALL-NET`) and its Level 4 misfire (`VERIFY-L04-PIT-FALL-NET`, closed the same day). |
| **PROVISIONAL-RELOCATION-CLEARANCE** — the details of the relocation rule (`VERIFY-L10-ARENA-RECESS-EXIT`'s boss half) | That row's acceptance asked only that a direct position write land with floor under it and clearance for the body, or fall back to such a point. No design source gives the numbers or the fallback. | *Provisional, agent-chosen* (`RelocationRules` constants): (1) **the numbers** — since the second review (2026-10-09) the wall half is a **sweep of the body's own box**: `RelocationRules.SweepBox`, the collision shape at its full width from its head down to the lowest wall ray's line, 30 % of the half-height above the shape's bottom (19.2 px on the 128 px boss body, 10.8 px on a standard enemy, 9.6 px on the hero), cast along the travel plus the clearance against Environment with the moving body excluded (`SafeRelocation`: two `CastMotion` passes, the second across the interval the first bracketed), the landing stopping 2 px short of the first contact (`AllowedTravelToContact`, `WallClearancePixels`); beside it the three wall rays at the centre and ±70 % of the half-height (`WallRaySpreadFraction`) still stop the body its half-width + 2 px short of a face (`AllowedTravel`), and the landing takes whichever stops first; floor support within 480 px below the feet (`FloorSearchDepthPixels`, eight units: deeper than any arena step-down — the recess is 140 px under the boards — and shallower than a fall out of an arena), the probe starting 24 px above the feet (`FloorProbeRisePixels`), a 16 px step-back (`StepBackPixels`), and no wall sweep or probe for a travel under 0.5 px (`MinimumTravelPixels`); (2) **the fallback order** — clip at the wall, then step back along the travel line toward the start, then refuse; the landing is never searched past the request or off that line, a refused Teleport raises no event, and a refused summon spawns at its emergence point (6), which is the summoner's feet whenever the minion has a floor there; (3) **an origin already over nothing** (`RelocationOutcome.OriginUnsupported`): the wall-clipped landing stands rather than being refused, since the body falls either way and `BossPhaseMechanicsTests.EachPhaseTwoTeleportLeavesASandDecoyWhoseStrikeBurstsOnThePlayer` teleports a boss in a world with no floor. Since 2026-10-09 (the review fix recorded in `VERIFY-L04-PIT-FALL-NET`) "over nothing" is read across the body's width: `RelocationRules.HasOriginSupport` probes at the centre and at both half-width extents, and any hit is support. A landing's support stays the single centre probe, because a body put down with only one extent on a floor would hang off its edge; (4) **the exemptions** read `EnemyData.Behavior == Flying` (no floor half and no emergence search) and `PhasesThroughWalls` (no wall half: neither the sweep nor the rays); bosses and Einstein are always grounded and solid; (5) **an unaskable world** keeps the old unvalidated write (`Resolve`, `ResolveSummon` and `EmergencePoint` all return before any query), and only the Quantum Entanglement blink defers itself off a physics flush; (6) **the summon's emergence point** (`RelocationRules.ResolveSummon` / `EmergencePoint`, `SafeRelocation.ResolveSummon`, `EnemyAbilityExecutor.PlaceSummon`, 2026-10-09) — a minion that flies or has a floor under the summoner's centre leaves from the summoner's feet, as before; otherwise it leaves from the nearest point under the summoner's body, every 16 px out to the summoner's half-width with the requested side first at equal distance, that has a floor under it and that the minion's own box reaches from the summoner's feet without meeting a wall (a short `Resolve` that lands as requested); with no such point (a summoner wholly over nothing) the feet stand. From there the ordinary rule runs on the minion's own measured shape: its box swept, its centre needing the floor where it lands. **Limits found by review.** *Fixed 2026-10-09:* (a) **the ledge origin** — the origin's support was one floor ray at the body's centre x, so a body standing on a ledge with its centre past the edge read as already over nothing (3), and a teleport, summon or blink toward the void side then landed over nothing — a boss caught only by the net (`PROVISIONAL-ARENA-FALL-NET`), an ordinary enemy or Einstein not at all. `RelocationRulesTests.ABodyOnALedgeWithItsCentrePastTheEdgeIsStillSupported` (an east and a west ledge, plus a body wholly past the edge, which still reads as unsupported) resolved to "OriginUnsupported at (1330, 1000)" on the centre-only probe and is held at the origin now. `HasOriginSupport` is reached only after the landing and every step-back point have failed, so the change can only turn `OriginUnsupported` into `HeldAtOrigin`: it refuses more writes into a void and allows none, and every pinned case in `RelocationRulesTests` and `SafeRelocationTests` (centre support, no floor anywhere, or no floor requirement) keeps its outcome. (b) **The thin slab** (fixed by the second review, the same day) — the three rays were 44.8 px apart on the 128 px boss body, so an Environment slab thinner than that lying between two of them was passed through; the box sweep (1) closes it. `RelocationRulesTests.AThinSlabBetweenTheOldRayLinesStopsTheBody` (slabs at two heights between the old ray lines; it also pins the sweep box and that a 10 px step and a floor seam are not walls) read "A board at y 905..920 let the body through: AsRequested at (1400, 1000)" with the sweep disabled, and `SafeRelocationTests.ABossTeleportStopsShortOfAThinBoardBetweenTheOldRayLines` (a 15 px board at chest height, real physics frames) read "The teleport landed at (122400, -50000) (AsRequested …), through the board whose face is at x 122150." on the untouched code. The rays stay beside the sweep rather than being replaced by it, against the fix's brief: a scratch Godot project outside the repository showed `cast_motion` ignoring any shape the swept box overlaps or touches where it starts (it returned `[1,1]` through such a wall), and the rays, which start inside the body, still catch that case. Because the landing is the shorter of the two, everything the three-ray rule blocked is still blocked. The floor band is load-bearing: the same scratch probe showed a box resting on its floor stopping at the seam with the next floor piece, and a run with the band removed failed the step pin and both summon cases. (c) **The narrow summon** (pre-existing, fixed the same day) — a summon's origin support was measured with the *minion's* half-width around the summoner's feet (`EnemyAbilityExecutor.PlaceSummon`), so a narrow minion summoned by a body standing on a ledge with its centre past the edge read as already over nothing and landed over the void; it now leaves from the supported part of its summoner (6). `RelocationRulesTests.ANarrowSummonFromALedgeLandsWhereItsOwnBodyHasAFloor` (east summons stay on the ledge, west summons keep their offsets, and the on-floor, flyer and wholly-off-ledge cases are unchanged) resolved to "…OriginUnsupported at (1126, 1000)" with the emergence point disabled, and `SafeRelocationTests.ANarrowSummonFromABossOnALedgeLandsOnTheFloor` (a boss with its centre 30 px past a ledge summoning four Holo Pages, real physics frames) read "A summon spawned at (142096, -50000), over the void past the ledge at x 141970." on the untouched code. No existing assertion changed for (b) or (c), and a skeptic review of both found no defect. *Not fixed:* an extent probe sits exactly on the body's side, so a body falling flush against a wall can read the wall as support — left alone deliberately, because it can only turn "already over nothing" into a refused write, the safe direction, and insetting the extent probes would let a body with 2 px or less on a ledge land over the void; a wall the swept box already overlaps where it starts is caught only by the three rays, as before; an obstacle inside the floor band (19.2 / 10.8 / 9.6 px on the boss / a standard enemy / the hero) is not a wall, deliberately, since the band is what lets floor seams and low steps pass; the two `CastMotion` passes place a contact to well under a pixel, but not to the 1/65536 of the motion that the comment in `SafeRelocation` claims — Godot's bisection sometimes takes uneven 0.75 / 0.25 steps instead of halving, so one cast brackets the contact only to about 1/171 of the motion (about 2.4 px on a 402 px sweep) and two to about 0.014 px, always on the safe side and well inside the tests' 0.5 px tolerance; `SafeRelocation.ResolveSummon` excludes nothing from the sweep by default (`minionBody` null, where `Resolve` defaults to the probing body), which has no effect today because no moved body is on Environment, and `PlaceSummon` passes the minion anyway; and no test pins the emergence search's requested-side-first order or its "the minion's box reaches the candidate without a wall" condition, since both summon cases have support on one side only. No bot run covers the second review's change, and nobody has played it. | Unassigned | A ruling on each, or a playtest that leaves them alone. The ledge case's criterion — the origin's support read across the body's width — was met on 2026-10-09, and the thin-slab and narrow-summon limits were closed the same day (above); what remains is the *Not fixed* list. `RelocationRulesTests` (13) and `SafeRelocationTests` (8) pin today's rule; the full suite ran green once at 2811 (2807 + those four cases), not yet three-run verified. |
| **PROVISIONAL-ARENA-FALL-NET** — a boss or Mirror clone that falls out of its arena (`VERIFY-L10-ARENA-RECESS-EXIT`'s boss half) | `VERIFY-STORY-PITS`: no campaign gap is lethal, and the 2026-10-04 feel pass's arenas (P1) bound a boss to its fight. The design has no rule for a boss that leaves the world by falling, which left a level unwinnable (the `ropefix` / `recessfix` Joan seed 0 timeouts). | *Provisional, agent-chosen, a backstop behind the relocation rule:* after the leash clamp each physics frame, a leashed, living `BossController` whose feet end the frame below its **net line** — its `ArenaBounds` bottom edge plus `BossController.ArenaFallNetMarginPixels` (240 px, four units), stated once as `BossController.ArenaFallNetLineY(arena)`, which the clone's net and the tests share, so the two nets cannot drift apart — is returned to its anchor (`BossController.RecoverFromArenaFall`): `HistoricalRecoveryAnchor` if it lies inside the arena (only Level 15's `apex_eraser` has one), else the spawn point, clamped into the arena if outside. Velocity is zeroed; HP, phase, rewards, the HUD, the T01b ring and its once-only latch are untouched. Each return prints `[arena-fall] boss '<id>' fell below its arena at (x,y); returned to (x,y).` and bumps `ArenaFallRecoveries` (reset on pool spawn and despawn). An unleashed boss is never moved. `MirrorParadoxController` keeps its own leash, so the clone has its own copy (`RecoverCloneFromArenaFall`: the spawn point, `[arena-fall] mirror clone …`). Nothing guards an ordinary enemy or a summon. **The margin** (2026-10-09, `VERIFY-L04-PIT-FALL-NET`): the net first fired at the bottom edge itself, which assumed every arena's bottom edge was the deepest floor under it — false on Level 4, whose walkable Phase 2 pit floor (`Level04Controller.TribunalPitFloorY`, 1120) lies 40 px under the 1080 edge. 240 px clears that, the deepest walkable floor under any arena edge, by 200 px and still catches a real fall soon: from rest at the edge a boss (980 px/s²) reaches the line in about 0.7 s, a body already falling sooner. The deepest floor against its net line in each special case: Level 4's pit 1120 / 1320, Level 10's recess 1260 / 1840, the Mirror arena's shelf 1900 and platforms 1825 / 2240, Level 12's floor and spire-lift deck 1900 / 2240; every other arena's bottom edge is its level's bottom (Level 8's, y 840–1800, included), and the geometry that changes mid-fight stays shallow (Level 1's barricades and scaffolding, Level 4's pit, built up front under its lid, Level 10's trap leaves at about 1120–1240). **The sweep:** `ArenaGeometryContentTests.NothingABossCanStandOnLiesBelowItsArenaFallNet` reads every upward-facing edge of every rect, polygon, circle, segment and moving-platform stop, enabled or not, over each of the fifteen arenas (fourteen boss arenas and the Mirror's) widened by 64 px either side, and fails on one on or below the net line; with the margin at 0 it flagged exactly Level 4's three pit edges (y 1120 against 1080) and nothing else. The net never fired in the `tpfix`, smoke or `netfix` runs (Levels 4 and 10, Joan and Lincoln). | Unassigned | A ruling on the net — keep it, keep it only as a logged diagnostic, or remove it once the relocation rule is trusted — and on its margin; the precondition this row set, `VERIFY-L04-PIT-FALL-NET` fixed, was met on 2026-10-09. `BossArenaFallNetTests` (5), `Level10BossRelocationTests.AKingWhoFallsOutUnderTheBoardsIsPutBackOnTheStage`, `Level04ContentTests.TheSquadSurvivorStandsInTheOpenPitWithoutTheFallNetReturningIt` and the `ArenaGeometryContentTests` sweep pin today's rule; a new floor on or below an arena's net line fails the sweep. |
| ~~**VERIFY-L04-PIT-FALL-NET**~~ — the arena fall net misfires in Level 4's Phase 2 pit (found 2026-10-09 by review of the boss-half fix; not seen in any run). **CLOSED 2026-10-09: the net keeps a 240 px margin under its arena's edge, and the relocation rule reads origin support across the body's width.** | Package 12 W9 (M19), `Level04Controller.BuildTribunalPit`: the Governor's Guard's Phase 2 pit is deliberately non-lethal and walkable — "the player, the surviving member and the summoned rioters can all walk in and out — it reshapes the fight, it does not end it". | *Original observation:* Level 4's arena rect (`Level04Controller.BossArenaBounds`) ends at `LevelHeight`, y 1080, but the Phase 2 pit's floor is at `PitFloorY` + `TribunalPitDepth` = 1000 + 120 = **1120**, and its ramps are below 1080 for x ≈ 9293–9567. The net (`PROVISIONAL-ARENA-FALL-NET`) therefore returns the surviving member to its spawn point whenever its feet pass y 1080 inside the pit. The squad spawns at (9400 ± 120, 950) (`BossEncounterController.SquadSpawnSpacing` 240), both points inside the arena and above the pit, so each is its member's anchor; `BossController` has no ledge stop, so a survivor chasing the player down a ramp is put back at (9280 or 9520, 950) with an `[arena-fall]` line each time, and member 1's anchor at x 9520 lies over the pit floor, so that survivor falls, is returned and falls again, in a loop. Reasoned from the code; no test or bot run covers it (`Level04ContentTests` checks only that the lid is disabled, and no Level 4 run was made). Introduced with the net, uncommitted on `d2e52e0` (committed as `2e9ca79` on 2026-10-12). *Correction, from the fix's physics runs:* the loop does not happen. A body dropped at member 1's spawn (x 9520, where the pit floor meets the east ramp) comes to rest with its corner on the ramp at y ≈ 1079, just above the old 1080 line, and was never returned; the new Level 4 test's drop-from-spawn half passed on the old net. What the old net did do was return a body standing on the flat pit floor at (9430, 1120) to (9520, 950) — the loop a survivor would ride each time it chased into the pit. The new test's doc comment (`Level04ContentTests`) still repeats the refuted loop claim. **Fixed 2026-10-09** (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`; committed as `2e9ca79` on 2026-10-12, beside the relocation rule): the net fires only once a body's feet pass `BossController.ArenaFallNetLineY(arena)`, the arena's bottom edge plus `ArenaFallNetMarginPixels` (240 px, four units), shared by the boss net and the Mirror clone's (`PROVISIONAL-ARENA-FALL-NET` carries the margin's reasoning and each special arena's depths), and a public `Level04Controller.TribunalPitFloorY` (1000 + 120 = 1120) now feeds the pit polygon. The same review pass fixed the relocation rule's ledge limit: `RelocationRules.HasOriginSupport` reads the origin's floor at the centre and both half-width extents (`PROVISIONAL-RELOCATION-CLEARANCE` (3)). The existing tests that put a body "just below the arena" now place it relative to the net line (`BossArenaFallNetTests`, `Level10BossRelocationTests`), with no assertion removed or loosened. **Tests (+4, one new case in each of four existing suites, each failing on the old code):** `ArenaGeometryContentTests.NothingABossCanStandOnLiesBelowItsArenaFallNet` (the fifteen-arena sweep; at margin 0 it listed exactly Level 4's three pit edges, y 1120 against a line at 1080), `BossArenaFallNetTests.FeetAPitFloorBelowTheArenaEdgeAreNotAFall` (feet at the pit's depth and 0.5 px above the line stand, 0.5 px below it are caught; at margin 0, "'40' but is '0'" on the margin check), `RelocationRulesTests.ABodyOnALedgeWithItsCentrePastTheEdgeIsStillSupported` ("OriginUnsupported at (1330, 1000)" on the centre-only probe) and `Level04ContentTests.TheSquadSurvivorStandsInTheOpenPitWithoutTheFallNetReturningIt` (the real scene in real physics frames: member 0 killed, which opens the pit, the boss-intro dialogue and name card played out, the survivor dropped from its spawn for 2 s and then stood on the pit floor for 3 s; on the old net the pit-floor half logged `[arena-fall] boss 'revolutionary_tribunal' fell below its arena at (9430,1120); returned to (9520,950).`). The first margin-0 batch (4 failed, 43 passed) failed that last case for the wrong reason — the boss-intro dialogue had paused the tree, so the survivor never moved, a harness bug since fixed; the right failure came from a later single-test run still at margin 0. The ten related suites 143/143; the full suite once at 2807/2807. **Bots,** Easy `play`, seed 0 (`netfix`, `summary_easy_netfix.csv`): Level 4 Joan and Lincoln and Level 10 Joan and Lincoln all `level_complete` (162.7 / 171.3 / 184.4 / 241.0 s; 2 / 1 / 2 / 0 deaths), and `[arena-fall]` appears in none of the eight run logs (stdout, which does carry `GD.Print`, and stderr) nor in `godot.log`. In the Level 4 Lincoln run the player spent 47 one-second trace samples inside the opened pit (y > 1080), 33 of them with the survivor alive (the boss fell on frame 9061); the traces record only the nearest enemy, so they cannot show whether the survivor itself followed. | — | **Met 2026-10-09**, against the criteria this row set: the net no longer fires for a body standing on authored floor — it triggers a fixed 240 px below the arena's edge, below the deepest floor under any arena, which the sweep pins — the real-scene test stands the survivor in the open pit with `ArenaFallRecoveries` still 0, and the two Level 4 bot runs printed no `[arena-fall]` line. Two halves are met in substance rather than to the letter: member 1's anchor (9520, 950) still lies over the pit's east ramp, but a body dropped from it rests on the ramp and is never returned; and the test drops the survivor in and stands it on the floor rather than walking it down a ramp and out again, while no bot trace shows the survivor following the player in. Closed on headless and bot evidence; nobody has played it. |

## Confirmed deviations — the F10 level-start resource reset, 2026-10-11

Found by a code trace of `STORY_PERSISTENCE.md`'s boundary table and fixed on `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-11 (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`; committed as `2e9ca79` on 2026-10-12). The shipped rule is in `StoryManager` (`ResetDurableResourcesForFreshAttempt`, called from `MintFreshAttempt`; `IsFreshAttemptHeroPending` / `TryConsumeFreshAttemptHero`; `TryGetAttemptHero` with `IsSceneTransitionInProgress`, backed by the new `GameManager.IsLoadingScene`) and in the shared `StoryLevelControllerBase.RestoreDurableResources`, which Florence also calls. No save field, schema version, component or protocol changed. Suite: `LevelStartResourceResetTests` (8, then 10 after the same day's fix-up). The fix-up (a review of the first pass) added the orderly-exit chokepoint `StoryManager.CommitOrderlyExit` / `ExitToMainMenu` and restores `CollapsedLevel` when a Smothered save is loaded; see the two rows after `PROVISIONAL-F10-FRESH-HERO-PENDING`.

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~**FIX-F10-FRESH-ENTRY-RESOURCES**~~ — Story HP and Ultimate meter carried into the next level and through a Restart | `STORY_PERSISTENCE.md` (F10): "Fresh level entry: … 100% HP, full difficulty rewind pool, full block charges, zero Ultimate meter, abilities ready"; "Full Restart Level: same initialization". An ordinary save, quit/resume or crash recovery retains the latest durable HP and meter; a death rewind, an Acts I–II Collapse resume and an Act III Anchor Snap keep the meter (the Collapse and the Snap at full HP). | **Closed 2026-10-11.** Three defects, all on `d2e52e0`. (1) The completion transaction never touched the save's root `CurrentHP` / `CurrentUltimateMeter` (`SaveManager.SaveLevelCompletion` only stamps the next level path and clears the attempt), and the next level's fresh entry read both back: `StoryLevelControllerBase.RestoreSavedCheckpoint` passed the level check, `RestoreDurableResources` returned `save.CurrentUltimateMeter` on every path despite its "a fresh entry restores nothing" comment, and `PlayerController.RestoreStoryCheckpoint` applied the HP and the meter. Florence (Level 1) used the same restore. (2) The pause menu's Restart Level, the hub's restart-from-beginning (`RestartCollapsedLevel(false)`) and `RestartFromGameOver` reset `save.CurrentHP` but never the meter. (3) `BeginLevelRun` arms the run before the scene changes, and the outgoing scene stays in the tree under the ≥ 2 s loading screen, so the per-second durable snapshot captured **its** hero into the new attempt: the previous Act III level's wounded, charged hero over the next level's fresh entry; the hub's full-HP, zero-meter hero over a resumed attempt (refilling HP and block, zeroing the meter and cooldowns of a quit/resume or a Collapse resume); and the dead hero over an Anchor Snap's full-HP recovery (a death-caused Snap would land at 1 HP). This third defect is reasoned from the code (the loading screen cannot run headless); the tests reproduce it by declaring the loading window through `StoryManager.SceneTransitionInProgressOverrideForTesting`. Now a minted attempt (fresh entry, the Act III next level, every full Restart, which all mint through `BeginLevelRun`) resets the root HP to the maximum and the meter to zero, and its level controller builds the hero on the factory kit (100% HP, full block charges, cooldowns ready, meter 0), rewrites the root from that hero and discards the attempt's resource record; a resume clears the pending flag and restores exactly as before. The three restart writers also zero the meter. The durable snapshot and the attempt's resource capture skip the Story hero while `GameManager.IsLoadingScene` is true. An old save parked at the hub with a carried meter is reset when its next level is entered (derived at mint, no migration). The Tutorial never reads the save, so its hero was always fresh. | level-start-reset (2026-10-11) | Closed on headless evidence: `LevelStartResourceResetTests` (8) — a wounded, charged completion then the next level, Florence after the Tutorial, the Act III next level with the previous hero still under the loading screen, Restart Level from the real pause menu, Restart from the Smothered Game Over and an old hub-parked save each start at full HP, zero meter, full block charges and ready cooldowns; a quit and resume through the hub with the hub's hero under the loading screen keeps the durable HP and meter; a death rewind keeps the meter. Seven of the eight fail with the fix disabled; the death-rewind case pins kept behaviour and passes either way, and the quit-and-resume case fails on its loading-screen half. Nobody has played it. |
| **PROVISIONAL-F10-FRESH-HERO-PENDING** — how the fix tells a fresh hero from a resumed one | F10 names the boundaries but not the mechanism; the step ruled out a save-schema bump. | *Agent-chosen:* (a) "fresh" means "the hero of an attempt minted in this session that no level controller has built yet" — an in-memory attempt-ID latch, never persisted. A crash between the mint and the level build loses nothing: the root was already reset at mint, and the next entry mints again (an empty `LastCheckpointID` is never a resume). A level that resumes mid-level consumes the latch without effect, so a stale latch cannot refill a parked hero. (b) At mint the root HP is `GetSelectedCharacterMaximumHP()`, which in the hub reads the hub's hero, built before any MaxHP grid node bought at the terminal; the level controller rewrites it from the freshly built hero, so the stale value lives only until the hero exists. (c) The capture guard covers every `GameManager.LoadScene`, not only level entries. That is only safe because no load leaves the attempt's own hero in the tree with the run still live: a level entry or Restart mints or resumes into the next scene, and every exit stops the run before it loads (Quit to Main Menu through `CommitOrderlyExit` since the fix-up, the hub through `ReturnToHub`, Game Over through `EnterSmothered`, completion through `CompleteLevelRun`). The first pass claimed "between scenes there is no hero of the attempt to read", which was false for Quit to Main Menu until the fix-up; a future exit that loads a scene without stopping the run would silently lose its quit-time HP and meter. (d) Not changed: the Tutorial still rebuilds a fresh hero on any load (it has no checkpoint resume), and `StoryAttemptState.RetireLegacyLevelV8` still leaves the root HP and meter to the entry mint. | Unassigned | Accept, or a ruling that the fresh-hero marker should be durable (a field for the next package's single schema bump) or that the Tutorial should resume with durable resources. |
| ~~**FIX-SMOTHERED-RELOAD-RESTART**~~ — Restart Level from a Game Over reached by loading a Smothered save restarted the wrong level | `STORY_PERSISTENCE.md` (F10): Smothered persists before its presentation, every later load returns to the same Game Over, and "only an explicit Restart Level" leaves it, restarting that attempt's level as a fresh attempt. | **Closed 2026-10-11** (level-start-reset fix-up; older than that step, on `d2e52e0`). `RestartFromGameOver` sets `CurrentLevel = CollapsedLevel`, and `CollapsedLevel` was set only in memory, by `BeginTimelineCollapse`, `BeginAnchorSnap` and `EnterSmothered`. Loading a Smothered save (`ResumeCampaign` → `RouteResumeByAttemptStatus` → Game Over) never restored it, so on a fresh boot it read `CampaignLevel.Tutorial` (and within one session it could be another slot's collapsed level): Restart Level re-stamped `save.CurrentLevelID` to the Tutorial and loaded it instead of the Act III level. Now the Smothered branch of `RouteResumeByAttemptStatus` sets `CollapsedLevel` to the parked level and `CollapsedCheckpointID` to the save's `LastCheckpointID`, exactly as `EnterSmothered` does. The Acts I–II hub restart (`RestartCollapsedLevel`) is not affected: its `HasPendingTimelineRestart` gate is in-memory too, so after a reload the portal resumes the parked attempt instead. | level-start-reset fix-up (2026-10-11) | Closed on headless evidence: `LevelStartResourceResetTests.RestartFromASmotheredGameOverLoadedFromTheSaveRestartsTheParkedLevel` (a fresh-boot Tutorial state, a JSON round-tripped Smothered Level 13 save, `ResumeCampaign`, `RestartFromGameOver`: Level 13 is requested, the save keeps its level, the meter is 0 and the rebuilt hero is fresh). It fails with the restoration removed. Nobody has played it. |
| ~~**FIX-F10-ORDERLY-EXIT-FLUSH**~~ — Quit to Main Menu neither flushed the latest HP and meter nor kept the 20% exit fee | `STORY_PERSISTENCE.md` (F10): "An orderly Save/exit flushes the latest state." The voluntary pause-menu exit forfeits 20% of the undeposited wallet (V7.2, unchanged by V7.6). | **Closed 2026-10-11** (level-start-reset fix-up). `PauseMenu.QuitToMainMenu` never stopped the level run (`StopLevelRun` had two callers, `ReturnToHub` and `EnterSmothered`), so the run stayed armed through the ≥ 2 s loading screen and on into the main menu. (1) Older than the first pass, on `d2e52e0`: the per-second `CaptureDurableSnapshot` kept writing the unpenalized in-memory wallet (`ChronalDustCollected`) over the fee `ApplyExitDustPenalty` had just written to the save, and queued a write every second, silently refunding the fee. (2) Introduced by the first pass: the snapshot's new loading-window guard turned the one HP/meter capture an exit got (from under the loading screen) into a no-op, so the save kept the values from up to a second before the pause. Now `ApplyExitDustPenalty` calls `StoryManager.CommitOrderlyExit(retainedWallet)`: it sets the post-fee wallet in memory and on the save; inside a live run it captures the hero's HP, meter and resource timers and the rewind pool and attempt record; it commits synchronously (`SaveStorySlot`) and stops the run. The scene change goes through `StoryManager.ExitToMainMenu` (the `RequestScene` seam). Outside a live run (the Story pause menu also serves the hub) only the wallet is written, as before. | level-start-reset fix-up (2026-10-11) | Closed on headless evidence: `LevelStartResourceResetTests.QuitToTheMainMenuFlushesTheLatestHPAndMeterAndKeepsTheExitFee` (the real pause menu's Quit button and confirmation: the run stops, the save holds the quit-time HP and meter and the post-fee wallet, three ticks of the autoload with the loading window declared change none of them, and a resume through the hub restores the quit-time HP and meter). It fails with the old exit restored. Not changed and not judged: a hub quit still charges the 20% to any held attempt wallet (see the step report). Nobody has played it. |
| **VERIFY-HUB-QUIT-EXIT-FEE** — a quit to the main menu from the hub charges the exit fee again on an attempt's held wallet | `STORY_PERSISTENCE.md` / `DUST_ECONOMY.md` (F05): "Collapse and voluntary exit apply the existing 20% undeposited-dust rule"; the V7.2 fee is described as the price of leaving an **incomplete level**. No adopted contract says whether a quit from the hub, where no level is running, is such an exit. | Found by the level-start-reset fix-up and recorded by the finalize step (2026-10-11); not changed, and older than both. The Story pause menu is also attached in the hub, and `PauseMenu.ApplyExitDustPenalty` charges 20 % on `StoryManager.ChronalDustCollected` wherever it runs (H02's comment: the whole wallet is the open attempt's undeposited dust). After an Acts I–II Collapse, which already charged 20 % and parked the rest as held attempt dust (`StoryManager.AttemptHeldDust`), every quit to the main menu from the hub charges another 20 % of what is left, without the level being re-entered. `StoryManager.CommitOrderlyExit` writes only the wallet there (no live run). | Unassigned | A ruling: either a hub quit is free (charge the fee only while a level run is live, `IsLevelTimerRunning` or an equivalent), or each held wallet pays the fee once per exit as now; then a pin in `LevelStartResourceResetTests` or `CompletionOnlyBankingTests`. |

## Confirmed deviations — the Influence meter rulebook and Mozart's build rate, 2026-10-11

Found by the 2026-10-11 code research and fixed on `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-11 (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`; committed as `2e9ca79` on 2026-10-12). **The rulebook half is hygiene, not a deviation:** the meter's cap (100), damage-dealt rate (1/1), damage-taken rate (1/4) and Fighter stock-loss retention (3/4) were re-typed in three code copies (the Story `UltimateMeter` node, literals across `FighterEntitySystems`, `FighterSimulationSystems`, `FighterSimulation`, `FighterCpuController` and `FighterSimulationDriver`) plus bare `100`s (and one `0.75f`) in the story save payload's clamp, the orb pickup, three `PlayerController` fallbacks (the Ultimate press, the checkpoint restore clamp, the stock-loss retention), three HUD models, the Story HUD and a playtest bot. They now live once, as integer rationals, in the Godot-free `FTT.Combat.InfluenceMeterRules`; Story reads its exact float views and gain functions, and the sim reads it only through `FTT.FighterSim.FighterMeterRules`, which repeats the old fixed-point operations exactly (`+ FromInt(damage)`, `/ FromInt(4)`, `* FromInt(3) / FromInt(4)`). No value changed: a throwaway probe of 90 seeded Hard CPU-vs-CPU matches (all ten stages, every roster pairing, 3600 ticks, items, meter pickups and hazards on) produced identical final hashes, per-tick hash folds, per-tick meter sums and end meters, stocks and HP on the old and the new sim code. Suites: `InfluenceMeterRulesTests` (3, pure C#), `InfluenceMeterSourceScanTests` (2), `StoryMeterGainFactorTests` (6), `FighterMeterGainTests` (2).

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~**FIX-MOZART-BUILD-RATE-UNREAD**~~ — Mozart's Minor Resonance did nothing | `design-godot.md` §5, Mozart's grid: "Minor Resonance \| 1 \| Ultimate Meter builds +10% faster \| `UltimateBuildRate`" (`resonance_mozart_resonance_description`: "The Influence meter builds 10% faster."). Story-only, like every grid lane. | **Closed 2026-10-11.** On `d2e52e0`, `ResonanceProgression` resolved the node (`mozart_resonance`, 50 dust, `StatModifierValue` 0.1) into `StoryStatProfile.UltimateBuildRateMultiplier` and `CharacterFactory` copied it to `PlayerController.StoryUltimateBuildRateMultiplier`, which nothing read, so the bought node changed nothing — and every Mozart node above it in the chain requires it. Now it is read once, by `PlayerController.StoryMeterGainFactor`, and the `UltimateMeter` node's two gain paths apply that factor through `InfluenceMeterRules.GainFromDamageDealt` / `GainFromDamageTaken`. A Minor Resonance hero earns 22 meter for 20 HP dealt and 5.5 for 20 HP taken (the Rally echo share included as it drains). The sim never reads it. **Since 2026-10-11 the lane is multiplied by D5's Story scale on the campaign hero** (`USER-STORY-METER-SCALE`, `PROVISIONAL-STORY-METER-SCALE-RULES` item 3): a campaign hero with the node earns 7.7 for 20 HP dealt; a non-campaign build (only the Hard Mirror clone can carry the lane, F20) still earns 22. | meter-rulebook step (2026-10-11) | Closed on headless evidence: `StoryMeterGainFactorTests` (dealt and taken/echo gain at 1.1 against a 1.0 control, a real save with the node bought, the Hard and Normal Mirror clones) and `InfluenceMeterSourceScanTests.TheSimReadsOnlyTheFixedPointViewAndTheBuildRateLaneIsReadOnce`. With the lane unread, the four gain cases and the lane pin fail; with the old damage-dealt grant under a 0.5 factor, the Level 0 case fails. Nobody has played a Mozart campaign with it. |
| **PROVISIONAL-STORY-METER-GAIN-SCOPE** — what "builds 10% faster" covers | The design says only "builds +10% faster". | *Provisional, agent-chosen, the most conservative reading:* (1) the factor scales **gain from damage**: damage dealt, damage taken at hit time, and the Rally echo's deferred meter as it drains. `PlayerController.RallyUncreditedMeter` (the outstanding echo's meter, saved for the load-time settlement) is recorded at the same factor, so the settlement adds it back unscaled and nothing is scaled twice. (2) It does **not** scale flat grants (the Holodeck's Chronal Orb meter boost, `UltimateMeter.AddFlat`, Joan's Shield of Orléans meter per block), direct sets (checkpoint and load restores, `PlayerController.SetUltimateMeter`), spends and drains (the Ultimate, Echo Step, the dampening beams, the Extractor discharge) or the stock-loss retention. (3) Ultimate-origin damage still earns the caster nothing (D03h is checked before the factor). (4) The Level 0 Meter & Defy lesson now fills the bar with a direct set to 100 instead of a damage-dealt gain of 100, so no gain factor can short-fill it; at a factor of 1 or more the two are identical. (5) The Level 13 Mirror clone mirrors the lane on Hard only, as F20 mirrors every purchased grid perk there (`MirrorParadoxController.MirrorsPurchasedPerks`); on Easy and Normal it gains at 1.0. (6) Fighter Mode, the Holodeck and the Calibration Drills build heroes with `applyStoryProgression: false` and never carry it. **Amended 2026-10-16:** item (2) — the Echo Step spend is scaled for the campaign hero by the D5 meter scale, not by this build-rate lane, so a Mozart campaign hero also pays 10.5 (decision 7.3, `USER-STORY-ECHO-STEP-COST`). | Unassigned | A ruling to keep or change the scope, in particular whether flat grants and the drained echo count as "building"; then state the scope in the design's Mozart grid row. **Amended 2026-10-25:** the Echo Step spend named in item (2) and in the 2026-10-16 amendment no longer exists (`USER-REMOVE-ECHO-STEP`); the rest of the scope stands. |

## Confirmed deviations — the Resonance grid repairs, 2026-10-11

Found by the 2026-10-11 code research and fixed on `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-11 (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`; committed as `2e9ca79` on 2026-10-12). Three bought nodes did nothing or the opposite of their description, and the V7.5 dormant-node gate had never been installed. No node ID, price, prerequisite, tier or the 975 total changed, so no save derivation is needed; no save field, schema version, component or protocol changed, and nothing under `scripts/FighterSim/` was touched. The Hard Mirror clone, which mirrors purchased grid nodes, inherits the fixes. Guards: the new `ResonanceReaderCoverageTests` (5) fails when any `StatModifierKey`, `AbilityScope` or perk key authored in the nine grids has no production reader, when a cooldown minor does not resolve below 1.0 on a slot with a cooldown, or when `scripts/FighterSim/` names a Resonance or Story-perk type. Other suites: `ResonanceProgressionTests` (+3, one rewritten in place), `ConductiveMarkTests` (+1, one rewritten in place), `StoryLorentzChainTests` (+1), and in-place additions to `ResonanceGridSceneTests` and `ResonanceRespecTests`. With the four defects re-created on top of the fixed code, 11 of the cases fail; all pass on the fix.

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~**FIX-GRID-SCOPED-COOLDOWN-INVERTED**~~ — three cooldown minors made their Special's cooldown longer | `design-godot.md` §5: Minor Piercing Cooldown "Divine Piercing cooldown −10%" (`:2555`), Minor Wave Cooldown "Fortissimo Wave cooldown −10%" (`:2796`), Minor Splitting Cooldown "Splitting Strike cooldown −10%" (`:2915`), each `CooldownReduction` scoped to the one ability. | **Closed 2026-10-11.** On `d2e52e0`, `ResonanceProgression.Resolve` stored every scoped lane as 1 + sum, so a scoped `CooldownReduction` of 0.1 resolved to 1.1, and `BaseSpecial.StartCooldown` multiplies the authored cooldown by it: each node made its Special's cooldown 10% **longer**. Lincoln's cheapest route to Kinetic Splitting runs through his. Now a scoped `CooldownReduction` is summed per scope and stored as the cooldown multiplier max(0.05, 1 − sum), exactly like the character-wide lane (the 0.05 floor is one constant, `ResonanceProgression.MinimumCooldownMultiplier`, also read by `PlayerController`'s tick-down). The additive scoped lanes (`AbilityDamage`, `AbilityRange`, `AbilityDuration`, `ConstructHP`) still resolve to 1 + sum; `BaseSpecial` is their only cooldown reader and nothing else reads the scoped cooldown. | grid-repairs step (2026-10-11) | Closed on headless evidence: `ResonanceProgressionTests.AScopedCooldownReductionResolvesToAMultiplierBelowOne` (the three authored nodes resolve to 0.9 on their own scope, stacking and the 5% floor, no leak into the character-wide lane, additive lanes unchanged), `AScopedCooldownMinorShortensItsOwnCastAndNoOther` (each hero's Special 2 arms 90% of its authored cooldown, Special 1 the full one) and `ResonanceReaderCoverageTests.EveryCooldownMinorResolvesBelowOneOnACooldownBearingSlot`. All three fail with the old 1 + sum. Nobody has played it. |
| ~~**FIX-TESLA-CONDUCTIVE-HOLD-UNREAD**~~ — Minor Conductive Hold did nothing | `design-godot.md` §5, Tesla (`:2670`, `:2675`; F07): "Finisher's non-disabling Conductive mark lasts 2.5 s instead of 1.5 s; Static Charge interrupt remains 0.4 s \| `AbilityDuration` (conductive_finisher_mark); gated on Special 2". `resonance_tesla_conductive_hold_description` promises the same. | **Closed 2026-10-11.** On `d2e52e0` the node (`tesla_conductive_hold`, 50 dust; Long Blink and Wardenclyffe Shield sit behind it) carried two effects and neither worked: a perk key `conductive_hold` that the application site never matched (`PlayerController.FinisherConductiveMarkFrames` checked `HasStoryPerk("tesla_conductive_hold")`), and the scoped lane `AbilityDuration` / `conductive_finisher_mark` (+0.666667) that nothing read. Now `FinisherConductiveMarkFrames` reads the lane once and rounds: 90 frames × 1.666667 = **150**. The dead perk key is removed from `tesla_grid.tres` and `PlayerController.ConductiveHoldPerkKey` is deleted, so the mark cannot be extended twice (the perk's 150 under the lane's ×1.667 would give 250). `BasicComboRules.ConductiveMarkUpgradedFrames` (150) stays as the design's stated target and the tests' oracle; gameplay no longer reads it. Fence marks (90) and Fighter Mode (90) are unchanged; the Static Charge interrupt is unchanged. | grid-repairs step (2026-10-11) | Closed on headless evidence: `ConductiveMarkTests.ABoughtConductiveHoldNodeExtendsTheCampaignFinisherMarkExactlyOnce` (a campaign save with the node bought, built by the factory's Story path through the real grid: exactly 150; the same save through the Fighter seam and the unbought node: 90), the rewritten `TheStoryNodeExtendsTheFinisherMarkToOneHundredFiftyFramesButNeverTheFence` (now through the resolved grid lane rather than an injected perk) and `ResonanceReaderCoverageTests` (the scope and the perk key each need a reader). All fail on the old code. Nobody has played it. |
| ~~**FIX-TESLA-PULSE-RADIUS-UNREAD**~~ — Minor Pulse Radius did nothing | `design-godot.md` §5, Tesla (`:2672`): "Lorentz Pulse radius +15% \| `AbilityRange` (pulse); gated on Special 2". | **Closed 2026-10-11.** On `d2e52e0` the Story Lorentz Pulse queried a fixed 2.5-unit (150 px) circle (`TeslaLorentzPulse.PulseRadiusPixels`), and the node's `AbilityRange` / `tesla_lorentz_pulse` lane (+0.15, Lorentz Attraction behind it) had no reader. Now `TeslaLorentzPulse.PulseRadius` is the 150 px base × `StoryScoped(AbilityRange, tesla_lorentz_pulse)`: 172.5 px with the node. Only the pulse's hit circle grows; the 8-unit chain coil range, Lorentz Attraction's pull and the sim's fixed-point pulse (`KitMotionRules.LorentzPulseRadiusUnits`) are unchanged. | grid-repairs step (2026-10-11) | Closed on headless evidence: `StoryLorentzChainTests.TheBoughtPulseRadiusNodeWidensThePulseByFifteenPercent` (an enemy whose near hurtbox edge is 161 px away is missed unbought and hit through the real grid's lane; the radius reads 150 / 172.5 px) and `ResonanceReaderCoverageTests.EveryAuthoredAbilityScopeHasAProductionReader`. Both fail on the old fixed radius. Nobody has played it. |
| ~~**FIX-GRID-DORMANT-GATE-UNINSTALLED**~~ — ability-gated nodes could be bought before their ability returned | `design-godot.md` §3 (`:308`, `:450`, `:468`): grid nodes that modify a Legacy-locked ability "stay unpurchasable and render as unlabeled dormant silhouettes until that ability unlocks". | **Closed 2026-10-11.** On `d2e52e0`, `ResonanceProgression.AbilityUnlockProbe` carried a `TODO(A5)` and only tests ever set it, so `EvaluateUnlock` treated every gated node as ungated: the grid sold, named and priced nodes for abilities the hero did not have yet, and never drew a dormant star. Now, with no test override installed, `ResonanceProgression.IsAbilityUnlocked(save, characterID, slot)` reads the evaluated save's Legacy Unlock Schedule state through the new engine-free `LegacyUnlockSchedule.UnlockedSlotsFor` / `IsUnlockedFor` (the persisted `UnlockedLegacyAbilities` keys, backstopped by `CompletedLevels`). `CharacterFactory.ResolveStoryUnlockedSlots` now calls the same helper, so the hero's ability gate and the grid's dormant stars cannot disagree. A locked node returns `AbilityLocked` (ahead of prerequisites and dust, as before) and the panel draws it as the unnamed dormant star. | grid-repairs step (2026-10-11) | Closed on headless evidence: `ResonanceProgressionTests.ARealGatedNodeStaysDormantUntilTheSavesScheduleRestoresItsSlot` (Joan's Piercing Cooldown refused after Level 2 with dust and prerequisites in hand, sold after Level 3), the rewritten `AGatedNodeRefusesPurchaseUntilItsLegacyAbilityUnlocks` and `ResonanceGridSceneTests` (a fresh Einstein save shows Rift Range as the dormant star and Mass by name). All fail with the gate transparent. Nobody has looked at the dormant stars in the editor. |
| **PROVISIONAL-GRID-DORMANT-GATE-SOURCE** — how the dormant gate reads the schedule | The design says only that a gated node stays dormant "until that ability unlocks". The A4 handoff named a global probe "installed at campaign load". | *Provisional, agent-chosen:* (a) the gate reads **the save being evaluated** (the one the grid panel shows, which is the active Story save at the hub Repository and at an Act III Warden Beacon), not a process-global "active save" probe, so no install/uninstall is needed per mode and Fighter Mode, the Holodeck, the drills and the Test Arena never reach it; `AbilityUnlockProbe` survives as a test override only. (b) **Not retroactive:** a gated node bought before this fix (possible on any existing save) stays bought, keeps resolving through `Resolve`, and refunds normally on respec; its ability is locked anyway until restored, so no migration or forced refund was added. (c) An unknown `GatedAbilityID` reads as ungated at runtime (`ValidateGrid` and `ResonanceProgressionTests` already reject one in authored data). (d) ~~Out of this step's scope and still open, with no row of its own yet: design `:531` applies a purchase's stat modifiers immediately, but the build applies a purchase or respec to the live hero only on the next scene load (`SaveManager` is the only `OnTalentNodeUnlocked` subscriber).~~ **Closed 2026-10-11** by ~~FIX-GRID-LIVE-APPLY~~: a purchase or respec now reaches the live campaign hero at once, under the provisional rules in `PROVISIONAL-GRID-LIVE-APPLY`. | Unassigned | Accept (a)–(c), or a ruling that already-bought dormant nodes should be refunded or suspended. |
| **VERIFY-GRID-DORMANT-NAME-IN-PREREQ-TEXT** — a visible node's tooltip names a dormant prerequisite | `design-godot.md` §3 (`:308`, `:450`, `:468`): a node gated on a Legacy-locked ability renders as an **unlabeled** dormant silhouette until that ability unlocks. | Found by the grid-repairs review and recorded by the finalize step (2026-10-11); not changed. Turning the gate on (`FIX-GRID-DORMANT-GATE-UNINSTALLED`) made the leak reachable: `ResonanceGridPanel.PrerequisiteNames` lists every unmet prerequisite by its display name, dormant or not, so the `MissingPrerequisite` tooltip of an ungated node names its gated prerequisite while that node's own chip reads `resonance_dormant_node`. Four authored nodes do this: `cleopatra_asps_bite` (← `cleopatra_venom_damage`), `einstein_quantum_entanglement` (← `einstein_extended_float`), `joan_shield_of_orleans` (← `joan_piercing_cooldown`) and `shakespeare_henrys_bastion` (← `shakespeare_lament_slow`, `shakespeare_tempest_apex`). | Unassigned | A dormant prerequisite reads as the existing `resonance_dormant_node` label (or is omitted) in the prerequisite text while its node is `AbilityLocked`, with a pin in `ResonanceGridSceneTests`; or a ruling that naming it there is acceptable. |

## Confirmed deviations — live application of grid purchases and respecs, 2026-10-11

Found by the 2026-10-11 code research and fixed on `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-11 (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`; committed as `2e9ca79` on 2026-10-12). No node, price, save field, schema version, component or protocol changed, and nothing under `scripts/FighterSim/` was touched. The shipped rule: `ResonanceProgression.TryUnlock` and `RespecAll` raise the new `EventBus.OnResonanceProgressionChanged(characterID)`; the campaign hero (`PlayerController.ReceivesLiveStoryProgression`, set by `CharacterFactory` on the full Story path only) subscribes while in the tree and re-resolves through `CharacterFactory.ReapplyStoryProgression`, which runs `CharacterFactory.ApplyActiveStoryProgression`, the same method the build now calls, then settles the live caps (`PlayerController.SettleLiveStoryProgression`, `BlockSystem.SetMaxCharges`). Suite: `ResonanceLiveApplyTests` (6, real physics frames at a Level 13 Warden Beacon through the real Repository screen; 4 from the step, 2 from its same-day review fix-up).

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~**FIX-GRID-LIVE-APPLY**~~ — grid purchases and respecs reached the hero only on the next scene load | `design-godot.md`, Resonance Grid UI Flow, Purchase (`:531`): "On confirmation, dust is deducted, the node activates … and stat modifiers are applied immediately." The free respec (same section) clears every node. | **Closed 2026-10-11.** On `d2e52e0` the only subscriber to `EventBus.OnTalentNodeUnlocked` was the autosave (`SaveManager.SaveTalentUnlock`), the respec raised nothing, and `CharacterFactory.CreateCharacter` resolved the grid once, at build. A node bought (or a respec made) at the hub Repository or an Act III Warden Beacon therefore changed nothing until the next character build: the next level, an Anchor Snap or a reload. A node bought at a Level 15 Beacon never reached the final boss without a paid reload. Now a purchase or respec re-resolves the stat lanes, the ability-scoped bucket and the perk set onto the live campaign hero at once, through the build's own method, so the two cannot drift. The idle factory-built kit hitboxes from `GetRequiredHitboxes` take the new damage lane. No kit reads that idle value: Lincoln's `OverheadHitbox` re-sets its damage when it activates, and the rest are never activated. The basic `MeleeHitbox` is left alone. Every swing writes its own per-hit damage when it **starts** (`StartComboHit` / `StartDirectionalAttack`: base × `StoryBasicDamageMultiplier` × the hit's tenths), so an inactive box already holds that swing's damage through its startup frames. The step as first shipped also refreshed the idle `MeleeHitbox` to the flat build value, and deferred only for the four ability slots, so a Beacon opened in a swing's startup changed that swing (a finisher lost a third of its damage). The same-day review fix-up removed the refresh and holds the re-resolve through a basic swing too (`PROVISIONAL-GRID-LIVE-APPLY` (b)). | grid-live-apply step (2026-10-11) | Closed on headless evidence: `ResonanceLiveApplyTests`. (1) Buying MaxHP, MoveSpeed, a scoped `AbilityRange` minor and a Traversal perk at a Beacon changes the hero's `MaximumHP`, run speed in real frames, `StoryScoped` lane and perk set at once, and the HUD hears the new maximum. A respec reverts all four, and the Legacy locks never move. (2) A respec and re-buy loop never heals. (3) A purchase made mid-cast lands when the cast ends. (4) A Hard-style Mirror clone and a Fighter-path body are never touched. With the re-resolve event removed, all four fail. With a healing settle and no cast deferral, (1)–(3) fail. With no clamp on a lowered cap, (2) fails. Review fix-up (same day): (5) a `BasicAttackDamage` node bought at a Beacon opened in a finisher's startup leaves that finisher's damage (base × its tenths) on the box and in its payload through the active frames, the multiplier stays put for the whole swing, the purchase lands on the first tick after it, and the next swing carries it. (6) No authored node grants block charges, so the block lane is set by hand and the settle called directly: a raised block cap does not refill the shield, a lowered one clamps it, and a settle that moves no cap raises no block event. With the swing deferral removed, (5) fails, with or without the old `MeleeHitbox` refresh. With a refill on a raise, or no clamp on a lowered block cap, (6) fails. Nobody has played it. |
| **PROVISIONAL-GRID-LIVE-APPLY** — what a live re-resolve may and may not change | The design says only that stat modifiers apply immediately. The task that shipped the fix (2026-10-11) set four rules: never heal through a respec or re-buy loop; keep the Legacy locks untouched; a cast in progress keeps its parameters; never touch the Mirror clone or any Fighter or drill path. | *Provisional. Rules (a), (b) and (e) come from that task. The rest, and how each rule is implemented, are agent-chosen.* (a) **HP and block charges.** A raised `MaximumHP` or block capacity is room, not a refill: current HP and charges stay where they are, and ordinary regen fills the new block charge. A lowered cap clamps them to it. So a respec at full HP with a MaxHP node bought loses those points, and re-buying does not return them. No authored node grants block charges today, so the block half is reachable only by a future node; it is tested with the lane set by hand. (b) **A cast or swing in progress keeps its parameters.** While any of the four ability slots is executing (`PlayerController.IsAnyAbilityExecuting`) or a basic swing is running (`CharacterState.Attacking`, its chain hold included), the whole re-resolve waits and lands on the first tick neither is running (`PlayerController.HoldsLiveStoryProgression`, `LiveStoryProgressionPending`). A swing writes its damage when it starts, sizes its box from `StoryAttackRangeMultiplier` when it activates and paces itself on `StoryComboSpeedMultiplier` every tick, so all three stay as the swing began. A string continued through the chain hold keeps the old grid until it ends. Only a Repository or Beacon opened mid-cast or mid-swing can hit this; neither the Beacon nor the hub Repository refuses to open then. (c) **What a cast left in the world follows.** Values fixed at spawn keep the spawn value: a construct's HP, lifetime and range, a projectile's base damage and speed, a granted shield's capacity. Lanes read when a hit lands follow the new value on the next hit, because they are read from the owner at that moment: every `Hitbox` payload (pooled projectiles included) reads the scoped `AbilityDamage`, knockback and status lanes; a `PlaceholderZone` tick reads `StorySpecialDamageMultiplier`; Leonardo's turret reads bolt speed per bolt; the serpent nest reads Venom potency per bite. An armed cooldown keeps its length. The character-wide `CooldownMultiplier` ticks running timers at the new rate, because `PlayerController.UpdateCooldowns` reads it live. The meter, Defy, statuses, the rewind pool and the Legacy locks are untouched. (d) **Who listens.** Only a hero built with both `applyStoryProgression` and `applyLegacyUnlockLocks`: the hub, Level 0, Florence and Levels 2–15. The Mirror clone keeps the grid it copied at spawn. On Hard, a Beacon purchase made during the Level 13 encounter therefore does not reach a clone that is already built. Fighter Mode, the Holodeck, the drills and the Test Arena never listen. (e) **Which grid.** The re-resolve reads the active Story save, exactly as the build does. It changes nothing when there is none: a slotless developer launch, or a unit harness. (f) **The HUD.** It hears the new maximum through an ordinary `OnPlayerHPChanged` payload with `DamageAmount` 0, which the haptics, telemetry and first-use tooltips ignore. Block pips rebuild from `OnBlockChargesChanged`. | Unassigned | Either accept the rules, or rule on: (i) whether a respec may cost HP above the new cap (the alternative keeps the overflow until the next damage); (ii) whether hero-wide stats such as MaxHP and MoveSpeed should apply mid-cast or mid-swing while only ability and swing parameters wait; (iii) whether the Hard Mirror clone should follow a mid-encounter Beacon purchase. |

## Confirmed deviations — basic-string hitbox geometry and the reach minors, 2026-10-11

Found by the 2026-10-11 code research and fixed on `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-11 (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`; committed as `2e9ca79` on 2026-10-12). The shipped change: the grounded basic-string boxes live once, in world units, in `BasicComboRules` (`StringBoxHalfWidthUnits`, `StringBoxHalfHeightUnits`, `StringBoxOffsetXUnits`, `StringBoxOffsetYUnits`). The Fighter sim converts them with `FP64.FromDouble` exactly as its literals did, so no Fighter hash moved, and Story derives pixels at `KitMotionRules.StoryPixelsPerUnit` (60) through `PlayerController.ResolveGroundStringBoxPixels`. The Story-only AttackRange minor now scales a chain box's width and forward offset together. No component, save field, schema version or protocol changed. Suite: `StringHitboxGeometryTests` (4). The six rows after the first three record divergences the same research found; this step changed none of them.

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~**FIX-STRING-BOX-ONE-TABLE**~~ — the grounded string boxes were two hand-mirrored copies | `design-godot.md`, Hitbox & Hurtbox Geometry (`:1360`): the three template boxes (1.2 × 1.0 @ (1.0, 0.0), 1.4 × 1.2 @ (1.0, 0.2), 1.8 × 1.4 @ (1.2, 0.0)) are normative in both modes. AGENTS.md: never a second canonical value. | **Closed 2026-10-11.** On `d2e52e0` Story held the boxes in pixels (`PlayerController.ComboHitboxSizes` / `ComboHitboxOffsets`) and the sim in world units (`FighterCombatSystem.StringBoxHalfWidths` / `HalfHeights` / `OffsetsX` / `OffsetsY`), equal only by copying. Now both read the one `BasicComboRules` table. The sim's four arrays are `FP64.FromDouble` of its entries, raw-identical to the old literals. The Story template boxes are still 72 × 60 @ (60, −32), 84 × 72 @ (60, −20) and 108 × 84 @ (72, −32) px. The Story aerial table and the directional boxes stay where they were (rows below). | Unassigned | Met: `StringHitboxGeometryTests` holds Story's far edge, near edge and height to the sim's × 60 for every hit and every profile, pins the template pixels, and pins the sim's fixed-point boxes raw-for-raw against the old literals for every profile and both facings. |
| ~~**FIX-REACH-MINOR-FORWARD**~~ — the AttackRange Resonance minors (Leonardo Brush Reach +8%, Shakespeare Quill Reach +10%, Lincoln Rail Reach +10%) | `design-godot.md` grid tables (`:2611`, `:2732`, `:2911`): "+8% / +10% basic attack range". `en.csv` `resonance_{leonardo_brush,shakespeare_quill,lincoln_rail}_reach_description`: "Basic attack reach +8% / +10%". | **Closed 2026-10-11.** On `d2e52e0` `PlayerController.StoryAttackRangeMultiplier` multiplied the box **size** about its centre and left the offset alone. A +10% node therefore moved the template far edge by only 3.75% / 4.1% / 4.3% (hits 1–3); Lincoln's hit 1 went from 81.6 to 84.66 px instead of 89.76. The near edge moved backward by the same amount. Now the minor scales a chain box's width and forward offset together, the way `ReachWidthPercent` does, so the far edge moves by the full percentage and the near edge scales with it. On every grounded hit and profile the near edge stays in front of the hero. Story-only: the sim never reads the minor. | Unassigned | Met: `StringHitboxGeometryTests` resolves each of the three authored nodes through the real grid and holds the live hit-1 far edge to (1 + percent) × the base, with the near edge scaled the same and in front of the hero. |
| **PROVISIONAL-REACH-MINOR-SHAPE** — what the AttackRange minor scales besides forward reach | The design says only "+8% / +10% basic attack range". The fixing task (2026-10-11) set three rules: the far edge moves by the full percentage, the near edge never crosses behind the hero, and the centred Up-Attack / Down-Air boxes keep size scaling. | *Provisional; the rest is agent-chosen, kept as on `d2e52e0` where the task was silent.* (a) The minor still scales the box **height** too. (b) The Story aerial chain boxes take the same width-and-offset scaling (the aerial marker's offset × the multiplier). Story aerial hit 3's near edge already sat 24 px behind the template hero. At +10% it sits 26.4 px behind, against 28.4 px under the old rule, so the "never behind" rule does not hold there (see `VERIFY-STORY-AERIAL-STRING-BOXES`). **Since 2026-10-11 (D6b, `USER-STORY-AERIAL-REACH`)** the aerial chain boxes share the grounded x-span through the same helper, so the minor scales them exactly as the grounded box and the "never behind" rule holds there too (Leonardo +8%: aerial hit 1 far 110.4 → 119.2 px, near 27.6 → 29.8 px); the minor still scales the aerial height about its centre, as (a) says for the grounded box. **Amended 2026-10-12 (`USER-AERIAL-SINGLE-STRIKE`):** the aerial chain boxes are retired; (b) now concerns the single air strike's box, which takes hit 1's x-span through the same helper (so the minor moves its far edge by the full percentage and the "never behind" rule holds) and scales its −64 … +40 px vertical span about its centre. (c) The Up-Attack and Down-Air boxes grow about their centre in both axes, as before. | Unassigned | Accept, or rule whether the minor lengthens only (no height), and whether it applies to aerial and directional strikes. |
| **VERIFY-STRING-REACH-OFFSET-SCALING** — how a profile's reach percentage moves the string boxes | `design-godot.md` `:1066`: the reach factor "scales the width of all three normative template boxes identically in Story and the sim, with offsets fixed". Finisher far edge: Lincoln 1.785 u, Cleopatra 2.52 u (fixed offsets: 1.965 / 2.28). Hit 1: 1.36 to 1.92 u (fixed offsets: 1.51 / 1.72). With fixed offsets every near edge would still stay in front of the hero at every authored width (at least 0.12 u at 120%). A pre-existing divergence, unchanged by this step. | Unassigned | User ruling. Either amend the design to "width and offset scale together" and its reach numbers to 1.785 / 2.52, or move both modes to fixed offsets. That moves every Fighter hash; retune the reach pins, the separation threshold and the CPU bands after it. |

The one-table fix kept that by applying the shared 0.2 lift downward in `ResolveGroundStringBoxPixels`. A pre-existing divergence, unchanged by this step. | Unassigned | User ruling. Either lift Story's hit 2 to −44 px (one sign in `ResolveGroundStringBoxPixels`, plus the template pin in `StringHitboxGeometryTests`), or record "lowered" as intended and amend the design. |
| ~~**VERIFY-STORY-AERIAL-STRING-BOXES**~~ — the Story aerial string boxes | The design authors one template table (`:1369`), front-only: "a swing never hits behind the attacker's back" (`:1375`). F09 (`:1089`) speaks of aerials' "shorter reach" but authors no aerial boxes. | **Closed 2026-10-11 by D6b** (`USER-STORY-AERIAL-REACH`, the combat-flow spacing section below): a Story aerial string hit now takes its grounded twin's x-span through `PlayerController.ResolveGroundStringBoxPixels` (far edge 1.6 / 1.7 / 2.1 u × width %, the sim's aerial reach; front-only), keeps the Story vertical span below, now authored in `BasicComboRules` (`AerialStringBoxCentreYUnits` / `AerialStringBoxHeightUnits`), and reads its facing at active start; `AerialComboHitboxSizes` / `AerialComboHitboxOffsets` and the `AerialHitboxMarker` node are deleted. **Residual, not closed:** the Story aerial vertical spans (centre −46 / −50 / −12 px from the feet) differ from the sim's grounded boxes, centred on the pivot, as the grounded Story spans already do (`VERIFY-STORY-STRING-HIT2-LIFT`); the directional boxes stay open under `VERIFY-DIRECTIONAL-HIT-MODEL`. *Before the close:* Story kept its own aerial pixel table (`PlayerController.AerialComboHitboxSizes` / `AerialComboHitboxOffsets`): 72 × 64 @ (58, −46), 82 × 82 @ (42, −50), 88 × 104 @ (20, −12). The sim swings its grounded boxes in the air. Template far edges: Story 94 / 83 / 64 px (1.57 / 1.38 / 1.07 u) against the sim's 1.6 / 1.7 / 2.1 u, so the Story aerial finisher reaches about half as far. Story aerial hit 3 also reaches 24 px (0.4 u) behind the pivot. Story fixes an aerial box's facing when the swing starts (`AerialHitboxMarker`, set in `StartComboHit`), but a grounded box's facing at active start. **History note, 2026-10-12:** the Story aerial string itself is retired in both modes (`USER-AERIAL-SINGLE-STRIKE`); an airborne press is one air strike whose box keeps D6b's x-span rule (hit 1's) with the old aerial hit 3's vertical span. | User (D6b) | **Met 2026-10-11** by the D6b ruling (`DECISIONS.md`): Story's aerial string takes the sim's aerial reach and is front-only, so the sim and every Fighter hash are unchanged. The original criteria: a user ruling under F09, either one shared front-only aerial table for both modes, or Story keeping its own table, recorded as intended, with at least aerial hit 3 made front-only. |

Turning during the active window therefore flips the box to the other side, behind the original front. The method's own comment says "current facing". Story places its grounded box once, at active start, and since 2026-10-11 (D6b) its aerial box too (the swing-start `AerialHitboxMarker` latch is deleted). A pre-existing divergence, unchanged by this step. **Amended 2026-10-12 (`USER-AERIAL-SINGLE-STRIKE`):** Story's aerial box is now the single air strike's, placed once at active start (`ResolveAirAttackBoxPixels`); the sim's air strike reuses `ResolveStringBox(0)` through `ApplyAirAttackSwing` on each active tick, so it inherits the per-tick read. **Closed 2026-10-16** by decision 10.1 ("Latch facing at active start, as in Story. Steering still moves the fighter; the box does not flip."), built by CM-SIM A0 (`b62bf1a`, merged `a6d9bf2`): `FighterBasicAttackRules.FlagActiveFacingRight` (305 `AttackFlags` 256) is latched on every Startup → Active transition, and the string, the air strike (the 2026-10-12 note above), the construct sweep and the ledge attack place their box from it; the centred Up-Attack and Down-Air need no latch. Pinned by a new `FighterBasicStringParityTests` case; Fighter hashes on turning swings moved. | Unassigned | User ruling. Either the sim latches the facing at active start (moves Fighter hashes on turning swings), or the design accepts per-tick facing in the sim. **Met 2026-10-16** by the first option, the user's ruling. |
