Against a Story hero the Up-Attack therefore connects out to 95 px (1.58 u) of pivot separation and the Down-Air to 82.5 px (1.38 u). A pre-existing divergence, unchanged by this step. **Amended 2026-10-12 (`USER-AERIAL-SINGLE-STRIKE`):** the third single strike, the air strike, does not use this model: it places string hit 1's box as the chain does (Story `ResolveAirAttackBoxPixels` against the hurtbox; the sim `ResolveStringBox(0)`). | Unassigned | User ruling on one hit model (box against hurtbox, or pivot) and one pixel scale, then both modes aligned. Aligning moves Fighter hashes if the sim changes. |

## Confirmed deviations — reach-aware CPU, Mirror and bots, and the finisher-separation bar, 2026-10-11

Found by the 2026-10-11 code research and fixed on `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-11 (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`; committed as `2e9ca79` on 2026-10-12), on top of the one string-hitbox table above. The CPU is an input source, so no component, snapshot field, save field or protocol changed, but **CPU-driven Fighter hashes move** for a CPU Lincoln or Joan, and the Level 13 Mirror clone's decisions move for every hero whose band below is under 2.0. No suite pins an absolute hash. The playtest bots' fixed 92 px strike range is now each hero's own opener reach against the target's hurtbox (`StoryBot.StrikeRangeFor`), so bot batches from before and after this change are not directly comparable. Suites: `CpuMeleeBandTests` (5), `PlaytestBotStrikeRangeTests` (1), and in-place additions to `MirrorParadoxTests` and `FighterKnockbackScalingTests`. **A review fix-up the same day** made a string continuation read the band of the hit it would start instead of the close range, re-reads that step when the press is delivered, kept the plan's 2.5-unit separation floor under the per-kit bar, and cached the hero's hurtbox width in the Mirror adapter: `CpuMeleeBandTests` 5 -> 8 (one case rewritten in place), `MirrorParadoxTests` +1, `FighterKnockbackScalingTests` in place. A CPU Tesla or Tubman (and every profile of 100 % reach or more) is still bit-identical, because every one of their hit bands is at least the 2.0 close range; only CPU Lincoln and Joan hashes move.

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~**FIX-CPU-REACH-BLIND-OPENER**~~ — the CPU pressed its string opener from beyond its reach | `design-godot.md`, CPU Fighter AI Specifications (`:3981`): the utility engine scores actions on "distance to opponent"; Easy "strictly uses basic ground attacks" (`:3990`) and Hard "executes frame-perfect basic attack strings" (`:4020`). An opener pressed from outside its own hitbox is neither. | **Closed 2026-10-11.** On `d2e52e0` `FighterCpuController.CloseRange` (2.0 units) gated the neutral BasicAttack press for every fighter. The sim opener reaches hit 1's box far edge plus the 0.5 victim half-width: Lincoln 1.86 and Joan 1.94 units, so from there to 2.0 their opener whiffed. The Level 13 Mirror clone used the same 2.0 against the hero's 40 px hurtbox, where even the template opener reaches only 1.93. Now only the close-range branch's BasicAttack fallback reads a per-fighter melee band (`FighterCpuController.MeleeBand`): hit 1's far edge from the one string table scaled by the fighter's `ReachWidthPercent` (`FighterCombatSystem.StringBoxFarEdge`, FP64), plus the target's hurtbox half-width (`CpuDecisionObservation.TargetHurtboxHalfWidthRaw`: the sim's live victim half-width, or the hero's real hurtbox from the Mirror adapter), less 0.1 unit. Inside the close range but beyond the band, the CPU walks in instead of pressing. A press that would continue the CPU's own string reads the band of the hit it would start (`CpuDecisionObservation.SelfNextStringStep`), and delivery re-reads that step on self-state and drops a press whose string has ended (fix-up, same day; see `PROVISIONAL-CPU-MELEE-BAND` (2) and (7)). Sim opener bands: Lincoln 1.76, Joan 1.84, Tesla and Tubman exactly 2.0 (the old range, so their neutral is bit-identical), Mozart and Shakespeare 2.08, Einstein 2.16, Leonardo 2.24, Cleopatra 2.32. Mirror bands against the 40 px hero: Lincoln 1.59, Joan 1.67, template 1.83, Mozart and Shakespeare 1.91, Einstein 1.99, Leonardo 2.07, Cleopatra 2.15. | Unassigned | Met: `CpuMeleeBandTests` (a CPU Lincoln or Joan 2.0 units from a standing dummy lands its first opener, where the reach-blind press whiffed; the Ultimate commit and the close Special ladder still fire at 1.9; Lincoln does not press from 1.9 during his finisher or before a hit 2 that would whiff; a press decided mid-string and delivered after the string ended is dropped; the template band equals the close range to the raw unit) and `MirrorParadoxTests.AShortReachCloneWalksInFromBeyondItsOpenerBandAgainstTheHerosRealHurtbox` (a Lincoln clone 1.8 units from the hero, past Story's real hit-1 reach, walks in; from 1.5 it swings). |
| **PROVISIONAL-CPU-MELEE-BAND** — the band's agent-chosen rules | The design names no CPU spacing number. The task that shipped the fix (2026-10-11) set the band per string hit: an opener reads hit 1's reach and a continuation the reach of the hit it would start. | *Provisional, agent-chosen.* (1) The margin is 0.1 unit (`MeleeBandMarginUnits`, also the bots' 6 px). (2) A press reads the band of the string hit it would start, `CpuDecisionObservation.SelfNextStringStep` (sim `FighterBasicAttackRules.NextChainStep`, Story `PlayerController.NextChainStepForPress`): the next hit (1 or 2) while a chain swing of hit 1 or 2 runs, any phase, the chain hold included; otherwise an opener, which covers rest, the finisher (it cannot continue) and a directional swing (it stands outside the chain). The first version exempted every press made during any swing and kept the close range for it. That was wrong twice: a press during the finisher or a directional swing can only become an opener, and Lincoln's hit 2 band is 1.845 units and Joan's 1.93, short of the 2.0 close range. Every hit band of a profile at 100 % reach or more is at least 2.0, so their behaviour did not move. (3) The close range stays 2.0 for everything else in its branch: the threat roll and block, the A02 Ultimate commit (`UltimateActivationCanConnect`), the lockout wait, the grab and the close Special ladder, and the Echo Step punish threat. (4) The approach walk-in happens only where the BasicAttack fallback would have pressed, so a fighter whose band exceeds 2.0 still closes to 2.0 before swinging and does not use its extra reach in neutral (Cleopatra, Leonardo, Einstein, Mozart, Shakespeare). (5) The Mirror reads the clone's base profile reach, not the Story AttackRange minor a Hard clone may mirror, so it walks slightly closer than it needs to. (6) A hand-built observation with no reach (0) keeps the legacy 2.0 press. (7) Delivery revalidation: a decision is delivered 4-8 (Hard), 15-20 (Normal) or 30-45 (Easy) frames after it is made, so a press admitted on a continuation band can arrive after the string has ended (the finisher ran out, the chain hold lapsed, an aerial string landed) and swing an opener. At delivery the CPU re-reads the step from its own current state, keeps the decision-time gap (the opponent read stays delayed, as `CPU_COMBAT_POLICY.md` requires), and drops the press if that step's band no longer covers the gap, walking toward the target instead. Like the recovery-jump revalidation it draws no RNG and touches no snapshot. (8) The Mirror adapter reads the hero's hurtbox width once per bound target, not every tick. **Amended 2026-10-12 (`USER-AERIAL-SINGLE-STRIKE`):** item (2)'s aerial-string case is gone: an airborne press is the air strike, a single strike for which `NextChainStep` / `NextChainStepForPress` return 0, so a press during it reads the opener's band, which is the air strike's own box (no CPU or Mirror code changed). Recorded: the step does not read the ground, so during a grounded-string hit that went airborne (a Story walk-off, a sim drop-through) the band reads the next chain hit although that press now ends the string or becomes the air strike (`PROVISIONAL-AERIAL-STRIKE-RULES`). | Unassigned | A user ruling or a hand-played pass against CPU Lincoln, Joan and the Mirror confirms or replaces these rules. Closing `VERIFY-SIM-HURTBOX-WIDTH` moves every sim band with the half-width (the template band then drops below 2.0, so Tesla and Tubman CPUs walk in too, and `CpuMeleeBandTests.ATemplateReachBandIsExactlyTheCloseRangeSoItsNeutralIsUnchanged` fails by design); re-run `CpuMeleeBandTests` then. |
| ~~**FIX-FINISHER-SEPARATION-BAR**~~ — the finisher-separation guarantee was pegged to a retired range | Gameplay-feel plan §2.5 (2026-08-10): the 4.5× finisher must leave the victim out of melee range when its hitstun ends, so the string ends in separation rather than a loop, with "≥ 2.5 units horizontal separation". That 2.5-unit floor is kept. Because the opener is what restarts the loop, a second bar is added on top of it (agent-derived, 2026-10-11): the kit's own hit-1 pivot reach plus 0.5 unit. The test asserts the larger of the two. | **Closed 2026-10-11.** `FighterKnockbackScalingTests` pinned a flat 2.5 units, "the 2-unit melee range plus a margin", and `BasicComboRules.KnockbackMultipliers` cited a nonexistent `FighterFinisherSeparationTests`. The V7.1 reach profiles had already retired the 2-unit range: Cleopatra's opener reaches 2.42. Measured 2026-10-11 with the suite's own protocol (full victim HP, mirror match), separation against the new per-kit bar: Cleopatra 3.19 / 2.92, Leonardo 3.71 / 2.84, Tesla 3.81 / 2.60, Einstein 3.95 / 2.76, Tubman 4.00 / 2.60, Shakespeare 4.22 / 2.68, Joan 4.33 / 2.44, Mozart 4.42 / 2.68, Lincoln 5.11 / 2.36. Every kit clears it. The test now asserts the larger of the 2.5 floor and the per-kit bar, read from the live box and hurtbox. The first version of this fix asserted the per-kit bar alone, which for Lincoln (2.36) and Joan (2.44) is below §2.5's locked 2.5 units, so a later change could have cut their separation to 2.4 and stayed green; the review fix-up the same day restored the floor. The comment now names the real test. No multiplier or width changed. | Unassigned | Met: `FighterKnockbackScalingTests.TheFinisherSeparatesEveryKitBeyondMeleeRangeAtFullVictimHP` passes against the larger of the 2.5 floor and the per-kit bar. If a later reach or knockback change fails it, bring it to the user (a width % or the finisher multiplier) rather than loosening the bar. |

## Confirmed deviations — the Story damage buff and ability-damage routing, 2026-10-11

Found by the 2026-10-11 code research and fixed on `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-11 (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`; committed as `2e9ca79` on 2026-10-12). **Story-only:** nothing under `scripts/FighterSim/` was touched, and no component, snapshot field, save field, schema version or protocol changed; no Fighter hash moves. Suites: the new `StoryAbilityDamageRoutingTests` (5), `ChronalOrbItemTests` (+2), `KitAlignmentContractTests` (+1, a source scan over `scripts/Characters/Abilities/` and a repo-wide check that nothing outside `PlayerController` applies the buff), and `ResonanceReaderCoverageTests.EveryAuthoredAbilityScopeHasAProductionReader` updated in place to accept the composition as the shared `AbilityDamage` reader. With the buff left out of the composition and a pooled projectile's Hitbox carrying the whole composition, all five routing cases fail.

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~**FIX-STORY-DAMAGE-BUFF-KIT-ROUTING**~~ — the item-drop damage buff skipped most Specials and Ultimates | `design-godot.md`, Items & Power-Up System, Story drop system (`:3267`–`:3269`): a temporary buff item "Boosts character Damage or Speed stats by +50% for 15 seconds" on Easy and "+25% for 10 seconds" on Normal; Hard spawns none. | **Closed 2026-10-11.** On `d2e52e0`, `PlayerController.StoryTemporaryDamageMultiplier` was read only in `Hitbox.CreatePayload` and the throw. Every payload a kit resolved itself (shape queries, ground waves, bursts, the Lorentz chain, every Ultimate, construct bites, arcs and the fence, zone ticks) multiplied only `StorySpecialDamageMultiplier` (plus the scoped lane for Joan's Smite, Lincoln's Emancipator and Mozart's Fortissimo), so a damage pickup changed nothing for most of the kit. Now the three Story attacker multipliers compose once, in `PlayerController`: `StoryAbilityDamage(base, abilityID)` / `StoryAbilityDamageScale(abilityID)` = SpecialDamage × AbilityDamage(abilityID) × the buff, split into a build-time half, `StoryAbilityHitboxDamage(base)` (SpecialDamage, for a value a Hitbox carries), and a contact-time half, `StoryHitboxContactDamageScale(abilityID)` (the buff × the lane), which `Hitbox.CreatePayload` alone applies. All 29 kit damage sites in 13 kit files, `PlaceholderZone` ticks, `CharacterFactory`'s kit hitboxes and `BaseSpecial.SpawnPlaceholderProjectile` go through it, so each factor applies once on every path. The throw keeps its own read of the buff. | damage-buff-routing step (2026-10-11) | Closed on headless evidence: `StoryAbilityDamageRoutingTests` (a buffed Righteous Smite and E=mc² burst deal ×1.5 once; a buffed Cosmological Constant deals 117 where it deals 78; a zone tick carries the buff once; Lincoln's factory-built Splitting Strike hitbox and E=mc²'s pooled contact land ×1.25, never ×1.5625) and `KitAlignmentContractTests.KitScriptsReadStoryAbilityDamageOnlyThroughTheOneComposition`. Nobody has played it. |
| ~~**FIX-STORY-ORB-BUFF-CASES**~~ — `ChronalOrbItem` ignored its two buff effects | `OrbEffect` authors `SpeedBuff` and `DamageBoost`; the Story buff values are the drop system's (`:3267`–`:3269`). The design also says Story campaign pickups are not orbs (`:3278`), and nothing spawns a Story orb. | **Closed 2026-10-11.** On `d2e52e0`, `ChronalOrbItem.OnPickedUp` had no case for either effect, so the pickup was consumed with no effect. Now both grant the Story drop buff through `ApplyStoryDamageBuff` / `ApplyStorySpeedBuff` at `StoryDropTable`'s profile for the current Story difficulty (`StoryDifficultyTuning.CurrentStoryDifficulty`, the drop system's own source). No orb was placed anywhere. | damage-buff-routing step (2026-10-11) | Closed on headless evidence: `ChronalOrbItemTests.BuffOrbsGrantTheStoryDropBuffAtTheCurrentDifficulty` and `OnHardABuffOrbIsConsumedWithoutCancellingARunningBuff`. |
| **PROVISIONAL-STORY-ABILITY-DAMAGE-ROUTING** — the composition's agent-chosen rules | The design says only that the buff boosts "character Damage". It does not say which delivery paths count, when the buff is sampled, or what a Story buff orb grants. | *Provisional, agent-chosen:* (a) the buff applies to every hit the hero's kit deals (Specials, Ultimates, movement-ability hits, construct bites, arcs and bolts, zone ticks, Tesla's coil detonation), as it already did to basics and throws; the Resonant Overdrive coil's direct Extractor arc (`TakeEnvironmentDamage`) keeps its unscaled value. (b) It is sampled when each hit lands (a Hitbox at contact, a kit hit at resolution), so a buff picked up mid-cast counts for that cast's later hits and one that expires mid-cast stops counting; Lincoln's Emancipator now resolves its damage per contact instead of once at cast. (c) Each ability's `AbilityDamage` lane now applies to every hit the ability authors, keyed by its ability ID, as `Hitbox.CreatePayload` already did. As a result, Mozart's Minor Chord Damage ("The Requiem Chord deals +10%") now also scales the 3 × 4 burst pulses; before, only the 4-damage contact took it. Joan's and Lincoln's lanes are unchanged. (d) The SpecialDamage lane has no grid node and carries only the Level 13 Mirror clone's F20 outgoing campaign multiplier (0.5 / 1.0 / 1.5). It now also reaches the pooled-projectile contacts (E=mc², Requiem Chord, Yorick), Tesla's coil arc, fence and detonation, and Leonardo's turret bolts, which ignored it. So on Easy and Hard the clone's projectile contacts and constructs now scale with F20; Normal is unchanged. (e) Mozart's Fortissimo rounds its authored base before the multipliers rather than base × SpecialDamage. That is identical for its whole-number base at every SpecialDamage value in use. (f) A speed or damage orb grants the drop profile's buff (Easy ×1.5 for 15 s, Normal ×1.25 for 10 s) and ignores its own `Value` and `Duration`. On Hard, which authors no buff, it is consumed with no effect and never overwrites a running buff. | Unassigned | A human playtest on Easy and Normal confirms the buff reads on Specials and Ultimates and is not out of proportion. A user ruling on (a) constructs and zones, (c) the Requiem burst, (d) the Mirror's projectiles and constructs and (f) the orb values ratifies or replaces each. Until then none is a design number. |

## Confirmed open deviations — combat flow (hit-confirm, per-hit Momentum, not-yet flash), 2026-10-11

User-directed combat-flow rules (`DECISIONS.md` D1, D1a, D2 and the 2026-10-11 addendum), built in both modes on `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-11 (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`; committed as `2e9ca79` on 2026-10-12) by spec A's agent A1, and the D10 "not yet" flash (`USER-NOT-YET-FLASH`) by its agent A2 the same day. The rules live once in `FTT.Combat.BasicComboRules` (`SpecialGateFrames`, `IsSpecialLocked`, `MomentumRefundFramesFor`, `MomentumRefundFramesForHitbox`, `MomentumGrant`, the 20 / 20 / 60 / 120 constants). Story keeps S, C and the freeze buffer on `PlayerController`; the sim packs them into one int of component 305 (the unread `AegisHits` slot, reused — 305 stays exactly 128 B, no component ID allocated, no protocol or save change). Every Fighter hash moves (per-hit Momentum, CPU Specials mid-string); no suite pins an absolute hash. Suites: `AbilityLockoutRulesTests` (+2), `FighterHitConfirmTests` (18), `StoryHitConfirmTests` (15); for the flash, `FighterLockoutRefusalRulesTests` (4), `StoryLockoutRefusalEventTests` (2) and one case each in `StoryHudPresentationTests`, `HudAbilityIndicatorModelTests` and `FighterHudSceneTests`. Nobody has played it, and nobody has looked at the flash.

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **USER-HIT-CONFIRM-CANCEL** — a landed basic swing cancels into Special 1/2 (D1, D1a) | **User direction 2026-10-11 (D1):** "A basic swing that **lands** a hit may cancel straight into **Special 1 or Special 2**. Whiffs still lock. … A Special accepted within the last 30 frames keeps blocking Specials, whatever lands afterwards. … No hit-confirm for the Ultimate, in either mode." **D1a:** a hit counts when "damage was applied, or it was absorbed by a shield or barrier layer"; it does not count when blocked by the block stance, refused by invulnerability, the arena ward, Time Freeze or the world hold, whiffed, or struck on a construct, prop or environment strike surface. **Orchestrator ruling on spec B §0.2:** spec A's mechanism is the single D1a predicate, and a payload with zero authored damage never counts as landed. Supersedes part of `USER-ABILITY-LOCKOUT`, and amends `design-godot.md` :782, :816, :1499, :3912 and the Cooldown System block from :1106 again. | Specials are refused while `BasicComboRules.SpecialGateFrames(A, S, C) = C ? S : A` is positive; the Ultimate reads A alone. **S** (Special-sourced lockout) is armed to 30 with A by every Special 1/2 and Ultimate acceptance and ticks with A (paused by the fighter's hitstop, and by Story Time Freeze); **C** is cleared by every swing start and set by the swing's first landed contact. **D1a, Story:** the receiver reports — `Hurtbox.ReportLanded` / `DidLand`, read back by `Hitbox.OnAreaEntered` into `HitPayload.ContactLanded` — from `EnemyController` (alive, not `DrillInvulnerable`, rounded payload damage > 0; a shield or armor taking it to 0 still lands), `BossController` (damage applied, after the ward and every invulnerability window), `PlayerController.ResolveIncomingHit` (damage applied, or a full Aegis / shield absorption on a body that is not post-rewind, tech or knockdown invulnerable) and `TrainingDummy` (alive, damage > 0); strike surfaces, props and constructs never report, and Time Freeze / the world hold / the M1 wall discard the contact before any receiver. **Sim:** `FighterDamageRules.ResolveFighterHit` returns `Refused` / `Blocked` / `Absorbed` / `Landed` (a partial barrier absorb followed by a block is `Blocked`; `ApplyFighterHit` is `== Landed`, hash-neutral for its callers); `Landed` or `Absorbed` with damage > 0 sets C. A Special's own hit never sets C, and a throw is not a swing. Measured first legal confirm after the template opener: sim T+s+F = T+10, Story T+s+2+F = T+12 (both previously T+30+F = T+34); the Ultimate is unchanged at T+30+F; a Special followed by a landed swing reopens Specials on the 30th unfrozen tick after the Special (previously after the swing). **Fighter consequence:** in PvP, hit 1 → any reaching Special is a guaranteed confirm from the last freeze tick (hit 1's hitstun is never block-cancelable and Specials resolve on the press tick, `DEFER-SIM-SPECIAL-PHASES`); D1 is ruled for both modes and no escape or proration was added. **Amended 2026-10-16 (decision 1.2, `USER-SIM-SPECIAL-TIMING`):** the Fighter consequence's parenthesis no longer holds — sim Specials run their authored startup instead of resolving on the press tick — but hit 1's 30-frame hitstun still covers every Special startup (at most 16 frames: Divine Piercing 13, Splitting Strike 14, the Emancipator 16), so hit 1 → any reaching Special stays a guaranteed confirm in PvP. Fighter D3 (`USER-FIGHTER-SPECIAL-RECOVERY-CANCEL`) adds a landed Special → basic → Special route with no scaling (decision 1.4), which plan §5 row 10 judges. | User (design amendment) | The GDD master (`design-godot-v7.md`) states the D1 cancel and the D1a definition at the cited lines and is re-synced to the mirror; a hands-on session in both modes; a Fighter session judges the hit-1 confirm against the No Juggling pillar (F09). |
| **USER-PER-HIT-MOMENTUM** — every landed basic hit refunds both Special cooldowns (D2) | **User direction 2026-10-11 (D2):** "Every landed basic hit refunds time on **both** Special cooldowns: string hits 1–2 20 frames, Up-Attack and Down-Air 20, finisher 60. Cap: 120 frames refunded per slot per cooldown cycle. Once per swing, not once per target." **Addendum ruling (2026-10-11):** "D2 applies to every landed basic hit on any enemy, boss included." Replaces the V7.1 finisher-only refund (60 frames, at most twice per cycle); supersedes `design-godot.md` :1108, :1352 and the snapshot struct :1766–1767 (`MomentumRefundsS1/S2` now count frames). | `BasicComboRules.MomentumGrant(nominal, refundedThisCycle, cooldownRemaining) = max(0, min(nominal, 120 − refunded, remaining))` on each slot, paid on the C 0 → 1 edge (so once per swing however many bodies a Story swing cleaves, and for an absorbed landing exactly as for a damaging one), bosses included. Counters: sim `FighterVerbComponent.MomentumRefundedFramesSlotOne/Two` (component 310, renamed in place from `MomentumRefundsSlot*`, same layout, now frames), Story `PlayerController._momentumRefundedFramesSlot*`; reset when that slot's cooldown is armed, zeroed by the F22 Sudden Death reset, kept through death, a death rewind, a checkpoint restore and a stock loss. Story refunds timer-seconds and sets a timer the grant fully covers to exactly 0. The Systems Card copy (`systems_card_echo_body`, value only) now reads "Every basic hit that lands takes time off both special cooldowns (a third of a second; a full second for the finisher), up to 2 seconds per cooldown." | User (design amendment) | The master amended and re-synced; `ROLLBACK_STATE_CONTRACT.md` "refund counts" reads "refunded frames"; a hands-on session. |
| **PROVISIONAL-HIT-CONFIRM-RULES** — the details D1/D2 left open | D1/D1a/D2 give the gate, the landed definition and the refunds only. | *Provisional, agent-chosen:* (1) S is armed by Special 1/2 **and an accepted Ultimate**. (2) The landed table above: absorbed = Aegis / Story shield / sim barrier / a mob's frontal or bubble reduction; a block taking any part wins; refusals are Foresight, projectile immunity, the Defy window, tech / knockdown / post-rewind invulnerability (including a Story full absorption during them, `PlayerController.IsConfirmInvulnerable`), the boss ward, transition, T01b, guarded and soliloquy; the `TrainingDummy` counts; a zero-damage payload never lands (orchestrator ruling). (3) Timing: S pauses in hitstop like A, so the landing's freeze delays the first confirm and a Special → landed swing → Special chain reopens on the 30th unfrozen tick; presses inside the freeze are held by the freeze-only buffer (`PROVISIONAL-FREEZE-SPECIAL-BUFFER`) rather than dropped. (4) A meter-paid Ultimate held back only by the lockout claims the tick from Special 1/2 (never from the movement ability): Story `_ultimateHeldBackThisTick`, set in `CheckUltimateInput` (every Story site polls it before `CheckSpecialInput`); sim `FighterCombatSystem.UltimatePressHeldBackByLockout`, read by the entity system's four Special branches and `BuildIntent`. (5) Storage: the reused `AegisHits` int of component 305 (an unread presentation mirror since D02e moved Aegis to component 312), packed as S (bits 0–15), C (16), freeze-buffer armed (17) and held presses (18–19); the four dead `AegisHits` writes are gone; the fallback, if the reuse is rejected, is growing component 320 by two ints. (7) The CPU reads the Special gate at delivery for its S1/S2 rungs and the mobility Special (new `CpuDecisionObservation.SpecialLockoutFrames` / `SwingLanded`, copied from 305; the Mirror adapter copies them from the clone); the Ultimate wait is unchanged, so a full-meter Hard CPU never hit-confirms against a grounded target in hitstun (it waits for its Ultimate); no hit-confirm preference tuning and no delivery-time revalidation. (8) Momentum: the grant counts frames removed; Story refunds timer-seconds and zeroes a timer the grant fully covers; counters are not persisted (a reload mid-cycle reopens the cap); an absorbed landing pays like a damaging one. (10) Test seams `FighterSimulation.SeedFighterInvulnerabilityForTest` and `SeedFighterSpecialCooldownsForTest` (both internal, both move the hash). (11) **Orchestrator default (`DECISIONS.md` addendum A):** hit 1 → Special confirms in Fighter PvP are accepted as D1 rules them ("both modes"); no escape, proration or hitstun change was added for them (the Fighter consequence in `USER-HIT-CONFIRM-CANCEL`), and a Fighter session judges them against F09. Also: the playtest telemetry's group-A seams (`PlaytestWorld`: `HasHitConfirmGate`, `SpecialGateFrames`, `IsLandedContact` = `ContactLanded`, `ConnectBasis` "d1a", `SwingLanded`) now read the A1 seams, so the `play` / `spam` bots gate Specials on the D1 gate; the refusal-event half (`HasLockoutRefusedEvent`, `SubscribeLockoutRefused`) waited for A2 and now counts the hero's `EventBus.OnAbilityLockoutRefused` events (`lockout_events` in the per-slot refusal stats). **Appended by agent A2 (2026-10-11):** (6) The "not yet" flash (`USER-NOT-YET-FLASH`): a 0.25 s `UIPalette.BossRed` `SelfModulate` tint back to white plus a hollow 2 px frame that appears at 1.35× and contracts to 1× as it fades (`FTT.UI.NotYetFlash`; the frame is the non-colour channel, and `Modulate` is left to the readiness writers, so a same-tick ready flash and a refusal flash never fight). It fires only when the lockout is the sole refusal: the slot unlocked and off cooldown (the Ultimate: the meter full), in a state that reads the press, and no Special or Ultimate accepted that tick. Under §1.7 the Ultimate alone flashes; Special 2 flashes beside a movement-ability acceptance (the chord at a non-full meter), because the movement ability is outside the lockout family. A press inside a landed swing's freeze is held and never flashes; if the gate refuses it on the thaw tick it flashes there (the addendum: "presses refused by the lockout are still dropped and get the D10 flash"). The Fighter classifier (`FighterLockoutRefusalRules.Evaluate`) reads the post-tick `PressedButtons`, which every input-discard path zeroes, with read-time cooldowns (one frame lower on an unfrozen tick, untouched on the last freeze tick, which the systems read) and the pre-tick meter, and flashes nothing for a fighter that is captured, knocked out or on the respawn platform after the tick. None in LAN. Known misses, never false flashes: a press on the tick the fighter is hit or its own swing lands with damage, on the tick an absorbed landing sets C, on the tick the meter fills inside `TickCounters`, and a sim Ultimate pressed from the block stance (`VERIFY-SAME-TICK-VERB-PARITY` (1)). Known differences: a same-tick BasicAttack + Special from neutral starts the swing in both modes, but only the sim flashes the Special — the A2 review classed this as a missing Story report site (a gap to close), not an intended mode difference; left open (the sim reads it after the swing armed the lockout; Story polls the swing first and never reads the Special); and a sim melee-intent Special pressed under the lockout while its target is out of reach flashes, although with the gate open its range gate would refuse it too (`DEFER-SIM-SPECIAL-PHASES`; Story casts at any range). The duration, scale, colour and border are provisional. (9) The Story HUD's three cooldown radials are written from the hero's own timers every frame (`StoryHUD.SyncCooldownAuthority` → `HudAbilityIndicatorModel.SyncCooldownRemaining`, after the fallback tick, so the arc is the hero's value that frame, not one frame ahead), keeping the denominator the cast armed and raising it only if the authority exceeds it (a cooldown restored on load raises no start event). A per-hit Momentum refund, a Time Freeze pause and `StoryCooldownMultiplier` now reach the radial; with no hero in the tree the old local countdown remains the fallback. **Amended 2026-10-16 (decisions 1.2, 2.3 and 9.3):** two of item (6)'s notes are retired. The known miss "a sim Ultimate pressed from the block stance" is gone, because the sim now refuses that press outright and keeps the meter (`USER-NO-ULT-FROM-BLOCK`); and the note that a sim melee-intent Special pressed under the lockout while out of reach flashes although its range gate would also refuse it no longer applies, because an accepted sim melee Special now spends and whiffs at any range, as Story's cast does (`FIX-SPLITTING-STRIKE-FREE-WHIFF`). A press during one's own cast is discarded and never classified (`FighterLockoutRefusalRulesTests.APressDuringOnesOwnCastNeverFlashes`). Item (7): the CPU also reads its own Special cast (`CpuDecisionObservation.SelfSpecialCastPhase`): its Special and Ultimate presses wait out the cast and are dropped at delivery while it holds the tick, and its grab and Echo Step mirrors refuse during it (`USER-CPU-SPECIAL-TIMING-ONLY`, `PROVISIONAL-CPU-SPECIAL-TIMING`). | Unassigned | A ruling on each, or a playtest that leaves them alone. |
| **USER-FREEZE-SPECIAL-BUFFER** — a Special 1/2 pressed during a landed hit's freeze fires after it (addendum to D10) | **User ruling 2026-10-11 (`DECISIONS.md` addendum):** "Freeze-only Special buffer: YES, both modes. A Special 1/2 press during the hitstop freeze of a *landed* hit fires on the first tick after the freeze ends, provided it is legal then. This is not a general lockout buffer. Presses refused by the lockout are still dropped and get the D10 flash. The Ultimate is not buffered. This amends D10's 'No input buffer' for exactly this case." It answers spec A §7 item 1. No design line states a Special input buffer, so this is new design content beside the D1 cancel. | Built in both modes by spec A's agent A1 (2026-10-11, uncommitted on `d2e52e0`; committed as `2e9ca79` on 2026-10-12). Its agent-chosen scope — which freezes arm it, one-deep latest press, the thaw tick per mode, the Ultimate-tick rule and the sim's packed bits on component 305 — is `PROVISIONAL-FREEZE-SPECIAL-BUFFER` below. Pinned by `FighterHitConfirmTests` and `StoryHitConfirmTests` (two cases each). Bot evidence (`absolum_research/flow/bots_after.md`): under the default press policy the `play` and `spam` bots' Special refusals labelled `hitstop` rose from 7 to 84 and from 5 to 70 per 24 Normal runs; the D6d telemetry resolves a press on the next tick, so it books a press the buffer fires after the freeze as refused. | User (design amendment) | The GDD master states the buffer beside the D1 cancel and is re-synced; `HUD_CONTRACT.md`'s D10 paragraph (`USER-NOT-YET-FLASH`) names it; a hands-on session in both modes judges the timing. |
| **PROVISIONAL-FREEZE-SPECIAL-BUFFER** — a Special pressed in a landed hit's freeze fires after it | **User ruling 2026-10-11 (addendum):** "Freeze-only Special buffer: YES, both modes. A Special 1/2 press during the hitstop freeze of a *landed* hit fires on the first tick after the freeze ends, provided it is legal then. This is not a general lockout buffer. Presses refused by the lockout are still dropped … The Ultimate is not buffered." Amends D10's "No input buffer" for exactly this case. | *Provisional, agent-chosen details:* **Which freeze:** only the attacker freeze of the fighter's own **landed basic swing** (D1a landed, with damage > 0, so the swing froze its attacker) arms the buffer; a victim freeze, a blocked or shatter freeze, a strike surface's freeze, a landing that does not freeze its attacker (an absorbed one; a lethal one in the sim, whose KO skips the freeze — Story's kill freeze does arm it) and a Special's own caster freeze buffer nothing (the S lockout would refuse those at the thaw in all but a slow projectile's late landing). **What is held:** one deep — the Special 1/2 bits of the latest frozen tick that pressed either (both if pressed on one tick; the ordinary same-tick order then casts Special 1). A frozen tick that also presses the **Ultimate** holds nothing new and leaves an earlier held press in place, so a same-tick LB+RB chord's Special 2 is never held. **Edge, recorded at the finalize step (the A1 review), unchanged:** a chord whose RB half (Special 2) arrives on an earlier frozen tick than its LB half holds that Special 2 on the earlier tick, and the chord's Ultimate press is not buffered, so the thaw casts Special 2 alone if its gate allows — the same split-chord outcome as outside a freeze (`VERIFY-GAMEPAD-ULTIMATE-CHORD` (a)). **When it fires:** Story re-presents the held press on its first unfrozen tick (`PlayerController.ReleaseFreezeSpecialBuffer`, OR-ed into that tick's `CurrentInputFrame.Pressed` before any state reads it); the sim re-presents it on the fighter's thaw tick — the tick the movement system takes `HitstopFrames` 1 → 0, whose presses the ability and combat systems read — by OR-ing it into `FighterRuntimeComponent.PressedButtons` (`FighterAbilityLockoutRules.ReleaseFreezeBuffer`). There the ordinary gates decide it: a press the D1 gate, a cooldown, Suppression, Time Freeze, hitstun or the block stance refuses is dropped, never retried. The buffer is emptied at that thaw tick, by every swing start, and by a death rewind, a checkpoint restore, Sudden Death and a stock loss. **Sim storage:** bit 17 (armed) and bits 18–19 (held S1 / S2) of the packed hit-confirm int on component 305 (`FreezeBufferArmed`, `FreezeBufferedSpecials`) — snapshotted and hashed, no ID, no protocol change. Story keeps `_freezeBufferArmed` / `_freezeBufferedSpecials` (never persisted; `FreezeBufferedSpecials` is a test seam). Pinned by `FighterHitConfirmTests` (2 cases, plus the corrected-press convergence case, whose boundary is now the landing tick against the freeze's first tick) and `StoryHitConfirmTests` (2 cases). | Unassigned | A hands-on session in both modes judges the timing (a freeze-time press against a release-tick press) and the chord rule; a ruling on whether a Special's own landing freeze should buffer too, and on the split-chord edge (clear a held press on a tick that presses the Ultimate, or keep it). |
| **USER-NOT-YET-FLASH** — a lockout-refused Special or Ultimate press flashes its HUD slot (D10) | **User direction 2026-10-11 (D10):** "When a Special 1, Special 2 or Ultimate press is refused by the lockout, flash that slot's HUD icon. No input buffer." **Addendum (2026-10-11):** the freeze-only Special buffer amends "No input buffer" for exactly that case; "Presses refused by the lockout are still dropped and get the D10 flash." **Owed amendments:** `docs-3/docs/HUD_CONTRACT.md` and its repo copy `docs/design-contracts/HUD_CONTRACT.md` gain a "Lockout refusal (D10)" paragraph in the M25 section and a Story equivalent, both naming the shape channel (the contracting frame) beside the tint, per the contract's "colour alone" rule; `design-godot.md` :3514 (HUD Micro-Behaviours) and the sprite-table rows :4916 (Ultimate meter ring), :4918 (Story cooldown icons) and :4919 (Fighter cooldown icons) each gain a "not-yet flash" state. | **Story:** `PlayerController` raises `EventBus.OnAbilityLockoutRefused` (`AbilityLockoutRefusedPayload { PlayerIndex, Slot }`) at the exact refusal site: `CheckSpecialInput`'s D1 gate (one event per pressed Special that is Legacy-unlocked, has its slot node and a timer at or below 0), `CheckUltimateInput`'s held-back point (meter full and unlocked: the Ultimate alone, §1.7), and `ProcessAttacking` on a tick a chain swing starts (the swing wins and re-arms the lockout, as in the sim; §1.7 first). Time Freeze, Suppression, a Dormant slot, a cooldown and an unpaid meter refuse first and raise nothing. `StoryHUD` flashes player 0's `Cooldown1_Icon` / `Cooldown2_Icon`, or the meter ring (the frame on the plain `UltimateMeter_BG`, the tint on the drawn `UltimateMeter_Fill`); the Level 13 Mirror clone raises the event as index 1 and the HUD ignores it. **Fighter:** `FighterSimulationDriver` captures a `LockoutRefusalBaseline` per fighter (hitstop, both Special cooldowns, meter, Ultimate busy) before each local `Simulation.Advance` and classifies the tick after it with the pure, engine-free `FighterLockoutRefusalRules.Evaluate` (presentation only, never called by an ISystem, ints and `FP64` only); `FighterHUD.FlashLockoutRefusal` then flashes that player's `S1_Cooldown` / `S2_Cooldown` label or `MeterBar`. The ability entity system's early-continue set became the shared `FighterAbilityLockoutRules.CanReadAbilityPress`, a hash-neutral refactor the classifier reads too. The look lives once in `FTT.UI.NotYetFlash` (`PROVISIONAL-HIT-CONFIRM-RULES` (6)). No sound, no text, no buffer, nothing persisted, snapshotted or hashed; the lockout itself is still not displayed; LAN sessions do not flash. | User (design amendment) | HUD_CONTRACT (master and repo copy) and the GDD master amended at the lines cited and re-synced to the mirror; a visual review of the tint and the frame at gameplay scale, Story and Fighter. |
| **VERIFY-STORY-INVULN-LAYER-ORDER** — Story spends a shield on a hit its body was invulnerable to | `DEFENSIVE_EFFECTS.md` D01: "hit eligibility / invulnerability → Defy → projectile immunity → Aegis → barrier → block → HP". | Story's `PlayerController.ResolveIncomingHit` checks only roll invulnerability, Defy and Foresight before `ResolveProtectionLayers`; post-rewind, landing-tech and knockdown invulnerability are checked later, inside `ApplyDamage`.
