Found by the 2026-10-11 hit-confirm review; this change only gates its own landed report (`IsConfirmInvulnerable`), it does not reorder the layers. **Amended 2026-10-16 (CM-FIXB-STORY, findings B7 / B21, `2ede072`):** the hazard half is met at the 7.4 entry point — `PlayerController.ApplyEnvironmentalHit` now refuses an invulnerable hero first (`RefusesEnvironmentalHit`: Dead or Respawning, roll invulnerability, `IsConfirmInvulnerable`), above Defy, Aegis, the barrier and the block, so a post-rewind-protected hero holding Block no longer spends a charge, takes shieldstun or shatters on a hazard tick, and spends no Aegis or barrier there (`StoryEnvironmentalDamageTests.APostRewindProtectedHeroSpendsNoChargeAegisOrGuardOnAHazardTick`). **Wider than first recorded:** the hurtbox path (`ResolveIncomingHit`) still spends a block charge, not only a shield, on an enemy hit that a post-rewind-protected, blocking hero is immune to, and `ApplyEnvironmentalDamage`'s unblockable penalties (drowning, the haul-out, the rift snap, the escape catch) still spend Aegis or the barrier on an invulnerable hero. | Unassigned | Move the three gates above the protection layers (or rule the Story order intended), with a pin in each mode. **Amended 2026-10-16:** met at `ApplyEnvironmentalHit`; still owed for `ResolveIncomingHit` (charges and shields) and `ApplyEnvironmentalDamage`. |

## Confirmed open deviations — combat flow (Story Special flow: the first Special untaxed, the Punish window, the on-hit Special recovery cancel), 2026-10-11

User-directed Story combat-flow rules from `DECISIONS.md` D4 and the 2026-10-11 addendum ("No count rule for poise"), built on `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-11 (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`; committed as `2e9ca79` on 2026-10-12) by spec B's sub-step B1. Spec B's later sub-steps (D8, the Punish window; D3, the on-hit Special recovery cancel) append their rows here. Every number is one constant in `FTT.Combat.StoryEnemyCombatRules` (`ChainSpecialRole`, `ClassifyChainSpecial`, `ChainHitScale`, `StandardBudgetCost(…, taxedSpecial)`, `StandardStaggerBudgetSeconds`, `StandardStaggerDecayPerSecond`, `FirstSpecialStunAllowanceFrames`); the behaviour lives in `EnemyController.ResolveIntakeScale` and its private `ApplyStun`. Story only: nothing in `scripts/FighterSim/` reads any of it, and no Klotho component, snapshot, hash, protocol, save field or `.tres` value changed. Suites: `StoryFirstSpecialTaxTests` (8), `StoryEnemyCombatRulesTests` (+1, 2 rewritten), `StoryRiftCollapseTests` (+1), `StoryEnemyCombatTests` (2 rewritten). Nobody has played it.

Spec B's sub-step B2 (2026-10-11, the same working tree) added D8, the Punish window: the two rows after D4's below. Its numbers are constants in the same rulebook (`PunishWindowFrames`, `PunishDamageScale`, `PunishWhiffNearMarginPixels`, `PunishWhiffNearVerticalPixels`, `PunishDamageNumberScale`, `IsPunishEligible`, `PunishWhiffIsNear`), plus the one shared mob melee-reach helper `StoryEnemyCombatRules.MeleePivotReachPixels` and the named Story hero hurtbox `StoryCombatRules.HeroHurtboxWidthPixels` / `HeroHurtboxHeightPixels` (40 x 64, now read by `CharacterFactory.BuildHurtbox`, no value change), which spec D's D6c reuses. The behaviour lives in `EnemyController` (the window, `ResolveIntakeScale`, `PresentDealtHit`, and the whiff rule on the executor's new `ActiveWindowEnded` event), `PlayerController.ResolveIncomingHit` (`ReportEnemyAttack`) and `EnemyAbilityExecutor` (the blocked Siphon Snare, the shooter's ID on its shots). Story only: nothing in `scripts/FighterSim/` reads any of it, and no Klotho component, snapshot, hash, protocol, save field, `.tres` value or translation key changed. Suites: `StoryPunishWindowTests` (8), `StoryEnemyCombatRulesTests` (+2). Nobody has played it.

Spec B's sub-step B3 (2026-10-11, the same working tree) added D3, the on-hit Special recovery cancel: the last two rows below. It reads the D1a predicate spec A's agent A1 had already built (`HitPayload.ContactLanded`, `Hurtbox.ReportLanded` / `DidLand`; the four receivers carry the orchestrator's 0-damage amendment, verified, so no D1a row of its own). The numbers are three constants and three predicates in `FTT.Combat.StoryCombatRules` (`SpecialRecoveryEvadeCancelFrame` 0, `SpecialRecoveryActionCancelFrame` 6, `SpecialCancelBufferFrames` 6; `SpecialRecoveryAllowsEvade`, `SpecialRecoveryAllowsAction`, `SpecialCancelBufferHolds`). The latch and the recovery frame index live on `BaseSpecial` (`LandedDirectHitThisExecution`, `RecoveryFramesElapsed`, set by `ConfirmAbilityHit`'s new first statement); kit shape-query hits are stamped by `BaseSpecial.Credit(Hurtbox struck, ...)`, whose hurtbox-less overload is deleted (all 25 kit call sites pass the struck hurtbox); `Hitbox.OnAreaEntered` now calls `ConfirmAbilityHitboxHit` for every contact, after the D1a stamp, so a fully absorbed (0-damage) ability contact reaches the latch (the feedback inside stays damage-gated). The cancel and its one-slot buffer live in `PlayerController.ProcessUsingSpecial` (`BufferSpecialCancelPress`, `TryFireSpecialCancel`, `TryCancelIntoJump`, and the pure extractions `TryStartRollOrAirDodge`, `StartBasicSwing`, `TryStartMovementAbilityCast`). `TubmanForesight.OnInterrupted` now ends the sidestep. Story only: nothing in `scripts/FighterSim/` reads any of it, and no Klotho component, snapshot, hash, protocol, save field, `.tres` value or translation key changed. Suites: `StorySpecialRecoveryCancelTests` (11), `StoryCombatRulesTests` (+1). Nobody has played it.

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **USER-FIRST-SPECIAL-UNTAXED** — the first Special in a stun chain is not taxed (D4) | **User direction 2026-10-11 (D4):** "The first Special in a stun chain lands at **full damage**: no ×0.6 confirm proration, and no chain-decay tax on its hits; charges standard poise at **×1.0**. A second or later Special in the same chain keeps the ×0.6 proration and the ×1.5 poise cost. **The Smite → string route must work.** … Righteous Smite → hit 1 → hit 2 → hit 3 must not trip standard poise. Re-derive the S3 budget so that one full string plus the largest authored first-Special stun fits, and so that a second Special still trips it." **Addendum (2026-10-11):** "No 'count rule' for poise. A second Special trips standard poise only through D4's stun arithmetic, not automatically." Amends `PROVISIONAL-STORY-CONFIRM-PRORATION` (S2) and `PROVISIONAL-STANDARD-POISE` (S3); new design content (the GDD has no PvE proration or standard poise). | The intake classifies each hit against the mob's stun chain (`StoryEnemyCombatRules.ClassifyChainSpecial`): the chain's first Special-class, non-Ultimate hit records its `AttackID` (`EnemyController.ChainFirstSpecialID`), and every hit with that ID is `FirstSpecial` — scaled ×1.0 at any chain index (`ChainHitScale`) and charging standard poise ×1.0 (`StandardBudgetCost(stun, taxedSpecial: false)`). Any other Special is `OtherSpecial`: the pre-D4 ×0.6 confirm cut times the chain decay, and ×1.5 poise. V7.4's diminishing special stun is unchanged (it still halves the stun of a Special-class hit inside 4 s of another, the first Special's later hits included); elites keep V7.4 poise; bosses were never prorated or poised. Standard budget **2.325 s** (was 1.9), drain **4.0 s/s** (was 3). Measured on a resistance-0 standard: Smite → hit 1 → hit 2 → hit 3 charges 132 frames and fits (it tripped at hit 2, 123 > 114); a second Special after it charges ≥ 9 frames and trips (141 > 139.5); a first Special fired into a string at chain hit 4 lands in full (an 8-damage hit: 8, was 4). | User (design amendment) | A bot or hand check of the Smite route and of "string + both Specials" on Normal; the design master gains the rule beside Enemy Stagger Discipline. |
| **PROVISIONAL-FIRST-SPECIAL-TAX-RULES** — the details D4 left open | D4 names the first Special, its full damage and ×1.0 poise, the ×0.6 / ×1.5 for later Specials, and the Smite route; nothing else. | *Provisional, agent-chosen (spec B §1.3, the orchestrator's defaults for its §7):* (1) **Identity by `AttackID`.** The first Special-class, non-Ultimate hit on a chain claims the slot; every hit of that Special shares the role (Divine Piercing's thrusts, E=mc²'s contact and burst, Requiem's pulses, Lorentz and its arcs). A Special-class **tick** is classified too (it stays proration-exempt: no scale, no chain step), so the Sandstorm Vortex's launching tick can be the first Special and charges ×1.0; a tick's claim on a mob it does not stun is cleared by the next classified hit. **The Rift collapse joins its E=mc² execution** (design E01: "a 0-damage, Special-class hit of the same E=mc² execution"): `EinsteinRelativityRift.TryCollapse(burstPoint, sourceAttackID)` carries E=mc²'s ability ID into the collapse payload, so the E=mc² + collapse route charges 12 + 6 + 15 = 33 frames, not 40.5. (2) **The first Special's hits are not scaled but do advance the chain counter** (the minimal reading; orchestrator default "the first Special's hits still count toward the chain's free-hit count"), so Smite → hit 1 → hit 2 → hit 3 lands its finisher at ×0.85 exactly as before D4. (3) **Movement-ability hits never claim the slot** (`HitPayload.SourceIsMovementAbility`, stamped by `BaseSpecial.WithAbilityContract` and `Hitbox.ApplyAbilityHitContract` from `AbilityData.Slot`; movement abilities author `Origin = Special`) and keep the pre-D4 S2/S3 treatment, as does a Special with no `AttackID`. (4) **The budget:** 2.325 s = 139.5 frames = the S5 string's 102 frames + `FirstSpecialStunAllowanceFrames` 36 (Requiem Chord with the Requiem Crescendo perk, 12 + 4 × 6, the worst-case total first-Special charge with kit and perk extras: E=mc² + collapse 33, Splitting Strike + Kinetic Splitting's 20-frame bounce 32, Smite 30, Requiem 30) + a 1.5-frame margin; the same budget trips the ruled route plus any second Special (132 + 9 = 141 frames, the smallest taxed second charge being one 12-frame hit × 0.5 × 1.5) with a 1.5-frame margin. Drain 4.0 s/s, so the 0.6 s getup armor (2.4 s) clears a full budget and each chain that did not trip starts the next empty. `StoryFirstSpecialTaxTests.NoFirstSpecialChargesMoreStandardPoiseThanTheAllowance` recomputes every authored Special from a per-kit hit model reading only authored data and named kit constants (`LeonardoGoldenRatio.ChurnTickHitstunFrames`, `TeslaLorentzPulse.ChainArcHitstunFrames`, `EinsteinRelativityRift.CollapseLaunchHitstunFrames`, `LincolnSplittingStrike.GroundBounceWindowFrames`, all named in this change with no value change). (5) **Residuals, no count rule** (addendum): a string plus both Specials trips only where first + second > 37.5 frames — always for Joan and Mozart, for Lincoln only with the Kinetic Splitting bounce (Emancipator → string → Splitting Strike charges 123 frames), never for Tubman (123 frames); Einstein, Leonardo, Cleopatra, Tesla and Shakespeare have one stunning Special each (the other is a zone, a construct or the Tempest's windbox, which never trips); `shock_shield_legionnaire` (resistance 0.15) does not trip on the ruled route (135.15 frames). **String → Special → string trips** at the restarted hit 1 (first Special > 27.5 frames) or hit 2 (any) — orchestrator default "poise trips on a restarted chain are accepted", so a chain allows one string plus one Special on a standard. (6) **A recast of the same Special inside one chain matches the recorded first Special by `AttackID`** and lands untaxed too; the orchestrator default "Recasting the same Special does not reset 'first'" is read as "the chain's first-Special record is not reset or re-keyed by a recast" (execution keying would need a serial on every Special payload, child hitboxes included; rejected). Practically elite-only: the cooldown must come back inside one chain. | Unassigned | A ruling on each (especially (5) and (6)), or a bot before/after and a playtest that leave them alone. |
| **USER-PUNISH-WINDOW** — a blocked, countered or whiffed mob attack opens a ×1.2 window on that mob (D8) | **User direction 2026-10-11 (D8):** "**Triggers** (the window opens on that enemy): a non-shatter block of that enemy's attack; a Foresight counter; an enemy whiff, meaning its melee attack's active window ended without hitting the hero while the hero was near. **Effect:** the hero's hits on that enemy deal **×1.2 for 60 frames**, shown with tinted damage numbers. **Scope:** mobs and elites, not bosses, because `VERIFY-BOSS-HP` is held." Orchestrator default (DECISIONS addendum): "The Punish window runs on world frames." New design content: the GDD has no PvE punish. | Every `EnemyController` (standard and elite tiers, summons included) carries a window (`IsPunishWindowOpen`, `PunishFramesRemaining`, `OpenPunishWindow()`) set to `StoryEnemyCombatRules.PunishWindowFrames` (60) by: the hero's chokepoint `PlayerController.ResolveIncomingHit` (`ReportEnemyAttack`) on a `BlockResult.Blocked` of a payload whose `SourceActorId` names that mob, and on a successful `TubmanForesight.TryCounter` of it; `EnemyAbilityExecutor` on a non-shatter block of the Eraser's Siphon Snare; and the mob's own whiff rule (`EnemyAbilityExecutor.ActiveWindowEnded`; `PROVISIONAL-PUNISH-WINDOW-RULES` (5)). The mob is named on its payloads through the new `Hitbox.SourceActorIdOverride` (stamped on the mob's attack hitbox by `ApplyScaledHitboxDamage`) and `EnemyProjectile.SourceActorId` (stamped by the executor after every `Setup`, pool-reset to 0). An eligible hit (`IsPunishEligible`: player-dealt, `DirectHit`, not a hazard: basics, directional strikes, Specials and Ultimates) on an open window multiplies `PunishDamageScale` (1.2) into `ResolveIntakeScale` after S2/D4 and S4, with the one `ScaleIntakeDamage` rounding, and its floating number is drawn in `UIPalette.GoldBright` at `PunishDamageNumberScale` (1.25×; `FloatingDamageNumber.Show(..., tint, scale)`). Measured in `StoryPunishWindowTests`: Joan's 6-damage hit 1 on a punished standard deals 9 (8 unpunished); Tubman's 20-damage Foresight answer on the countered mob deals 24. Bosses (`BossController`) and the Level 13 Mirror clone have no window, and a blocked boss payload changes nothing. | User (design amendment) | The design master amended (Section 4, beside Enemy Stagger Discipline) and re-synced; a hands-on session confirms the window reads and pays off. |
| **PROVISIONAL-PUNISH-WINDOW-RULES** — the details D8 left open | D8 names the three triggers, ×1.2, 60 frames, tinted numbers, and mobs and elites only; nothing else. | *Provisional, agent-chosen (spec B §1.4 and the orchestrator's defaults for its §7):* (1) **World clock:** 60 of the mob's own unfrozen ticks, decremented at the **end** of `EnemyController._PhysicsProcess` (the old body is now `ProcessMobTick`), after every trigger that tick could fire, so a window opened by a block, a counter or a whiff during tick T is open for every hit resolved through tick T + 59. It pauses only while the mob's body is frozen (`_rewindFrozen`: Time Freeze, the rewind freeze, the Post-Landing Hold) and keeps running through the mob's victim hitstop, a grab hold and a thrown flight, so a punish string's own freezes spend it (roughly 20 of the 60 frames on a three-hit string). A re-trigger refreshes to 60 and never stacks. It clears on death, pool spawn and despawn, and a rewind restore, and is never persisted. (2) **Eligible hits:** player-dealt `DirectHit` payloads that are not hazards; ticks, construct hits and hazards never punish, and Story throws and bowling never reach the intake path (`TakeDamage(int)`). (3) **Composition:** ×1.2 multiplies into the intake scale after S2/D4 and S4, with one rounding (half away from zero, at least 1). (4) **Block** means `BlockResult.Blocked` only: projectiles, Guard-Crush and a blocked Siphon Snare count; a shatter (`GuardBroken`), a full protection-layer absorption and every refusal open nothing. (5) **Whiff:** a MeleeStrike or ChargeDash active window that ends naturally (`ActiveWindowEnded`, raised first in `EnterRecovery`; never on a telegraph interruption or a hard stop), with no contact of that ability (matched by the active ability's `AttackID`) having reached the hero (HP damage, a full absorption by a body that was not invulnerable to it, a block of either result, or a counter), the swing not swallowed whole by a wall (`LastHitboxWallBlocked`), and the mob's target near at that moment: within `MeleePivotReachPixels(HitboxOffset.X, HitboxSize.X, 0, 20)` + `PunishWhiffNearMarginPixels` (40) horizontally and `PunishWhiffNearVerticalPixels` (96) vertically, feet to feet, using the authored box and no dash travel (positions are read where the swing ended; the legacy scalar melee is near within 110 px). Roll, air-dodge, tech and post-rewind invulnerability, Defy protection, a Foresight sidestep and projectile immunity never report a reach, so **rolling through a swing opens the window**; a swing that strikes a decoy or a construct instead of the hero is a whiff too. AreaPulse, Projectile, Shockwave and the other archetypes never whiff. (6) **Presentation:** `UIPalette.GoldBright` plus a 1.25× number scale (the non-colour channel); every other player-dealt number stays white at 1×. No HUD element, `EventBus` payload or string. (7) Bosses and the Mirror clone are excluded. (8) **Pooled owners:** a pooled mob keeps its Godot instance ID across a recycle, so a shot fired before the recycle and blocked after it opens the new spawn's window (rare, accepted); a blocked shot from a distant shooter opens that shooter's window even when the hero cannot reach it within 60 frames (the ruling's "that enemy's attack"). (9) **The shared reach:** `StoryEnemyCombatRules.MeleePivotReachPixels(offsetX, width, dashTravel, targetHalfWidth)` = abs(offset) + width / 2 + travel + half-width, with the named hero hurtbox `StoryCombatRules.HeroHurtboxWidthPixels` (40), is the one statement of a mob swing's reach; spec D's D6c commit cap reads the same helper. | Unassigned | A ruling on each (especially (1), the world clock, and (5), the whiff's "near" and the roll-through), or a bot before/after and a playtest that leave them alone. |
| **USER-SPECIAL-RECOVERY-CANCEL** — a Special that landed a direct hit cancels its recovery (D3) | **User direction 2026-10-11 (D3):** "Applies to a Special whose execution has **landed a direct hit** (the D1a definition; ticks, constructs and zones do not count). … Roll or jump: from recovery frame 0. Basic hit 1, or the movement ability: from recovery frame 6. … Whiffs keep their full recovery. No Echo Step out of a Special (A06). Special → Special is still gated by the lockout. … Fighter: Specials resolve on the press tick there (`DEFER-SIM-SPECIAL-PHASES`, not being closed now), so this is Story-only for now." **Addendum (user, 2026-10-11):** "Out of a landed Special's recovery (D3), every basic attack variant is allowed from recovery frame 6: Up-Attack, Down-Air, or hit 1, according to the held direction. A held direction never eats the press." **Orchestrator default:** the Mirror clone inherits D3. **Supersedes,** for Story: `design-godot.md` :784–785 (`UsingSpecial` leaves only when the ability completes) and :783 / :1500 (only Block cancels a Special's recovery). | A Special execution whose own direct hit LANDED (`BaseSpecial.LandedDirectHitThisExecution`: the payload's `ContactLanded` — the receiver's D1a report — on a `DirectHit` payload whose `SourceExecutionSerial` is 0 or this execution's) cancels in the Recovery branch of `ProcessUsingSpecial`, after the unchanged grounded Block cancel: a roll (the air dodge when airborne) or a jump from recovery frame 0; a basic attack — hit 1, the Up-Attack or the Down-Air by the direction held at the press, through the same `BasicComboRules.SelectAttackVariant` as Idle — or the movement ability from frame 6. Recovery frames count the cast clock (`RecoveryFramesElapsed`, 0 on the owner's first Recovery tick, paused by hitstop). The verb starts, then the cast is interrupted: its world objects, cooldown and lockout stand, and a basic swing arms the swing lockout as any swing does. A whiff keeps its whole recovery; neither a Special nor the Ultimate is polled there, and Echo Step never is. What arms it, per hero: Einstein E=mc² (not the Rift); Joan both; Leonardo Golden Ratio (not the turret); Lincoln both; Tesla Lorentz Pulse and its arcs (not the coil); Shakespeare Yorick (the Tempest builds no payload); Mozart both; Tubman both (Foresight through its lantern answer); **Cleopatra never** (the nest is a construct, the Vortex ticks). The Mirror clone is a `PlayerController` and inherits it; its Specials arm only on the hero's damage or a full absorption. **Fighter:** none until `DEFER-SIM-SPECIAL-PHASES` closes; the sim must then adopt the same table. **Amended 2026-10-16** by decision 11.1 ("Extend D3 to Fighter: one rule in both modes — a whiff keeps its full recovery; a Special that landed a direct hit cancels into roll/jump from recovery frame 0, into a basic or the movement ability from frame 6."): the "Story-only for now" clause and this row's "**Fighter:** none until `DEFER-SIM-SPECIAL-PHASES` closes" are superseded. The D3 table lives once in `BasicComboRules` (`SpecialRecoveryEvadeCancelFrame` 0, `SpecialRecoveryActionCancelFrame` 6, `SpecialCancelBufferFrames` 6 and the three predicates), `StoryCombatRules` forwards to it, and the sim adopts it (`USER-FIGHTER-SPECIAL-RECOVERY-CANCEL`). In both modes neither cancel may end Foresight while its counter strike is still owed (CM-FIXB-SIM B12; Story's `ProcessUsingSpecial` skips its Recovery branch while `TubmanForesight.IsAnswerPending`), so Foresight's cancel waits one tick after a catch. | User (design amendment) | The master amended (the FSM table :783–785 and :1500) and re-synced; a hands-on pad session confirms the feel. **Amended 2026-10-16:** the master's FSM rows (:783–786) and :1500 state D3 for both modes (`USER-FIGHTER-SPECIAL-RECOVERY-CANCEL`), and a pad session in each mode confirms the feel. |
| **PROVISIONAL-SPECIAL-RECOVERY-CANCEL-RULES** — the details D3 left open | D3 and the addendum give the verbs, the two frame gates, the landed definition and the exclusions only. | *Provisional, agent-chosen:* (1) "Landed" is spec A's D1a predicate (a 0-damage payload never lands), on a `DirectHit` payload only, never a late pooled shot from an earlier cast (the R12 serial); a shape-query hit is stamped from the struck hurtbox by `Credit(struck, ...)`. A hit that lands during recovery is seen on the controller's next tick; the latch is read only in Recovery, so a landed hit never shortens startup or active. (2) Recovery frames count the cast clock and pause in hitstop (the caster freeze of the landed hit included). (3) Same-tick order: Block (existing, grounded), then the buffered verb; the verb starts, then the cast is interrupted. (4) A one-slot buffer of 6 unfrozen controller ticks (`SpecialCancelBufferFrames`): fed on every `UsingSpecial` tick including hitstop ticks, aged only on unfrozen ones, the latest press wins (priority Roll, Jump, Attack, Movement within one tick), cleared on entering and leaving `UsingSpecial`; a verb whose gate is open but which cannot start yet (no air jump, the air dodge spent, Root, a cooldown) stays buffered until it ages out; a whiff never fires it. Roll or Jump pressed in the caster freeze or up to 6 ticks before frame 0 fires at frame 0; an attack or movement press at frames 0–5 fires at frame 6. **Feel risk:** a press made just before a landing can fire a cancel the player no longer wants; without the buffer, presses in the freeze or at frames 0–5 would be silently dropped. (5) **The basic attack's variant is read from the direction held at the press** (Up = the Up-Attack, airborne Down = the Down-Air, otherwise hit 1; grounded Down is the string, as from Idle) and from whether the hero is airborne when it fires; the grab chord (Block held, grounded) is not a basic attack and is neither buffered nor fired (a grounded held Block takes the Block cancel first anyway). (6) Chord guards: a tick whose Ultimate bit is pressed (the gamepad LB+RB chord, recognized from its two buttons whatever the meter) neither fires nor buffers the movement cancel and clears a buffered one, and a Roll on a tick whose Echo Step bit is held (the Block+Roll chord, or a direct bind) never rolls (A06). A same-tick LB+RB chord out of a Special's recovery therefore does nothing. **Corrected at the finalize step (the B3 review; behaviour unchanged):** the guard reads only the tick's Ultimate bit, so (a) a split chord whose LB half arrives a tick before RB is caught only while that LB press is still buffered — before recovery frame 6 it is buffered and then cleared on the RB tick, but at frame 6 or later the LB press fires the movement cancel on its own tick, before RB arrives; and (b) an LB press while Special 2's button (RB) is still held forms the chord and is never taken as a movement cancel, at any meter. (7) The jump cancel refills the jump budget when grounded (as Idle does), spends an air jump when airborne, refreshes the held-Jump read before the jump (so a held Jump rises like an Idle jump; measured equal to within 1 px) and arms no jump buffer. (8) The movement cancel refuses under Time Freeze, Root, the Legacy lock and its cooldown (its bypass included); under Suppression it plays the refusal null-tone once and drops the press. (9) Foresight: its counter enters Recovery itself and the lantern answer lands on the ability's tick, after the controller has read recovery frame 0, so its earliest cancel is frame 1 (a press at frame 0 is buffered); `TubmanForesight.OnInterrupted` now ends the post-counter sidestep, so a cancel out of it — and Package 12 W3's Block cancel, which used to keep the invulnerability by accident — ends the invulnerability. (10) The Mirror clone inherits D3 (orchestrator default). (11) Cleopatra (nest, Vortex) and the Tempest (no payload) never qualify. **Amended 2026-10-16 (decision 11.1):** the constants in (4) — the 6-tick buffer and the frame 0 / frame 6 gates — now live in `BasicComboRules`' D3 section, which `StoryCombatRules` forwards (`SpecialRecoveryCancelTableTests`); these items stay Story's, and the sim's matching choices are `PROVISIONAL-FIGHTER-SPECIAL-RECOVERY-CANCEL`. Item (9): since CM-FIXB-SIM (B12) the Recovery branch is skipped while Foresight's lantern answer is still pending (`TubmanForesight.IsAnswerPending`), so a held Block no longer cancels the counter strike Foresight owes; the answer lands that tick and both cancels open on the next (`StorySpecialRecoveryCancelTests.AHeldBlockWaitsForTheAnswerForesightOwes`). Item (10): the Mirror clone inherits that too. | Unassigned | A ruling on each, or a playtest that leaves them alone. |

## Confirmed open deviations — combat flow (the Story meter scale), 2026-10-11

User-directed Story meter rule from `DECISIONS.md` D5 and the orchestrator's default C for its open question (a Hard Mirror clone keeps the Mozart lane it mirrors), built on `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-11 (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`; committed as `2e9ca79` on 2026-10-12) by spec C's package C2, on top of the meter-rulebook step's `StoryMeterGainFactor` seam (`FIX-MOZART-BUILD-RATE-UNREAD`). The scale is one Story-only constant, `FTT.Combat.StoryCombatRules.MeterGainScale`; nothing under `scripts/FighterSim/`, nor the shared `InfluenceMeterRules`, names it (source-scanned). No component, snapshot, hash, protocol, save-schema or localization change.

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **USER-STORY-METER-SCALE** — the Story Ultimate is harder to get: meter gain ×0.35 (D5) | **User direction 2026-10-11 (D5):** "The Ultimate is harder to get: Story meter gain ×0.35. **Scope:** Story only. The scale applies to all meter **gain**: damage dealt, damage taken, and drained Rally echo. It multiplies with Mozart's build-rate node. **Unchanged:** flat grants and spends. **Fighter is unchanged.** The Level 13 Mirror clone is exempt, because it plays Fighter rules. **Accepted side effect:** Defy History and Echo Step come around less often." **What it supersedes, for Story only:** `design-godot.md` :1268 ("Each **1 HP** of damage dealt … increases the meter by **1.0 point**"), :1269 ("**0.25 points**" per HP taken, and the drained-echo accrual) and the build-rate reading of :2793 (Minor Resonance, now 0.35 × 1.1 on the campaign hero). :1267 (cap 100, start at 0) and every spend are untouched. The design master states 1.0 per HP, so this is a user-directed amendment. | **Built 2026-10-11** (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`; committed as `2e9ca79` on 2026-10-12). `StoryCombatRules.MeterGainScale = 0.35f` and `MeterGainFactor(storyScaled, lane) = (storyScaled ? MeterGainScale : 1) × max(0, lane)`; `PlayerController.AppliesStoryMeterGainScale` (never persisted), set true only by `CharacterFactory`'s campaign-hero branch (`applyStoryProgression && applyLegacyUnlockLocks`: the hub, Levels 0–15, rebuilt heroes, slotless dev and bot launches) and written false on the Level 13 Mirror clone; the existing `PlayerController.StoryMeterGainFactor` is now `StoryCombatRules.MeterGainFactor(AppliesStoryMeterGainScale, StoryUltimateBuildRateMultiplier)`, read by `UltimateMeter`'s two gain paths through `InfluenceMeterRules.GainFromDamageDealt` / `GainFromDamageTaken` and by `RallyUncreditedMeter`. The campaign hero earns 0.35 meter per HP dealt and 0.0875 per HP taken (echo included as it drains); with Mozart's node 0.385 / 0.09625. Flat grants (Shield of Orléans +5, the MeterBoost orb, the Level 0 lesson's direct set), the durable restore and every spend are unscaled; the cap stays 100. Fighter, the sim, `FighterMeterRules`, the CPU and every Fighter hash are untouched. **Measured** (same build, the new diagnostic `--story-meter-scale=off` against the default `on`; Levels 5, 7 and 11, Joan and Mozart; slotless bot runs, so no lane): Normal (`cd5off` → `cd5on`, `play`/`spam`/`basics`, 18 runs each): `play` median clean bar fill 12.0 → 34.6 s (2.9×), Ultimates 6.5 → 2.83 per run, Defy procs 0.67 → 0.17 per run, `gain/min` 223 → 106 (0.47×); `spam` bar fill 19.9 → 51.4 s (2.6×); `basics` 21.5 → 71.1 s (3.3×). Easy `play` (`cd5offE` → `cd5onE`, 6 runs each): bar fill 14.1 → 42.3 s (3.0×), Ultimates 4.83 → 2.0 per run, `gain/min` 217 → 110 (0.51×). `gain/min` does not fall to 0.35× because it is a rate of observed rises per tracked minute: `spam` and `basics` never spend the bar and sit at the cap for 66–78 % of their frames with the scale off (36–38 % with it on), so their `gain/min` is cap-limited and barely moved (63.9 → 63.9, 53.2 → 49.6); and the `play` bot, casting fewer Ultimates (whose damage earns nothing, D03h), deals more meter-earning damage. The exact per-HP rate is pinned by the unit suites, not by the bots. In the tree at measurement: groups A (D1/D1a/D2/D10 and the freeze buffer) and B (D3/D4/D8) and the C1/C3 telemetry; not groups D or E. Output kept in `absolum_research/flow/C2-story-meter-scale.batches.txt`. **After every combat-flow group** (2026-10-12, `absolum_research/flow/bots_after.md`, scale on, seeds 0–3 pooled, against the pre-A, pre-D5 `bots_before.md`): Normal `play` clean bar fill 14.6 → 38.2 s (2.6×), Ultimates 7.08 → 2.75 per run, Defy procs 0.54 → 0.46 per run; Easy `play` 14.0 → 39.5 s (2.8×), Ultimates 4.92 → 1.79 per run. The fill rises a little less than a pure ×0.35 would (2.86×) because D1, D2 and D4 raise the meter-earning damage. Suites: `StoryMeterGainScaleTests` (8), `InfluenceMeterSourceScanTests.TheStoryMeterScaleLivesInStoryCombatRulesAndNeverReachesTheSimOrTheSharedRulebook`, `MirrorParadoxTests.TheCloneIsExemptFromTheStoryMeterScaleAndKeepsItsMirroredLane`; in-place expectation changes in `StoryRallyTests`, `StoryEnvironmentalDamageTests`, `StoryDefenceOrderTests`, `PlaceholderZoneTests`, `StoryFsmM04TransitionTests`, `PuzzleEnvironmentToolkitTests`, `Level09ContentTests` and `StoryMeterGainFactorTests` (a campaign hero's gain now reads its own `StoryMeterGainFactor`; a fill through a gain became a direct set). **Amended 2026-10-16 (decision 7.3, `USER-STORY-ECHO-STEP-COST`):** "spends are unchanged" has one exception: the campaign hero's Echo Step costs `StoryCombatRules.StoryEchoStepMeterCost` = 30 × 0.35 = 10.5, while Fighter, the drills, the Holodeck, the Level 13 clone and a `--story-meter-scale=off` bot run pay 30. | User (design amendment) | (1) The GDD master (`design-godot-v7.md`) amended at the lines cited and re-synced to the mirror (this file does not edit the mirror). (2) The measured row above recorded — done, 2026-10-11. (3) A hands-on session confirms the bar time reads right. |
| **PROVISIONAL-STORY-METER-SCALE-RULES** — the details D5 left open | D5 gives only the scale, the three gain sources, the Mozart multiplication, the Mirror exemption and the unchanged flat grants and spends. | *Provisional, agent-chosen (spec C §1.1, §2 and the orchestrator's default C):* (1) **The campaign hero** is any build on `CharacterFactory`'s `applyStoryProgression && applyLegacyUnlockLocks` branch: the hub, Level 0, Level 1, Levels 2–15, heroes rebuilt by an Anchor Snap, a Collapse resume or an ordinary load, and slotless dev or bot launches. No D5 scale: the Mirror clone on every tier, the Calibration Drills, the Holodeck, the Test Arena and Fighter proxies. (2) **One flat value on every difficulty** (difficulty already scales mob HP and enemy damage, which feed meter as HP removed). (3) **The Mozart lane** multiplies all three gains on the campaign hero (0.385 per HP dealt). A non-campaign build keeps its lane with no scale; only the Hard Mirror clone can carry one (F20 mirrors purchased perks), so a Hard clone of a Mozart save earns 1.1 per HP, as `StoryMeterGainFactorTests` and the new Mirror case pin (orchestrator default C: "the 0.35 scale applies only to the campaign hero"). (4) **The uncredited Rally record** (F10 attempt state, `StoryPlayerResourceTimers.RallyUncreditedMeter`) is stored in the hero's scaled units and settled unscaled on load; a record written before this change settles once at the old basis. These are development saves only, so no save derivation is declared. (5) **Flat sources stay flat:** Shield of Orléans +5, the MeterBoost pickup, the Level 0 lesson's direct set and the durable restore; spends are unchanged. (6) **No rounding and no HUD change:** the meter stays a float, and the `UltimateReady` cue, the gold ring and the Defy seal fire on the same thresholds, later. (7) **The Mirror CPU's read of the hero's meter** (`TargetInfluenceRaw`) is unchanged; only the Hard shield-orb heuristic reads it, and Level 13 spawns no orbs. (8) **The Eraser's Siphon Snare** is refused at a hero meter ≤ 0.0001 (`EnemyAbilityExecutor.IsSiphonEligible`) and its tether breaks on `MeterEmpty` (`SiphonTetherChannel`): at ×0.35 it is committed less often (never at a fresh level start, where the meter is 0) and breaks sooner, while each point it drains costs about 2.9× as much dealt damage to win back; its 10/s rate and 30-point cap are unchanged, and like the Extractor's −20 it weighs about 2.9× more against bar fill. (9) **Diagnostics:** the playtest runner's `--story-meter-scale=on\|off` (default on) clears the flag on every new hero instance when off, so a slotless bot runs at the pre-D5 rate for a same-build A/B; the report carries `story_meter_scale`, the combat summary `meter_scale`, `compare_runs.py`'s "meter and presses" table a `scale` column and the batch CSV a `meter_scale` column. (10) **Orchestrator defaults (`DECISIONS.md` addendum C):** the `play` bot's Ultimate policy is unchanged (it taps every other frame with a full bar), and no slotted chained-run bot option was built (bot runs stay slotless, so `meter.level_start` is 0 by construction). **Amended 2026-10-16:** item (5)'s "spends are unchanged" now excepts the campaign hero's Echo Step, 10.5 (decision 7.3, `USER-STORY-ECHO-STEP-COST`); the Ultimate and every other spend are unchanged. | Unassigned | A ruling on each, or a playtest that leaves them alone. |

## Confirmed open deviations — combat flow (spacing and walls), 2026-10-11

User-directed Story spacing rules from `DECISIONS.md` D6 and D9, built from spec D (spacing and walls) in two sub-passes. **Sub-pass (i), D6b** (the Story aerial string reach) was built on `main`'s working tree on 2026-10-11 (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`; committed as `2e9ca79` on 2026-10-12), on top of the reach-geometry step's one grounded string table (`FIX-STRING-BOX-ONE-TABLE`) and its Story helper `PlayerController.ResolveGroundStringBoxPixels`. It is **Story-only**: nothing under `scripts/FighterSim/` was touched, and no component, snapshot field, save field, schema version, protocol or translation key changed; no Fighter hash moves. Suite: the new `StoryAerialStringReachTests` (2). It closes the reach-geometry step's `VERIFY-STORY-AERIAL-STRING-BOXES` (above). **Sub-pass (ii), D6a / D6c / D9** (the finisher carry, mob melee range, the wall bounce) was built on the same working tree on 2026-10-11, after group C's telemetry (C1, C3) and the `bots-before` batches, with the user's post-spec addendum applied: **elites also get the shorter finisher carry** and **thrown standard mobs wall-bounce like launched ones**. It is Story-only in the same sense (no file under `scripts/FighterSim/` changed; the one `BasicComboRules` addition, `WidestStringReachWidthPercent`, is a read-only int nothing in the sim reads; no component, snapshot, save, protocol, hash or translation key moved). Suites: `StoryEnemyCombatRulesTests` +3 (pure), the new `StoryFinisherCarryTests` (6) and `StoryWallBounceTests` (6), `EnemyControllerTests` +6 and `EnemyRosterContentTests` +1, so +22: the spec's +20 with the addendum's elite-carry and thrown-bounce cases as two more. Four `EnemyControllerTests` cases changed in place for D6c (no count change): the stand-off shuffle reads `StandOffResumePixels`, the pressure-exit target moved 60 → 50 px, and the stand-off halt and cadence cases assert `StandOffEngagePixels` / `CommitRangePixels`.

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **USER-STORY-AERIAL-REACH** — the Story aerial string boxes take the grounded reach, front-only (D6b) | **User direction 2026-10-11 (D6b):** "Story air attacks. Lengthen the Story aerial string boxes to match the sim's aerial reach, which reuses the grounded boxes: far edge 1.6 / 1.7 / 2.1 u × width%. Make them front-only (no reach behind the hero). Keep their vertical coverage. Put the table in `BasicComboRules` beside the grounded table. Close the ledger row the bug batch added." This supersedes `design-godot.md` `:1089` (F09's "shorter reach" for aerials, now true in neither mode) and `:1099`–`:1101` (aerial boxes placed from a per-frame `Marker2D`), and satisfies `:1082` (aerial hitboxes authored in `BasicComboRules`) for the vertical span and `:1375` (front-only). | `PlayerController.ResolveAerialStringBoxPixels`: an aerial string hit's **x-span is its grounded twin's**, from `ResolveGroundStringBoxPixels` unchanged (the profile's width % and the Story AttackRange minor scale it exactly as they scale the grounded box); its **vertical span is Story's pre-D6b aerial span**, now authored in `BasicComboRules.AerialStringBoxCentreYUnits` (centre −46 / −50 / −12 px from the feet, Y down) and `AerialStringBoxHeightUnits` (64 / 82 / 104 px, × `ReachHeightPercent` × the minor about the centre), so template coverage stays hit 1 −78 … −14, hit 2 −91 … −9, hit 3 −64 … +40 px. `OnAttackActiveStarted` reads the facing at active start, as for the grounded box; `AerialComboHitboxSizes` / `AerialComboHitboxOffsets`, the `_aerialHitboxMarker` field, its swing-start write in `StartComboHit` and the factory's `AerialHitboxMarker` node are deleted. Aerial far edges h1 / h2 / h3, before → after (hit-3 near edge): Lincoln (85 %) 79.9 / 70.6 / 54.4 → 81.6 / 86.7 / 107.1 px (−20.4 → +15.3); Joan (90) 84.6 / 74.7 / 57.6 → 86.4 / 91.8 / 113.4 (−21.6 → +16.2); Tesla, Tubman (100) 94 / 83 / 64 → 96 / 102 / 126 (−24 → +18); Mozart, Shakespeare (105) 98.7 / 87.2 / 67.2 → 100.8 / 107.1 / 132.3 (−25.2 → +18.9); Einstein (110) 103.4 / 91.3 / 70.4 → 105.6 / 112.2 / 138.6 (−26.4 → +19.8); Leonardo (115) 108.1 / 95.5 / 73.6 → 110.4 / 117.3 / 144.9 (−27.6 → +20.7); Cleopatra (120) 112.8 / 99.6 / 76.8 → 115.2 / 122.4 / 151.2 (−28.8 → +21.6). The Level 13 Mirror clone's Story aerial strings lengthen the same way (116 / 122 / 146 px against the hero's 20 px half-width, template), matching the sim reach its Fighter CPU table assumes; no CPU code changed. D6b's rule lives on in it: the air strike's box (`PlayerController.ResolveAirAttackBoxPixels`) is string hit 1's x-span from `ResolveGroundStringBoxPixels(0, …)`, front-only, with the facing read at active start, and the retired aerial hit 3's vertical span (−64 … +40 px about the feet, `BasicComboRules.AirAttackBoxCentreYUnits` / `AirAttackBoxHeightUnits`), so a jump-in reaches a grounded foe. `ResolveAerialStringBoxPixels`, `AerialStringBoxCentreYUnits` / `AerialStringBoxHeightUnits` and the aerial frame arrays are deleted. `StoryAerialStringReachTests` keeps its name and its turned-in-startup case, and its first case now pins the air strike's box (`TheAirStrikeTakesHitOnesXSpanInFrontAndReachesBelowTheFeet`).
