its exit runs from the sky down to the east cellar wall's top, y 1180, so the tiring-house stair stays inside, and the traps keep cycling) and galleries 6080–8330 (the bridging span is the exit); **the audience's stillness clock pauses while a lock is engaged** (`AudienceHeldByEncounterLock`; an armed throw still resolves); the upper-tier slasher (7940, 370) and the middle-tier prompt-boy (7000, 610) → the yard floor (7400, 6900), the floor prompt-boy 5960 → 6300; the Middle 5400 → 6040 (a Middle resume marks the galleries reached, FIX-LEVEL) and the PreBoss 8420 → 8460; triggers 2560 → 2600 and 8350 → 8390; no gate column. **L11:** Seminary Ridge 300–3200, Wheatfield 3220–6090 (requires array Alpha, so a run cannot leave Alpha standing behind the Angle's closed entry; a Middle resume breaks it) and the Angle 6400–8890 (the ridge line pre-placed, the stone wall pair at "alive at most 1", then the Cyber-Cavalry Commander at "alive at most 1"; requires Alpha and Bravo; exit at the breastwork, y 440–900, which still opens on the arrays alone); artillery stays live; six spawns and one patrol moved out of cover; the Middle 6250 → 6340 and the PreBoss 9060 → 9080; triggers 3200 → 3260 and 6420 → 6460; GU-C's 340 px railcut perch kept. **L12–L15:** three approach locks each, every area full height; **the pre-boss garrisons fold into the last approach lock** as an `AfterPreviousCleared` second wave, because the aprons (1216 / 900 / 350 px on Levels 12 / 14 / 15) are narrower than a lock (moving a boss arena's west edge instead needs a ruling); **the salted hunters became manifest sources** — the Erasers of Levels 12–15 and Level 14's Chrono-Warden appended to their rows, at elite weight within unchanged pools (25 / 35 / 35 / 35), Easy's prefix still dropping each. L12: tranquility 320–3000, vent field 3020–5860 (on engage), relay spire 6220–7540 (GO Up; it ends in the mast-head / ferry gap so the cache cul-de-sac stays an optional detour, after a first cut over the whole floor cost the bot four deaths there); the spire lift held at its bottom stop until the spire clears (`HoldMoverWhileLockHolds`), the ferry never; the dead-end troopers moved onto the route, wave 4 off the pad, the Eraser 9900 → 6560. L13: threshold 320–3200, drift 3220–5100, approach 5500–6900 (on engage, with the Eraser pair; the y-limited pre-arena trigger, which could be ridden over, is deleted); the Titanic-deck and Globe-stage shards held at their west stops while their locks hold; the `level_13.eraser_pair` line plays on the approach's engage only when the prefix fields an Eraser; the phantoms onto the shelf (y 1850); the Erasers (6900, 940 mid-air; 7180 beside the Mirror seam) → (5780, 1850) and (6820, 1850); the Middle 5180 → 5290; the font 6850 → 6970. L14: breach gallery 320–2900, containment wing 2920–5782 (its exit stands on the bulkhead's top, GO Up), fabrication spine 6640–9030; **a corridor grid pauses while its lock is engaged** and re-arms on its authored phases at the clear or a revert (`GridPausedBy`); wave 4 into the spine (the drone at 10450, inside the arena, → 8620; the Eraser 10150 → 8300); the PreBoss 9180 → 9220. L15: portico 320–3000, stacks 3020–5900, collapsing hall 6420–7900 (the rotunda guard sallies as its second wave); **the firestorm starts on the hall's clear** (`PROVISIONAL-ESCAPE-RULES`); wave 4 out of the arena (the Eraser 10420 → 7180). Lock widths run from 1280 px (Level 5's boat deck, Level 8's tomb) to 3200 px (Level 7's quay, Level 9's Lantern Watch). **Recorded, not fixed:** nobody has judged the pacing (the 8–10-member folds; Level 13's approach ambush, five members on engage and three of them elites, where a gentler stagger needs a ruling because a two-entry row's Easy prefix is 1; Level 7's 2700 px boarding lock with its ramp crew waiting at the water; the flood timing; the head starts; the cell-B rioter pressing against its cell); Act III Resonance Hold nodes inside lock areas (Level 13 5980/940; Level 14 4070/730 and 8990/610) can be channelled mid-fight; a high-mobility hero might reach Level 12's mast head without the lift (a dead end until the clear); Level 10's audience still runs in the boss arena; the Galleries' lower-tier slasher stays on its tier; the Level 4, 6 and 8 puzzle props still revert cosmetically on a rewind after the solve (`VERIFY-LOCK-PUZZLE-PROPS-REWIND`). | Unassigned | A hands-on run of each level confirming the layouts read, and rulings on the recorded items. `EncounterLockContentTests`, `AnchorApproachContentTests` and the per-level suites pin today's layouts. |
| **PROVISIONAL-LOCK-MEMBER-REACH** — keeping engaged members within the hero's reach (FIX-ENEMY) | The plan's content rule: "every member spawn stands on a floor inside its `Area`, reachable by the slowest hero … (no posted-on-a-ledge-only mobs)", and "the fall net is a backstop, not a fix". The Phase C bot batch (`guc*`, 180 runs) found locks a human must also clear held shut by flyers out of reach, a thrown flyer, members posted on ledges and Level 5's ramps. | *Provisional, agent-chosen (FIX-ENEMY, 2026-10-13):* (1) **The flyer hover cap:** `EncounterLockRules.FlyerHoverCapPixels` 160 — an engaged flying member never keeps its feet (its hurtbox bottom) more than 160 px above the hero's feet in Chase, Patrol, Returning or Idle (waiting on the ring, holding its ranged band or waiting for a permit, resting, the stand-off band); above the line it descends at up to its own chase speed and lands on it (`FlyerHoverLineY`, `FlyerSettleVelocityY`; `EnemyController.SettleEncounterFlyer`, one hook after the AI state switch), at or below it its own vertical speed stands; attacks, stuns and dormancy keep their own hover; flyers outside locks are unchanged. Every hero's air strike reaches a flyer held at the cap (weakest, Lincoln's single jump: 132.6 + 71.8 = 204.4 px against a 160 px hurtbox bottom, a 44 px margin; `EncounterMemberBehaviourTests.EveryHeroReachesAFlyerHeldAtTheHoverCap`). Before, a drone held its ranged band straight over the hero (Level 14's at 292 px, over Lincoln's 132 px jump). (2) **The view top:** `ApplyEncounterBounds` stops a member flyer's body at the top of its lock's camera rect (`ClampFlyerFeetY`; the rect read at `JoinEncounter`), zeroing upward speed; the bottom of the band stays the 240 px fall net. (3) **Thrown flyers**, every flyer, member or not: `ProcessThrownFlight` brakes a thrown flyer at `StoryEnemyCombatRules.StunnedAirDragPixelsPerSecondSquared` (600 px/s²) and ends the flight at rest, so its throw stun runs out in the hover (before, a flyer kept the throw's velocity forever: a Level 13 phantom thrown by Joan rose from y 1899 to −3455). (4) **The ledge step-off:** an engaged, non-dormant **melee** ground member whose target's feet are more than `StoryEnemyCombatRules.LedgeProbeDepthPixels` (64) below its own (`EncounterLockRules.SeeksWayDown`) never holds its stand-off band over the hero, and walks off a ledge when it stands on a floor, no wall is ahead, the landing keeps it inside the area (`StepOffLandsInArea`) and the chase's edge probe finds floor within `RelocationRules.FloorSearchDepthPixels` (480); the way down is latched (toward a target more than 24 px aside, else the facing) and flips at a wall or a bottomless drop (`ResolveEncounterWayDownSign`, `TurnAtEncounterDeadEnd`); waiters on the ring step off the same way; ranged members keep their posts; outside a lock P4 is unchanged. (5) **The watchdog's destination:** `EncounterLockRules.RelocatesBesideHero` — a stalled member within `StallAnchorTolerancePixels` (16) of its anchor, or whose anchor's feet stand more than 200 px above the hero's, is put down beside the hero (`EnemyController.RelocateBesideEncounterHero`: its own side first, at the director's rank-0 gap for the difficulty, 200 / 170 / 150 px, inside the area, swept by `SafeRelocation` out from the hero's feet with a floor needed under a ground member, never into pushbox contact, the other side next, the anchor as the fallback; logged `[encounter-lock] … put down beside the hero`); otherwise it goes back to its anchor; it is never killed. (6) **The flyer stall:** `IsFlyerStalledOutOfReach` counts a flyer above its hover line, moving under 4 px and not in a knockback flight as stalled whatever its attack, the line taken from the hero's last floor-standing feet (`EncounterLock._watchdogHeroFloorFeetY`), so Level 3's drone resting on the lamp gantry over the hero is put down beside it after 10 s. (7) **The retaliation exception, telemetry only:** a melee-class attacker near the hero that holds no slot is retaliating (the V7.4 pressure exit, never gated); the bots now report `melee_retaliating_near_max` and `frames_gated_melee_over_slots` (the turn-taking invariant, which should be 0) beside the unchanged `frames_melee_over_slots`. On Level 13 Easy and Normal (39 and 49 over-slot frames) every extra attacker was a struck waiter's pressure exit (the guard elite's `blink_rush` on Easy, an Eraser on Normal; none on Hard), and no holder count ever exceeded the slots. (8) **Level 5's listing ramps meet their decks:** all five were authored by centre, width and angle and none met its decks (risers of 18.7 / 18.7 / 26.9 / 38.5 / 41.3 px under the upper deck's west face, and corners 0.5–1.6 px proud of a deck that the chase reads as a wall), which stopped every chasing mob (the bots' Level 5 "staircase" stall) and made the hero jump; they are now authored by their joints (`Level05Controller.ListingRampJoints`, `BuildListingRamp(lowJoint, highJoint)`: low joints 800 / 2000 / 4280 / 5230 / 7150, high joints at the deck corners `DeckD1WestX` 1100, `DeckD2WestX` 2300, `DeckE1WestX` 4600, `DeckE2WestX` 5500 and `BoatDeckWestX` 7500, with 16 px of buried overlap at the low end), steepening 12 / 12 / 13 / 13 / 16° → 14.9 / 14.9 / 17.4 / 20.3 / 21.8°, footprints and spawns unchanged; since `4ca4280` the builder is the shared `StoryLevelControllerBase.BuildRampBetweenJoints`, which Level 7's flagship ramps use too (`VERIFY-L07-ARENA-RAMP-RISERS`, closed). (9) **Investigated, no game change:** the Level 6 Hard legionnaire that took no damage stood behind the vault's basalt boulder (a 56 px `WeightedObject`), where the M1 player-melee line of sight refuses every basic; a human jumps it, so it was a bot gap (FIX-BOT's walk-in rule). **Recorded, not fixed:** a member stalled 10 s is moved visibly (a teleport); Level 7's ranged `laser_pistol_deckhand` posted on the launch yard keeps its post by design (a human climbs to it, and since FIX-BOT so does the bot); a solid platform right over where the hero fights a flyer makes the flyer wait 10 s on it; Level 7's boss-arena ramps (`VERIFY-L07-ARENA-RAMP-RISERS`). | Unassigned | A ruling on each, or a hands-on pass that leaves them alone. `EncounterMemberRulesTests`, `EncounterMemberBehaviourTests` and `Level05ListingRampTests` pin today's rules. |
| **PROVISIONAL-LOCK-OBJECTIVE-LATCH** — a lock-required objective, once met, stays met (FIX-OBJECTIVE) | Plan §2.3: "Cleared latches (a later puzzle reset never re-closes it)" — but the clear itself read each required objective live. The Phase B review found the defect four times (Florence, Chicago, Hakata Bay, Gettysburg). | *Provisional, agent-chosen (FIX-OBJECTIVE, 2026-10-13).* **The defect:** a death rewind restores every `PuzzleManager`, `DamageableEnvironmentObject` and puzzle prop to the last checkpoint's snapshot (`StoryRewindPolicy.RestoreCheckpointState`); Phase B moved every anchor out of the lock fights and FIX-E holds a fracture past an uncleared lock, so that snapshot always predates the solve, and a rewind could un-meet an objective the lock read live, leaving the barrier shut (from a stale objective with a hidden re-solve, Florence and Chicago, to hard traps: Level 9's re-cut relay could never set its condition again, and Level 11's Alpha came back behind the Angle's closed entry). (1) **The latch:** from its first tick until it clears, Armed or Engaged, held or not, a lock latches each required puzzle (solved live or persisted) and breakable (destroyed or freed) the first time it reads it met, and never reads it again (`EncounterLock.LatchRequiredObjectives`, run in `Tick` before the containment check); the hard cap reads the latch; the read surface `RequiredObjectivesMet`, `IsRequiredPuzzleMet(id)` and `IsRequiredBreakableMet(breakable)` is side-effect free (latched, or met now; true for a Cleared lock or an object the lock does not require). Latching from Armed matters: Level 11's Alpha falls in the Wheatfield while the Angle is still Armed. (2) **Per instance, never saved:** every reload rebuilds the lock, which latches a persisted solve or a resume-broken breakable (Level 11's `ForceArrayDestroyed`, Level 2's generator) on its first tick. (3) **The world half:** on the latch the lock switches the objective's `PuzzleManager` or breakable to `PreserveCurrentState`, so a lock-required objective is one-way for the rest of the scene (and the save keeps the completion: `RestoreCheckpointState` used to write `SetPuzzleCompleted(false)`); a lock must never require a puzzle that legitimately resets on a rewind (none does today). (4) **Props stay the level's:** Florence keeps its gear puzzle and both gears from the solve (`Level01Controller.OnGearPuzzleSolved`, also run for a completion the save restores); Chicago keeps the beam puzzle and its three coils (`Level03Controller.KeepBeamRoutedThroughRewinds`); Hakata Bay keeps the feed puzzle from build and each relay from its cut, and sets the line's condition even under an already-dark lantern (`OnFeedRelayCut`). (5) Level 9's `FeedsCut` counts lines whose condition newly flipped, never a repeated cut. (6) **A level's puzzle objective reads `EncounterLock.IsRequiredPuzzleMet`**, never the puzzle alone, so it agrees with the barrier (Florence's gears and Chicago's reroute objectives). Coverage: of the 48 locks nine require something, and all nine are covered (`EncounterLockObjectiveContentTests.EveryLockRequiredObjectiveStaysMetOnceItsLockHasSeenItMet` sweeps all sixteen scenes; four real-frames cases land a death rewind inside the engaged lock on Florence, Chicago, Hakata Bay and Gettysburg on Hard); Level 11 needed no level change. | Unassigned | A ruling, or a hands-on pass that leaves it alone. `EncounterLockObjectiveLatchTests` and `EncounterLockObjectiveContentTests` pin it; the cosmetic leftovers are `VERIFY-LOCK-PUZZLE-PROPS-REWIND`. |
| **PROVISIONAL-ANCHOR-APPROACH** — every Middle and PreBoss anchor is reachable before the next lock engages (FIX-LEVEL) | The plan (§2.3 content rules) keeps anchors out of lock areas; FIX-E holds an anchor past an uncleared lock. The Phase B review found Level 7's Middle anchor, under the solid receiving yard, unreachable before the boarding lock engaged, so the boarding fight had no checkpoint. | *Provisional, agent-chosen (FIX-LEVEL decision 7, 2026-10-13, with FIX-E's hold and swing clearance).* For every live Middle and PreBoss anchor (`AnchorApproachContentTests`, all sixteen scenes): its footprint (x ± 30) lies outside every lock area; the **next lock** is the one whose area starts at or east of the footprint, its engage line `Area.X` + 20 (the hero's half-width) + `EngageInsetPixels`; from the anchor a search walks its standing surface (the nearest standable top within 60 px below) both ways until a wall in the hero's 64 px body band, the surface's end or the engage line, and succeeds where the walked stretch has an open column (nothing solid over the hero's head; one-way platforms, moving bodies, lock barriers, doors, forcefields and breakables are never ceilings or walls) or reaches within 40 px of the previous lock's area (or the spawn); otherwise it moves on to surfaces the hero could have come from — onward at the same height (±2 px) across a seam, down from a higher surface whose edge (± the half-width) is over the stretch or through a one-way one, up from a lower surface within the roster's weakest double-jump rise beside either end, or through this surface if it is one-way; rect shapes only (a rotated shape reads as a walkable ramp); no ropes, swings, movement abilities, Down-Air through a floor or air drift, because a checkpoint must be reachable by ordinary means. With Level 7's Middle at (5650, 672) on the receiving yard (`Level07Controller.CheckpointPlacements[1]`, `ReceivingYardEastX` 5950, the yard's run over the hull added to `SolidDeckSpans`) every level passes, and no level deliberately differs; the suite's second case refuses the old (5700, 880). **Limits:** a coarse rectangle graph that can pass an anchor a nav graph would refuse (an open column over a stretch nothing leads into), with no ropes, moving lifts or polygon collision. **By design, recorded:** an anchor a hero walks past without striking is refused from inside the next lock until that lock clears (the guard's "hero inside an uncleared lock" half); the measured swing clearances run from 14 px (Level 8's Middle and PreBoss, Levels 9 and 10's PreBoss) to 394 px (Level 6's PreBoss), and several Middle anchors stand 40–80 px short of the next lock's area (Level 5's, and every Middle in Levels 8–11), so a hero, or a bot, that runs on engages the next lock before striking (the Level 10 bot looped on exactly this); the PreBoss anchors stand past every lock, where the risk is running on to the boss (the bots did on Levels 5, 9 and 15); Level 7's rope overshoot is `VERIFY-L07-ROPE-OVERSHOOT-BOARDING-ENGAGE`. | Unassigned | A ruling, or a hands-on pass confirming each anchor is found before its next fight. `AnchorApproachContentTests` pins the rule. |
| **PROVISIONAL-ESCAPE-RULES** — the Level 6 and Level 15 escapes under locks | Plan §5: Level 6's Ash Road enemies are fought in lock(s) before the lava front, which starts when the last road lock clears; Level 15's hall lock(s) clear before the firestorm, which starts on the last clear. The fronts' × 0.8 speeds are `USER-MOVEMENT-SPEED-CUT`'s. | *Provisional, agent-chosen (GU-L4 decision 9, GU-L12 decision 7, FIX-LEVEL decisions 1–3):* (1) **The escapes start on a lock's clear**, never on entering its area: Level 6's lava front on the Ash Road lock's clear from Engaged (`BeginEscape()`; the road room trigger no longer starts it or spawns the crowd), Level 15's firestorm on the collapsing hall's clear (`OnCollapsingHallLockStateChanged`, which ignores a lock restored Cleared; `BeginFirestorm(frontStartX)`), so the hero is never shut in a lock with the front at its back. (2) **The head start:** the front starts `Level06Controller.EscapeHeadStartPixels` 240 (about one second at 224 px/s) or `Level15Controller.FirestormHeadStartPixels` 432 (2.0 s at 216 px/s) behind the hero **wherever it stands** — since FIX-LEVEL only the end clamps (`EscapeStartFor(heroX)` = min(heroX − 240, `EscapeEndX` 8800); `FirestormStartBehind(heroX)` = min(heroX − 432, `EscapeEndX` 9000)), because the authored starts (5700, 6500) lie inside the clearing lock, and flooring the front there put a hero who took the last kill near the entry inside the front's zone; a front may start west of its authored start (Level 6 down to about 5440, Level 15 to about 6008). Level 15's run is now the 7900 → 9100 stretch past the shelves and the third pylon; Level 6's second civilian waits at 8700 in the run, so the rescue still costs the lead. (3) **The finish line:** `EscapeSequenceController.AdvanceFront` completes the run on the first running tick its `Runner` (both levels set it to the hero; unset, the scene's first `StoryPlayer`) stands at or past `FinishX`, the front parked at `EndX` included; the 96 px `FinishTrigger` is only the visible line; a dead runner past the line also completes it. (4) **A death rewind restarts the front** behind the landing (`min(StartX, landing − head start)`; both escapes reset with `ResetToInitialState`, Level 15's `Firestorm` node set to it in the scene; `Level06Controller.OnStoryRewind`, `Level15Controller.OnStoryRewind`), **unless the run is already won**: a level-side latch (`Level06Controller.EscapeSurvived`, `Level15Controller.FirestormSurvived`, set on completion or a resume past the run) re-completes it instead, so a death in the caldera or the rotunda never restarts the fire. **Recorded:** Level 6's authored 90 px head start is gone; nobody has judged whether 240 / 432 px read as a race. | Unassigned | A ruling, or a hands-on pass of both runs. `Level06ContentTests` and `Level15ContentTests` pin the rules. |
| ~~**FIX-L15-ESCAPE-NEVER-COMPLETED**~~ — a walking hero never completed an escape run (Levels 15 and 6; fixed 2026-10-12 and 2026-10-13) | An escape is won by reaching its finish line (`design-godot.md` :275 for Level 15's firestorm; the Level 6 lava run). | **Fixed.** The escape template's `FinishTrigger` is 96 px wide and centred on `FinishX`; a walking hero entered it 20–70 px short of the line (Level 6 at about 8812 against 8880), `NotifyPlayerReachedFinish` refused, and `BodyEntered` never fired again on the way through, while the front parked at `EndX` (Level 6 8800, Level 15 9000), short of the line. So neither run was ever completed, only halted (Level 15's by entering the rotunda), and the `play` bot idled past each line in its `escape` task. GU-L12 fixed Level 15 with a level-side `FrontAdvanced` hook (run `gul12c`); the Phase B review found Level 6 unfixed (review items 3 and 14; FIX-BOT's `L06_joan_play_normal_fxbot2` idled at x 8953); FIX-LEVEL replaced both with one rule in `EscapeSequenceController.AdvanceFront` (`PROVISIONAL-ESCAPE-RULES` (3)) and removed the Level 15 hook. Pinned by `Level06ContentTests.AHeroWalkingPastTheFinishLineCompletesTheLavaRun` and `Level15ContentTests.TheFirestormStartsOnTheHallLocksClearBehindTheHeroNotBefore` (rewritten so the trigger itself cannot complete the run, review item 10). FIX-BOT asked for a `VERIFY-*` row for the Level 6 half; it is recorded here, closed. The bot now goes on past `FinishX` + 20 with a nav note ("still running with the hero past its finish"), so a recurrence stays visible. | Unassigned | Closed. Reopen if a bot run's nav notes show an escape still running past its finish. |
| ~~**VERIFY-L15-ESCAPE-REWIND-HALT**~~ — a death mid-firestorm stopped it for the rest of the attempt (closed 2026-10-13 by FIX-LEVEL) | A death rewind during an escape resumes the escape. | **Closed by FIX-LEVEL.** Named in `GU_L12.md` but never written into this ledger until now. The `Firestorm` node used the default `RestoreCheckpointState`; its front starts on the hall's clear, between the Middle and PreBoss anchors, so no checkpoint could capture it running, and every death rewind parked it stopped at the Middle anchor's capture (6500) with `FirestormTriggered` still latched (review item 15). Now the node is `ResetToInitialState` and the level restarts the front behind the landing, or re-completes a run already survived (`PROVISIONAL-ESCAPE-RULES` (4)). Level 6 had the same dead read — its `OnStoryRewind` guarded on `_escape.IsCompleted`, which the reset always cleared first, so a death after the run restarted the lava behind the hero — and got the same fix. Pinned by `Level15ContentTests.ADeathRewindMidRunRestartsTheFirestormBehindTheLandingButNeverOnceItIsSurvived` and `Level06ContentTests.ADeathRewindRestartsTheLavaBehindTheLandingButNeverOnceTheRunIsWon`. | Unassigned | Closed. |

| **PROVISIONAL-SPEED-CUT-RULES** — the details request 4 left open | The request gives "about 20%" for speed and jump distance and "unchanged" for height. | *Provisional, agent-chosen (plan §2.4; GU-C decisions 1–6):* (1) **Exactly × 0.8** (one decimal), not the 2026-08-10 rounding to 0.25, which would have cut 18.5–21 %. (2) **The roll keeps its distance:** `RollSpeedMultiplier` 1.875 (= 1.5 / 0.8, exact in binary, so its `FP64.FromDouble` is exact), read at all four roll sites (Story travel and get-up, sim travel and get-up). **The reason, corrected** (GU-C §4): the plan said 1.875 keeps Lincoln's Story roll-through and sim cross-up, but measured from pushbox contact with the multiplier left at 1.5 both still pass (Story ends 46 px past a `chrono_slasher`'s centre, the sim 0.600 u past), so the compensation keeps the distance and every margin (72 px and 0.706 u with 1.875), not the pass-through itself. **Side effect, accepted:** the 4-frame stop ramp after a Story roll and after a roll get-up (both modes) scales with the cut run speed while the roll speed did not drop, so the trailing slide grows (Story roll total Lincoln 103.3 → 108.8 px, Joan 168.6 → 177.5; Story get-up slide Lincoln 17.8 → 23.3 px; sim get-up slide Lincoln 0.297 → 0.388 u); the sim roll's own travel is unchanged (it zeroes velocity at travel end). The health effects of the knockback should only apply to fighting mode." **Scope:** Story only; the Fighter sim keeps the missing-HP ramp. **What it supersedes, for Story:** `design-godot.md` :97 ("Knockback still scales with damage taken"), the "you fly farther" clause of :98 (Desperation Resonance), :1238 (`HitPayload.Knockback`, "before weight / low-HP scaling"), :1282 ("Low-HP Scaling … Applies to every hit in both modes … the `DamageCalculator` victim-HP overload (Story)"), :1303 (the knockback formula), :1530 (the Forward Throw, "KO-capable at low HP via the universal low-HP knockback scale") and :1534 ("Throw impulses obey … the low-HP knockback scale"): each is now Fighter-only. | **Built 2026-10-12 (GU-D, `75843b7`), Story only.** `DamageCalculator.CalculateKnockback(baseKnockback, targetWeight, targetCurrentHP, targetMaxHP)` is deleted; every Story victim takes `CalculateKnockback(baseKnockback, targetWeight, attackerFacingRight)` = `baseKnockback / (1 + max(0, weight))`: the hero and the Level 13 Mirror clone (`PlayerController.ResolveIncomingHit`; a hero at 25 % HP used to take ×1.75), the Story throw launch (`ResolveStoryThrow`, which read the post-throw HP), and mobs and elites (`EnemyController.TryApplyKnockback`, which crowd bowling and the `ApplyKnockback` seam also use). Bosses were already static (`BossController.ApplyKnockback`). The weight term and the attacker-side `StoryKnockbackMultiplier` stay. `BasicComboRules.LowHealthKnockbackScale` stays as the documented float statement of the sim's `FighterDamageRules.ScaleByMissingHP`, Fighter-only; the sim changed only comments, so no Fighter hash moved. Story knockback no longer depends on difficulty either (Easy's ×0.7 and Hard's ×1.5 mob HP used to change the post-hit fraction: a full string launched an Easy mob about ×1.93 and a Hard one about ×1.43, by the recon's estimate). It ships with the D6a fix (the finisher flight brakes at its carry deceleration alone; `USER-FINISHER-CARRY` amendment): with static knockback and the old `max(S1, carry)`, Cleopatra's finisher landed 104.1 px, 34 px inside her own reach. Suites: `StoryCombatRulesTests.NoStoryScriptReadsTheFighterOnlyLowHealthKnockbackScale` (a source scan of `scripts/` outside `scripts/FighterSim/` and `BasicComboRules.cs`, plus a reflection check that `CalculateKnockback` has exactly one overload), `StoryCombatRulesTests.StoryKnockbackOnTheHeroIsStaticAtEveryHP` (rewritten in place from the HP-scaled pin; FIX-F made its "fifth of max HP" case real), `StoryEnemyCombatTests.AnIdenticalFinisherWritesTheSameVelocityOnAMobAtFullHPAndAtThreeHP` and `StoryFinisherCarryTests.AFinisherFlightBrakesAtItsCarryDecelerationAloneEvenWhereS1IsStronger`. | User (design amendment) | (1) The master amended at :97, :98, :1238, :1282, :1303, :1530 and :1534 (Fighter only; Story static) and re-synced. (3) **`LowHealthKnockbackScale` is kept**, documented Fighter-only, so `FighterKnockbackScalingTests`' helper pin stands. (5) `StoryFinisherCarryTests`' ±6 px landing gate is re-premised on `CarryStoppedInAir` (the old gate, `CarryDeceleration > 1800`, encoded the retired `max`). (6) Two measurement columns kept in its printed table (`stopped in air`, `D9 bounce r`). **Recorded, not changed:** `BossController.ApplyKnockback` still adds to velocity with a hard-coded weight of 2 (the HP half of that old inconsistency is moot now); kit direct velocity writes (Rift Collapse, Lincoln's spike and bounce, the Tempest) and Story environmental pushes were already static; Story Rally and Desperation Resonance still scale with missing HP (not knockback); `ResolveStoryThrow`'s HP independence is pinned by inspection only. | Unassigned | A ruling, or a hands-on pass that leaves them alone. |
| **USER-ENEMY-TURN-TAKING** — mobs keep their distance and take turns to attack (request 6) | **User direction 2026-10-12 (request 6):** "Currently the pathing of enemies, when they are in range or in aggro, is that they all come at the player until they get to the player. I want the enemies to maintain more separation, especially on normal mode. They shouldn't just make a beeline for the character. They should wait their turn to come and attack the character. Right now it doesn't feel right. Something feels wrong about the way the pathing is functioning." **Scope ruling (user, 2026-10-12):** attackers at once **Easy 1 / Normal 2 / Hard 3**; Story only. **What it supersedes:** `design-godot.md` :2996 (Aggro/Chase: the mob "moves directly toward the player at `moveSpeed`"), :2997 (Attack: "immediately transitions to `Attacking`") and :3004 (the Stand-Off Band, now with the wait ring and the 560 px ranged hold); "especially on normal mode" has no home in the Unified Difficulty Scaling Table (:2301–2323), which carries no crowd row. | **Built 2026-10-12 (GU-F, `c5cb7b7`; FIX-F, `c80f316`), Story only.** A per-target **engagement director** lets only `StoryEngagementRules.MeleeSlotsTotal` (1 / 2 / 3) mobs commit to melee on the hero at once, at most `MeleeSlotsPerSide` (1 / 1 / 2) per side; the rest hold on a **wait ring** on their own side, the nearest at `WaitRingRankZeroGapPixels` (200 / 170 / 150 px, pivot to pivot) and each further rank 64 px out, following and backing off at speeds capped below 0.9 × the hero's run speed and stopping at ledges and walls in either direction. A holder hands its slot to a rested, eligible waiter when its attack ends (round-robin), and a holder that cannot commit for 2 s while someone waits gives it up. `RangedPermits` (1 / 2 / 3) caps concurrent ranged attacks, and a projectile mob holds no farther than 560 px (`RangedHoldMaxPixels`) and releases at 600 (`RangedHoldReleaseMaxPixels`), inside the zoomed half view. Only decisions are gated (`ProcessChase`'s commit and `TryEngageBlink`), never `BeginAttack`, `CommitArmoredAnswer`, `TryPressureExitAttack` or `TryReactivePhaseSkip`: a struck waiter still retaliates in reach (V7.4) and an owed Armored Recovery answer still walks in. A lone mob is frame-identical to before. Bosses never hold a slot (their summons do). `EnemyController.FindNearestPlayer` and `BossController.FindNearestPlayer` skip `IsStoryHostile`, so nothing targets the Level 13 Mirror clone. Dormant lock members never join; since FIX-E a lock's wake joins all its members at once. Code: `scripts/Combat/StoryEngagementRules.cs` (pure, difficulty a parameter), `scripts/Enemies/StoryEngagementDirector.cs`, `scripts/Enemies/EnemyController.Engagement.cs`; agent choices in `PROVISIONAL-ENGAGEMENT-DIRECTOR-RULES`. Suites: `StoryEngagementRulesTests` (9, pure C#) and `StoryEngagementDirectorTests` (19, real frames). **Measured** (single bot runs, not a batch): Level 2 Normal, all four bots (GU-BOT, `gubot3`) — at most 2 mobs attacking within 250 px and at most 1 melee attacker against 2 slots, no holder over the slot total, ranged attacks in flight at most 1 of 2 permits, the front-waiter median 175–200 px (the 170 px ring plus approach); Level 13 (FIX-ENEMY) — Easy 39 and Normal 49 frames with more melee attackers near the hero than slots, every one the pressure exit of a struck waiter, `frames_holders_over_slots` 0, and `gated_over_slots_f` 0 on the Easy rerun (`fxen6`). | User (design amendment) | (1) The master amended at :2996, :2997 and :3004 (and a crowd row for the difficulty table) and re-synced. (2) The Phase C bot batch's crowd table (`compare_runs.py`) reads `frames_gated_melee_over_slots` 0 and `frames_holders_over_slots` 0 on every difficulty (`frames_melee_over_slots` includes the deliberate retaliation exception). (3) A hands-on pass on Normal confirming that the mobs "wait their turn". The two director suites stay green. |
| **PROVISIONAL-ENGAGEMENT-DIRECTOR-RULES** — the director's agent-chosen rules | The user gives the slot totals (Easy 1 / Normal 2 / Hard 3) and "maintain more separation", "wait their turn" only. | *Provisional, agent-chosen (plan §2.6; GU-F decisions 1–10; FIX-F):* (1) **Numbers** beyond the user's totals: per side 1 / 1 / 2; ranged permits 1 / 2 / 3; rank-0 gap 200 / 170 / 150 px; `RestFramesAfterHandoff` 90 / 45 / 20 own ticks; `WaitRingRankSpacingPixels` 64; `WaitRingTolerancePixels` ±12; `StaleSlotFrames` 120; `WaiterApproachSpeedFraction` 0.8, `WaiterBackOffSpeedFraction` 0.6, `WaiterHeroSpeedCapFraction` 0.9 (`WaiterSpeedPixels` = min(mob speed × fraction, 0.9 × the hero's run speed); against Lincoln's 228 px/s a `chrono_slasher` waiter approaches at 205.2 and backs off at 180 px/s); `PriorityGapBucketPixels` 32; `CrossingCooldownFrames` 60. The ring stays above 125 px (the reactive Phase Skip radius), above the farthest D6c commit (77 px) and above the widest hit-1 pivot reach (144.2 px). (2) **The priority is a plan deviation (FIX-F):** rest elapsed → elite → the side with fewer holders → waited frames → the gap bucket → instance id (`ComparePriority`). The plan's literal "elite before standard → rest elapsed" let an elite that had just handed off take its slot straight back, so standard waiters never got a turn and the stale rule did nothing for an elite. A resting waiter is never *handed* a slot but may take a free one that no rested candidate can use. (3) **Stale** = 120 consecutive own Chase ticks holding a slot without committing while a waiter could take it; it resets when the holder leaves Chase. (4) **Hand-off eligibility:** a rested waiter in Chase, not crossing, whose next attack needs a melee slot and whose side has room once the holder's slot frees (`CanTakeFreedSlot`); a holder keeps its slot through a stun, a grab, a thrown flight and a freeze, and hands off at a finisher's touchdown only to an eligible waiter. (5) **Ranks** are counted per side by current gap (ties by id; crossing waiters last on the side they cross to); a hand-off re-ranks the ring. (6) **Ranged fairness (added):** among shooters refused a permit, the one refused longest fires next (ties: the lower id); the permit count stays stateless. (7) **Side rebalancing**, read as "out of room", not a head count: a waiter of rank 1 or more whose place is cut off (a blocked step, a wall in between, or no floor there), with the other side holding fewer engaged melee mobs and its ring position there floored and wall-free, off its 60-frame cooldown, crosses with its pushbox off until it clears pushbox contact (`VERIFY-ENGAGEMENT-CROSSING-PRESENTATION`); on open floor nobody crosses. (8) **The ranged hold cap** applies to every `AttackRange`-based stand-off band, lone mobs included, and binds only `infrared_border_sentry` (engage 688.5 → 560 px; release 891 → 600 px since FIX-F's `RangedHoldReleaseMaxPixels`, so a waiting sentry follows a hero who backs away instead of standing off screen); both lines derive from `StoryCameraRules` through the compile-time `ZoomedHalfViewPixels` (640) less `RangedHoldViewMarginPixels` 80 and `RangedHoldReleaseViewMarginPixels` 40. (9) **The hero's speed for the cap** is `MaxMoveSpeed × StoryMoveSpeedMultiplier × StoryTemporarySpeedMultiplier × 60`, leaving out status and terrain multipliers, so a rooted or wading hero does not freeze the ring. (10) **The ring centre** is the mob's `TargetAimPosition`, so a live Cleopatra sand decoy moves the ring. (11) **The engage blink (FIX-F)** is granted a slot only on the side it lands on (`EnemyAbilityExecutor.TeleportLandingSide`, `EnemyController.EngageBlinkReady`, `StoryEngagementDirector.IsGrantedMeleeSlot(mob, sideOverride)`, `SideHasRoomForHolder`) and counts there from its first telegraph frame (`EngagementHolderSide`); known limit: the landing side is taken against the hero's position and the director's sides against `TargetAimPosition`, which differ under a decoy. (12) **Locks (FIX-E):** a lock's engage joins every member before any member ticks, and a member going dormant leaves. **Recorded, not fixed:** waiters never strike first, even with the hero adjacent (they retaliate only after being hit; an "in reach and the hero is facing away" exception would need a ruling); a mob that aggroes from another room can hold a slot until the stale rule hands it off (locks largely remove the case); a ranged-class waiter with a short range (an elite whose next attack is a projectile signature, such as `cyber_centurion` from 132 px) holds close to the hero without firing while no permit is free; flyers ring horizontally only; the boss half of the Mirror rule has no test (inspection only); an AreaPulse commit keeps its authored range. | Unassigned | A ruling on each, or a hands-on pass on all three difficulties that leaves them alone. `StoryEngagementRulesTests` and `StoryEngagementDirectorTests` pin today's rules. |
| **VERIFY-ENGAGEMENT-CROSSING-PRESENTATION** — a waiter cut off on its side walks through the hero | Request 6: the mobs "shouldn't just make a beeline for the character". | `PROVISIONAL-ENGAGEMENT-DIRECTOR-RULES` (7): a waiter whose ring place is cut off by a wall, ledge or barrier crosses to the less crowded side with its pushbox off, visibly walking through the hero's body (the game is 2D, and the pushboxes would otherwise stop it), and re-enables the pushbox once it clears contact, so it never shoves the hero. Pinned by `StoryEngagementDirectorTests`' crossing case. Nobody has looked at it. | Unassigned | A hands-on look; if it reads badly, turn crossing off (the waiters then stand at the wall) or rule another rebalance. |
| **DEFER-MOB-PUSHBOX-JOSTLE** — mobs never jostle each other | Plan §2.6 and §8: mob-to-mob pushbox jostling is deferred out of this package. | Story mob pushboxes are on layer 2 with mask 1 (`StandardEnemy.tscn`, `EliteEnemy.tscn`), so mobs never collide with each other: waiters walking to their ranks pass through each other and through holders. The director's 64 px rank spacing keeps settled waiters apart. | Unassigned | A ruling, then a mob-to-mob soft push with the director's suites re-run. |
| **PROVISIONAL-BOT-ENCOUNTER-RULES** — the playtest bots inside locks and the lock / crowd telemetry (diagnostics only) | Plan §5 GU-BOT: lock members get boss-like targeting (no 620 px cap, no 720-frame give-up inside the lock); a truly unreachable member ends the run `stuck_lock:<id>`; `bypass` fights only what a lock or boss requires; telemetry learns `air_attack`, a `mob_engagement` block and a `locks` block; the auto run length carries the par scale. Diagnostics only: no design line covers the bots. | *Provisional, agent-chosen, diagnostics only (GU-BOT decisions 1–12; FIX-BOT decisions 1–12; nothing here is gameplay state):* (1) **Lock targeting:** a woken member of the lock the hero is Engaged in has no 620 px engage cap, no line-of-sight filter and never joins the 1500-frame ignore list; one not hurt for 720 frames is deprioritized (+2000 score, `LockTargetDeprioritizedPenalty`) instead of given up; the 330 px overhead filter still applies, with `lock_hunt` (go to the surface under the nearest member) as the fallback; dormant members are never targets. (2) **Entering and waiting:** an Armed lock on the way is walked into (`lock_enter`; the goal `PlaytestWorld.EntryPointX`, `LockEntryMarginPixels` 96 past the engage line); with nothing to fight or work the bot holds in the middle 40 % of the area (`lock_wait`); an engaged lock outranks an escape front. (3) **The stuck-lock watchdog** (`StoryBot.LockStallFrames` 3600, the bot's own decided frames): no progress (member HP, the living or remaining count, a required breakable's HP, the current task) for 60 s with no member reachable, or for 120 s regardless, ends the run `stuck_lock:<lockID>` with a nav note. (4) **Checkpoints a lock holds are skipped** as tasks (`PlaytestWorld.IsHeldByLock`). (5) **`bypass` redefined:** it fights only what the run requires (the members of the lock it stands in, bosses, a blocker in an escape), so with locks it is a lock-regression probe. (6) **Telemetry:** `mob_engagement` (near = 250 px pivot to pivot; an engaged frame has a mob in the hero's rotation or attacking near it; a hand-off is Slot → Waiting on the same body; the front waiter is the nearest per side; a far waiter is a ringing waiter more than 600 px out, logged once per mob per 60 frames, at most 60 rows; 8 px histograms and raw sums so batches pool exactly), and since FIX-ENEMY `melee_retaliating_near_max` and `frames_gated_melee_over_slots`; `locks`, since FIX-BOT one record per lock **instance** (an Act III Anchor Snap rebuilds every lock as a new node: `count` and `cleared` are distinct IDs, `instances` and `reloads` are new, a respawn is not a leave, and kills never exceed the plan per instance — the Phase C `gucn0` batch had read 435 killed against 372 planned); `passed_while_alive` keeps its old meaning beside the new `left_lock_with_members_alive`; `air_attack` is its own swing family (`swings.by_variant`); the auto run length applies the par scale before the 300 s floor. (7) **The nav graph rebuilds on the frame a barrier toggles** (every `EncounterLockBarrier` watched each frame; `nav_barrier_rebuilds`). (8) **FIX-BOT, for lock targets or inside an engaged lock only:** the walk-in at a member needs a clear solid ray from chest to centre (`PlaytestWorld.ClearWalk`), one nav surface and no 30-frame stall (`LockWalkInStallFrames`; the agent then drives for `LockWalkInDetourFrames` 180), else the agent drives (`approach_around`, the member's own surface as the goal, `break_path` through a breakable in the way); a member at least 60 px off the hero's level on another reachable surface is reached by climbing to it (`LockTargetPerch`), and a member standing on a surface is struck from the ground; a short-hop Down-Air when a full jump would overshoot (`_airStrikeShortHop`); `PrefersBeside` strikes a breakable from beside its base before a jumping Up-Attack; a gear train of at most `MaxGearTrain` 6 that an engaged lock requires is solved in the fewest presses (`PlaytestPuzzleMath.GearPresses`, one `gear` press per 30 frames); a required plate counts as reachable when any of its stand points is. (9) **FIX-BOT, everywhere:** a dodge never runs off a ledge with less than `DodgeLedgeRoomPixels` (64) of surface (`brace_discharge` / `brace_hazard` instead); a Walk edge boards a lift stop flush with the floor; an aerial edge onto a platform stop steers to where the platform is now (`NavAgent.AirTargetX`); a platform-to-platform edge costs `PlatformTransferPenaltySeconds` (6); past an escape's `FinishX` + 20 the bot goes on, with one nav note. **Recorded:** the bots do not wait their turn (they fight the nearest member, waiters included), so their crowd numbers measure the director against an aggressive player; puzzle progress reaches the watchdog only through the bot's own task progress (a pressure plate shows none); the bots do not model the mover hold; `RunsOffLedge`'s wiring, the platform-transfer penalty and `PrefersBeside` have no test of their own (they rest on the Level 11 and 12 runs). **Amended 2026-10-16:** the combat and movement update's after batch ended two Normal Lincoln runs `stuck_lock` on Level 7's quay lock; it is a flight-model gap, not content, recorded in `PROVISIONAL-BOT-FLIGHT-MODEL`. The three `stuck_lock` endings of the before batch were Level 7's boarding lock, the known skiff-channel gap. | Unassigned | None as game rules. Reopen if a `stuck_lock` end proves to be a bot gap rather than content. `PlaytestEncounterTelemetryTests` (15) pins the behaviour. |

## The 2026-10-11 user rulings — index, scope decisions and the after-measurement

The combat-flow follow-up — the user's goal, in their words: "more synergy between standard attacks and specials. I want the game to flow better in combat" — was ruled on 2026-10-11 in `DECISIONS.md` (outside this repository, `D:/Projects/absolum_research/DECISIONS.md`, with its addendum) and built on `main`'s working tree the same day (uncommitted on `d2e52e0`; committed as `2e9ca79` on 2026-10-12). The five sections above hold its rows; this one indexes them, records the rulings that changed no game rule, and records the one finding of the bot after-measurement that needs a ruling. **Every user ruling has a user row:** D1 + D1a → `USER-HIT-CONFIRM-CANCEL` (and the amended `USER-ABILITY-LOCKOUT`); D2 (bosses included, addendum) → `USER-PER-HIT-MOMENTUM`; D3 (every basic variant, addendum) → `USER-SPECIAL-RECOVERY-CANCEL`; D4 (no count rule, addendum) → `USER-FIRST-SPECIAL-UNTAXED`; D5 → `USER-STORY-METER-SCALE`; D6a (elites too, addendum) → `USER-FINISHER-CARRY`; D6b → `USER-STORY-AERIAL-REACH`; D6c → `USER-MOB-MELEE-RANGE`; D6d → `USER-BOT-COMBAT-TELEMETRY` (below); D7 and "Not doing now" → `USER-COMBAT-FLOW-NOT-NOW` (below); D8 → `USER-PUNISH-WINDOW`; D9 (thrown mobs too, addendum) → `USER-STORY-WALL-BOUNCE`; D10 → `USER-NOT-YET-FLASH`; the freeze-only Special buffer (addendum) → `USER-FREEZE-SPECIAL-BUFFER`; D11 → `USER-GAMEPAD-TIME-FREEZE-L3`; D12 → `USER-DUST-NEVER-EXPIRES`; D13 → `USER-RESTORATION-FONT-EVERY-LEVEL`. Every agent choice and every orchestrator default sits in that ruling's `PROVISIONAL-*` row (`PROVISIONAL-HIT-CONFIRM-RULES`, `PROVISIONAL-FREEZE-SPECIAL-BUFFER`, `PROVISIONAL-FIRST-SPECIAL-TAX-RULES`, `PROVISIONAL-PUNISH-WINDOW-RULES`, `PROVISIONAL-SPECIAL-RECOVERY-CANCEL-RULES`, `PROVISIONAL-STORY-METER-SCALE-RULES`, `PROVISIONAL-SPACING-WALLS-RULES`, `PROVISIONAL-DUST-SETTLE-RULES`, `PROVISIONAL-RESULTS-CARRIED-DUST`, `PROVISIONAL-FONT-PLACEMENT`). **Design-master lines owed (owner User; the repository mirror `design-godot.md` was deliberately not edited; line numbers are the mirror's as of 2026-10-12):** D1 / D1a and the freeze buffer — :782, :816, :1499, :3912 and the Cooldown System block from :1106 (the 2026-10-08 lockout's own citations in `USER-ABILITY-LOCKOUT` were taken against an older mirror and have drifted); D2 — :1108, :1352 and the snapshot struct :1766–1767, plus `ROLLBACK_STATE_CONTRACT.md`'s "refund counts"; ~~D3 — :783–785 and :1500~~ (written 2026-10-16 at :788 / :1533); D4, D8 and D9 — new Story content beside Enemy Stagger Discipline (no line states them today); D5 — :1268, :1269 and the build-rate reading of :2793; D6b — :1089 and :1099–1101; D10 — :3514, :4916, :4918 and :4919, plus `HUD_CONTRACT.md` (master and the repo copy); D11 — :405, :2110 and :2290, plus `COMFORT_SETTINGS.md` :104; D12 — :891, plus `DUST_ECONOMY.md` :12 (and :76 and :64 if `PROVISIONAL-DUST-SETTLE-RULES` (2) and `PROVISIONAL-RESULTS-CARRIED-DUST` are accepted). D6a, D6c and D13 need no master line (D13 is what :2132 already asks for). **Test baseline:** 2887 → **3021** (+134 `[TestCase]`s; 2882 → 3021 counting the C1 telemetry's +5; the arithmetic is in `AGENTS.md`'s validation section), three consecutive green full runs on 2026-10-12. **Nobody has played any of it, and nobody has looked at the flash, the fonts or the aerial poses.**

## Confirmed open deviations — combat and movement update, 2026-10-16

The user's combat and movement decisions of 2026-10-15 and 2026-10-16 (rounds 1–10, the follow-ups 11.1–11.3 and the eight plan questions Q1–Q8, recorded in `D:/Projects/genre_review_2026-10-13/DECISIONS_combat_movement.md` from the genre review's `REPORT_1_PLATFORMERS.md` and `REPORT_2_FIGHTERS.md`; finding IDs such as PFC-08 or CMB-01 point there), planned in `docs/COMBAT_MOVEMENT_UPDATE_2026-10-16_PLAN.md` (plan commit `735296f`: §0 the rulings it amends, reopens and keeps, §0.2 the question rulings, §1 the standing decisions, §2 the per-decision dossiers, §5 the pad-session checklist, §9 the deviation log) and built on worktree branches merged into `main` between `cfba295` and `14374d7` (2026-10-16): Phase A by CM-BOT (the re-hit counter), CM-STORY (Story combat feel), CM-ULT (the Ultimate telegraph), CM-INPUT (devices, the pad chord, the CPU jump hold) and CM-SIM (component 323 and the Fighter Special phase machine; its foundation tag `cm-sim-a0`, merged as `a6d9bf2`, then `5434151`); the Phase A review fixes CM-FIXA-SIM (`891b954`), CM-FIXA-STORY (`33e1a86`), CM-FIXA-INPUT (`98e5e4a`) and CM-FIXA-PRES (`2d44fa9`); Phase B by CM-HIT (`bb5ba58`, hit reaction and protection), CM-BLOCK (`629a816`), CM-MOVE (`23fcd22`), CM-CANCEL (`8647fd4`, D3 in Fighter, the CPU and the Mirror) and CM-PADUI (`740d712`, pad menus — added by the orchestrator, not in the plan), with the orchestrator's merge fix-ups `85ce072` and `fa18442`; and the Phase B review fixes CM-FIXB-SIM (`33fc9b5`), CM-FIXB-CPU (`2fbfa6b`), CM-FIXB-STORY (`2ede072`) and CM-FIXB-PADUI (`14374d7`). Phase A merged at `cfba295` (CM-BOT), `7051f53` (CM-STORY), `d4d0043` (CM-ULT) and `da08d70` (CM-INPUT). The handoffs are `docs/handoffs/CM_*.md`; where a fix-round handoff (`CM_FIXA_*`, `CM_FIXB_*`) differs from the Phase A or B handoff it reviews, the rows below carry the later facts. **Where the rules live (one value each):** the Fighter Special phase machine in `FighterSpecialCastRules` (`scripts/FighterSim/FighterSpecialCast.cs`, component 323), its clock in `FighterMovementSystem.SpecialCast.cs` and its fire pass in `FighterAbilityEntitySystem.FireActiveSpecials`, with the cast motion read from `UniversalMovementRules.RunDecelerationFrames` and `AerialSpecialGravityMultiplier` (Story's `ProcessUsingSpecial` forwards); the action chokepoint `FighterActionRules.OnActionAccepted`; block in `BasicComboRules`' block section (`ShieldstunFramesFor`, `PerfectBlockWindowFrames`, `PerfectBlockRearmFrames`, `GuardCrushBlockChargeCost`, `PerfectBlockArms`, `PerfectBlockApplies`), `FighterBlockTimingRules` (`FighterMovementSystem.Block.cs`), `PlayerController.PerfectBlock.cs` and `PlayerController.EnvironmentalBlock.cs`; D3 in `BasicComboRules`' D3 section (`StoryCombatRules` forwards) and `FighterSpecialCancelRules` (`FighterSpecialCancel.cs`, `FighterMovementSystem.SpecialCancel.cs`); the tumble in `FighterVerbRules` (`TumbleAfterHitstun`), `FighterMovementSystem.HitReaction.cs` and `PlayerController.Tumble.cs`; respawn protection in `FighterMatchFlowRules` (`RespawnInvulnerabilityMaxFrames`, `RespawnInvulnerabilityMinFrames`, `RespawnInvulnerabilityFor`); the short hop, the drop-through window and corner forgiveness in `UniversalMovementRules` (`ShortHopGravityMultiplier`, `DropThroughDoubleTapFrames`, `HeadClipNudgeUnits`, `LipPopUnits`, `SimOneWayLipPopUnits`), `FighterAirPhysics`, `StoryAirPhysics`, `FighterMovementSystem.Movement.cs` and `PlayerController.CornerForgiveness.cs`; the ledge get-ups in `BasicComboRules`' ledge section (`Ledge*`), `FighterLedgeRules` and `FighterCombatSystem.LedgeAttack.cs`; Story feel in `StoryCombatRules` (`UltimateStartupArmorPreventsInterruption`, `StoryEchoStepMeterCost`) and `StoryEnemyCombatRules` (`StandardBasicDamageFloor`, `ScalesAbilityShakeWithDamage`, `IsProrated`, `ProratedDamageNumberScale`), with `UIPalette.DamageNumberReduced`; the telegraph in `FTT.Combat.UltimateActivationTelegraph`, `FTT.Combat.TelegraphGlyph` (moved from `FTT.Enemies`, now the one statement of the three attack-class colours) and the glow arbiter's `SetUltimateTelegraph`; input in `FTT.Core.UltimateChordWindow`, `InputManager` (device follow, the local join, `InputManager.MenuPress.cs`), `FTT.UI.PadMenuInput` and `project.godot` (pad A on `ui_accept`, pad B on `ui_cancel`, no pad events on `gameplay_ultimate`); the CPU in `FighterCpuController` and `CpuDecisionObservation`; the hurtboxes in `FighterHurtbox.HalfExtents`, `FighterBodyOverlap.HalfExtents`, `StoryCombatRules.HeroHurtboxHeightPixels` and `CharacterFactory.HeroBodyBoxWidthPixels` / `HeroBodyBoxHeightPixels`; the re-hit counter in `RehitTracker` (`scripts/Diagnostics/PlaytestCombatStats.cs`) over the observation-only `PlayerController.DamageTaken`. **Mode scope:** both modes for 2.1 (presentation), 3.2, 3.4, 4.1 / 11.2, 4.3, 5.3, 6.1, 6.2 (Q6), 6.3, 8.4 (no number change), 9.1, 9.2 and 11.1; Fighter only for 1.1(2)–(4), 1.2 and its Q1 motion (Story's cast brake changed too), 1.3, 2.3, 4.2 (Q8), 5.1 (Q5's own-jump gate also changed Story's band), 5.2 and 10.1; the CPU and the Level 13 Mirror for 1.1(5) and 9.3; Story only for 1.1(1), 2.4, 7.1–7.4, 8.1 and 8.2. Nothing under `scripts/FighterSim/` names a Story rulebook (`SpecialRecoveryCancelTableTests` source-scans it, beside the existing scans). **Every Fighter hash moved** (the Special phases and component 323's hashed state, the scaled shieldstun, the perfect-block clock, the tumble, the respawn curve, the short-hop latch, the lip pop, the ledge get-ups, D3 and the CPU's cast reads); no suite pins an absolute hash value. Builds from before and after this update would desync on LAN; LAN is de-scoped to Package 7, so the protocol stays **v4** (plan §1.7: `PlayerInputFrame` 12 B, `RollbackInputPacket` 47 B, `GameplayButtons` unchanged). **One new Klotho component and no other ID:** `FighterActionStateComponent` (**323**, MaxCount 2, nine ints, 36 B per fighter, 72 B per snapshot; added in `SpawnFighter`, held at zero on the respawn platform, zeroed by the F22 setup): `SpecialSlot`, `SpecialPhase`, `SpecialPhaseFrames`, `SpecialPhaseElapsed`, `SpecialFlags` (1 `FlagStartedAerial`, 2 `FlagEffectFired`, 4 `FlagMeleeResolved`, 8 `FlagLandedDirectHit`, 16 `FlagFallbackTiming`), `SpecialCancelBuffer`, `BlockTiming` (bits 0–7 the window, 8–15 the lowered age, 16 the stance last tick, 17 observed, 18 the escape tick, 20–23 the perfect-block count), `DownTapFrames` and `ActionFlags` (bit 0 `RespawnProtection`, 1 `JumpReleasedSincePress`, 2 `LedgeStickRearmed`, 3 `RiseFromOwnJump`; bits 4–31 reserved, 0). Zero-byte reuses: component 305 `AttackFlags` 256 (`FlagActiveFacingRight`) and 512 (`FlagLedgeAttack`), 305's universal-movement values 8–10 (`LedgeClimb`, `LedgeRoll`, `LedgeAttackClimb`), and 310 `Tumble` value 2 (`TumbleAfterHitstun`). **No save schema change** (v8): device follow, the join, chord leniency and the Story Echo Step cost are runtime or constants, and the stale pad Ultimate row is repaired at load by a pure function. **Translation keys:** added `select_press_to_join` (CM-INPUT), `movelist_ledge_options` (CM-MOVE) and `select_press_to_join_player_one` (CM-PADUI); values changed in place: `systems_card_echo_body` (CM-STORY, no cost named), `systems_card_tech_body` (CM-HIT), `systems_card_block_body` (CM-BLOCK), `tooltip_first_use_one_way` (CM-MOVE, both inputs) and `pause_controller_disconnected` (CM-PADUI, a key press resumes). **Tests:** 3228 → **3518** (+290): Phase A +104 = 3332 (CM-BOT 7, CM-STORY 21, CM-ULT 7, CM-INPUT 24, CM-SIM 45 including A0's 12); its review fixes +35 = 3367 (CM-FIXA-SIM 10, CM-FIXA-STORY 8, CM-FIXA-INPUT 12, CM-FIXA-PRES 5); Phase B +122 = 3489 (CM-HIT 23, CM-BLOCK 22, CM-MOVE 37, CM-CANCEL 22, CM-PADUI 18) and the orchestrator's fix-up `fa18442` +1 (`FighterLedgeGetUpTests.TheLedgeAttackCanBePerfectBlocked`) = 3490; the Phase B review fixes +28 = 3518 (CM-FIXB-SIM 8, CM-FIXB-CPU 6, CM-FIXB-STORY 6, CM-FIXB-PADUI 8). The first full run after CM-MOVE's merge failed one CM-HIT case (`StoryAirDodgeTests.BeingHitRefreshesTheAirDodgeSoItCanDodgeAgainAfterHitstun` tapped Jump, and since 6.1 a released Jump short-hops with no hang, so the hero landed before the dodge ended); `85ce072` holds Jump through that fixture, assertions unchanged. **Three consecutive green full runs on 2026-10-17** at `14374d7` (`Failed: 0, Passed: 3518, Total: 3518`; 1 m 40 s, 1 m 34 s, 1 m 35 s; lock held, orphans drained before each). **Rollback re-measure** (2026-10-16, `RollbackReadinessTests`, depth-7 resimulation, 300 corrections per stage, 8.0 ms budget): 0 breaches on all ten stages; worst median 0.084 ms (`alexandria_chambers`), worst p95 0.168 ms (`chicago_exposition`), largest snapshot 4473 B (`globe_theatre`), against `docs/PERFORMANCE_BASELINE.md`'s 0.058 ms / 0.104 ms and 2,699 B of 2026-08-08, recorded before Packages 11–13 and this update. **Smoke runs** (2026-10-16 at `14374d7`): MainMenu, HubWorld, Level 0, Level 7 and Level 13 (300 frames), TestArena (1800) and `FighterStage_Paris` (300) exit 0 with no script error; the `6 ObjectDB instances were leaked` / `3 resources still in use at exit` pair on Levels 0, 7 and 13 also prints on the plan commit's Level 7 control, so it is the documented exit noise. **Bots** (before `cmbefore0` / `cmbefore1` at `735296f`, after `cmafter0` / `cmafter1` at `14374d7`, in the clean worktree `D:/Projects/ftt-wt/cm-bots`; Levels 2, 6, 7, 12 and 13, Joan and Lincoln, `play`, Easy and Normal, seeds 0 and 1, `-MaxSeconds 0`, 40 runs a side; `D:/Projects/genre_review_2026-10-13/closeout/bots_compare.json`): deaths and damage taken per run fell on both difficulties with no overlap between the per-seed ranges (deaths Easy 1.5–1.6 → 1.2, Normal 3.9–4.2 → 2.9–3.1; damage Easy 236.8–266.9 → 225.2–233.8, Normal 435.4–465.5 → 368.0–387.2), most of it hazard and Extractor damage while enemy damage stayed flat (`USER-STORY-HAZARD-BLOCK`); completions rose (Easy 17 → 19, Normal 9 → 12 of 20) but the per-seed ranges overlap (Easy 8–9 → 9–10, Normal 4–5 → 5–7), so that rise is not shown to be real; the two new `stuck_lock` endings are a bot gap on Level 7's quay lock (`PROVISIONAL-BOT-FLIGHT-MODEL`). Batches from before and after this update are not comparable for run time or short-hop and hang flights, and only the after batch carries the `rehits` block. **Row names:** the handoffs' proposals are kept, with these choices — the eight plan questions get one row each (Q5 keeps CM-MOVE's `USER-SHORT-HOP-OWN-JUMP-ONLY`, Q7's built half is CM-STORY's `FIX-STORY-RAIL-CHARGE-RECOVERY-CARRY`); CM-SIM's "the 8.4 sim half" is `USER-HURTBOX-ONE-VALUE`; the two leaks CM-SIM asked to record and CM-FIXA-SIM fixed are `VERIFY-SIM-KNOCKDOWN-LANDING-TICK` and `VERIFY-SIM-A02-THAW-TICK`, closed; CM-FIXA-SIM's throw-grant row is CM-HIT's `FIX-SIM-THROW-GRANT-OUTLIVES-THROW`, closed; CM-MOVE's CPU ledge row is `VERIFY-CPU-LEDGE-GETUPS`, closed by `fa18442`; CM-FIXA-INPUT's owner-less pad row is `FIX-PAD-MENU-ACCEPT-CANCEL`, closed by CM-PADUI; CM-FIXB-CPU's four findings are one closed `FIX-CPU-*` row each; Cleopatra's Desert Mirage startup (CM-FIXA-STORY §5.1, fixed by CM-MOVE) is `FIX-STORY-DESERT-MIRAGE-STARTUP-CARRY`; the CM-FIXB-SIM, CM-FIXB-STORY and CM-FIXB-PADUI findings are folded into the rows they amend. **Nobody has played or looked at any of it:** every claim below is a headless suite, a smoke run, a probe or a bot run. §5's pad session is the user's tool for every "judge by hand" item.

A pending press that carries Jump while the sustain runs first releases Jump for `EdgeReleaseGapFrames` (1) tick, so a double jump is still its own edge, one tick later than before (`PROVISIONAL-MOVEMENT-FEEL-RULES` (9)). `MirrorParadoxDecisionAdapter` gained an internal constructor taking a `CpuBandTuning` override. **Verified first, as the decision asked:** the Einstein clone's approach arc (Hard boss band, `ApproachJumpPercent = 100`) peaked at 131.4 px for two presses against 186.4 px for one held single jump. After CM-FIXA-INPUT's rewrite of the Mirror case (finding 4: the first version measured a double-jump arc), one ground jump peaks at **186.4 px**, exactly a held single jump; a mutation that ends the ground jump's sustain at once fails it at 89.1 px. **Amended by CM-FIXB-CPU (B0/B20):** the hitstun-defense hold now lets the sustain run once hitstun ends (`FIX-CPU-HITSTUN-HOLD-EATS-RECOVERY`). Pinned by `MirrorParadoxTests.TheCloneHoldsJumpForAFullHopInsteadOfShortHoppingEveryJump`, `FighterCpuBehaviorTests.JumpStaysHeldAfterItsPulseUntilTheFighterFallsAtTwoUnitsPerSecond` and `TwoQueuedJumpsStillProduceTwoPressedEdgesWithOneReleaseTickBetween`. | Unassigned | Closed. | **USER-SIM-SPECIAL-TIMING** — every Fighter Special runs its authored startup, active and recovery (decision 1.2) | **User decision 2026-10-15 (1.2):** "Sim Specials resolve on the press tick (DEFER-SIM-SPECIAL-PHASES) [PFC-08, FGS-02, KIT-01, TWO-03]" — "**Full timing now**: every Fighter Special gets its authored startup, active and recovery frames, matching Story. Reopens the 2026-10-11 "not doing now" item. Likely needs a new Klotho component (check the inventory; never allocate an ID without checking)." Q1 rules the cast's motion (`USER-SPECIAL-CAST-BRAKE`). Closes `DEFER-SIM-SPECIAL-PHASES` and `SIM-SPECIAL-POSE-THIRDS`; reopens `USER-COMBAT-FLOW-NOT-NOW`'s "closing `DEFER-SIM-SPECIAL-PHASES`" item. | **Built (CM-SIM A0 `9ca55ea` … `b62bf1a`, then `366bfa2`, `c130fa6`, `36b1ee7`; merged `a6d9bf2` and `5434151`; amended by CM-FIXA-SIM `083f5cc`, merged `891b954`).** Component 323 (`FighterActionStateComponent`, the only new ID; checked free in the inventory) holds the cast. **Acceptance** in `FighterAbilityEntitySystem.Update` spends at once (cooldown, the Momentum counter reset, the lockout, `CancelString`, `OnActionAccepted(Special)`), then `FighterSpecialCastRules.Begin(slot, contract, startedAerial)`; Special 1 resolves before Special 2; Tubman's Foresight starts its 322 stance and records `BeginDelegated`. **The clock** (`FighterMovementSystem.AdvanceSpecialCast`, after the hitstop gate): the interrupt set (a hit, a grab, a KO and, since CM-FIXA-SIM, a knockdown) clears the cast first; `Advance` returns true on the active-entry tick T+S, where Divine Piercing's lunge starts (13 frames in place, 12 lunging, 20 in place: exactly the active window). **The fire pass** (`FireActiveSpecials`) fires the slot's existing effect — projectile, construct, zone, the Rift placement, the Vortex anchor, the Tempest lift — once, on T+S. **Melee contact** (`FighterCombatSystem.BuildIntent`) is tested on every active tick until it resolves one contact (`FlagMeleeResolved`). **The cast lock:** while `IsBusy` the caster's input is discarded every tick, in the cast region and in the hitstop branch, and `CanStartGrab`, `CanReadAbilityPress` and `UltimatePressPayable` refuse; only the grounded Block cancel ends a recovery early (and D3 since Phase B, `USER-FIGHTER-SPECIAL-RECOVERY-CANCEL`); no ledge capture while busy. **Motion:** the Q1 brake, and an airborne-started cast steps gravity × `UniversalMovementRules.AerialSpecialGravityMultiplier` (0.5, Story's old literal, now forwarded by `ProcessUsingSpecial`) in Startup and Active, with no fast-fall (Down is discarded). **Data:** `FighterAbilityHitData.StartupFrames` / `RecoveryFrames`, projected by `FighterLoadoutFactory.HitData` (loadout data, never snapshot state). **Fallback:** a contract with `ActiveFrames <= 0` (a hand-built `FighterAbilityLoadout.Default` slot; no production Special) runs 0 / 1 / 0 with `FlagFallbackTiming`, reproducing press-tick timing exactly — the seeded hand-built replay hash did not move across the phase-machine commit, while 23 of 24 seeded production and CPU hashes did. **Presentation** (read-only): the driver poses `special_1` / `special_2` from 323 for the whole cast (`SpecialCastPose`: wind-up, strike, follow-through). Hit 1's 30-frame hitstun still covers every Special startup (at most 16 frames), so hit 1 → a reaching Special stays a true confirm in PvP. Measured: Story fires a Special's effect one tick before the sim (`VERIFY-SAME-TICK-VERB-PARITY` (8)); the Shield-Breaker gap is in `USER-SHIELD-BREAKER-STARTUP`; rollback in CM-SIM's worktree 0.115 ms median, 0.240 ms p95, 4429 B, 0 breaches (the closeout's re-measure is in the section intro). CM-SIM's own deviations: no Fighter ability VFX existed to retime; the driver's "not yet" classifier passes no 323 (safe, because the cast discard zeroes `PressedButtons`). Suites: `FighterSpecialCastRulesTests` 6 (pure C#), `FighterSpecialTimingTests` 18, `ShieldBreakerTimingTests` 3, `FighterActionStateTests` 4, `StorySpecialTimingProbeTests` 2, `StorySpecialCastMotionTests` 2, `RollbackReadinessTests.ProductionLoadoutsConvergeWithSpecialsCastThroughTheirFullPhases`, and cases in `DivinePiercingLungeTests`, `SplittingStrikeSpikeTests`, `FeelPassSimTests` and `FighterLockoutRefusalRulesTests`; 23 suites were re-timed through the non-suite helper `tests/Determinism/SpecialCastTestKit.cs`, assertions unchanged. | Unassigned | Plan §5 row 8: each of the nine kits judged for whiff punishability, the Conductor's Call's 60-frame lock, Divine Piercing's 13-frame startup and the cast brake (the row's "sliding at its entry speed" text predates the Q1 ruling; it now checks the brake); the design master states the Fighter Special phases. |
| **PROVISIONAL-SIM-SPECIAL-PHASE-RULES** — the phase machine's agent-chosen rules (1.2) | The decision gives "authored startup, active and recovery frames, matching Story" only. | *Provisional, agent-chosen (CM-SIM decisions 1–14, amended by CM-FIXA-SIM rulings (a)–(d)):* (1) **Tick convention:** the press tick T is the first startup tick; the effect fires on T+S (the basic-swing convention); the caster is locked through T+S+A+R−1 and free on T+S+A+R, whose input is live. Story fires on T+S−1 (`VERIFY-SAME-TICK-VERB-PARITY` (8)). (2) The 0 / 1 / 0 fallback for `ActiveFrames == 0`. (3) **Interrupts:** a hit the combat system lands (PostUpdate) clears the cast on the next tick's movement pass, before any system reads it; the fire pass re-checks the set; a hit landing on T+S trades. Since CM-FIXA-SIM ruling (a) the set includes the knockdown (`IsInterrupted` / `ClearIfInterrupted` take `knockedDown`), so a cast accepted before a missed tech never fires from the floor. (4) One melee contact per execution. (5) **The hitstop-branch discard** sits after the chain and freeze buffers and after `ClearIfInterrupted` (an interrupted victim keeps its DI read); since CM-FIXA-SIM the branch also ends with `DiscardButtons` (buttons only, never the stick, which a frozen victim's DI reads) for a knocked-down fighter or a busy A02 caster. (6) The Q1 motion goes through the existing run-stop step (no new constant). (7) Aerial half gravity only for a cast that started airborne, only in Startup and Active; since CM-FIXA-SIM also a Delegated Foresight cast (`BeginDelegated(startedAerial)`) in its startup and window, and Foresight's stance is planted (X = 0 while component 322 is busy, as Story's `TubmanForesight` writes), so a grounded Foresight stops at once rather than over the 4-frame brake. (8) No ledge capture while busy, Delegated included. (9) Root still cancels 305's universal-movement slot every tick, so Divine Piercing's lunge dies under Root — reasoned, not tested. (10) The Tempest lift happens on its active entry (T+8), not on the press. (11) `FlagEffectFired` is set for every slot the fire pass visits, melee slots included; never read it as "landed" (use `FlagMeleeResolved` or `FlagLandedDirectHit`). (12) The recovery Block cancel is grounded only; an aerial cast that lands mid-recovery with Block held cancels on its landing tick. (13) **Superseded by CM-FIXA-SIM ruling (d):** the Block-cancel tick keeps only the stance (`PressedButtons &= BlockButton` after `Clear`; held buttons and the stick stay), so Roll, Jump, drop-through, the grab chord, the direct grab, Echo Step, the movement ability, the Specials and the Ultimate need a fresh press from recovery frame 1, matching Story. (14) A same-tick Splitting Strike + movement press casts Splitting Strike. **Also CM-FIXA-SIM:** Divine Piercing's Block cancel opens on its recovery frame 0 (`AdvanceSpecialCast` ends the spent lunge's 305 slot on the Active → Recovery tick, hash-neutral on paths that never Block-cancel); on a stock-loss tick `CanReadAbilityPress` refuses on the respawn platform and `BuildIntent` resolves no melee contact from it. | Unassigned | A ruling on each, or plan §5 row 8 leaves them alone. |

Not the default. A kit that writes its own velocity (Divine Piercing's lunge, the Tempest lift, kit motions) overrides the brake. | **Built (CM-SIM `366bfa2` and `c130fa6`, merged `5434151`).** Sim: a grounded cast runs `ApplyNormalMovement` with the discarded (zero) stick, which is the run-stop ramp (`UniversalMovementRules.RunDecelerationFrames`, 4); airborne, `CastKeepsAirDrift` skips air control, so the entry drift is kept (no stick, no added friction). Story: `PlayerController.ApplySpecialCastBrake` (new partial `PlayerController.SpecialCastMotion.cs`), called from `ProcessUsingSpecial`, runs `DecelerateHorizontal(dt)` while `IsOnFloor()`, except during Divine Piercing's Active lunge. Measured (`StorySpecialTimingProbeTests`): before, a running grounded Story caster slid at 324 px/s on every cast tick; after, it stops on exactly the 4th tick. Divine Piercing from a run slides 15.5 px through its startup (the ramp), then lunges 180 px. Foresight is planted at once in both modes (`PROVISIONAL-SIM-SPECIAL-PHASE-RULES` (7)). Pinned by `StorySpecialCastMotionTests` (2) and `FighterSpecialTimingTests`' grounded-brake and aerial-drift case. | Unassigned | Plan §5 row 8 (a running cast now brakes; whether that reads right), and the design master's FSM `UsingSpecial` rows state it. |

| **USER-STORY-ULTIMATE-ARMOR** — Story Ultimates are armoured through their startup (decision 2.4) | **User decision 2026-10-15 (2.4):** "Story Ultimate paid at startup, no armour [CMB-01, TWO-06]" — "**Armour the startup**: `GrantsHyperArmor` on all nine Story Ultimates (the sim reads the flag only for the movement ability, so Fighter is unchanged)." **Q2** narrows it to the startup through a Story rule (`USER-STORY-ULTIMATE-ARMOR-STARTUP-ONLY`). | **Built (CM-STORY `4882d85`, merged `7051f53`; Story only).** `StoryCombatRules.UltimateStartupArmorPreventsInterruption(phase, incoming)` = `phase == Startup && incoming != Ultimate`; the new partial `PlayerController.UltimateArmor.cs` ORs `UltimateHasStartupArmor` into `HasActiveHyperArmor` and `HasActiveHyperArmorAgainst`. It is keyed to the Ultimate's own cast clock, so the caster's hitstop and Time Freeze pause it; all nine `ultimate.tres` keep `GrantsHyperArmor = false`, so Fighter loadouts are untouched. A hit's Static Charge no longer stuns through any armour window (`ApplyHitStatus` sets `ArmorAbsorbsStatusStun`; `StaticChargeStrategy.OnApply` returns before `ApplyStun`; the charge still enters the control slot). The gold hyper-armour shell shows for the 30-frame startup. The Level 13 clone's post-contact Story Ultimate runs through the same controller, so its startup is armoured too (`VERIFY-STORY-MIRROR-HOLD`). **Amended by CM-FIXA-STORY (`3becde4`, merged `33e1a86`):** Einstein's Rift Collapse launch called `ApplyStun` directly on a caught `PlayerController`, cancelling a paid Ultimate in its armoured startup (both directions of the Level 13 Einstein match); the direct launch now also requires `!caughtPlayer.HasActiveHyperArmorAgainst(hit.AttackClass)`. Suites: `StoryUltimateArmorTests` (5), `StoryRiftCollapseTests` +1. | Unassigned | Plan §5 row 23 (Ultimates armoured through their startup only); the design master's Hyper-Armor spec and Ultimate block state it. |

(2) The gold shell and `EventBus.OnHyperArmorChanged` show for the startup, as for any armoured body. (3) The Rift Collapse launch is a second armour read site, read at the launch, not at the catch (an Ultimate accepted during the six-frame pull is armoured too); an armoured body is still pulled, as no kit pull reads armour (the Lorentz pull, the Cataclysm drag, `PullThroughCollision`); the sim's `FighterRiftRules.TryCollapse` was not examined. | Unassigned | A ruling on each, or plan §5 row 23 leaves them alone. |

Amends M05/M07's Launch Flag & Knockdown and Landing Tech (`design-godot.md` :1286–1287, the FSM tumble rows :819–820) and `VERIFY-M05-KNOCKDOWN-SCOPE` (the Story hero is in; Story mobs stay out, plan §8). | **Built (CM-HIT `2b9214c`, `eddc3bd`, `0f705e2`, merged `bb5ba58`; amended by CM-FIXB-SIM `aa77531`, merged `33fc9b5`).** Sim: 310 `Tumble` reads `FighterVerbRules.TumbleNone` 0, `TumbleInHitstun` 1 (set by `StashPendingLaunch`) and `TumbleAfterHitstun` 2; `UpdateTumble`, at the top of every unfrozen movement tick (`ApplyHitReaction`, new partial `FighterMovementSystem.HitReaction.cs`), keeps an airborne post-hitstun tumble at 2 and ends it on the ground; the landing resolution keys on `IsTumbling` (`TryLandingTech`, `TryBeginKnockdown`, `FighterSlamRules.ClearIfStale` and `TryBounce`), so a passive post-hitstun fighter lands in the bounce → tech → knockdown chain and an owed Down-Air bounce outlives the slam's hitstun; `ExitTumbleOnAction` runs first in `OnActionAccepted` for every action kind, keyed on `HitstunFrames <= 0`; drift and fast-fall keep it. Story: `_tumbleAfterHitstun` (new partial `PlayerController.Tumble.cs`, seam `IsTumblingAfterHitstun`), set at `ProcessStunned`'s expiry when airborne with a launch tumble; the hero returns to `Airborne` with control, and `TryResolveTumbleLanding` (the first thing `ProcessAirborne` does on ground contact) resolves the owed slam bounce, then the 12-frame tech on held Block (`BeginLandingTech`), else the knockdown; it animates `hitstun`. The driver plays `hitstun` for an airborne value-2 tumble; `systems_card_tech_body` names the exits. Story mobs are untouched; Story throws still never tech (F23) while sim throws launch and tumble, a kept divergence. **CM-FIXB-SIM (B1):** a non-launching hit ends a sim tumble inside the launch's hitstun (value 1) as well as after it, as Story's `ApplyStun` already did (`ResolveFighterHit`: `if (!(launches && hasImpulse)) Tumble = TumbleNone` inside the hitstun write; an owed bounce is then dropped by `ClearIfStale`). **Consequence for F09 (No Juggling):** a juggled victim hit by a non-launcher (the air strike, a coil arc) lands in that hit's ordinary hitstun with no tech or knockdown protection, as M05 intends. The CPU techs a post-hitstun tumble through its existing hitstun-defense Block hold, which since CM-FIXB-CPU also lets its recovery presses through (`FIX-CPU-HITSTUN-HOLD-EATS-RECOVERY`). Suites: `FighterTumbleTests` (7, pure C#), `StoryTumbleTests` (6), in place `DownAirSlamTests` and `CalibrationDrillScriptTests`. | Unassigned | Plan §5 row 13 (knockdown frequency, acting out of tumble, a post-hitstun rise never cut by the short hop); the design master's landing-tech and knockdown lines and FSM rows state it. |

Drift and fast-fall keep the tumble. (2) The 1 → 2 conversion runs at the top of the next unfrozen tick, so a landing on the tick hitstun reaches 0 still resolves as a tumble landing; the exit keys on `HitstunFrames <= 0`, not on the value. (3) **Amended by CM-FIXB-SIM (B1):** any fresh stunning hit without a fresh launch ends the tumble, value 1 or 2, in both modes (before, the sim ended only value 2); a launcher re-tumbles; shield, barrier, armour and Defy absorptions apply no hitstun and keep it. A perfect block of any contact the ordinary block would absorb spends no charge, sets no shieldstun and re-arms no regen; the 2-frame blocked hitstop stays; the outcome stays `Blocked` (no D1a confirm, no Momentum); hazard ticks can be perfect-blocked; the Ultimate activation never reaches the block. Sim: `FighterBlockTimingRules` (`FighterMovementSystem.Block.cs`) over component 323's `BlockTiming`, `BeginTick` once per unfrozen tick and `Observe` on the normal-control path; every blockable hit site passes the victim's 323 to new `ResolveFighterHit` / `ApplyFighterHit` / `ApplyEnvironmentHit` overloads (the old ones resolve an ordinary block); `fa18442` threaded the ledge attack's site, which CM-MOVE had built in parallel (pinned by `FighterLedgeGetUpTests.TheLedgeAttackCanBePerfectBlocked`, red without the fix). Story: `PlayerController.PerfectBlock.cs` (the window is owed on the press tick and opens on the first live stance tick, one tick later; the hit-2 escape never arms); `BlockSystem.ResolveHit`'s perfect branch; Henry's Bastion still grants, Shield of Orléans does not pay (design :2561 needs a consumed charge); a perfect block opens the D8 Punish window, a perfect-blocked Siphon Snare included. Presentation in both modes: a (0.85, 1, 1) hit flash for 0.12 s (`BlockSystem.PerfectBlockFlashColor` / `PerfectBlockFlashSeconds`), the 3 / 0.08 block shake and the Guard Impact haptic; Calibration Drill 1 counts a perfect block as a block. `systems_card_block_body` states the window. Suites: `FighterPerfectBlockTests` (8, pure C#), `StoryPerfectBlockTests` (6), `CalibrationDrillScriptTests.HoldTheLineCountsAPerfectBlockAsABlock`; an instrumented audit run found the fixtures that reached a perfect block and re-timed five of them to an ordinary block (`FighterHazardBehaviorTests`, `StoryBlockClassificationTests` with its missing one-charge assertion added, `StorySpecialRecoveryCancelTests`, `StoryHitConfirmTests`), leaving `RollbackReadinessTests`' random inputs to cover the new state. | Unassigned | Plan §5 row 11 (whether the 5-frame window is learnable, whether the anti-mash rearm holds, how often a Hard CPU perfect-blocks, whether the flash reads); the design master's block section and `DEFENSIVE_EFFECTS.md` D01/D02b state it. |
| **PROVISIONAL-PERFECT-BLOCK-RULES** — the perfect block's agent-chosen rules (3.4) | The ruling gives "about 4–6 frames", no charge, no shieldstun, no reflect and no attacker stun. | *Provisional, agent-chosen (CM-BLOCK §3, amended by CM-FIXB-SIM and CM-FIXB-STORY):* (1) A 5-frame window and a 20-frame rearm (the plan's numbers); arming needs the down → up stance edge, a Block press on that tick and a lowered age of 0 ("never raised") or at least 20, so a held raise, an escape-raised stance and a press in shieldstun never arm and a `Pressed = Held` stream arms once. (2) A Guard-Crush (an authored charge cost ≥ `GuardCrushBlockChargeCost` 2, now the one statement of that 2, read by `HitClassification.BlockChargeCostFor`, `EnemyAbilityExecutor` and `EnemyProjectile`) keeps its effect; the Siphon Snare's cost-1 cast is perfect-blockable; no production player ability authors a Guard-Crush cost, so the term is inert in the sim. (3) Bookkeeping inside `BlockTiming`: bit 17 (observed) and bit 18 (the escape tick), so a tick the normal-control path never observes (hitstun, daze, knockdown, the tech lock, a grab, a capture, a hang) is finalized as lowered; and bits 20–23, a perfect-block count cycling 1..15, read only by the flash and the drill. **Amended by CM-FIXB-SIM (B5):** a tick that starts in hitstun or daze also finalizes the stance as dropped, so a hit-2 escape on the first tick after a hit keeps no window armed before the hit; the lowered age after a hit or a shatter now starts one tick earlier, matching Story (`FighterPerfectBlockTests.AStanceAHitFromBehindBreaksKeepsNoWindowThroughTheHitTwoEscape`). (4) A stance raised during the fighter's own hitstop never arms, in both modes. (6) The flash: (0.85, 1, 1) for 0.12 s on the hit-flash channel (the arbiter has no outline flash), the ordinary block shake and the Guard Impact haptic; no cue row. (7) Drill 1 counts any change of the 323 count except to 0. **Recorded limits:** a perfect block inside a rollback resimulation that is later corrected can flash twice (the driver's cache is cosmetic); a Story stance raised on its press tick is not live that tick (pre-existing), so a hit in that tick's flush lands; nobody has seen the flash. | Unassigned | Plan §5 row 11. |
