| ~~**FIX-SIM-THROW-GRANT-OUTLIVES-THROW**~~ — a grabber stayed invulnerable for about 12 ticks after its throw (found by CM-FIXA-SIM; fixed 2026-10-16 by CM-HIT) | F23: the grabber's throw-animation invulnerability covers the animation only. | **Fixed (CM-HIT `2b9214c`, merged `bb5ba58`).** The 12-frame grant `AdvanceGrabPhase` gives the grabber at the throw animation sat frozen through the grab-busy branch (which skips the normal decrement), and `ResolveThrow` zeroes only the victim's, so the grabber left every throw with about 12 free invulnerable ticks against projectiles, constructs and hazards (CM-FIXA-SIM §5, the same root cause as the tech's grant). `ApplyHitReaction` now decrements `InvulnerabilityFrames` while `GrabPhase == FighterGrabRules.PhaseThrowAnimation`: 12 on the animation's first tick, 11 … 1 after it, 0 on the throw's own tick, which is hittable. A larger unrelated grant on the same counter now also counts down through the animation. Pinned by `FighterGrabTests.TheGrabbersThrowInvulnerabilityEndsWithTheThrow`. | Unassigned | Closed. |
| **USER-FIGHTER-NO-COUNTER-HIT** — no counter-hit or punish reward in Fighter (decision 4.4, a record) | **User decision 2026-10-16 (4.4):** "Counter-hit / punish reward in Fighter [FGS-19]" — "**None.**" | None is built. The D8 Punish window stays Story-only (`USER-PUNISH-WINDOW`), and the perfect block gives no reflect and no attacker stun (3.4). | Unassigned | A new user ruling reopens it. |
| **USER-SIM-SHORT-HOP** — the Fighter sim gains Story's ×2.5 short hop (decision 5.1) | **User decision 2026-10-16 (5.1):** "No short hop in the sim [PFC-09]" — "**Add Story's rule to the sim**: gravity ×2.5 while rising with Jump released (about 45–55 % of full height). Prerequisite: bug fix 1.1(5) (CPU and Mirror hold Jump for full hops). Re-run the CPU recovery matrix on the three Open stages." **Q4** keeps ×2.5 (`USER-SHORT-HOP-MULTIPLIER-KEPT`); **Q5** limits the band to the fighter's own jump in both modes (`USER-SHORT-HOP-OWN-JUMP-ONLY`). Closes the short-hop half of `DEFER-H7-STORY-TERMINAL` (`design-godot.md` :957, :977, :987 and :1464–1467 call the short hop Story-only). | **Built (CM-MOVE `4d3c69d`, `54ab809`, merged `23fcd22`).** One constant, `UniversalMovementRules.ShortHopGravityMultiplier = 2.5f`, which `StoryAirPhysics.ShortHopGravityMultiplier` forwards; the sim converts it once (`FighterAirPhysics.ShortHopGravityStep`). `FighterAirPhysics.StepMovement(vy, fastFalling, floating, jumpedThisAirtime, jumpHeld, riseFromOwnJump)` picks one band per tick, first match wins: fast-fall; the float; **the short hop** (`!jumpHeld && riseFromOwnJump && vy > 0`, gravity × 2.5); the apex hang, now only while `jumpHeld` (`USER-APEX-HANG-HELD`); the fall; base. The old four-argument overload is `jumpHeld: true, riseFromOwnJump: false`, exactly the old step, so the CPU's arc predictions, `CpuRecoveryProfile`, the harnesses and the aerial cast step still plan full hops. "Jump held" is component 323's latch (`PROVISIONAL-SIM-SHORT-HOP-LATCH`). The prerequisite is met (`FIX-CPU-JUMP-HOLD`), and `CpuRecoveryMatrixTests` re-ran green on Paris, Vesuvius and Nassau (all nine kits recover as before; its kinematic drills model no ledge or platform pop). Heights per hero are in `PROVISIONAL-SIM-SHORT-HOP-HEIGHT`. The launch states keep `StepLaunch`. Suites: `FighterShortHopTests` (5), `FighterMovementFeelTests` +3, `StoryAirPhysicsTests` +3, `StoryMovementFeelTests` +1. | Unassigned | Plan §5 row 14 (taps against holds in both modes; the hop's height against 45–55 %) and row 6 (short-hop air strikes); the design master states the short hop for both modes. |
| **PROVISIONAL-SIM-SHORT-HOP-LATCH** — how the sim knows Jump is held (5.1, 6.1) | The ruling gives "rising with Jump released". The sim's input frames carry `Pressed`, `Held` and `Released` edges (`Released` is already on the wire, bytes 10–11; no protocol change). | *Provisional, agent-chosen (CM-MOVE decisions 1–2, amended by CM-FIXB-PADUI):* (1) `FighterMovementSystem.UpdateJumpLatch` writes 323 `ActionFlags` bit 1 `JumpReleasedSincePress` from the tick's raw edges (`Pressed & Jump` clears it, `Released & Jump` sets it) above the hitstop gate and every input discard, so for any `PlayerInputFrame.Create` stream it equals "Jump not held" tick for tick, through a freeze and a cast; hand-built frames with no `Released` never set it and stay full hops (plan §1.8). (2) Bit 3 `RiseFromOwnJump`'s set and clear points are Q5's (`USER-SHORT-HOP-OWN-JUMP-ONLY`), checked at the top of each normal tick before its gravity; a kit motion or float window clears it for good. **CM-FIXB-PADUI (B14 / B22 (a), `663ec7d`, merged `14374d7`):** the one-tick menu-press consume (`InputManager.ConsumeHeldPresses`) zeroed `Released` as well as `Pressed`, so a Jump let go of during a pause never reached the sim as released and the rest of that airtime kept the full rise and the held hang; the consume now spends press edges only (`UpdateJumpLatch` is the only production reader of `Released`), pinned by `PadMenuConfirmCancelTests.AJumpLetGoOfDuringAPauseReachesTheFighterSimAsReleased`. Two edge losses remain (`VERIFY-SIM-JUMP-LATCH-EDGE-LOSS`). | Unassigned | A ruling, or plan §5 row 14 leaves it alone. |
| **USER-SHORT-HOP-OWN-JUMP-ONLY** — the short-hop band cuts only the fighter's own jump, both modes (plan question Q5) | **Plan question Q5 (2026-10-16, plan §0.1):** "×2.5 applies to any released rise (post-hitstun, kit lifts)" — read literally, as Story's `SelectBand` did, the band also cut rises the fighter did not jump into: a post-hitstun rise, the Tempest owner lift, an airborne non-launching hit. The drafted default kept that reading. **Ruling (§0.2):** "**Own jump only, both modes** (Story's band gains the same gate)." Not the default. | **Built (CM-MOVE, merged `23fcd22`; both modes).** Sim: 323 `ActionFlags` bit 3 `RiseFromOwnJump`, set by an accepted ground jump and air jump (`ApplyNormalMovement`) and by the ledge climb jump (`FighterLedgeRules.Process`), cleared by `TickMovementLatches` when grounded, in hitstun or daze, grab-busy, hanging or climbing a ledge, in a kit motion or float window, as an Ultimate caster or captive, and once Shakespeare's Tempest has lifted its caster (`CastLiftedTheCaster`). Story: `StoryAirPhysics.SelectBand` / `StepVertical` gain `riseFromOwnJump` (default true), and `PlayerController`'s `_riseFromOwnJump` latch (`PlayerController.CornerForgiveness.cs`, seam `RiseFromOwnJump`) is written with `JumpedThisAirtime` (now a property that sets both) and cleared in Stunned, Thrown, Dazed, Grabbing, UsingMovementAbility, UsingUltimate, LedgeHanging, Dead and Respawning and while the Tempest's active frames lift the hero. Story's launch branch keeps its own short-hop clause (it stays in `DEFER-H7-STORY-TERMINAL`; plan §8). Pinned by `FighterShortHopTests.APostHitstunRiseAndTheTempestLiftAreNotCutButAReleasedOwnJumpIs`, `StoryAirPhysicsTests.TheShortHopCutsOnlyTheHerosOwnJump` and `StoryMovementFeelTests.AReleasedHopHasNoHangAndARiseAfterAStunIsNotCut`. | Unassigned | Plan §5 row 13 (a post-hitstun rise is never cut) and row 14. |

Pinned by `FighterShortHopTests.TapsOfOneTwoAndThreeTicksRiseMonotonicallyAndStayUnderFiftyFivePercentOfTheHeldApex`. | Unassigned | Plan §5 row 14; a user ruling on whether the 1-tick tap's 41.6–41.9 % stands (an open question in the closeout's list). |
| **VERIFY-SIM-JUMP-LATCH-EDGE-LOSS** — two ways the sim's Jump latch can miss an edge | `PROVISIONAL-SIM-SHORT-HOP-LATCH`: the latch equals "Jump not held" for any real input stream. | Recorded by CM-FIXB-PADUI (§6), not changed: (a) **the converse of B14** — Jump pressed during a pause and held through the resume while the latch already read "released" (a short hop in progress before the pause): the consume spends the press, so the sim keeps cutting the rise × 2.5 although Jump is now held; Story, which reads `Held`, does not; (b) `InputManager.AssignDevice`'s `_frames.Remove` drops a held Jump's release edge on a mid-air device switch (FollowLastUsed, pressing another device's button while holding Jump), so that airtime keeps the full rise and the held hang with Jump up. Each lasts at most one airtime. Making the latch read `Held` would turn every hand-built test frame into a short hop (plan §1.8). | Unassigned | A ruling, or a fix that carries the edge (a sim pin with `PlayerInputFrame.Create` input). |
| **USER-FIGHTER-LEDGE-GETUPS** — Fighter ledge get-ups and first-grab intangibility (decision 5.2) | **User decision 2026-10-16 (5.2):** "Ledge exits are climb-jump or drop only [PFC-12]" — "**Add get-up options**: neutral climb, ledge roll, ledge attack, plus brief intangibility on the first grab of an airtime. Fighter now; Story's `LedgeGrabPoint` later. Needs new sim state." Amends the 2026-08-10 sim ledge grab (`docs/GAMEPLAY_FEEL_2026-08-10_PLAN.md` §2.11; `design-godot.md` :790, "Up/toward-stage is not a separate climb input", and :994–1003). | **Built (CM-MOVE `51373d0`, merged `23fcd22`; the orchestrator's `fa18442`; amended by CM-FIXB-SIM `33fc9b5` and CM-FIXB-CPU `2fbfa6b`).** `FighterLedgeRules.Process` → `HangTick`, one action a tick: a Jump press climb-jumps (unchanged; now through `OnActionAccepted(Jump)` and the own-jump bit); a Roll press without Block or the Echo Step bit starts the ledge roll; a BasicAttack press without Block or the Grab bit starts the ledge attack; Down held releases (unchanged); a **fresh** toward-stage or Up tilt (\|stick\| > 30) once the hang is 6 frames old and 323 `ActionFlags` bit 2 `LedgeStickRearmed` is set starts the neutral climb. The climbs take 305's universal-movement values 8–10 (`LedgeClimb`, `LedgeRoll`, `LedgeAttackClimb`) and move the fighter linearly from the hang to `FighterStageGeometry.TryGetStandPosition` (0.5 u inside the edge); the neutral climb hands the tick back after 20 ticks, the ledge roll after 8 starts the ordinary roll toward the stage, and the ledge attack after 8 starts the strike (`FighterBasicAttackRules.StartLedgeAttack`, 305 `AttackFlags` 512 `FlagLedgeAttack`; `FighterCombatSystem.ApplyLedgeAttackSwing`: string hit 1's box at the latched facing, hit 1's knockback, one basic, a 30-frame block-cancelable hitstun, no launch); a hit cancels a climb. `StartGetUp` calls `OnActionAccepted(GetUpActionKind(phase))` first (`Swing` for the ledge attack, so it ends respawn and Defy protection) and then max-assigns the climb's intangibility. The first grab of an airtime max-assigns 30 intangible frames. Presentation plays `ledge_pull_up` for the climbs, then `roll` or `basic_attack_1`; the Move List has a Fighter-only `movelist_ledge_options` row. **`fa18442`:** the ledge attack can be perfect-blocked (its hit site now passes the victim's 323), and a hanging CPU stays climb-jump only (`VERIFY-CPU-LEDGE-GETUPS`). **CM-FIXB-SIM (B8 / B19, B23):** the first-grab intangibility carried off the hang into the climb jump and every get-up, removing their vulnerable frames; it now ends at every hang exit (`EndFirstGrabIntangibility`, `PROVISIONAL-LEDGE-GETUP-FRAMES`). Story's `LedgeGrabPoint` has no get-ups (plan §8). Suites: `FighterLedgeGetUpTests` (11, pure C#), `FighterStageGeometryTests.HeldJumpsEscapeEveryOpenStagePitAndTheNeutralClimbStandsOnTheFloor`, and in place `FighterLedgeTests` (the mash now holds Block), `MoveListScreenTests` and `CpuRecoveryMatrixTests` (the real-simulation CPU ledge drill). | Unassigned | Plan §5 row 17 (whether the intangibility or the ledge attack is too safe, judged on the CM-FIXB-SIM frames; whether the fresh-tilt climb is easy on purpose and hard by accident); the design master's ledge lines and the §2.11 supersession note. |
| **PROVISIONAL-LEDGE-GETUP-FRAMES** — the ledge get-ups' numbers and rules (5.2) | The ruling names the three get-ups and "brief intangibility" on the first grab. | *Provisional, agent-chosen (CM-MOVE decisions 7–9, amended by CM-FIXB-SIM):* `BasicComboRules`' ledge section: `LedgeFirstGrabIntangibleFrames` 30, `LedgeGetUpMinHangFrames` 6, `LedgeNeutralClimbFrames` 20 with `LedgeNeutralClimbIntangibleFrames` 12 (its last 8 vulnerable frames stand at the stand position), `LedgeActionClimbFrames` 8 for the roll and the attack, `LedgeAttackStartupFrames` 8 / `ActiveFrames` 4 / `RecoveryFrames` 14, `LedgeAttackDamageMultiplier` 1.0, `LedgeAttackHitstunFrames` 30, `LedgeAttackLaunches` false, `LedgeStandInsetUnits` 0.5; a tilt threshold of 30 of 127 (the sim's "direction held" elsewhere); the linear climb path over the intangible frames; the ledge attack's Momentum as string hit 1's 20 frames; the neutral-climb re-arm stays set for the rest of the hang once the stick has been neutral, away or down; the Move List row is Fighter-only. **CM-FIXB-SIM (B8 / B19):** `FighterLedgeRules.EndFirstGrabIntangibility` runs at every hang exit — the climb jump, `StartGetUp` (before the chokepoint and the climb's max-assign), the Down release, the auto-release and `ResolveLedgeTrump` — and zeroes the shared counter only when it is no larger than the first grab's remainder, so a longer respawn grant survives and a shorter one merged into the grab ends with it; the climb jump and the Down release are hittable on their own tick, and the ledge attack and the ledge roll are intangible for exactly their 8 climb frames. Every first-grab CPU recovery now leaves with an ordinary vulnerable climb jump (before, about 20–25 intangible airborne frames), so CPU and bot recovery numbers from before and after are not comparable. **Recorded limits:** a grab that seizes a fighter in the neutral climb's vulnerable frames leaves the climb in the 305 slot until the throw's hitstun cancels it (nothing reads it meanwhile); on the legacy flat arena a double tap of Down drops through the historical drop-through ground, as Down+Jump always did. | Unassigned | Plan §5 row 17. |

Measured with the CPU unchanged (90 drills per band over the three Open stages): Easy 38 get-ups in 170 hangs, Normal 15 in 135, Hard 6 in 173, nearly all neutral climbs. **Closed by the orchestrator's Phase B fix-up `fa18442`:** `FighterCpuController.Sample` sends a hanging CPU no stick, Roll or BasicAttack (CM-MOVE's measured one-line fix: 0 get-ups on every stage and band), and `CpuRecoveryMatrixTests.CpuClimbJumpOnlyEnforced = true`. CPU-driven Fighter hashes moved. The line zeroed the stick on a struck hang's thaw tick too, which CM-FIXB-CPU fixed (`FIX-CPU-HANG-DI-ZEROED`). | Unassigned | Closed. |

Amends A03 (`design-godot.md` :777, :829, :996: once per airtime, refreshed by landing, a ledge grab or a stock loss; `COMBAT_VALIDATION.md` :32). | **Built (CM-HIT, merged `bb5ba58`).** Sim: `ApplyHitReaction` calls `FighterAirDodgeRules.Refresh` on every unfrozen tick while `HitstunFrames > 0` (stateless). Story: `ApplyStun` clears `_airDodgeUsed`. Any applied hitstun refreshes it, a throw's and a zone tick's included; a blocked, absorbed, armoured or Defy-zeroed hit applies none. Pinned by `FighterAirDodgeTests.BeingHitRefreshesTheAirDodgeSoItCanDodgeAgainAfterHitstun` and `StoryAirDodgeTests.BeingHitRefreshesTheAirDodgeSoItCanDodgeAgainAfterHitstun` (whose fixture `85ce072` made hold Jump, because a released Jump now short-hops with no hang). | Unassigned | Plan §5 row 19 (whether escapes become too easy); the design master's A03 lines and `COMBAT_VALIDATION.md` :32. |

A held full jump is unchanged tick for tick (`FighterShortHopTests.AHeldJumpIsUnchangedTickForTick`). **CM-FIXB-STORY (B11):** Story's movement-ability gravity now reads Jump live, so its hang follows the same rule (`FIX-STORY-MOVEMENT-ABILITY-GRAVITY`). The bots' flight model holds a flight's final jump through the hang band (`PROVISIONAL-BOT-FLIGHT-MODEL`). Inside an aerial Special the two modes still differ slightly (`VERIFY-AERIAL-SPECIAL-HANG-PARITY`). Pinned by `StoryAirPhysicsTests.TheApexHangNeedsJumpHeld` and `AReleasedHopDropsThroughItsPeakWithNoHangAndAHeldJumpKeepsIt`, and `FighterMovementFeelTests.TheShortHopAndHangBandOrderHoldsAndAHeldJumpIsTheLegacyStep`. | Unassigned | Plan §5 row 14 (a released hop drops through its peak; the hop is not too floaty). |

| **PROVISIONAL-SIM-LIP-POP** — the sim's one-way lip pop (6.2, Q6) | Q6: "a fighter within 0.2 u below a one-way top at its apex or falling lands on it, resolving before ledge capture". | *Provisional, agent-chosen (CM-MOVE decision 6 and the dossier):* `FighterMovementSystem.TryLipPop`, one call before the ledge capture, on a normal-control airborne tick (no hitstun, daze, Special or A02 cast, kit motion or ledge climb) with vertical velocity ≤ 0, for feet that came from below a one-way platform's top and are now at most `UniversalMovementRules.SimOneWayLipPopUnits` (0.2, a `double`, so `FighterAirPhysics.OneWayLipPopBand` is exactly `FP64.FromDouble(0.2)`) under it, over the platform: the fighter is placed on the top, its vertical velocity zeroed, grounded and its jumps refilled (the air dodge and the regrab budget refresh through the ordinary grounded path). Floor segments are never popped (`VERIFY-SIM-PIT-FLOOR-SNAP`); a fighter dropping through is exempt for the whole 30-frame `DropThroughFrames` window. **Measured:** with `TryLipPop` disabled, `FighterLedgeTests`, `FighterLedgeGetUpTests`, `FighterStageGeometryTests` and `CpuRecoveryMatrixTests` were green either way, so no existing ledge case depends on it; in the real-simulation CPU drills one capture became a landing (Vesuvius Hard 19 → 18 hangs) and one recovery reached a ledge it had missed (Paris Normal 89 → 90). `FighterCpuController`'s arc predictions do not model the pop. | Unassigned | Plan §5 row 15 (the sim pop reads as a clean landing and does not steal a wanted ledge grab). |

| **VERIFY-SIM-PIT-FLOOR-SNAP** — the sim lifts a fighter onto a floor segment from any depth (recorded by 6.2's dossier) | An Open stage's pit is a pit: a fighter that falls into it recovers by its ledges or its movement, or falls to the bottom blast zone. The design does not state a depth limit for the ground snap. | Pre-existing since Package 11 A9 and unchanged: the sim's ground snap is per-x (`FighterStageGeometry.HasFloorSupport`), and a fighter anywhere below the floor plane but above the bottom blast zone that drifts back over a floor segment is placed on it (`Position.y = 0`, grounded, jumps refilled) at once, from any depth, with no ledge or climb. (1) The observation carries `HitboxID`, to tell a pooled shot from a melee hit. (2) Kinds partition by owner first, then delivery (`standard`, `elite`, `boss`, `mirror`, `projectile` for a standard or elite mob's shot, `hazard`, `status`, `other`); **corrected by CM-FIXA-PRES:** "excluding bosses and the Mirror" is recomputed exactly from the log's new unique `key`, not from `by_kind`. (3) Same-frame cross-source doubles are counted apart (`xsrc_same_frame`), outside the half-open [F − 30, F) window. (4) A hit can be cross-source and same-source at once. (5) **Extended by CM-FIXA-PRES:** every periodic tick — status ticks, zone pulses (`HitDelivery.Tick`) and periodic environmental ticks (drowning, stage-hazard damage over time, marked at the source by `ApplyEnvironmentalDamage`'s observation-only `periodicTick`) — is never a hit, re-hit or predecessor, and is counted apart (`ticks`, `tick_damage`, `ticks_by_kind`). (6) Discrete hazard contacts stay in the headline as hits and predecessors; **corrected by CM-FIXA-PRES:** `by_kind` books a re-hit by the second hit's kind, so it removes a hazard only as the second hit, and the new combat-only figures (`xsrc_30_combat`, `xsrc_20_combat`, `xsrc_damage_30_combat`, `xsrc_combat_pct`) leave hazards out on both sides. (7) Every observation of one physics frame maps to one telemetry frame. (8) Extra keys: `xsrc_damage_30`, `xsrc_pct`, the gap histogram, the log's `xgap` / `xk` / `key` / `xc`, and the `unobserved_hits` self-check (0 unless a damage path bypasses the three raise sites). (9) `damage_by_source` and the per-enemy hit counts keep their nearest-live-enemy attribution, for before / after comparability. **Recorded limits:** hazard identity is a heuristic (unrecognised hazards share `hazard:env`); the Jackal Priest's decoy bursts are `boss` by their attack-ID prefix; blocked, absorbed and refused hits take no HP and are not observed; status ticks are not attributed to their applier; construct deliveries (the clone's coil arcs, turret bolts, serpent-nest bites at 6 HP a second) remain hits. | **USER-LAST-USED-DEVICE** — player 1 follows the last-used device, and Fighter select is press-to-join, both modes (decision 9.1) | **User decision 2026-10-16 (9.1):** "Keyboard is always player 1; a lone pad is player 2 [KIT-03]" — "**Last-used device + press-to-join**: player 1 follows the most recently used device until a second human joins; on Fighter select the first press on any device claims the next free slot. Amends the keyboard-as-P1 join workflow (`design-godot.md` :660, :715, :1834–1837, :4048–4053; `COMFORT_SETTINGS.md` :134). | **Built (CM-INPUT `842de2a`, `5bb49d9`, merged `da08d70`; amended by CM-FIXA-INPUT `2be9623`, merged `98e5e4a`, CM-PADUI `c8b1cf7`, merged `740d712`, and CM-FIXB-PADUI `663ec7d`, merged `14374d7`; local input only, no protocol, snapshot, hash or save change).** `FTT.Core.LocalJoinMode { FollowLastUsed = 0, Joined = 1 }` on `InputManager`. **FollowLastUsed** (the default; Story, Versus CPU, the Holodeck, the drills): `PollDeviceActivity` runs once per tick before any slot is sampled, reading device state only (a new pad button press among 0–20, a bound pad axis or the left stick crossing out of its deadzone, a new press of a key bound to a gameplay, direct or menu action, a mouse button; never mouse motion), and moves slot 0 to the active device; the switching press reaches the game as a fresh edge, and the switch drops the Toggle Block latch and the chord window; slot 1 is never filled. **Joined** (the Local Versus select): `BeginLocalJoin` empties every slot (since CM-FIXA-INPUT, slot 0 included; an input source keeps its slot), and the first two devices to make a join press claim player 1 and player 2; the join press is consumed on the select so it acts on nothing else; `ui_cancel` belongs to the device that pressed it; a pad that drops during the join refills its slot on reconnect; `EndLocalJoin` hands an unclaimed slot 0 back to the keyboard; `ApplyJoinLifetimeForDestination` keeps the join for the character select and any Fighter VS load (Rematch and Back-to-select keep it) and ends it elsewhere. The select paints player 2's token only when joined or locked, and shows `select_press_to_join` on P2's panel and, since CM-PADUI, `select_press_to_join_player_one` on P1's while slot 0 is unclaimed in Local Versus. A Story controller loss resolves on any key press (`PauseMenu.ResolveControllerLost(KeyboardDevice)`), and outside a join `LocalFighterPause` resolves a lost player-1 controller on a key or that pad's face button. Haptics and the dialogue glyph follow the active device unchanged. **CM-FIXB-PADUI (B18):** a pad that disconnects raises `DeviceAssigned(slot, UnassignedDevice)` from `ApplyDeviceTopology`, so the select's join prompt returns. Test seam `InputManager.DeviceActivityProbeForTesting` (it replaces every raw device read; non-suite `FakeInputDeviceProbe`). | **PROVISIONAL-DEVICE-FOLLOW-RULES** — the device-follow and join details (9.1) | The ruling gives "last-used device" and "the first press on any device claims the next free slot". | *Provisional, agent-chosen (CM-INPUT, amended by CM-FIXA-INPUT, CM-PADUI and CM-FIXB-PADUI):* (1) The polled sets: pad buttons 0–20, the left stick at the device's own `StickProfile` deadzone, and, since CM-FIXA-INPUT, every bound pad axis (`PolledPadAxis`: one entry per axis and direction from the gameplay, direct and menu actions' `InputEventJoypadMotion` events, at most 32, "out" while `raw × sign` reaches the action's deadzone, so LT and RT move player 1 onto the pad and that pull reaches the game as a fresh press); keyboard keys from the gameplay, direct and menu actions, plus three mouse buttons and any bound one; the key list rebuilds on `InputBindingService.Generation`; a device's first poll only records its state. (2) Tie-break when several devices move in one tick: the current device keeps slot 0, then the keyboard, then the lowest pad id. (3) The join-press set: a face button, or any key, pad button or (since CM-FIXA-INPUT) mouse button bound to `ui_accept`, `gameplay_interact` or `gameplay_basic_attack`; this amends design :1836. (4) In a join a reconnecting pad refills the slot a disconnect emptied; in FollowLastUsed an emptied slot 0 falls back to the keyboard. (5) **CM-FIXA-INPUT:** no slot is pre-claimed at `BeginLocalJoin` (before, slot 0 kept whatever drove the menus, so after keyboard-driven menus pad A joined as player 2 and pad B could not join at all); `ui_cancel` is routed to the pressing device's own slot (a key or mouse event is the keyboard; a joypad event is its pad), only player 1's cancel leaves the screen, an unclaimed device's cancel is ignored once anyone has joined but still leaves the screen before then (so a keyboard user can back out with Escape), and a pad button that is also a token confirm never cancels during the token flow. (6) **CM-PADUI and CM-FIXB-PADUI:** the select's footer is player 1's — another device's GUI confirm that is also one of its token inputs is swallowed, and since CM-FIXB-PADUI (B17) every directional focus move from another device is swallowed (a keyboard player 2's Up arrow walked player 1's focus onto Back); a pure menu key (Enter, Tab) still passes, so a joined keyboard player 2's Enter presses the focused button. (7) The prompt copy and the FollowLastUsed resolution in `LocalFighterPause`. **Recorded limits:** the poll does not run while the tree is paused unless a paused UI captures a frame (the controller-lost paths use input events for that reason); a stick that drifts across its deadzone edge can take player 1 from the keyboard each time; a Fighter stage or `TestArena.tscn` launched directly never begins a join (a dev path); a mouse user's first click on the Local Versus select only joins; LAN reports no chord readiness; there are no device glyphs. | Unassigned | Plan §5 row 22. |

A saved pad half of exactly {9, 10} or {4, 5} is dropped at load (`InputBindingSet.RepairStaleUltimatePadChord`), and since CM-FIXA-INPUT a pre-update "chord off" that leaves the pad Ultimate no route is turned back on (`RestoreUnreachableUltimateChord`, run by `SaveManager.ApplySavedInputBindings`). A full-meter chord inside the lockout now casts nothing: the Ultimate is refused and both components stay masked (`UltimateChordStoryTests.AFullMeterChordInsideTheLockoutCastsNoMovementAbility`). Suites: `UltimateChordWindowTests` (9, pure C#), `UltimateChordStoryTests` (2), and cases in `GamepadDefaultBindingTests`, `GamepadOverrideRepairTests` and `InputBindingSchemaTests`. | Unassigned | Plan §5 row 21 (whether 3 frames of latency are felt; whether the chord still leaks); the design master's :2286, by hand in each copy, and `COMFORT_SETTINGS.md` C01c. |
| **PROVISIONAL-ULTIMATE-CHORD-LENIENCY** — the chord window's details (9.2) | The ruling gives "2–3 frames", "while the Ultimate is affordable", the live toggle and no direct bind. | *Provisional, agent-chosen (CM-INPUT decisions, amended by CM-FIXA-INPUT and CM-FIXB-PADUI):* (1) `LeniencyFrames` 3. (2) While lenient, any tick with both components held is the chord, including a partner that arrives after the first component's window ran out, which then masks the component already emitted (the old conjunction fired there too). (3) A masked component stays masked through a loss of affordability until it is released, so letting go of a spent chord never fires its components. (4) Affordability: Story — a full meter, the Ultimate slot unlocked, not Suppressed, not time-frozen, reported at the top of `_PhysicsProcess` (hitstop and the tech lock return early from its end); Fighter — Influence at the cap, not a busy caster, not captured, slot 1 only when no CPU plays it; the ability lockout is excluded in both modes; a report older than `UltimateReadinessMaxAgeTicks` (2) reads unaffordable; LAN pushes none, so its chord passes through. (5) The windows reset on a device hand-over. **CM-FIXA-INPUT:** a pause or focus loss forgets waits but keeps masks and holds (`ForgetChordWaits` → `UltimateChordWindow.ForgetWaits`): a shoulder released during the pause never returns as a tap, and a chord held across a pause fires no component on the way back. **CM-FIXB-PADUI (B22 (b)):** on a menu-consume tick a held component in Up or Waiting goes straight to Emitting, so it reaches the game held on that tick and its edge is spent with every other button (before, a shoulder pressed in a pause and held through the resume was emitted as a fresh press 3 ticks later, casting Special 2 or the movement ability). | Unassigned | Plan §5 row 21. |

| ~~**FIX-CPU-HITSTUN-HOLD-EATS-RECOVERY**~~ — a defended CPU's hitstun Block hold ate its recovery presses (found by the Phase B review, B0 / B20; fixed 2026-10-16 by CM-FIXB-CPU) | V7.4's CPU hitstun defense holds Block for the hit-2 escape and the tech; `CPU_RECOVERY.md`: the CPU recovers with its jumps and movement ability. | **Fixed (CM-FIXB-CPU `f6cf1cc`, merged `2fbfa6b`; input side; CPU-driven hashes move).** Pre-existing since V7.4 and Package 11 A9b, exposed by 3.2 (tumble outliving hitstun) and 5.2: the defense hold (Normal 45 %, Hard 85 %) stayed exclusive until the CPU was grounded, so every recovery Jump and movement-ability press was eaten, and a hanging CPU's climb jump too; a defended CPU whose hitstun ended over a pit fell into the blast zone, and one the ledge caught after hitstun hung, holding Block only, until the 300-frame auto-release; the eaten activation was still charged to Easy's and Medium's one per episode. `ApplyHitstunDefense` now returns `Block \| (held & (Jump \| MovementAbility))` once hitstun is over while airborne or hanging (`PostHitstunAirborneButtons`; Block plus BasicAttack or Roll is a chord, so those stay masked) and leaves the jump sustain running; during hitstun and the grounded 45-frame linger the hold stays exclusive; the activation is charged when MovementAbility goes out as a pressed edge, not at delivery (`AccountMovementActivationEmission`). Pinned by `FighterCpuHitstunDefenseTests.AHundredPercentDefenseCpuLaunchedOverAnOpenStagePitRecoversOnceItsHitstunEnds` (before: 8 of 9 runs lost the stock), `AHundredPercentDefenseCpuCaughtOnAPitFacingLedgeAfterHitstunClimbJumps` and `AMovementActivationTheDefenseHoldMasksIsNotChargedToTheEpisode`. | Unassigned | Closed. |
| **PROVISIONAL-CPU-HITSTUN-HOLD-PASS-THROUGH** — when the CPU's defense hold lets its recovery through (CM-FIXB-CPU) | The orchestrator's wording for the fix: "keep Block for the tech; once hitstun is over let the planner's Jump / movement-ability edges and the jump sustain through, keep masking BasicAttack and Roll". | *Provisional, agent-chosen (CM-FIXB-CPU §3 (1)–(2)):* (1) the pass-through applies whenever the CPU is airborne or hanging after hitstun, not only over a gap (two checkers would have limited it to hanging or offstage), so over a Sealed floor a defended CPU can take its planner's own Jump or movement press out of a post-hitstun tumble, which 3.2 allows, while Block stays held so a landing it does not act out of is still teched; (2) the activation is charged at emission, which is usually the delivery tick or one or two ticks later, so the cap's timing barely moves, and a press masked or overwritten before it goes out is no longer charged. | **USER-FIGHTER-SPECIAL-RECOVERY-CANCEL** — D3 in Fighter: one rule in both modes (decision 11.1) | **User decision 2026-10-16 (11.1):** "With 1.2's Special timing, extend D3 (the on-hit Special recovery cancel) to Fighter?" — "**Extend D3 to Fighter**: one rule in both modes — a whiff keeps its full recovery; a Special that landed a direct hit cancels into roll/jump from recovery frame 0, into a basic or the movement ability from frame 6." Amends `USER-SPECIAL-RECOVERY-CANCEL` and `PROVISIONAL-SPECIAL-RECOVERY-CANCEL-RULES` (Story only → both modes) and the design's `UsingSpecial` rows (`design-godot.md` :783–786, :1500). | **Built (CM-CANCEL `dec3c21`, `e5c3fc6`, merged `8647fd4`; amended by CM-FIXB-SIM `aa77531`, merged `33fc9b5`).** One D3 table in `BasicComboRules` (after the ability-lockout region): `SpecialRecoveryEvadeCancelFrame` 0, `SpecialRecoveryActionCancelFrame` 6, `SpecialCancelBufferFrames` 6 and the predicates `SpecialRecoveryAllowsEvade`, `SpecialRecoveryAllowsAction` and `SpecialCancelBufferHolds`; `StoryCombatRules`' five D3 names forward to them, and Story's behaviour is unchanged (`StorySpecialRecoveryCancelTests` green, unedited by CM-CANCEL). Sim (`FighterSpecialCancelRules`, new `FighterSpecialCancel.cs` and `FighterMovementSystem.SpecialCancel.cs`): component 323's `SpecialFlags` 8 `FlagLandedDirectHit` is set by `NoteLanded` only for a `Landed` or `Absorbed` outcome with damage > 0, on a busy cast of the same slot whose projected `Delivery` is `DirectHit` — at Divine Piercing's thrusts, the Special projectile and ground-wave contact, the zone pulse, the projectile burst, the Lorentz chain's arcs, the Foresight answer and the melee `ApplyIntent`; Golden Ratio, the Lorentz Pulse and the Requiem burst (zone pulses Story authors as direct hits) arm it, while the Rift, the Vortex and the Tempest (ticks), constructs and both of Cleopatra's Specials never do. `SpecialCancelBuffer` holds one verb (roll, jump, basic, movement), its age and the stick at the press. The cast region feeds the buffer, then lets a grounded Block cancel the recovery (now also Foresight's whiff recovery and sidestep; `PressedButtons &= BlockButton`), else fires a landed cast's buffered verb once its frame gate is open and its ordinary path could start it (the basic as the variant of the direction held at the press; a jump never a drop-through), else discards the caster's input. Foresight's stance ends through `FighterForesightRules.CancelStance`, which cuts the sidestep's own invulnerability only. **CM-FIXB-SIM (B12):** neither recovery cancel may end Foresight while its counter strike is still owed, in both modes (sim `FighterForesightRules.IsAnswerOwed`, reached only after a caught projectile, whose answer lands the next tick; Story `TubmanForesight.IsAnswerPending`, which defers every answer one tick); the cancel waits one tick and the D3 press stays buffered. D3 in Fighter has no presentation of its own. Suites: `FighterSpecialRecoveryCancelTests` (14), `SpecialRecoveryCancelTableTests` (1, pure C#, which also scans `scripts/FighterSim/` for any Story rulebook name), `StorySpecialRecoveryCancelTests` +1, `TubmanKitTests` extended in place. | Unassigned | Plan §5 row 10 (whether landed ranged Specials, the Emancipator at 5 u above all, are now safe; Special → basic → Special routes with no scaling, 1.4); the design master's `UsingSpecial` rows for both modes. |
| **PROVISIONAL-FIGHTER-SPECIAL-RECOVERY-CANCEL** — the sim's D3 choices (11.1) | 11.1 gives one rule in both modes; the Story choices are `PROVISIONAL-SPECIAL-RECOVERY-CANCEL-RULES`. | *Provisional, agent-chosen (CM-CANCEL decisions 1–6):* (1) The buffer's verb order and exclusions are Story's `BufferSpecialCancelPress` exactly — roll, jump, basic, movement; the latest press wins; the Echo Step chord's Roll, the grab chord's BasicAttack and the Movement half of an Ultimate tick never buffer (the sim reads the chords from Block held with chords on, as its own resolver does). (2) A verb that cannot start yet stays buffered until it ages out (`CancelVerbCanStart` mirrors each verb's gates), so a cancel never ends the cast for a verb that then does nothing. (3) The hitstop-branch feed sits after `ClearIfInterrupted` (the plan's guide put it before), so an interrupted cast's buffer is emptied at once; both are before the discard. (4) The buffer opens empty without touching `Begin`: every feed that finds no busy cast zeroes it. (5) Foresight's sidestep cancel zeroes `InvulnerabilityFrames` only when it is at most `PhaseFrames + 1`, the sidestep's own grant; a larger grant stays. (6) The slot attribution of a projectile, zone or burst relies on a lifetime invariant rather than new state on components 302 / 309: none outlives its slot's earliest re-cast, pinned over the nine production kits by `FighterSpecialRecoveryCancelTests.NoDirectHitProjectileOrZoneOutlivesItsSlotsEarliestRecast`. | Unassigned | Plan §5 row 10. |
| **USER-COMBAT-MOVEMENT-JUDGE-BY-HAND** — what the 2026-10-16 rulings kept, or left to the pad session (a record) | **User decisions 2026-10-15 / 16:** 2.2 "**Measure first** (CPU-vs-CPU batch + a two-player pad session), then decide. D15 totals unchanged for now." 3.1 "**Keep held Block** (V7 pillar); bug fix 1.1(3) locks its 12 invulnerable frames." 3.3 "**Keep, measure first** …" (`VERIFY-WALL-PIN`). 5.4 "**Keep; judge 1v1 pace in the first two-player pad session.**" 6.4 "**Keep both builds** (2026-10-09 ruling)." 8.3 "**Keep 4–11; judge by hand in the pad session.**" 9.4 "**Pad session only**: no frame tables, combo enumerator or CPU-vs-CPU batch. The pad session (with a written checklist) decides 2.2 Ultimate damage, 3.3 walls, 5.4 speed and 8.3 hitstop. 7.2's bot re-hit counter is Story telemetry and is still in." 10.2 "**Keep as is**; revisit only if the pad session shows stalls." 10.3 "**Keep the single air strike; judge the air game in the pad session** (with the new short hop)." 10.4 "**Judge by hand**; on the pad-session checklist." The decisions file's pad-session checklist: "2.2 Fighter Ultimate damage share · 3.3 wall pinning / corner loops · 5.4 Fighter 1v1 pace after the speed cut · 8.3 hitstop 4–11 · 10.2 Sudden Death stalls · 10.3 the air game with short hops · 10.4 comeback strength · and the feel of everything above". | Nothing was built for these: the D15 Ultimate totals, the held-Block landing tech, the Fighter walls, the −20 % speed cut, both builds' jump spreads, hitstop 4–11, F22 Sudden Death, the single air strike and the Fighter comeback stack (orb heals, Rally, Defy, missing-HP knockback) are as they were, apart from what other rows changed around them (the Ultimate telegraph, the tech lock, Defy ending on attack, the short hop). No frame tables, combo enumerator or CPU-vs-CPU measurement batch exist (9.4); the CPU smoke and the re-hit counter are functional checks. The rows that carry each are `VERIFY-ULT-ACTIVATION-TUNING` (2.2), `VERIFY-WALL-PIN` (3.3), `USER-MOVEMENT-SPEED-CUT` (5.4), `PROVISIONAL-HITSTOP-WEIGHT` (8.3) and `USER-AERIAL-SINGLE-STRIKE` (10.3). | Unassigned | Plan §5 rows 1–7, the user's first two-player pad session; each finding that calls for a ruling goes to its row. |
| **PROVISIONAL-BOT-FLIGHT-MODEL** — the playtest bots' jump model under the held-Jump hang, the short hop and corner forgiveness (diagnostics only) | The bots fly as the controller does (6.1, Q5, 6.2); no design line covers the bots. | *Provisional, agent-chosen, diagnostics only (CM-MOVE):* `PlaytestNav.StepFlight` holds a flight's **final** jump through the apex-hang band (released at `vy ≥ ApexHangSpeedBandPixels`), while a jump followed by a planned air jump is still released at its apex, and it passes `riseFromOwnJump: f.Jumped`; `NavAgent`'s jump hold follows the same rule; `TryNudgeFlight` and `TryPopFlight` model the head-clip nudge and the rising lip pop from the controller's constants (`PlaytestFlightModelTests`, 2). **Comparability:** batches from before and after this update are not comparable for run time or short-hop and hang flights; 7.4's block (the bots brace hazards and discharges with Block) and 3.2's tumble move the damage and knockdown figures; only the after batch carries the `rehits` block (`PROVISIONAL-REHIT-COUNTER-RULES`). **Known gap (the 2026-10-16 after batch): Level 7's quay lock stalls the Lincoln `play` bot on Normal**, on both seeds and with 0 deaths, the two `stuck_lock` endings of `cmafter*` (the three of `cmbefore*` were all Level 7's boarding lock, the known skiff-channel gap). Diagnosed with a fine-trace probe of the same build (`--trace-every=3`, tag `cmprobe`, which reproduced the loop frame for frame): the last quay member on Normal (Easy's per-row prefix drops it) is a `laser_pistol_deckhand` on the launch yard's west end (3330, 672; `Level07Controller.LaunchYardY` 672, the yard 3300–3600). The before bot reached it with one planned double jump from the 2950/820 step (812 → 672, take-off 3028); the after planner never plans that edge on either seed, likely because the flight model now releases the first jump at its apex before a planned air jump and the hang needs Jump held, so the modelled double jump is shorter — inferred from the plan strings, not traced in the planner. Instead it loops two flights that fail in execution: (a) the 812 → 752 single hop onto the 3200/760 step, taken off about 90 px before its planned point (2935 against 3028), bonking under the 2900/640 ratline and falling back to the deck; (b) a deck → 752 double jump planned from under the launch yard (take-off 3360, heading west), started from a standstill by an agent arriving eastbound, which lags the plan and bonks its air jump on the yard's underside at x 3320. **Not a content block:** the human route deck → 2950/820 → 3200/760 → yard needs at most 80 px of rise per step, well inside Lincoln's single jump (about 133 px), and Joan clears the quay lock on Normal in every run; Level 7 is the only level where it appeared. | Unassigned | None as a game rule. A planner fix that plans the step → yard double jump again (or models the first jump's hold before an air jump), with a Level 7 Normal Lincoln rerun; reopen if another level shows the pattern. |

## The 2026-10-16 combat and movement update — index and design lines edited

The user's combat and movement decisions of 2026-10-15 and 2026-10-16 and the eight plan-question rulings (quoted in the rows of "Confirmed open deviations — combat and movement update, 2026-10-16" above) were planned in `docs/COMBAT_MOVEMENT_UPDATE_2026-10-16_PLAN.md` and built on 2026-10-16. **Unlike the 2026-10-11 and 2026-10-12 indexes, this closeout edits the design master (`D:/Projects/fighters-through-time-docs-3/design-godot-v7.md`) and the repository mirror `design-godot.md` together, hunk for hunk** (plan §1.1 and §7 step 5, the 2026-10-09 convention), and the contracts in both copies (the repository's and docs-3's). That design pass runs beside this ledger pass, so the last column states what each ruling requires — the lines the design pass edits — with `design-godot.md` line numbers at `e2c655a`. The `DOC-MASTER-UNBOUND-BINDING` lines (`:2286`, `:2288–2289`, `:4141`) are edited by hand in each copy, keeping each copy's own wording; 9.2 touches `:2286`. Lines owed by the 2026-10-11 and 2026-10-12 indexes stay owed, except that a paragraph this pass rewrites gets its owed line applied too (plan §1.1); where a cell below meets such a line it says so, and the earlier index's entry is struck once the design pass reports the hunk. The docs-3 copy of this ledger is not edited (plan §1.1). **Test baseline:** 3228 → **3518** (+290; the arithmetic is in the section intro above and in `AGENTS.md`'s validation section), three consecutive green full runs on 2026-10-17. **Nobody has played or looked at any of it.**

## Confirmed open deviations — retro v2 sprite and VFX pass, 2026-10-18

The user asked on 2026-10-18 for a pass over every roster character's spritesheets, animations and ability effects, in the retro-pulp style of the docs mockups (`fighters-through-time-docs-2/mockups/level_art_styles/04_chicago_tesla_retro.png` and the per-character retro style sets), and ruled on four questions the same day: loops get more frames, the art goes to 2× resolution, all nine characters are done before review, and image generation is paced across the plan's usage windows. The art is generated through Codex's `image_gen` and processed by `tools/retro_sprites/` (built on top of `d12e8eb` in the working tree, 2026-10-18 and 2026-10-19); the source records, prompts and QA sheets are in `fighters-through-time-docs-2/mockups/implementation_assets_v3/retro_roster_v2/README.md`. It is still development placeholder art under the V7.6 scope ruling, and the content manifest keeps `AssetStatus = Placeholder`. **Follow-up, 2026-10-20:** the user asked for three loop changes — "Idle should be a fighting stance with hands up ready to fight, right now it's just a standing breathing animation. Run needs more frames, it looks jerky and doesn't look complete. Falling moves the characters legs up and down until they hit the ground." — built the same day (USER-IDLE-FIGHTING-STANCE, USER-RUN-EIGHT-FRAME-CYCLE and USER-FALL-HOLDS-POSE, with the agent choices in PROVISIONAL-RETRO-V2-LOOP-RULES): 18 new sheets and two redrawn idles; the suite stays at 3529, three green full runs on 2026-10-20 (1 m 39 s, 1 m 40 s and 1 m 41 s). **Test baseline:** 3518 → **3529**, three consecutive green full runs on 2026-10-19 (+11: `RetroSpriteScaleNormalizerTests` 4 → 8, `OwnershipOutlineTests` 4 → 5, `CharacterPresentationTests` 7 → 8 and the new `AbilityVisualPresentationTests` 5; `CharacterPresentationTests`' frame-count and VFX-cell cases and `OutlineGlowShaderTests`' thickness range changed in place). **Nobody has looked at the new art in play.**

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **USER-SPRITE-LOOP-FRAMES** — looping `idle` 4 frames, `run` 6 (8 since 2026-10-20); every other animation keeps three key poses | **User ruling 2026-10-18** (question "Frame count", option "Expand loops (Recommended)"): "Run gets 6 frames and idle gets 4; everything else stays at 3 key poses. I'd amend the GDD line, the builder and the frame-count tests." Amends M28 and the V7 style decision: `design-godot.md` :3810 (the style decision), the frame table :3819–3820, :3928 (M28) and :4877 (the pipeline note), edited identically in the master and the mirror. Attacks and abilities keep three poses because `AttackPoseRules` picks wind-up / strike / follow-through by phase. | **Built.** `tools/retro_sprites/pack.py` `PLAYBACK` and `FRAME_COUNT` (idle 5 fps × 4, run 12 fps × 6, the rest unchanged; since 2026-10-20 the idle plays at 6 fps and the run has 8 frames at a per-character rate, USER-RUN-EIGHT-FRAME-CYCLE and PROVISIONAL-RETRO-V2-LOOP-RULES); `CharacterPresentationTests.ExpectedFrameCount` (idle 4, run 8, else 3). | Unassigned | The idle and run loops read smoothly in Story and Fighter. |
| **USER-IDLE-FIGHTING-STANCE** — the idle is a fighting stance | **User request 2026-10-20:** "Idle should be a fighting stance with hands up ready to fight, right now it's just a standing breathing animation." `design-godot.md` :3819 (the frame table's idle row) now says so, in the master and the mirror. | **Built.** Each character's `roster_spec.CHARACTERS[…]["stance"]`, written by the agent and kept distinct from its `guard` (the block pose): Einstein the glowing chalk raised like a duelling wand and a fist up, Joan a two-handed sword guard, Leonardo the brush held like a short sword, Lincoln a wrestler's crouch with the rail held like a quarterstaff, Cleopatra the scepter raised and the free hand clawed like a striking cobra, Tesla the cane held like a sabre with sparks at the free hand, Shakespeare a stage duellist's en garde, Mozart a conductor's raised hands, Tubman the cane raised like a fighting stick and the lantern up. One sheet per character (the `idle` job: the standing reference figure, then four bends of the stance), drawn 2026-10-20; Cleopatra's first sheet (a plunging neckline, off her model sheet) and Tubman's (the cane planted like a walking stick) were redrawn, the rejects kept under `raw/_superseded_2026-10-20/`. | Unassigned | The stance reads as ready to fight, and as distinct from the block, in both modes. |
| **USER-RUN-EIGHT-FRAME-CYCLE** — an 8-frame run cycle | **User request 2026-10-20:** "Run needs more frames, it looks jerky and doesn't look complete." Amends USER-SPRITE-LOOP-FRAMES (run 6 → 8) and the lines it names (`design-godot.md` :3810, the frame table :3820, :3928, :4877), in the master and the mirror. The 6-frame sheets were drawn pose by pose with no cycle structure: the stride and the arms did not alternate consistently. | **Built.** `SHARED_POSES["run"]`: contact, down, passing and flight for the near leg, then for the far leg, with per-character arm phrases (`arms_a`, `arms_b`, `arms_pass`, `run_arms`; Lincoln's rail never swings); `JOB_NOTES["run"]` asks for one seamless cycle whose frames 5–8 repeat 1–4 with the limbs swapped; two rows of the reference figure plus four frames. `FRAME_COUNT["run"]` 8; `CharacterPresentationTests.ExpectedFrameCount` 8. | Unassigned | The run reads as one smooth, complete cycle with no foot skating, in both modes. |
| **USER-FALL-HOLDS-POSE** — the fall plays once and holds | **User request 2026-10-20:** "Falling moves the characters legs up and down until they hit the ground." `design-godot.md` :3822 (the Jump Fall row), master and mirror. | **Built.** `PLAYBACK["fall"]` no longer loops, and `plan.REQUIREMENTS["fall"]` derives "settle": the drawn knees-bent pose first (it leads out of the jump's apex tuck), then the two legs-extended poses, the last held until landing. Story's `PlayAnimation` and the Fighter driver's `PlayPresentationAnimation` restart an animation only when its name changes, so a non-looping fall holds. No new drawing. `CharacterPresentationTests` fails on a looping fall, and on an idle or run that does not loop. | Unassigned | No leg movement while falling, in both modes. |
| **PROVISIONAL-RETRO-V2-LOOP-RULES** — how the new loops are packed | Agent choices, 2026-10-20. | **Built** (`pack.py`). **The bob** (`derive` "bob"): the four drawn bends play in their drawn order, which carries the drawn sway (forward going down, back coming up); a bend deeper than `BOB_MAX_DIP` (6 % of the stance's height; the model drew 9–15 % on most sheets, a squat) is left out and the loop rests a beat on the top frame, so eight characters play top, sinking, rising, top and Mozart all four. A ping-pong through the middle frame was tried first and dropped: it zigzagged the head. **Feet:** each idle frame stands on its own soles ("lowest") and is registered two parts shoes to one part upper body (`REGISTER_BANDS`; about 2 texels of slide against 4 of sway, where the shoes alone swayed the head 6); a run frame with a foot down stands on its own soles and the two flight frames keep their drawn lift above the row's floor, never below it ("cycle" feet, `roster_spec.RUN_FLIGHT_FRAMES`) — standing the frames on the reference figure's feet floated whole runs by up to 12 texels. **Run rate** (`run_fps`): `MaxMoveSpeed` × 2.6 frames per second, rounded and clamped to 10–16, read from the `CharacterData` at pack time so the speed keeps one value: Lincoln 10, Tesla 12, Einstein, Leonardo, Shakespeare, Mozart and Tubman 14, Cleopatra 15, Joan 16, so each stride covers about 0.9 of a body height; a speed change needs a re-pack. **Idle rate** 6 fps (5 before). | Unassigned | A human pass over the bob depth, the sway and the cadence. |
| **FIX-RETRO-V2-SPLIT-STUBS** — a cut left a stub of one figure in its neighbour's frame | Observed 2026-10-20 on Lincoln's new idle sheet: where two figures' props nearly touch, `segment._split` cuts the joined pair down one straight column, so a rail tip past the cut landed in the next frame. | **Fixed** (`segment._return_split_islands`): after ownership is assigned, a piece of a figure that is not connected to its main body but touches another figure's ink goes to that figure, with its faint rim; loose sparks touch nobody and stay. It also corrected four frames of the 2026-10-18 sheets: Leonardo `p07` (boot soles), Tubman `p05` (a dress hem), Einstein `p04` (a chalk tip) and Joan `p05` (a glint), each returned to the figure it belongs to. | — | Closed by the re-pack; the review sheets show no stubs. |
| **FIX-RETRO-V2-ATLAS-STRIPS** — atlases came out as long strips | Observed 2026-10-20: the smallest-area shelf layout put a sheet of equal-height cells on one shelf (Shakespeare's combat atlas 9384 × 280, Lincoln's abilities 6552 × 304), past the 8192 texels some GL3-class GPUs allow. | **Fixed** (`pack.shelf_pack`): no side over `MAX_ATLAS_SIDE` (4096), and among layouts within `AREA_SLACK` (3 %) of the smallest area the squarest wins (Shakespeare's combat atlas is now 2504 × 1080); a sheet no layout can fit stops the pack with an error instead of writing an oversized atlas (the Codex cross-review's nit, 2026-10-21; the re-pack after it was byte-identical). | — | Closed: every roster atlas is at most 4096 on a side. |
| **VERIFY-RETRO-V2-GROUNDED-FLOAT** — some grounded frames of the 2026-10-18 sheets hover | Grounded poses stand on the floor. Measured 2026-10-20 (texels between a frame's feet line and its lowest row of solid ink): Joan `hitstun` 10–12 (her sword tip reaches the floor in two frames, so a "lowest" feet override would not fix it), Shakespeare `defeat` 12, Tesla `death` 6–11, Tubman `basic_attack_1` 11–13 on two frames (the cane tip reaches lower), Cleopatra `death` frame 3 10, and Leonardo `basic_attack_2` +12 and −12 on its first two frames; 12 texels are about 5 world pixels. Those rows were grounded on the sheet's reference figure, whose feet the model drew a few pixels lower than the poses'. | Open. | Unassigned | Each listed frame stands on the floor (per-frame `feet_at` pins after a visual check), or a human pass accepts it. |
| **USER-RETRO-2X-SPRITES** — 2× texel resolution, figures never shrunk to fit a cell, VFX at 384 px | **User ruling 2026-10-18** (question "Resolution", option "2× plus wide attack cells (Recommended)"): "192×256 cells, 384 px wide for attacks/abilities, VFX at 384. Crisper at every resolution … Needs factory scale, glow-edge thickness and VFX call-site scale updates." Built with per-animation cells sized to each animation's art instead of two fixed widths, which serves the same intent (no attack or ability pose is drawn small to fit). | **Built.** `SpriteFrames` metadata `retro_texel_scale` (2.0), `retro_baked_scale` (the figure scale is baked, so the normalizer applies factor 1) and `retro_feet_gaps` (a feet line per animation), read through `FTT.Combat.RetroSpriteMetadata`; `CharacterFactory.CharacterSpriteWorldScale` 0.845 ÷ the texel scale with `LinearWithMipmaps`; `GlowPresentationController` multiplies both outline thicknesses by the texel scale (`outline_glow.gdshader`'s ranges doubled); `VfxEffect` and `AbilityVisualLibrary` divide their call-site scales by it; mipmaps on every v2 atlas. A standing figure is still about 103 world px. Texture memory per character is 41–51 MiB uncompressed with mipmaps (it was about 4 MiB); `pack.py` shelf-packs each atlas (one row per animation left them about 40 % empty) and keeps a 12-texel margin on every side of a cell, under the feet too (the cross-review's finding: a 3-texel feet margin clipped the doubled status outline, up to 5 texels, at the quad edge; the per-animation feet lines absorb the margin, so no figure moved), and trimming each cell's empty space through `AtlasTexture` margins would roughly halve it again if memory ever matters. `CharacterPresentationTests.EveryRosterSpriteSetIsRetroV2AtTwoTimesTexels` fails on a roster set left at 1× or without its feet lines. | Unassigned | Crisp at 1080p, 1440p, 4K and on a Steam Deck (800p), no shimmer, outlines at their old weight. |
| **PROVISIONAL-RETRO-V2-DERIVED-POSES** — poses built in code from drawn ones | Agent choice: image generation is metered, and a code-built subtle loop cannot boil between frames. | **Built** (`pack.py` `derive`): crouch, block and defeat breathe (a 1.4 % and 0.7 % vertical stretch about the feet) from one drawn pose; `ledge_hang` sways ±2° about its grip; `dazed` wobbles ±1.8° about the feet; `respawn` is a cyan hologram of idle frame 0 (scanlines, rim), half-solid, then idle; `ledge_drop` is the hang plus the drawn fall poses 1 and 3 (legs extended, then bent to land); `roll_recovery` ends on idle frame 0; a `skid` whose middle frame the model drew as a full slide reuses its braking pose (Joan, Einstein, Lincoln, Leonardo). | Unassigned | A human pass judges the derived loops; any that read as static get drawn frames. |
| **PROVISIONAL-RETRO-V2-FIGURE-HEIGHTS** — standing heights per character | Agent choice. Hurtboxes stay the universal 40 × 64 px, so heights vary only a little. | **Built** (`roster_spec.CHARACTERS[…]["target_height"]`, 2× texels): Lincoln 258 (with the hat), Tesla 250, Einstein, Leonardo and Shakespeare 244, Joan and Cleopatra 240, Mozart and Tubman 236. Before, every sheet filled 122 of 128 px except Cleopatra 112, Tubman 108 and Shakespeare 98, so Shakespeare and Tubman were drawn small. | Unassigned | A human pass. |
| **PROVISIONAL-RETRO-V2-AIRBORNE-ANCHOR** — an airborne strike's body sits on its hurtbox | Agent choice. The Story Down-Air box is 125 × 125 px centred 62.5 px below the feet (`PlayerController.DownAirHitboxOffset`), so its weapon and impact belong below the body. | **Built.** The packer takes a frame's lowest solid ink as its feet, which on the Down-Air strike was the bottom of the beam, blade or impact, so the body was drawn 40–160 texels above its hurtbox for one frame. The body's lowest point is pinned by hand per frame (`overrides/<id>.json` `feet_at`, keyed by source frame): every Down-Air strike and follow-through, Shakespeare's three Prospero's Flight frames and Mozart's first Sonata Drift frame; the weapon and effect now hang below the origin (Down-Air feet lines 92–141 texels). | Unassigned | No body jumps between a Down-Air's frames; the strike reads where it hits. |
| **FIX-LEDGE-HANG-GRIP** — a hanging character's hands meet the ledge | The hang presents the character gripping the edge. Observed at `d12e8eb`: `ledge_hang` was feet-anchored with its hands about 103 world px above the origin, while Story hangs the origin 42 px below the ledge (`LedgeGrabPointTemplate` `HangAnchor`) and the sim 60 px (`FighterLedgeRules.HangDepth`), so the hands floated 45–60 px above the edge. | **Built.** `ledge_hang` is anchored by its grip, `HANG_GRIP_WORLD_PX` 52 world px above the origin (between the two modes), its legs carried below the origin by its feet line; `ledge_drop` opens on that frame. The 18 px Story/sim difference remains. | Unassigned | Hands on the edge in both modes. |
| **FIX-VFX-PROJECTILE-TRAVEL-FRAME** — a Story projectile froze on its impact frame in flight | Each ability's VFX row is onset / body / burst; a projectile should show its body. Observed at `d12e8eb`: `PlaceholderProjectile` played the non-looping row once (0.3 s) and held frame 2 (the burst) for the rest of the flight. | **Fixed.** `AbilityVisualLibrary.Apply(…, holdFrame)` with `TravelFrame` 1; projectiles hold it. `AbilityVisualPresentationTests`. | — | Closed by the tests. |
| **FIX-FIGHTER-VFX-PROXY-RESTART** — Fighter projectile, construct and zone proxies were stuck on frame 0 | Observed at `d12e8eb`: `FighterSimulationDriver.ConfigureProxy` stopped and hid each proxy's visual every sync, and `ConfigureAbilityProxy` re-applied it from frame 0. | **Fixed.** The proxy path is idempotent (`AbilityVisualLibrary.IsShowing`); projectile proxies hold the travel frame, constructs and zones animate, a released proxy is hidden (`HideAuthoredVisual`). `AbilityVisualPresentationTests`. | — | Closed by the tests. |
| **FIX-STORY-CONSTRUCT-GRAYBOX** — the four Story constructs had no art | Observed at `d12e8eb`: `TeslaCoil`, `ClockworkTurret`, `SerpentNest` and `SonataPlatform` drew a tinted `graybox_tile.svg`. | **Built.** `assets/sprites/constructs/*.png` (2×, mipmapped) on each scene's `Visual` (`tools/retro_sprites/construct_pack.py`); HP bars lifted above the art. Fighter construct proxies still draw the ability's VFX row. | Unassigned | A human pass. |
| **PROVISIONAL-RETRO-V2-VFX-PLACEMENT** — new VFX keep the old rows' footprint | Agent choice: the VFX family scenes draw an atlas cell centred on the spawn point (cast flashes at the caster's feet) with additive blending, and the earlier pass checked the old full-width, bottom-aligned rows in live scenes. | **Built** (`vfx_pack.py`): each new row is fitted to the old row's width and bottom line, at least 55 % and at most 94 % of the 384 px cell, cut from its sheet at the emptiest gutters. | Unassigned | Cast and impact flashes sit where they should; moving cast flashes to the body centre (`PlayerController.BodyCentreGlobalPosition`) is a possible later change. |
| **PROVISIONAL-TUBMAN-RETRO-DESIGN** — Harriet Tubman's look, portrait and effects | Agent design from her kit notes (`design-godot.md` :2960, `docs/handoffs/P13_W5.md`, the `tools/asset_src/tubman/` docstring), replacing the flat vector placeholder (`P13_W5.md`: "nobody has looked at it"). | **Built.** Dark headwrap, grey shawl, brown wool jacket, long dark-blue dress, worn boots, brass lantern and wooden cane; dignified and realistic; a new 512 px retro portrait. Her effects: Conductor's Call as pale-blue spectral Union scouts, Foresight as a gold North Star halo, North Star Leap as a star streak, and The Freedom Line as a spectral locomotive of lantern light (the Underground Railroad's "line"). | Unassigned | The user approves her look and effects. |
