## Confirmed open deviations — feel and UI update, 2026-10-25

The user's seven requests of 2026-10-25 and their four rulings R1–R4 (plan §0 and §0.1), planned in `docs/FEEL_UI_UPDATE_2026-10-25_PLAN.md` (§1 standing decisions, §2 workstreams, §9 deviation log, §10 closeout) and built on worktree branches `fu/menu`, `fu/block`, `fu/lock`, `fu/knock` and `fu/echo` merged into `main` in that order, plus an integration pass (`3d5aa099` regenerated the compiled translation, `7e90d1f5` made two presentation suites run player two as a local human) and a cross-review fix (`07cb30fa`); the handoffs are `docs/handoffs/FU_*.md` and the recon is `docs/recon-2026-10-25/`. Observed values below are read at **`07cb30fa`**, the fully integrated package (full suite `Failed: 0, Passed: 3541, Total: 3541`, three consecutive runs). No save schema (v8), protocol (v4) or Klotho component ID changed; components 313–317 were retired. **Nobody has played or looked at any of it.** Every earlier mention of Echo Step in this file (the 2026-10-16 rows especially) is history; the rows `USER-STORY-ECHO-STEP-COST` (superseded), `DEFER-TOGGLE-BLOCK-ECHO-CHORD` (closed as moot) and the Echo halves of `DEFER-M04-SUBPHASE-FLAGS` and `PROVISIONAL-STORY-METER-GAIN-SCOPE` carry in-place notes.

| ID / area | Intended behavior (decision) | Observed build value (revision) | Owner | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **USER-REMOVE-ECHO-STEP** — Echo Step does not exist in either mode (request 1) | **User request 2026-10-25:** "Remove echo step — it doesn't seem to do much and I think its just adding extra complexity to the game. If you have different thoughts about if we can improve it instead let me know." The counter-proposal given (plan §0.1): remove it as asked — it was unbound by default and reachable only by a chord, overlapped the roll, the air dodge, Time Freeze and the death rewind, spent meter the Ultimate wants, and its 31-sample ring was the largest block in the rollback snapshot; the one variant worth reconsidering later is a meter-priced combo breaker. Nothing replaces it: Block + Roll is the ordinary stance plus roll (block cancel in a recovery, air dodge in the air, nothing on a ledge hang). **What it supersedes:** the V7.6 Echo Step contract (`design-godot.md`, about 60 lines in the master and the mirror; `TEMPORAL_STATE_CONTRACT.md`'s Echo Step section, `COMFORT_SETTINGS.md` C01c's third recipe, `HUD_CONTRACT.md` M25's fourth radial, `CPU_COMBAT_POLICY.md` F19's Echo column, `ROLLBACK_STATE_CONTRACT.md`'s ring), and the rows `USER-STORY-ECHO-STEP-COST` (superseded), `DEFER-TOGGLE-BLOCK-ECHO-CHORD` (moot) and the Echo halves of `DEFER-M04-SUBPHASE-FLAGS` and `PROVISIONAL-STORY-METER-GAIN-SCOPE`. | **Built (FU-ECHO, `fu/echo`, merged; `07cb30fa`).** Removed in both modes: the sim verb and its history ring (`FighterEchoStepRules.cs` deleted; Klotho components **313–317** retired for good; four 310 `FighterVerbComponent` fields; `FighterActionKind.EchoStep` deleted with ordinal 10 left as a gap), the CPU decision (`TryDecideEchoStep`, `CpuVerbAction.EchoStep`, `CpuBandTuning`'s three Echo fields, and the observation and state fields that became dead), the Story ring, wind-up and 10.5-meter cost (the Mirror clone follows), `BasicComboRules`' five Echo constants and the whole `RecoveryVerb` / `SelectRecoveryVerb` chord table, the `gameplay_echo_step` action and its C01c recipe (two recipes remain: Ultimate and Grab), `GameplayButtons` bit 12 (reserved like Dash's bit 11; `DirectOrigin` is now `!grabShortcut`), the Fighter HUD's fourth cooldown radial (P1 and the mirrored P2), the sixth Calibration Drill and its two-beat objective machinery, three core cues, and eight translation keys; the Systems Card's `echo` section became `momentum`; both D3 Roll exclusions were dropped together. **Protocol stays v4; save schema stays v8** (a stale `gameplay_echo_step` row loads and is ignored; `StoryPlayerResourceTimers.EchoStepCooldownFrames` stays as documented dead data). Every Fighter hash moved and the Medium and Hard CPU RNG stream shifted (no seeded expectation flipped). Rollback re-measure on the `fu/echo` branch: largest snapshot **3,277 B** (was 4,473 B), worst-case resimulation 0.090 ms median / 0.137 ms p95, no breach. −26 tests (4 suites and 6 cases deleted; the whiff-recovery case kept as a roll case). Retained Rally "echo" (`EchoPool`, HUD echo bands, `collectsEcho`) is a different system and untouched. | Unassigned | (1) A search for `EchoStep|Echo Step|echo_step|EchoRing` across `scripts/`, `scenes/`, `resources/`, `tests/`, `project.godot` and `localization/en.csv` finds only the retired-ordinal and reserved-bit comments, the dead save field and test names that assert its absence; (2) a hands-on pass confirms Block + Roll behaves as stated and the three-radial HUD reads; (3) the design master, the mirror and the contracts drop the verb (the closeout's design edits). A meter-priced combo breaker may be reopened as a new proposal. |
| **USER-MENUS-CENTERED** — every menu appears centred and on screen (request 2) | **User request 2026-10-25:** "Some menus appear off center and fall off the screen. Look into this and find a fix so that all menus appear centered on the screen." A menu panel is centred under a full-rect container, stays inside the viewport at UI scale 1.0 and the maximum 1.4, and no menu is positioned from its top-left corner at the screen centre. | **Built (FU-MENU, merged; `07cb30fa`).** Four code-built panels called `SetAnchorsPreset(LayoutPreset.Center)` without offsets, so their top-left corner sat on the screen centre: `HolodeckConsolePanel` (the hub Holodeck and the Versus CPU stage phase; measured rect (960, 540, 640 × 629), 89 px off the bottom), `LocalFighterPause` (off-centre by (184, 182)), `NetworkSelectScreen` (LAN is de-scoped) and `TimelineRestartPanel` (centred only by a hard-coded `Position`); each now sits under a full-rect `CenterContainer` (node paths `Panel/Layout/...` → `Center/Panel/Layout/...`; the pause's `PanelCenter`). `FirstUseTooltipPresenter` anchors top-centre with grow both. Found beyond the recon: `ResonanceGridPanel` (the hub Repository terminal and Warden Beacons) and `BossPhaseNotchOverlay` called `SetAnchorsPreset(FullRect)` on a node already in the tree, which recomputes offsets to keep a 0 × 0 rect, so the whole grid was centred on the screen's top-left corner; both now use `SetAnchorsAndOffsetsPreset`. `MenuLayoutBoundsTests` (4 cases: a sweep of every menu surface at UI scale 1.0 and 1.4 with an allow-list of the MainMenu root screen and the first-use tooltip, the pure fit rule, a real shrink, and a source scan for `SetAnchorsPreset(Center…)` without offsets) fails on the unfixed code. +4 tests. Out of scope and open: the visual redesign (`USER-UI-REDESIGN-MOCKUPS`). | Unassigned | A hands-on pass at 1920 × 1080 and at UI scale 1.4 confirms the Holodeck console, the Fighter pause, the collapse choice, the Resonance grid and the Calibration Drill list read centred and complete; sub-panels opened from the pause (Settings, Move List) are measured only as standalone surfaces, which a human pass should also see nested. |
| **PROVISIONAL-MENU-SAFE-AREA-FIT** — the shared fit for tall centred panels | The request gives no fit rule; the agent chose a shrink over a `ScrollContainer` because it keeps node paths, focus chains and authored layouts intact. | **Built** (`scripts/UI/MenuSafeAreaFit.cs`, `MenuSafeAreaFit.Attach(panel)`): a uniform scale of at most 1 so the panel's combined minimum size fits the viewport minus `MarginPixels` **24** per side, floored at `MinScale` **0.5**, written to the centring parent about the screen centre (a `Container` resets its children's scale on every sort); re-applied on resize, minimum-size change, visibility change and viewport resize. Only the Calibration Drill list (nine rows, 1,151 px at 1.4) needs it today, at about 0.9; the MainMenu root panel (886 px at 1.0) passes at 1.4 without it. Tolerances in the sweep: centre 2 px, bounds 1.5 px. | Unassigned | A human pass at UI scale 1.4 on the Holodeck console, the Fighter pause and the Drill list; the user approves or retunes the margin and the floor. |
| **USER-LOCK-NO-CAMERA-SHIFT** — entering an encounter area never pans the camera (request 3, ruling R4) | **User request 2026-10-25:** "When moving into a 'room' in the story mode the player/screen is shifted to the right quickly and there is a blue line showing the edge. Fix the shift and remove the blue line." **Ruling R4 (2026-10-25, "Invisible walls"):** the area still holds the hero but nothing is drawn; "the camera only moves when the hero moves and tightens onto the area as the hero walks in". **What it supersedes:** the GU-E camera-ease rule (`EncounterLockRules.CameraEaseSeconds` 0.6 s, `PROVISIONAL-ENCOUNTER-LOCK-RULES`) and the camera sentence of `docs/handoffs/GU_E.md`. | **Built (FU-LOCK, merged; `07cb30fa`).** The cause was `EncounterLock.Engage` → `StoryCameraConfiner.SetLockBounds`, which eased the Camera2D limits over 0.6 s toward the lock's camera bounds; the hero engages about 44 px inside the area's edge, so the view's edge started about 600 px outside and the confine dragged the camera about 600 px. Replaced by a **ratchet** (`StepRatchet`, `IsRatchetingBounds`, `BeginRatchet`; `BeginBoundsEase`, `StepBoundsEase`, `IsEasingBounds` and `CameraEaseSeconds` deleted): on `SetLockBounds` / `ClearLockBounds` the working limits become the target merged with the current visible rect and are written at once; each follow step, each side then moves toward the target only when the rig's unclamped desired view has passed it, inward and never back. Room `SetBounds` is still swallowed while a lock holds; `SnapToTarget` folds into the ratchet; boss and Mirror framing is untouched; with no rig the target is written at once. `EncounterLockCameraRatchetTests` (7) — with the ratchet forced to write the target directly, 3 of the 7 fail, including the stand-still case (camera 588 px from the hero). | Unassigned | A hands-on entry into a lock from either side and from above and below shows no pan; the view tightens as the hero walks in; Level 3's vertical lock rects and haul-out landings (covered headless only by the vertical unit case) look right. |
| **USER-LOCK-BARRIERS-INVISIBLE** — encounter walls hold the hero with nothing drawn (request 3, ruling R4) | **Ruling R4 (2026-10-25):** the blue line (the closed barrier's `UnboundCold` curtain with hollow diamonds) is removed; the walls remain. **What it supersedes:** the "placeholder look" sentence of the encounter-lock rules and `GU_E.md`'s "Placeholders only: the curtain". | **Built (FU-LOCK).** `EncounterLockBarrier` keeps its collision, `IsClosed`, the closing push-out, groups and `Create` (still `Visible = false`); `_Draw`, the visibility toggling and the glow and pattern constants are deleted. A test asserts a closed barrier blocks a ray but is not visible. | Unassigned | No visible line at any of the 48 locks; a human check that an invisible wall reads acceptably — see `VERIFY-INVISIBLE-ENCOUNTER-WALL-CUE`. |
| **USER-LOCK-HUD-REMOVED** — no "Enemies left" count and no GO prompt in Story (request 3, ruling R4) | **User request 2026-10-25:** "Also remove the 'Enemies left' count and the GO that appears." **What it supersedes:** `USER-ENCOUNTER-LOCKS`'s HUD clause and the owed `HUD_CONTRACT.md` amendment for the lock panel (withdrawn). | **Built (FU-LOCK).** `scenes/ui/StoryHUD.tscn` `SafeArea/EncounterLockPanel`, `scripts/UI/StoryHUD.EncounterLock.cs` and its four hooks in `StoryHUD.cs`, the keys `hud_encounter_enemies_left` and `hud_encounter_go`, and `EncounterLockRules.GoPromptSeconds` / `GoGlyph` are deleted. **Kept:** `EventBus.OnEncounterLockChanged` and `EncounterLockPayload` (including `GoDirection`), the authored `GoDirection` on every lock spec (now read by no presentation), the `EncounterGoDirection` enum, and the objective line (`encounter_lock_objective_clear`). `EncounterLockTests.TheHudCountsTheEnemiesLeftAndShowsTheGoArrowAfterTheClear` and `StoryHudEncounterGoPromptTests` deleted; a ratchet-suite case asserts the HUD has no panel. | Unassigned | No lock text on the Story HUD in a play session; the objective line is unchanged; if the user later wants a count or an arrow back, `GoDirection` is the authored data to read. |
| **VERIFY-INVISIBLE-ENCOUNTER-WALL-CUE** — a player who walks back into a closed encounter wall gets no cue why they stop | R4 asked for invisible walls; a design question the ruling did not answer is whether a hero should get any cue (a flash on contact, a sound, a hint at the camera edge). | Open. Nothing is drawn or played; the ratcheted camera tightens onto the area, which frames the arena but marks no wall. Noted by FU-LOCK as the first thing to check by hand. | Unassigned | A play session finds the invisible walls acceptable, or the user rules a cue and it is built. |
| **USER-FIGHTER-FINISHER-2X** — the Fighter finisher knocks back 2× as far (request 4, ruling R1) | **User request 2026-10-25:** "In fighting mode I want to increase the knockback effect from the final combo hit so the enemy is knocked back further." **Ruling R1 (2026-10-25):** "2× the distance" (chosen over the recommended 1.5×); Story's finisher is unchanged; no per-kit cap. | **Built (FU-KNOCK, merged; `07cb30fa`).** The Fighter finisher launches with horizontal speed 2 × the force and vertical speed the force (`BasicComboRules.FighterFinisherHorizontalScale` 2.0, read through `FighterKnockbackFeelRules`; the intent builds `knockback × 2` with vertical scale 0.5), after the missing-HP ramp and the weight term; DI still rotates the stash. Keeping the vertical speed keeps the arc and airtime, so the travel doubles — scaling both axes would lengthen the airtime past the 24-frame launch hitstun, where post-hitstun air control strips the extra speed. Measured on the default stage at full victim HP (mirror matchups): 2.00–2.14× on every kit that does not reach a wall (Cleopatra 2.09 → 4.17 u, Leonardo 2.60 → 5.25, Tesla 2.70 → 5.43, Einstein 3.17 → 6.54, Shakespeare 3.41 → 7.10, Tubman 3.25 → 6.68, Mozart 3.82 → 8.17, Joan 3.63 → 7.62); Lincoln's 4.98 u becomes 8.85 u and reaches a wall, as do most kits at about 50 % victim HP. `FinisherKnockbackTenths` and `KnockbackMultipliers` (hence Story) are untouched; a source scan fails on the new names outside `scripts/FighterSim/` and `BasicComboRules.cs`. `FighterFinisherKnockbackTests` (3). | Unassigned | A pad session finds the finisher's distance right at spawn distance and at low HP, and Lincoln's wall-reaching range acceptable (the ruling gave no cap); a design-master amendment to the knockback description. |
| **USER-FIGHTER-WALL-BOUNCE** — a launched fighter rebounds off a side wall (request 4, ruling R2) | **User request 2026-10-25:** "I also want there to be wall bounce effects if the enemy gets hit into a wall." **Ruling R2 (2026-10-25, "Bounce, no damage"):** rebound at about half speed, an impact burst, a short freeze and camera shake, once per launch; Fighter only. **What it amends:** `design-godot.md` :112 ("The sides and top of the screen are bounded by solid physical boundaries") gains the bounce. | **Built (FU-KNOCK).** At the side-wall clamp in the movement system: if the position was clamped this tick, the fighter is launched (310 `Tumble` ≠ 0, covering the launch hitstun and the post-hitstun tumble), its `vx` points into the wall at ≥ `FighterWallBounceMinSpeed` **5 u/s** and it has not bounced this launch, then `vx = −vx × FighterWallBounceRestitution` (**0.5**), the bouncing fighter alone takes `FighterWallBounceHitstopFrames` (**6**, raised to 6 and never shortening a longer freeze) and 323 `ActionFlags` bit 4 `ActionFlagWallBounced` (value 16) is set; the flag clears at every launch in `ResolveFighterHit` (`RearmBounce`) and whenever `Tumble == 0`. Bits 8–11 are a wrapping 4-bit bounce counter that `FighterSimulationDriver.WallBounce.cs` edge-detects (a short forward step only, since the review fix) for an impact burst (`res://scenes/vfx/VfxImpact.tscn`, at the chest 40 px up, inset 24 px toward the wall), `CameraShake.Instance.Shake(6, 0.15)` (so the accessibility scale applies) and `HapticFeedbackManager.OnGuardImpact`. No damage, no hitstun change, no meter, no audio cue. Grounded slides, the knockdown roll and the ceiling never bounce. No Klotho ID allocated. `FighterWallBounceTests` (7), `FighterWallBouncePresentationTests` (1). | Unassigned | It reads as a bounce, not a stutter; whether the freeze should also hold the opponent; the design-master line for :112. |
| **USER-FIGHTER-HITSTUN-SHOVE** — a grounded victim is shoved a short way and planted, not slid (request 5, ruling R3) | **User request 2026-10-25:** "In fighting mode when an enemy is hit by an attack the hit stun does a strange slide. The enemy should be more fixed in place and just hit back a bit, try to remove the sliding that is occuring." **Ruling R3 (2026-10-25, "Short shove, scaled"):** strong friction stops a grounded victim within about 6–8 frames, then it is planted for the rest of the stun; light hits move about 0.2 units, heavier ones about 0.5. **What it closes:** the F09 question "grounded knockback has no friction during hitstun", for the Fighter. | **Built (FU-KNOCK).** In the hitstun branch, for a grounded fighter with `DazeFrames <= 0` and `Tumble == 0`, the horizontal speed steps through `FighterKnockbackFeelRules.StepShove` (×`HitstunShoveDecay` **0.79** a tick, snapped to 0 below `HitstunShoveSnapSpeed` **0.25 u/s**), and the hit site multiplies a grounded non-launching hit's horizontal speed by `HitstunShoveScale` **3.0**. Before, the speed was untouched for the whole 30 / 40-frame stun (a constant glide of 0.50–1.28 u for hit 1 and 0.85–2.17 u for hit 2, then a dead stop on the 4-frame run-stop ramp). Now 0.21 u (Cleopatra hit 1) to 0.36 u (Lincoln hit 1) in about 12–13 moving ticks (stopped by 15–17 ticks counting the impact freeze); hit 2 0.26–0.38 u. Launches, airborne non-launchers and the guard-break daze push (±2.0, −1.0 for 60 frames) are unchanged; hazard knockback takes the same path and gets the same shove. `FighterHitstunShoveTests` (7). | Unassigned | A human check that the victim reads planted rather than stiff. **Ruling owed:** the heaviest real grounded shove measures 0.36–0.38 u against the ruling's "about 0.5" (the recon's 0.5–0.6 assumed a weight-1.0 victim; the harness used mirror weights); raising `HitstunShoveScale` 3.0 → 4.0 raises every shove proportionally (0.28–0.51 u) and is left for the user. |
| **USER-STORY-HERO-HITSTUN-SHOVE** — the Story hero's grounded hitstun is the same short shove as Fighter mode's (follow-up to request 5 / R3) | **User ruling 2026-10-26**, asked whether the Story hero, which glided through its hitstun when a mob hit it, should get the same short shove: "Yes, fix it in Story too" - a grounded non-launching hit on the Story hero is the same short shove, then planted, as in Fighter mode; one feel rule across both modes. | **Built (FU-STORYSHOVE).** The three constants are mode-neutral in `BasicComboRules` (`HitstunShoveScale` 3.0, `HitstunShoveDecay` 0.79, `HitstunShoveSnapSpeed` 0.25 u/s; renamed from `Fighter…`). `PlayerController.OnHurtboxHit` multiplies a grounded non-launching hit's horizontal speed by the scale; `ProcessStunned` decays it by the decay each tick (snap below 15 px/s) while the hero is on the floor and not tumbling, and the decay pauses under the hero's own hitstop. Measured on a flat floor, a Standard-mob-style hit (hitstun 0.6 s): Einstein force 2 moves 10 px (0.17 u, stops tick 11), force 5 moves 27 px (0.45 u, tick 15); Lincoln 7.6 px (0.13 u) to 20.7 px (0.34 u); Joan 9.7 to 25.9 px; before, a constant glide of 36 px (0.6 u) to 90 px (1.5 u) for Einstein over the 36-tick stun. Launches (every elite and boss hit, a Standard mob's only if authored), the guard-break daze push, knockdown and airborne hits are unchanged; the Level 13 Mirror clone inherits it; Story mobs keep S1; an environmental hit carries no knockback, so a hazard tick pushes nothing. `StoryHitstunShoveTests` (5). | Unassigned | A human check that the hero reads planted rather than stiff when a mob hits it. |
| **PROVISIONAL-FIGHTER-KNOCKBACK-FEEL** — the agent-chosen knockback numbers and their consequences | R1 gives only the 2×; R2 and R3 give "about half speed", "a short freeze", "about 0.2 / 0.5 units" and "6–8 frames". | **Built.** All in `BasicComboRules`' final section "Fighter knockback feel (2026-10-25)", the single home: bounce threshold 5 u/s (Story D9's 300 px/s), restitution 0.5, freeze 6 ticks, shove scale 3.0, decay 0.79, snap 0.25 u/s (the recon's model, calibrated so the weakest real hit moves about 0.2 u and 90 % is done in about 12 ticks; the snap trades 0.02 u of distance for a stop by about 15 ticks), camera shake 6 / 0.15 s and the 24 px / 40 px burst offsets (placeholders in the family of the block's 3 / 0.08 s shake). **Consequences recorded:** (a) a bounce adds no hitstun, but its 6-tick freeze suspends the victim's hitstun counter like any hitstop, so the stun lasts 6 ticks longer in wall-clock terms; (b) the slam-bounce relaunch in `FighterVerbRules` (~383) does not re-arm the wall bounce, so a slam bounce counts as part of the same continuous tumble; (c) the once-per-launch flag clears only when the tumble ends or a new launch is applied, so a corner-trapped victim can bounce once per finisher; (d) Lincoln's finisher reaches a wall from almost anywhere (the ruling set no cap); (e) every Fighter hash moved (LAN builds from before and after would desync; LAN is de-scoped); (f) no `KLOTHO_DET002`. | Unassigned | The user approves or retunes each number after a pad session; rulings on (a), (b) and (d) if they matter in play. |
| ~~**VERIFY-STORY-HERO-HITSTUN-GLIDE**~~ — the Story hero still glides through its stun — **CLOSED 2026-10-26 by the user's ruling (FU-STORYSHOVE)** | Request 5 and R3 were Fighter mode only; the recon found the same constant-speed glide in the Story hero. | Closed: `USER-STORY-HERO-HITSTUN-SHOVE` below. | Unassigned | Closed. |
| **VERIFY-PRESENTATION-COUNTER-ROLLBACK** — counter-driven presentation edges under a real rollback correction | The wall-bounce burst and the perfect-block flash and spark fire on the rise of a wrapping counter in component 323 (bits 8–11; `BlockTiming` bits 20–23). | Cross-review (round 1, `APPROVE WITH NITS`) found that a rollback correction which lowers a counter could replay the effect; fixed in `07cb30fa`: `FighterSimulationDriver.IsForwardCounterStep` fires only on a short forward step of the wrapping counter, so a lowered or unchanged counter never replays it (`FighterWallBouncePresentationTests.ARisingBounceCounterSpawnsOneImpactBurstAndAnUnchangedOrLoweredOneSpawnsNone`, `FighterBlockSparkTests.ARiseOfThePerfectBlockCountShowsOnePerfectSparkAndALoweredCountShowsNone`). Reasoned and unit-tested, not exercised under a real rollback; LAN is de-scoped. | Unassigned | Re-check when Package 7 brings real rollback: a correction that lowers a counter shows no second effect and a correction that raises it shows exactly one. |
| **USER-BLOCK-SUCCESS-EFFECT** — a successful block is announced by a visual animation (request 6) | **User request 2026-10-25:** "When a block is successful I want there to be some visual animation that occurs that gives a clear indicator that the block was successful." Both modes; three outcomes told apart by shape as well as colour; presentation only. **What it amends:** the Block / feedback lines of the design master (an on-body effect for each of the ordinary, perfect and guard-break outcomes). | **Built (FU-BLOCK, merged; `07cb30fa`).** `scripts/Combat/BlockSparkEffect.cs` (+ `scenes/vfx/BlockSparkEffect.tscn`): a pooled `Node2D` (`PooledNode, IPoolable`) drawn with `_Draw()` primitives, no textures or particles, registering its own pool in `Show(kind, worldPosition, facingRight, parent, scale)` (warm-up 4, capacity 16, RecycleOldest) so no pool config needs a row. Ordinary: a ~110° cyan-white arc across the front plus an inner white arc, a hexagonal facet flash for the first 0.08 s and 6 spark streaks, 0.28 s. Perfect: the arc, a gold-white double ring to 70 / 52 px, a white core flash for 0.06 s and 10 sparks, 0.40 s (the existing 0.12 s sprite tint stays). Shatter (guard break): a dashed ring growing 12 → 46 px and 7 triangular shards under gravity with spin, red-orange, 0.45 s. Bright fills follow `ComfortSettings.FullScreenFlashAllowed` (false: no core flash, facet alpha halved); nothing strobes; no text, no localization, no audio. Hooks: Story `PlayerController.BlockFeedback.cs` (the block branch and `PlayerController.EnvironmentalBlock.cs`'s hazard block, the Mirror clone included; position `GlobalPosition + (facing × 28, −42)`), Fighter `FighterSimulationDriver.BlockFeedback.cs` (the block-charge drop, the daze edge, the perfect-block counter rise; `+ (facing × 30, −46)`, scale 1). `BlockSparkEffectTests` (3), `StoryBlockSparkTests` (5), `FighterBlockSparkTests` (3). Not delivered: no contact point in Fighter (a fixed offset), nothing for enemies or bosses (no block stance), no core audio cue. | Unassigned | A hands-on pass confirms each of the three outcomes reads at once in Story (at the 1.5 zoom) and in Fighter (at the Fighter camera), including when the blocker's front is near a wall. |
| **PROVISIONAL-BLOCK-SPARK-LOOK** — the effect's shapes, colours, sizes, durations and offsets | The request gives none; the plan (§2.3) gives the three kinds, the durations (about 0.28 / 0.4 / 0.45 s) and the placement (the blocker's front at chest height). | **Built.** Colours: ordinary (0.65, 0.95, 1.0); perfect (1.0, 0.92, 0.35), the `UIPalette.GoldBright` family, with white; shatter (1.0, 0.38, 0.12) and (1.0, 0.65, 0.25). Ordinary arc radius 22 → 38 px, line 6 → 1 px; facet radius 16; perfect core radius 14; shatter gravity 420 px/s². Story offset (28, −42) px, Fighter (30, −46) px at scale 1.0, not judged against the Fighter camera framing. | Unassigned | The user approves or retunes the look after a hands-on pass, including the Fighter scale. |

| 1 — remove Echo Step | `USER-REMOVE-ECHO-STEP` | — | Superseded: ~~`USER-STORY-ECHO-STEP-COST`~~. Closed as moot: ~~`DEFER-TOGGLE-BLOCK-ECHO-CHORD`~~. Amended: `DEFER-M04-SUBPHASE-FLAGS`, `PROVISIONAL-STORY-METER-GAIN-SCOPE` | The Echo Step lines of `design-godot.md` and the master (about 60 each, including the Controls table's `DOC-MASTER-UNBOUND-BINDING` lines, edited by hand in each copy); `TEMPORAL_STATE_CONTRACT.md` (the Echo Step section, retired with a banner), `COMFORT_SETTINGS.md` C01c (two recipes), `HUD_CONTRACT.md` M25 (three radials), `CPU_COMBAT_POLICY.md` F19, `ROLLBACK_STATE_CONTRACT.md` (components 313–317 retired). Edited by the design closeout of the same package. |

| 3 / R4 — encounter areas without the shift, the line, the count or the GO | `USER-LOCK-NO-CAMERA-SHIFT`, `USER-LOCK-BARRIERS-INVISIBLE`, `USER-LOCK-HUD-REMOVED` | `VERIFY-INVISIBLE-ENCOUNTER-WALL-CUE` | Amended: `USER-ENCOUNTER-LOCKS`, `PROVISIONAL-ENCOUNTER-LOCK-RULES` (the camera ease, the curtain look and the HUD clause are superseded) | `HUD_CONTRACT.md`: no `EncounterLockPanel`; the owed amendment is withdrawn. |
| 4 / R1, R2 — the Fighter finisher and the wall bounce | `USER-FIGHTER-FINISHER-2X`, `USER-FIGHTER-WALL-BOUNCE` | `PROVISIONAL-FIGHTER-KNOCKBACK-FEEL`, `VERIFY-PRESENTATION-COUNTER-ROLLBACK` | — | `design-godot.md` :112 gains the wall bounce; the combat section's knockback description gains the 2× finisher. |
| 5 / R3 — the hitstun shove | `USER-FIGHTER-HITSTUN-SHOVE`, `USER-STORY-HERO-HITSTUN-SHOVE` (2026-10-26) | ~~`VERIFY-STORY-HERO-HITSTUN-GLIDE`~~ (closed) | The F09 "no friction during hitstun" question closed for the Fighter | The combat section's knockback description gains the shove. |
| 6 — the block-success effect | `USER-BLOCK-SUCCESS-EFFECT` | `PROVISIONAL-BLOCK-SPARK-LOOK` | — | The Block / feedback lines gain the three effects. |
| 7 — UI and HUD redesign | `USER-UI-REDESIGN-MOCKUPS` | — | — | None until a choice is made. |

## Historical leads — dispositioned by Package 11

These four rows were opened as **verification candidates** when no game build was available to this docs workspace. Package 11 supplied that evidence and closed all four. **The records are retained rather than deleted**, per this file's own rule.

| ID / area | Historical lead and current target | Disposition (2026-09-13, Package 11) |
|---|---|---|
| ~~VERIFY-01~~ — Time mechanics | The report describes manual rewind, Stasis Echo and a 12-second cooldown. F03 now specifies Story-only escape Time Freeze for 5 seconds, 45-second cooldown after thaw, no charges and continued Integrity drain; death rewind remains separate. | **Confirmed and resolved.** The lead was accurate: the build did carry manual rewind, the Stasis Echo and a 12 s cooldown. Package 11 A2 **deleted** all three — `StasisEcho.cs`, `ChronalRewindManager`'s whole manual/scrub region, `IRewindScrubbable.CancelRewindScrub` and their translation rows — and shipped `TimeFreezeController` to the contract: 5.000 s / 300 ticks, no charges, a 45 s cooldown armed at thaw, identical on all difficulties, legal at zero rewind charges, not lockable by Suppression, and with `chronal_extractor` deliberately excluded from the freeze sweep so Integrity keeps draining. `TimeFreezeTests` (21 cases) plus a source gate that fails if the retired verb's name reappears anywhere in `scripts/` or `tests/`. The death rewind is untouched. |
| ~~VERIFY-02~~ — Integrity and recovery | The report includes siphon shares/restoration paths that F01 retired. Current design uses normalized live drain, fixed starting denominator, no time refill, role-based PreBoss lock and F10/F11 recovery. | **Confirmed and resolved, with one measurement gap left open.** Package 11 A3 deleted the entire siphon model (the 600 px engagement, the 10 s grace, the 10-point share cap and every restoration path) and rewrote `TimelineIntegrityRules` around normalized live drain, difficulty multipliers 2.0/1.5/1.2, a fixed starting-Extractor denominator, tier lines 50/20 and collapse at zero on every difficulty; A3 also shipped role-based checkpoints and the F11 minima, and A3b shipped the F10 attempt record with status-driven load routing. **The code conformity is closed; the route measurement is not** — see the open `VERIFY-PAR-SECONDS` above, which is exactly the "measured route gaps recorded separately from code conformity" this row asked for. |
| ~~VERIFY-03~~ — Dust and reward commits | The report names 15-dust Extractors, unapplied tier bonuses and a wallet-direct Mirror award. F05 now defines budgeted finite rewards, physical boss pickups and once-only completion banking. | **Confirmed and resolved, with one content gap left open.** Package 11 A10 replaced the per-enemy rate card with 25 authored `LevelRewardManifest` resources compiled per difficulty by `LevelRewardDirectory`: an unauthored source pays nothing (retiring "every enemy guarantees dust"), a difficulty changes the distribution but never the level total, the tier bonus is applied, all sixteen bosses pay 25 as physical pickups, and the Mirror Paradox's wallet-direct path is gone. Claims persist per attempt through `ClaimedRewardSourceIDs`, so a respawned enemy cannot reissue a collected reward. **Open:** the discoverable half of every optional pool has no physical source — see `VERIFY-SECRET-CACHE` above. N01's pending boss pickup is `DEFER-SEALING-ANCHOR`. **Package 12 W2 (2026-09-27):** "once-only completion banking" is now literally true — H02 moved every deposit into `StoryManager.CommitCompletionTransaction` for all levels and deleted the hub's arrival and Repository deposits; the nine Level 4A manifests now bind `.eraser_debut` to `unbound_eraser`, the enemy the trigger actually spawns (GAP-02, 15-dust collection walk pinned in `RewardManifestTests`); and `SummonMinions` spawns carry `IsSummoned` so a summon can no longer consume a skipped mandatory source (GAP-04). Still open: `VERIFY-SECRET-CACHE`, and the Eraser's interim `ChronalDustDrop` (D15, held). |
| ~~VERIFY-04~~ — Content and onboarding | The report describes deferred Open stages/drills and missing placements; current decisions require launch drills, Open stages before Fighter tuning, and 24 campaign level/boss slots including nine Legacy routes. | **Confirmed and resolved as coverage; unreviewed as experience.** Package 11 A9 re-authored Paris, Vesuvius and Nassau as Open stages with real, reachable pits and floor-segment ledges — before any Fighter balance pass, as V7.6 2.D requires. A11 shipped F18's six Calibration Drills with a standalone main-menu route available on a first boot with no Story save. A12 plus B1/B2/B3 authored all nine Level 4A routes with their own bosses under `resources/Bosses/legacy/`, taking the campaign to 24 non-tutorial boss encounter slots (15 shared + 9 Legacy) and seventeen route slots. A7a placed the Eraser and gave the Chrono-Warden its first spawn sites. **This row's own caution still bites in the other direction:** coverage is proved by test, but **nobody has played a drill, a 4A variant or an Open-stage match**, so nothing here is validated as an experience. Production art for all of it remains Package 10 (`PRODUCTION_SCOPE.md`). |

| Source | Current availability and role | Follow-up |
|---|---|---|
| Main GDD, adopted contracts and HTML mirrors | Available intended-design documents, including current edits. | Commit/pin the reviewed revision when establishing a build comparison; this task does not create a commit or pretend working-tree edits are already pinned. |
| [F05 dust economy](DUST_ECONOMY.md) | Available current replacement specification with per-level budgets. It is not a recovered copy of the older Package 5 economy and not proof of placed pickups. | Compare against verified game reward manifests when available. |
| `docs/PACKAGE5_CAMPAIGN_PLAN.md` | ~~Missing from this checkout~~ — **corrected 2026-09-13 (Package 11 Phase C): the file IS present in the game repository**, at that exact path, complete with its per-level dossiers, the authored boss stat table (§4.1) and a long §9 deviation log. The "unavailable source" flag was a property of the isolated docs workspace, not of the project. | **No recovery work is needed.** The flag in the design master should be corrected the next time the master is edited. Earlier claims of authored Levels 2–15 are verifiable and were verified: all sixteen campaign scenes exist and are covered by per-level content suites. |
| Game code/resources, scenes, builds and test reports | ~~Not available here~~ — **corrected 2026-09-13.** Package 11 was implemented directly in the game repository against a pinned baseline (`31fed14`, 1638 passing tests, clean tree), with nine reconnaissance dossiers reading the build in full and twenty workstreams verifying their own areas. The confirmed entries in the Package 11 table above carry file identifiers, branch names and test evidence. | Keep pinning a revision on every new entry. The evidence standard this row asked for is now met and should stay met: a resource path alone is still not proof that the game loads or applies the value. |

If an original document is later recovered, retain its repository/archive location and immutable commit/release or content hash plus date. Reconcile it against current accepted rules before using it as current authority. Do not revive old reward budgets, checkpoints, time mechanics or retired content just because they appear in a pinned historical file.

P04 delivers the authority rule and ledger structure. **Package 11 (2026-09-13) delivered the first build verification against it**, closing VERIFY-01 through VERIFY-04 and opening ten confirmed/deferred entries in their place. The listed implementation and content work remains pending as itemized above.

**How to use this file.** Before assuming a shipped number is the target, search it here. Before deferring something, add a row with a stable ID, the intended behaviour and its decision reference, the observed value with its pinned commit and file identifier, an owner (`Unassigned` unless claimed), and acceptance criteria concrete enough that someone else can tell when it is done. Never delete a closed row — disposition it in place.
